# Development Log

Everything about how this app came to be built the way it is: the decisions made, what got built, what deliberately deviated from the original plan and why, and the bugs found along the way (including *why* they happened, not just what fixed them). Organized by feature/area, not by when the work happened — within a section, older material comes first so the reasoning still reads in the order it accumulated. See `docs/TODO.md` for what's still outstanding, and `docs/CHANGELOG.md` for a terse, commit-by-commit record of *what* changed without the rationale.

## Origins

This app started as a migration of an earlier static HTML/CSS/JS game suite (Boggle, Yahtzee, Family Feud) built for photosensitive and migraine-prone users, into a proper Laravel application: user accounts with passkey authentication, game history/stats, and real-time multiplayer via Laravel Reverb, while preserving the low-stim design language (no animation, no flashing, muted low-contrast palette) exactly.

Foundational decisions made before any code was written:
- Custom `docker-compose.yml` (not Sail), with MariaDB + Redis.
- Fortify + `laravel/passkeys` for auth — no Breeze; custom Blade/Livewire views throughout.
- Boggle built first, to validate the architecture (service classes, Room/multiplayer system, stats) before repeating the pattern for Yahtzee and Family Feud.
- Tailwind v4 for styling, with the legacy CSS ported in as real classes rather than reinterpreted as Tailwind utilities (see "Site-wide design system & chrome" below).

**The `legacy/` directory (the original static suite, kept around as a migration reference) was removed once the migration was confirmed complete (2026-08-17).** A final pass compared every file in it against this app: all five original pages (Boggle, Boggle group-play, Yahtzee, Yahtzee dice-only roller, Family Feud, About) have real equivalents; `shared.css`/`boggle-shared.css`'s design tokens were ported near-verbatim into `resources/css/app.css`; `words.txt` was copied to `storage/app/words.txt`; the Netlify judge function became `JudgeService` (`OPENROUTER_API_KEY`); and every static asset (favicons, `manifest.json`, `sw.js`, `og-image.png`) was already copied into `public/`. `legacy/` was its own nested git repository with a separate GitHub remote (confirmed clean and fully pushed before deletion), not a submodule of this repo, so removing it locally doesn't lose its history. `README.md` was rewritten from scratch at the same time — it had been tracking migration status (a "what's ported so far" framing) rather than describing the app as it exists now.

## Infrastructure & tooling

- Custom `docker-compose.yml` + nginx config + PHP Dockerfile (`app`, `nginx`, `mariadb`, `redis` services).
- `.env` updates (MariaDB, Redis, Reverb keys).
- Installed Fortify, Livewire, Reverb, Larastan.
- Tooling: `phpstan.neon` (level 5), pre-commit hook, composer scripts (`format`/`analyse`/`check`).
- Design system: CSS tokens, dark mode, global no-animation rule.

Decisions and bugs along the way:
- Larastan is wired via a direct `includes: vendor/larastan/larastan/extension.neon` in `phpstan.neon` rather than `phpstan/extension-installer`, to avoid adding another Composer plugin to `allow-plugins`.
- Design tokens in `resources/css/app.css` use Tailwind v4's class-based dark mode (`@custom-variant dark (&:where(.dark, .dark *))`). The base `@theme` values are the *light* theme; `.dark` overrides them with the muted palette that is this app's actual default appearance (legacy's default, unmarked theme).
- Did **not** install the standalone `alpinejs` npm package (removed after installing it) — Livewire v4 bundles its own Alpine.js instance and injects it via `@livewireScripts`; adding a second copy causes a documented "multiple instances of Alpine" runtime error. Any `x-data` interactivity relies on Livewire's bundled Alpine.
- End-to-end verification via the actual `docker compose` stack (not just `php artisan test` on the host) surfaced three real infrastructure bugs, all fixed in `docker/php/Dockerfile`/`docker-compose.yml`:
  1. `pecl install redis` was failing (`phpize`/`autoconf` not found) because the preceding `docker-php-ext-install` step installs its own build toolchain and removes it again within that same `RUN` layer. Fixed by wrapping it in its own `apk add --virtual .build-deps ${PHPIZE_DEPS} && ... && apk del .build-deps`.
  2. `app`, `queue`, and `reverb` each declared their own **anonymous** `/var/www/html/vendor` volume — anonymous volumes are per-service, not shared, so `composer install` inside `app` never reached the other two, which crash-looped on a missing `vendor/autoload.php`. Fixed by switching to named, shared volumes (`vendor_data`, `node_modules_data`).
  3. Compiled Blade/Livewire view caches under `storage/framework/views/` record the **absolute source path** for staleness checks (`filemtime()`), and that directory was bind-mounted — shared byte-for-byte between host and container. Running tests on the host then hitting routes through docker (or vice versa) produced `filemtime(): stat failed` errors and pages silently rendering as the default `welcome` view. Fixed with its own named volume (`storage_views_data`), seeded as the `nobody` user (php-fpm's configured user) via `mkdir -p storage/framework/views && chown -R nobody:nobody storage/framework/views` in the Dockerfile.
  4. Separately: after `docker compose up -d` recreates a service (e.g. `app`) without also recreating `nginx`, nginx keeps proxying to the old container's now-stale IP and returns 502 until `docker compose restart nginx` (`fastcgi_pass` resolves the hostname once, not per-request). Not a bug, just an operational step to remember.
- Bumped `phpunit.xml`'s `memory_limit` to 512M — Boggle's `Solver` (see the Boggle section below) builds an ~80k-word dictionary per test process, and a whole test file resolving it repeatedly (Laravel's `TestCase` boots a fresh app per test) could exceed the 128M CLI default and fail with an unhelpful empty/truncated test run.
- **Laravel Cloud build failure: `npm ci` missing a platform-specific lock file entry.** Adding `playwright` as a devDependency (for the Pest browser-testing setup, see below) triggered `npm install`, which rewrote parts of `package-lock.json` — and exposed a pre-existing gap in it: `@napi-rs/wasm-runtime` (a transitive optional dependency of Vite 8's Rolldown bundler) declares a peer dependency on `@emnapi/runtime@^2.0.0-alpha.3`, but the lock file only had a separate, non-satisfying `1.11.1` copy resolved elsewhere in the tree for a different platform variant. This didn't surface in local `npm ci` runs on macOS/arm64 (the platform the lock file was generated on) — only in Laravel Cloud's Linux build container, which needed the platform-specific resolution the existing lock file never captured. Fixed by deleting `package-lock.json` entirely and letting `npm install` regenerate it from `package.json` (unchanged) in one pass, which correctly recomputes the full cross-platform optional-dependency graph rather than incrementally patching it. Verified with a genuine clean-room `npm ci` (no `node_modules` present) and `npm run build` succeeding locally before committing — a `--dry-run` or a `npm ci` with `node_modules` already present will not reliably catch this class of bug, since it doesn't force full re-resolution the way a truly clean install does.

**`docker compose exec app php artisan test` failing/hanging where host `php artisan test` passed clean, root-caused instead of worked around (2026-08-18).** The host machine's own PHP (Homebrew) and the container's PHP (`docker/php/Dockerfile`, Alpine-based) had quietly drifted apart over the course of adding several dependencies via the host's `composer`/`npm` directly — `vendor_data`/`node_modules_data` are separate named volumes from the host's own copies (see this section's own entry above on why those are named volumes at all), so nothing about installing a package on the host ever reaches the container automatically, and nobody had run the equivalent install inside the container since. Diagnosed by running `docker compose exec app php artisan test --testsuite=Unit,Feature` directly and reading each real failure rather than guessing:
- **`pestphp/pest-plugin-browser` wasn't in the container's `vendor/` at all** (`composer install` inside the container had never been re-run since that plugin was added) — every Browser test failed immediately with "Using the visit() function requires the Pest Plugin Browser to be installed." `docker compose exec app composer install` alone then failed on a second, real gap: the plugin requires `ext-sockets` (its Playwright driver talks over a raw websocket), which the Alpine image never had — added `sockets` to `docker-php-ext-install`, plus the `linux-headers` apk package its compile step actually needs (`sockets.c` includes `linux/sock_diag.h`, present on Debian/Ubuntu images by default but not Alpine's).
- **`gd` was built without JPEG support** — `libpng-dev` was already installed, but nothing ever passed `--with-jpeg` to `docker-php-ext-configure gd`, so `imagejpeg()` didn't exist. Silent on every route that doesn't touch it, but `Illuminate\Http\Testing\FileFactory` (behind `UploadedFile::fake()->image('*.jpg')`, used by the avatar-upload tests added this same session) calls it directly and threw a `LogicException` at runtime, not a build-time failure. Added `libjpeg-turbo-dev` and an explicit `docker-php-ext-configure gd --with-jpeg` step before the install.
- **`ext-intl` was never installed at all** — present by default on the host's Homebrew PHP (masking this entirely in every host test run all session), but Alpine's base image doesn't bundle it. `App\Analytics\Geo\CountryNames::name()` already guards this correctly (`class_exists(Locale::class)` returns false, falls back to the raw country code instead of throwing), so this didn't crash — it just silently degraded `DashboardTest`'s expected "United States" down to "US", a genuine assertion failure once the other two gaps above stopped masking it by failing first. Added `icu-dev`/`intl` to the Dockerfile, and declared `ext-intl` as a real `composer.json` platform requirement (`CountryNames.php`'s own docblock already claimed it was "already required by this app" — it wasn't, until now).
- **Confirmed real, not fixable by more packages: Playwright's Chromium binaries are glibc-linked and cannot run on Alpine (musl libc) at all.** `npx playwright install` downloads successfully (with an explicit "your OS is not officially supported" warning, silently falling back to an Ubuntu build) but fails to launch with `spawn ... ENOENT` — Alpine's kernel can't resolve the glibc dynamic linker path baked into the binary's ELF header, a well-known category of Alpine/Playwright incompatibility, not a missing file or permission issue (confirmed the binary exists, is executable, correct architecture). Asked the user directly rather than picking a fix unilaterally, since the real fix (rebasing the whole image to Debian/Ubuntu) is a substantially bigger, riskier change than everything else in this entry: **decided to keep the Alpine image and exclude the Browser suite from docker-run tests specifically** (`docker compose exec app php artisan test --testsuite=Unit,Feature`), with the *host* `php artisan test` (unaffected, real Chromium works fine there) remaining the way to run Browser tests and the full suite together. Not revisited unless docker-native browser testing becomes a real requirement later.
- A `docker compose exec app npx playwright install` run, done *before* `npm install` had put `playwright` in the container's own `node_modules`, tripped npm's package-not-found-will-be-installed fallback path and corrupted the *host's* bind-mounted `package-lock.json` in the process (renamed its root `name` to `"html"`, dropped several `peer: true` flags) — caught via a routine `git status`/`git diff` before committing anything, not by a test failure, and reverted with a plain `git checkout -- package-lock.json` rather than hand-fixing the diff. Worth remembering: running `npx <package>` for a package not yet actually installed can rewrite the lock file as a side effect, even when the intent was just "run a CLI command."

## Authentication & passkeys

- Fortify + passkeys config, custom auth views (no Breeze templates).
- Profile extension migration (`username`, `is_public`) + profile view.

Decisions and bugs along the way:
- `laravel/passkeys` is not a separate install step — Fortify v1.37 (resolved by `composer require laravel/fortify`) depends on it directly and ships native passkey support via `Features::passkeys()` in `config/fortify.php`, including `PasskeyAuthenticatable`/`PasskeyUser` contracts. No standalone `passkeys:install` command exists.
- The `@laravel/passkeys` **npm** package (`resources/js/app.js`) is still needed separately — that's the browser-side WebAuthn ceremony helper, unrelated to the server-side package above.
- Ran `fortify:install`; enabled `registration`/`resetPasswords`/`emailVerification`/`updateProfileInformation`/`updatePasswords`/`passkeys` in `config/fortify.php` (`twoFactorAuthentication` left disabled — not in scope, see `docs/TODO.md`). `User` implements `MustVerifyEmail` + Fortify's `PasskeyUser`/`PasskeyAuthenticatable`. No standalone `config/passkeys.php` — Fortify owns passkey config directly under `fortify.passkeys` (`relying_party_id`/`allowed_origins`/`timeout`), derived from `APP_URL`. Built `resources/views/layouts/app.blade.php` (theme-init script + footer toggle) and `resources/views/auth/{login,register,forgot-password,reset-password,verify-email,confirm-password}.blade.php`, wired via `Fortify::viewPrefix('auth.')`. Passkey WebAuthn ceremonies (login, confirm) are wired via `@laravel/passkeys`' vanilla JS API in `resources/js/app.js`, triggered by `[data-passkey-login]`/`[data-passkey-confirm]` buttons — passkey *registration* (`/user/passkeys`) requires an authenticated user, so that UI lives on the profile page, not the login/register screens. Added a placeholder `/home` route+view as the post-login destination until the real dashboard existed. Added `tests/Feature/Auth/{RegistrationTest,AuthenticationTest}.php`.
- Added `username`/`is_public` columns (migration `add_profile_fields_to_users_table`), added both to `User`'s `#[Fillable]` list and cast `is_public` to `boolean`. Built the profile editor as a Livewire v4 full-page single-file component at `resources/views/pages/⚡profile.blade.php`, routed via `Route::livewire('/profile', 'pages::profile')` (the ⚡ prefix and `pages::` namespace are Livewire v4 defaults). It lists/deletes the user's passkeys (via `$user->passkeys()`) and exposes an "Add a Passkey" button wired to the same `@laravel/passkeys` client used on the auth pages. Found and fixed a real bug: `UserFactory` didn't set `is_public` explicitly, so a freshly-created-but-not-reloaded `User` instance had `is_public === null` in memory (a DB-side default Eloquent doesn't pull back after insert), throwing a `TypeError` when assigned to the component's `bool $is_public` property. Fixed by setting `is_public => true` explicitly in the factory. Added `tests/Feature/ProfileTest.php`.

**Passkey login "remember me", the password-confirmation dead end, and native browser dialogs throughout passkey management.** Three related reports: passkey logins never persisted past the normal 2-hour session lifetime (password-login sessions already had a working "Remember me" checkbox — Fortify's `AttemptToAuthenticate` already wires `$request->boolean('remember')`, nothing to fix there); adding a passkey from `/profile` just showed an alert reading "Password confirmation required." and went nowhere; and naming/removing a passkey used `window.prompt()`/`wire:confirm`'s native `confirm()`, both OS-chrome dialogs this app's low-stim design avoids everywhere else.
- **Passkey remember-me**: `@laravel/passkeys`' JS client (`node_modules/@laravel/passkeys/dist/passkeys-yRK3MD0m.js`) hardcodes its login request body to `{ credential }` — there's no way to send a `remember` field through its public API, so the backend's `PasskeyVerificationRequest::remember()` (`$this->boolean('remember', false)`) could never see anything but its `false` default. Fixed by binding `Laravel\Passkeys\Http\Requests\PasskeyVerificationRequest::class` to a new `App\Http\Requests\RememberedPasskeyVerificationRequest` (in `AppServiceProvider::register()`) that overrides `remember()` to always return `true` — passkeys are already phishing-resistant public-key credentials, so treating every passkey login as "remembered" was the simplest correct default. The same request class also backs the *password-confirmation* passkey ceremony (`PasskeyConfirmationController`), but that controller never calls `remember()`, so the override is a no-op there.
- **Inline password-confirmation modal, replacing a dead-end redirect**: `config/fortify.php`'s `Features::passkeys(['confirmPassword' => true])` gates `/user/passkeys/options`/`/user/passkeys`/`/user/passkeys/{id}` behind Laravel's `password.confirm` middleware. `Passkeys.register()` calls those via `fetch()` with `Accept: application/json`, so a stale/missing confirmation trips `RequirePassword`'s JSON branch (423, `{"message": "Password confirmation required."}`) instead of its redirect branch — meaning `session('url.intended')` never gets set. The original `[data-passkey-register]` handler just alert()'d that raw message and stopped. Rather than bouncing the user to the standalone `/user/confirm-password` page (which would've needed a second, separate click to resume the passkey ceremony after redirecting back — losing the click that WebAuthn requires), `resources/views/pages/⚡profile.blade.php` now has an in-page `<dialog>` that confirms via `fetch()` and, on success, immediately retries the original action within the same call chain — same trick as the "Confirm with a passkey" flow already used, chained: WebAuthn's `navigator.credentials.create()` needs a live user gesture, and a quick same-task `await fetch()` doesn't break the browser's "sticky" activation the way a full page navigation does, so confirming and continuing feels like one action instead of two. `config/fortify.php` still gained `redirects.password-confirmation => '/profile'` as a non-JS fallback for the standalone confirm page.
- **Root cause of a much bigger, pre-existing bug found while wiring the modal**: wrong-password submissions silently appeared to *succeed*. Traced to `bootstrap/app.php`'s `shouldRenderJsonWhen(fn ($request) => $request->is('api/*'))` — copied from Laravel's docs example for API-only apps, but this app has zero `api/*` routes. It meant every `ValidationException` thrown outside `api/*` (wrong password on `/user/confirm-password`, and — previously unnoticed — wrong credentials on `/login` too) rendered as an HTML `back()->withErrors()` redirect regardless of the request's `Accept` header, instead of Laravel's own default (`$request->expectsJson()`). `fetch()` follows redirects transparently, so the confirm-password modal's `!response.ok` check saw a followed 200 and treated a wrong password as confirmed. Fixed by removing the override entirely, restoring Laravel's default — verified directly against the route (bypassing the JS) with curl before and after, and confirmed `/login`'s own JSON error path was equally broken beforehand. No prior code in the app depended on the broken behavior (plain `<form>` posts never send `Accept: application/json`, so this never affected non-JS form submissions), so this was a pure latent bug, not a deliberate choice being overridden.
- **Naming, password-confirming, and removing a passkey all use custom `<dialog>`s now** (matching the "Rules" dialog pattern already in `layouts/app.blade.php`), replacing `window.prompt()`, `wire:confirm`, and every `alert()` in the passkey flows (including the sign-in and standalone confirm-password pages' failure alerts, now inline `<p>` errors). Removing a passkey also now requires password confirmation for the first time — `pages::profile`'s old `deletePasskey()` deleted the model directly via Eloquent, bypassing `password.confirm` entirely; it's gone, replaced by a `fetch()` `DELETE` straight to the passkeys package's own `/user/passkeys/{id}` route (with a hand-rolled `X-XSRF-TOKEN` header read from the cookie, the same fallback `@laravel/passkeys` itself uses since there's no `csrf-token` `<meta>` tag in the layout), which already had `password.confirm` wired up and just wasn't being used.
- Added `tests/Feature/Auth/PasskeyTest.php` (remember-me override + its container binding, the confirm-password redirect target, and adding/removing a passkey requiring confirmation, including a cross-user ownership check on delete). Full WebAuthn ceremonies aren't covered by Pest (they need real signed assertion data from a virtual authenticator) — verified manually instead via Claude in Chrome against the local Docker stack: wrong password shows the inline error and stays put; correct password closes the confirm dialog and automatically retries registration in the same gesture (observed reaching the real WebAuthn call, which then fails with "The passkey operation was cancelled." since the automated browser has no platform authenticator — shown inline, not alert()'d); and a seeded passkey's full remove flow (custom confirm dialog → DELETE → list refresh) end to end.

**Sessions should stay signed in a long time, "maybe indefinitely"** (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #1, originally filed as "mobile users get signed out frequently" — that specific mobile-only framing was never confirmed and turned out not to be the point; it's just the same fixed-length session expiring, more noticeable on a phone that gets opened sporadically after long idle gaps than on a desktop used continuously within the window). Two changes:
- **The password-login "Remember me" checkbox (see above — the mechanism already worked, it just defaulted unchecked) now defaults to checked** (`@checked(old('remember', true))` in `auth/login.blade.php`), bringing plain password logins in line with passkey logins, which have always force-remembered unconditionally since the entry above. Still a real checkbox, not force-true like passkeys — password is a weaker credential than a passkey, so leaving a shared/public-device opt-out makes sense here in a way it didn't there.
- **`SESSION_LIFETIME` raised from the framework default of 120 (2 hours) to 525600 (1 year)** — in `.env`, `.env.example`, and `config/session.php`'s own `env()` fallback (so a fresh clone/deploy that never sets the env var explicitly still gets the long lifetime, not the framework's short one). This is the plain, non-remembered session's own idle timeout and the session store's GC sweep window; "Remember me" checked (now the default) rides on top of it via Laravel's own ~5-year remember-cookie mechanism regardless, but this covers the case a session isn't remembered for whatever reason (checkbox unchecked, or the identical guest-session path) without falling back to a 2-hour cliff.
- `.env` is gitignored, so this only affects this machine directly — a Laravel Cloud (or any other) deployment needs its own `SESSION_LIFETIME` environment variable updated separately; `.env.example` is what documents the intended value for any future setup.
- Added two tests to `tests/Feature/Auth/AuthenticationTest.php`: the login form's checkbox renders checked by default, and a login with `remember` truthy actually populates `remember_token`.

**Two-factor authentication toggle (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #2, 2026-08-18).** `Features::twoFactorAuthentication(['confirmPassword' => true])` was left disabled at Fortify install time (see above) — enabled now, matching `Features::passkeys()`'s own `confirmPassword` setting. The `add_two_factor_columns_to_users_table` migration already existed unused since that same install; no new migration needed, just `TwoFactorAuthenticatable` added to `User`'s trait list (and its two secret/recovery-code columns added to `#[Hidden]`, alongside the existing `password`/`remember_token`).
- **Login challenge view**: `resources/views/auth/two-factor-challenge.blade.php`, resolved automatically by the existing `Fortify::viewPrefix('auth.')` call (no new `Fortify::twoFactorChallengeView(...)` wiring needed — the prefix convention already covers it). A single page with two forms (authentication code, recovery code) toggled via a plain button — `app.js`'s new toggle listener, not inline `<script>`, matching every other page-specific interaction in this app living there instead.
- **Management UI lives on `/profile`, reusing the passkey management dialogs' own `confirmPassword()`/`csrfHeaders()` helpers** rather than duplicating them — Fortify's 2FA endpoints (`/user/two-factor-authentication`, `/user/confirmed-two-factor-authentication`, `/user/two-factor-recovery-codes`) require the identical password-confirmation dance (`confirmPassword`'s config option) that adding/removing a passkey already does, returning the same 423 on an unconfirmed session. New panel shows "Enable Two-Factor Authentication" or, once `two_factor_confirmed_at` is set, "View Recovery Codes"/"Disable Two-Factor Authentication" (the disable path reuses `<x-confirm-dialog>`, same as Danger Zone's Clear Stats/Delete Account, since it's a plain confirm-then-fetch rather than a multi-step setup ceremony). Two new plain `<dialog>`s (matching the existing passkey-dialog visual pattern, not Livewire-driven — these are vanilla-JS `fetch()` calls straight to Fortify's own routes, the same reasoning passkey management already uses): a setup dialog (QR code rendered from `/user/two-factor-qr-code`'s `{svg}` response, code input, POSTs to `/user/confirmed-two-factor-authentication`) and a recovery-codes dialog (`GET`/`POST /user/two-factor-recovery-codes` for viewing/regenerating). Closing the recovery-codes dialog reloads the page, the same "let the next server render pick up the real state" pattern the passkey add/remove flows already use, rather than trying to keep `$twoFactorEnabled` in sync with actions Livewire never sees.
- Added `tests/Feature/TwoFactorAuthenticationTest.php` (real HTTP requests against Fortify's own routes end-to-end — enable → confirm with a real TOTP computed via `PragmaRX\Google2FA\Google2FA::getCurrentOtp()` → recovery codes → disable — plus the 423-without-confirmation and profile-page-copy cases) and `tests/Browser/ProfileTwoFactorAuthenticationTest.php` (the full click-through UI flow in a real browser, per `CLAUDE.md`'s browser-testing rules — this is exactly the class of thing a Livewire-only test can't see, since none of these dialogs or endpoints go through Livewire at all). The disable-flow browser test also had to complete a real `/two-factor-challenge` login first (a seeded-already-confirmed user doesn't reach `/dashboard` straight from `/login` the way every other browser test's login helper assumes) and re-confirm password separately from the enable test, since logging in doesn't itself set the session's password-confirmation timestamp — only an explicit `/user/confirm-password` call does.

## Game tracking data model

- `GameSession`/`GameParticipant` models, migrations, and factories.

Decisions:
- `type`/`mode`/`result` are plain `string` DB columns backed by PHP enums (`App\Enums\GameType`, `GameMode`, `GameResult`, string-backed, TitleCase cases) cast via Eloquent's `casts()`, rather than native MySQL `enum` columns — easier to extend later without an `ALTER TABLE`. `game_participants.user_id` is `nullable()->nullOnDelete()` so deleting a `User` preserves game history instead of cascading. `GameSession`/`GameParticipant` factories set every column explicitly rather than relying on DB-side defaults Eloquent wouldn't see until a fresh reload. Added `tests/Feature/Models/{GameSessionTest,GameParticipantTest}.php`.

## Room system (shared multiplayer infrastructure)

Built alongside Boggle, reused as-is for Yahtzee's later online-rooms work (see that section) and scoped for Family Feud's own follow-up (`docs/TODO.md`).

- `rooms` table + `App\Enums\RoomStatus` + `Room` model.
- Livewire `GameLobby` component: real-time player list, ready states, host-only config panel, start button.

Decisions and bugs along the way:
- Room system required actually finishing Reverb/broadcasting installation, which infrastructure setup had only partly done (package installed, nothing configured). `php artisan install:broadcasting --reverb` crashed mid-command in this sandbox (no TTY for its npm step), leaving `config/broadcasting.php` + `routes/channels.php` published but no `REVERB_*` env vars and no `laravel-echo`/`pusher-js` npm packages — critically, that half-applied state meant `routes/channels.php` unconditionally crashed **every** artisan command (`Broadcast::channel()` eagerly resolves the `reverb` broadcaster, which throws if the Reverb env vars are null). Fixed by hand: added `REVERB_APP_ID`/`KEY`/`SECRET` + `REVERB_HOST=reverb`/`PORT=8080`/`SCHEME=http` (server-side, internal docker network) and mirrored `VITE_REVERB_*` (client-side, `VITE_REVERB_HOST=localhost`) to `.env`/`.env.example`; ran `npm install laravel-echo pusher-js` manually; ran `php artisan reverb:install` after. **If a fresh clone ever needs this re-run, expect the same TTY crash in non-interactive/CI shells — use the manual env-var + npm-install fallback instead of re-running `install:broadcasting`.**
- Room codes are generated in a `static::creating()` hook using a hand-written random-character-picker (**not** `Str::random()`, which has no charset parameter despite looking like it might) over an alphabet that excludes `0/O/1/I` since codes are read aloud/typed on phones. `Room::notExpired()` local scope. Chose **not** to add a `room_participants` table — "who's currently in the room" is inherently presence-channel membership (ephemeral, tracked by Echo/Reverb), and pre-game "ready" state is similarly transient, so both live only in `GameLobby`'s server-side state, reconstructed from broadcast events, not a DB table (this assumption was later revisited for reconnect-across-the-lobby, see Yahtzee's online-rooms section below — the DB table added there, `RoomSeat`, is scoped to *seat assignment*, not to re-introducing a general "who's in the room" table).
- `App\Events\RoomPlayerReadyToggled` and `RoomGameStarting` broadcast on a `PresenceChannel("room.{code}")`; `routes/channels.php` authorizes by checking the room exists and isn't expired, returning `{id, name, is_host}` (the presence-channel member payload).
- `GameLobby` (`resources/views/components/⚡game-lobby.blade.php`) is a plain **embeddable** Livewire SFC (not a full-page/`pages::` component) since it's meant to be dropped into each game's own room page. Listens for presence events via `getListeners()` returning `echo-presence:room.{code},{here,joining,leaving,RoomPlayerReadyToggled,RoomGameStarting}` — Livewire wires these to Echo automatically as long as `window.Echo` exists (`resources/js/echo.js`).
- Found and fixed a real bug while writing `GameLobbyTest`: the "Start game" button's `@disabled(! $this->allReady)` was cosmetic HTML only — the server-side `startGame()` action didn't actually check `allReady` before flipping the room to `Active` and broadcasting `RoomGameStarting`, so a host could start an unready game by calling the action directly. Fixed by adding `abort_unless($this->allReady, 403)` alongside the existing host check.
- Test-writing gotcha: `Event::fake()` also fakes **Eloquent model lifecycle events**, so any test calling `Event::fake()` before `Model::factory()->create()` on a model with a `static::creating()` hook (like `Room`'s code generator) will silently skip that hook and hit NOT NULL constraint errors. Fix: create fixtures first, `Event::fake()` only around the action under test.
- **Guests can join an online room without an account** (and, since `docs/TODO.md`'s former #1 below, host one too). Laravel's private/presence channel authorization always authenticates via the guard with no "let anyone in" option — there is no way to admit a truly anonymous visitor to a Reverb presence channel. Solved with a throwaway account: `User::createGuest(?string $name)` creates a real `users` row (`is_guest = true`, a `guest-{uuid}@guests.invalid` email nobody can log back into, a random unusable password, `email_verified_at` pre-filled) and logs it in on the spot — from the lobby's `joinRoom()`, from the room page's `mount()` (for a bare shared room URL), and now from `createRoom()` too. `routes/web.php`'s room route dropped its blanket `auth` middleware entirely in favor of this per-visitor provisioning. `routes/channels.php`'s presence-channel closure needed no changes — once a guest is a real (if synthetic) authenticated user, it's indistinguishable from a registered one.
- **Guest account cleanup** (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #1 — no longer an accepted trade-off, now that hosting can create guests too, not just joining). New `App\Console\Commands\PruneGuestAccounts` (`users:prune-guests`), scheduled daily via `bootstrap/app.php`'s `->withSchedule()` (kept out of `routes/console.php` — following that file's own documented preference to reserve it for command *definitions*, not schedule wiring). Deletes `is_guest` accounts older than 24 hours — a day is a generous buffer past `Room::LIFETIME_MINUTES` (60), not a value with any other significance. A single bulk `User::where(...)->delete()`, not a per-row loop — the app's existing FK constraints (`passkeys.user_id`/`room_seats.user_id` cascade, `rooms.host_user_id` cascades, `game_participants.user_id` nulls out) are enforced at the database level regardless of whether the delete happens through a single Eloquent model or a bulk query, the same reasoning "Delete Account" already relies on. **Deployment note:** like any Laravel scheduled task, this only actually runs if the deployment target has its own `php artisan schedule:run` cron entry (or equivalent) configured — adding the schedule definition here doesn't make it run anywhere on its own. Covered by `tests/Feature/Console/PruneGuestAccountsTest.php`.
- **Hosting while logged out** (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #1). `createRoom()` in all three games (`boggle-lobby`, `pages::yahtzee.play`, `pages::family-feud.play`) used to `abort_unless(Auth::check() && ! Auth::user()->is_guest, 403)` — hosting specifically required a real, non-guest account, even though joining never had that restriction (see the entry above). No real technical reason for the asymmetry once guest accounts already existed for joining; just never revisited. Changed `createRoom()` to the exact same `Auth::guest()` → `User::createGuest($this->guestName)` provisioning `joinRoom()` already used, and removed every setup screen's `@if (Auth::check() && ! Auth::user()->is_guest)` gate around the "Host an Online Room" button and its paired "Sign in to host" message — the button (and the optional guest-name field above it, now shared by both hosting and joining) shows unconditionally. An *already*-authenticated guest (e.g. one who joined a different room earlier) does not get a second throwaway account created on top — `Auth::guest()` is false for them, so `createRoom()` just proceeds directly using their existing identity. Nothing downstream needed to change: `Room.host_user_id` already just `belongsTo(User::class)` with no assumption it's a non-guest, and every `isHost` check (including `switchGame()`, see below) is a plain `Auth::id() === $room->host_user_id` comparison that works identically either way.
- **Ready state, reconnect, and the "reconnecting resets you to not-ready" bug**: `RoomPlayerReadyToggled` only ever reaches whoever's actively connected to the presence channel at the instant it fires — a reconnecting client's `onHere()`/`onJoining()` has no way to recover another member's current ready status from the presence payload alone (it only ever carries `id`/`name`/`is_host`, see `routes/channels.php`), so before this fix, everyone re-appeared "not ready" to a client that had just reconnected, even players who'd never actually un-readied. Fixed by persisting each toggle to `Cache::put("room:{code}:ready:{userId}", $ready, now()->addHours(3))` in `toggleReady()`, and reading it back (`Cache::get(..., false)`) in both `onHere()`/`onJoining()` instead of hardcoding `'ready' => false`. `startGame()` now clears every present player's cached flag once consumed — otherwise a replayed room ("Back to room" after a finished game) would have every returning player's `onHere()`/`onJoining()` immediately read back "ready" from the *previous* round, enabling Start Game again before anyone actually re-confirmed for the new one.
- **Seat-carrying payload for game-specific start logic**: `startGame()` now passes its own `$this->players` array along with the existing local `room-game-starting` dispatch (`dispatch('room-game-starting', players: $this->players)`) — Livewire binds dispatched payload args to listener params *by name*, so Boggle's own `onRoomStarting(BoardGenerator $boardGenerator)`, which never declares a `$players` param, keeps working completely unmodified. Yahtzee's own room page is what actually consumes it (see below).

**Decoupled rooms from specific games** (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #1): a room's players can now stay in the same room (same code, same seats) and move to a different game entirely, instead of the host needing to create a brand new room and everyone re-joining with a new code. Turned out to need very little new machinery, because `Room` (no `type` column at all) and `GameLobby` (already fully game-agnostic — see above) were already decoupled; the actual coupling was just that nothing ever exposed a way to *change which game's route a room was currently pointed at*.
- **`GameType::roomRoute()`** is the one new mapping (`boggle.room`/`yahtzee.room`/`family-feud.room`), added to the enum itself rather than duplicated in `GameLobby` or copy-pasted per game, so `GameLobby` needs zero game-specific knowledge beyond "here's a `GameType` value, go look up where it lives."
- **`GameLobby::switchGame(GameType $type)`** (host-only) resets `game_session_id` to `null` — the exact same reset `startGame()` already does for a same-game replay, reused as-is since switching games is really the same "clear the slate" operation, just followed by a redirect to a different route instead of the current one — then broadcasts a new `RoomGameSwitched` event (mirrors `RoomGameStarting`'s `ShouldBroadcastNow` reasoning: without it, or without a queue worker, everyone but the host would be silently stranded on the game they just left) and redirects the host's own browser. `GameLobby::onGameSwitched()` is what every *other* present player's own connection reacts to, redirecting them to the same place. New "or play something else in this room:" buttons (one per other `GameType`, via a new `otherGames` computed property) sit right below Start Game — unconditional on `$this->isHost`, not gated on `$players`/`allReady` at all, since switching doesn't require anyone to have readied up for anything yet.
- **A real, previously-unnoticed bug found and fixed as a prerequisite**: both Yahtzee's and Family Feud's `onRoomStarting()` called `RoomSeat::create()` unconditionally inside the "no session yet" branch, with nothing clearing old rows first. That branch runs on *every* replay (not just the first game in a room) because `startGame()` resets `game_session_id` to `null` every time — so a genuine second game in the same room (same players, replaying, no code involved with switching at all) hit a real `UNIQUE(room_id, user_id)` constraint violation and 500'd outright the moment the second player's seat tried to insert. Confirmed by temporarily reverting the fix and re-running the new regression test, which failed with exactly that SQL error. Fixed with `RoomSeat::where('room_id', $this->room->id)->delete()` before recreating, in both games' `onRoomStarting()`. This had nothing to do with switching games specifically, but switching reuses the identical reset mechanism, so it would have inherited (and been much easier to trigger than) this same crash if left unfixed.
- Test coverage, given the complexity here: `tests/Feature/GameLobbySwitchGameTest.php` (route mapping, `otherGames` filtering, host-only enforcement, the broadcast's payload, another player's own redirect on receiving it, `RoomSeat` staying clean across a real cross-game switch, and switching repeatedly through all three games in sequence without accumulating stale state); real regression tests added to `YahtzeeRoomTest.php`/`FamilyFeudRoomTest.php` for the `RoomSeat` bug specifically (a genuine replay via `game-lobby`'s own reset, not the existing "call `onRoomStarting()` twice in a row" tests, which never actually re-opened the reset branch and so never exercised the real bug); and `tests/Browser/GameLobbySwitchGameBrowserTest.php` for the real button click and redirect, since a Livewire-level test can't prove the button is actually visible/clickable in the rendered page.

**Rooms became a persistent, first-class thing a user belongs to, independent of any one game (`docs/concerns.md` #6-#11, 2026-08-06).** Until now, "being in a room" only ever existed as long as you were physically on that room's own game pages — there was no durable "I am a member of this room" fact anywhere, only ephemeral presence-channel membership (who's connected *right now*) and `RoomSeat` (who has a seat in the *currently active game*, recreated every time one starts). That gap was the one real blocker behind every one of these reports: no way to see "your rooms" from anywhere but a specific game's own page, no way for new people to join a room once a game had started without detouring through that same game's setup screen, and — as a fully separate bug found while investigating #6/#7 — a "Back to room" button that looked broken but was actually a symptom of the same underlying gap (see its own entry above).

- **New `room_members` table + `RoomMember` model**: `room_id` + `user_id`, unique on the pair. `Room::addMember(User $user): RoomMember` (`firstOrCreate` on `user_id`) is the one entry point every join/host/direct-URL path now calls, instead of each duplicating the bookkeeping — wired into `joinRoom()`/`createRoom()` (until Phase E removed those, see below), every game's own `⚡room.blade.php::mount()` (the shared-URL entry point), and the two new pages below. `Room::booted()` gained a `static::created()` hook calling `$room->addMember($room->host)` so the host becomes a member the instant a room exists, with no separate call needed at any of its `Room::create()` call sites.
  - **Deliberately dropped, not shipped**: an initial version also refreshed `expires_at` inside `addMember()` (reasoning: a room meant to be revisited across multiple games and page loads shouldn't silently vanish after the original 60-minute window). Reverted before landing — `RoomFactory::expired()`'s own test state sets `expires_at` in the past at creation time, and the auto-host-membership hook above calls `addMember()` on every single room creation, which would have silently overwritten that intentional test fixture on every use. No user request was actually driving the expiry change, so it was simplest to just not build it rather than special-case around the conflict.
  - This is a third, deliberately distinct bookkeeping mechanism alongside `RoomSeat` (current game's seats) and presence-channel membership (who's connected right now) — same "different questions, different mechanisms" reasoning the original Room system entry above already used for `RoomSeat` vs. presence.
- **New room hub, `pages::room.show` (`/room/{code}`, route name `room.show`)**: the game-agnostic "this room" page — code, a live member list (persisted `RoomMember` rows for the roster, presence `here`/`joining`/`leaving` events layered on top for an Online/Offline indicator per member, reusing `GameLobby`'s own presence-listener pattern), a link into the currently active game if any (`GameType::playRoute()`, a new sibling to the existing `roomRoute()`, mirrors its own `match` shape) labeled "Resume your seat" or "Watch" depending on whether the viewer has a `RoomSeat` row — not new logic, just finally surfacing what `mount()` on every game's play page already resolved correctly on its own. Host-only: one button per `GameType` linking into that game's own lobby (`roomRoute()`, reusing 100% of `GameLobby`'s existing start/replay/reset machinery — this is just one more doorway into it) and "End Room" behind an `<x-confirm-dialog>` (new `App\Events\RoomEnded`, mirrors `RoomGameSwitched`'s `ShouldBroadcastNow` shape, then `$room->delete()`, cascading `room_seats`/`room_members` via their FKs). Non-host: "Leave Room," same confirm-dialog treatment, deletes just that member's own `RoomMember` row. Neither destructive action lives in the navbar dropdown below — deliberately, so "shouldn't be easy to accidentally hit the wrong button" (the user's own wording) costs nothing.
- **New navbar `<livewire:rooms-menu />`, embedded directly in `layouts/app.blade.php`** (not a `@teleport` target like `#game-actions` — needs to render on every page including plain non-`pages::` Blade views like `welcome.blade.php`, which never trigger the Livewire round trip a teleport reacts to): a "Rooms ▾" toggle button + panel listing `RoomMember::where('user_id', Auth::id())->whereHas('room', notExpired)`, each linking to that room's hub; a "Join a Room" `<x-dialog>` (the app's own dialog, not a native `prompt()` — the user's own explicit ask) with a code field; a "Create a Room" action. Both actions mirror the provisioning/redirect logic every game's own `createRoom()`/`joinRoom()` used to have, just centralized here — being global means "create/join from the home page or a game page" (the user's own wording) falls out for free, no separate homepage-only affordance needed.
  - **New toggle mechanism, JS-only, not Livewire state**: the toggle button and panel both carry `wire:ignore.self` so their own JS-managed `hidden`/`aria-expanded` state survives this component's own re-renders (a plain incidental Livewire round trip would otherwise reset both back to closed) — a new delegated `document.addEventListener('click', ...)` block in `app.js`, matching the existing dialog-open/close delegation's own reasoning (bound elements can be replaced by a Livewire re-render at any time).
  - **Same `dialog[open]`-morph bug class as Yahtzee's scoreboard-drawer (see that section below), caught before shipping this time**: the "Join a Room" `<x-dialog>` needed its own `wire:ignore.self` too — without it, a failed `joinByCode()` (invalid code) re-renders the component to display `$joinError`, which morphs the `<dialog>` element's own `open` attribute back to the server-rendered (absent) state and silently closes it the instant before the user would see why it failed. Caught by a real browser test asserting the error message is actually visible, not just present in a Livewire property.
- **Setup screens across all four games dropped their own "Host an Online Room"/"Join" UI entirely** (`docs/concerns.md` #11 — the user's own stated leaning, confirmed): `createRoom()`/`joinRoom()`/`$joinID`/`$guestName`/`$joinError` deleted from all four `⚡play.blade.php` (and Boggle's own `⚡lobby.blade.php`, which was *entirely* this UI plus the embedded `boggle-setup` component — reduced to just a `hostedRooms` dropdown check). Replaced with a single `getHostedRoomsProperty()` (`Room::where('host_user_id', Auth::id())->notExpired()->get()`) driving a "play in a room you host" button row, shown only when non-empty — matching the concern's explicit "if you are not hosting any rooms, that dropdown isn't there." The dead mobile-Safari OTP-autofocus-blur script each of these pages carried for their own `#join` field (see the Boggle/Yahtzee "Mobile Safari auto-focus" entries elsewhere in this file) was deleted alongside it, now dead code with the field itself gone.
  - **An "auto-select room" branch turned out to be unreachable, not needed**: the plan initially called for showing "Playing in Room {CODE}" on this same setup screen when arriving via `?room=`, per the concern's own "autoselects your room and tells you that" wording. Investigation found `mount()` on every game's play page already sets `$started = true` unconditionally the instant `$room !== null` (hydrating straight from the existing `GameSession`), so the `@unless ($started)` setup block this branch would have lived in can never actually render when a room context is present — arriving via `?room=` always skips straight to gameplay. The concern's own "auto-select, tell you" requirement is satisfied by the room hub itself instead: its "start a new game" buttons already go through each game's own `{game}.room` lobby (which prominently shows the room code) rather than this setup screen, so there was never a real gap here to fill.
- **A real, unrelated test bug found while wiring this up**: `tests/Browser/FarkleLocalPlayTest.php` checked for the literal string `'rolled a Farkle'` to detect a bust and stop early — stale after the "Farkle" → "10,000" rename (see above) changed that in-game message to `'busted'`. Left unfixed, a bust before the loop's own turn cap would never be detected, and the very next line would try to click `#rollAgainBtn` after a bust already cleared the dice — a selector matching zero elements, which this same file's own docblock already documents as an indefinite Playwright hang, not a clean failure. Found and fixed only because the rename happened in the same session as this work, not because anything else surfaced it.
- **Three real "grabs the wrong Livewire component" bugs found the hard way, all the same root cause**: `<livewire:rooms-menu />` rendering in the layout, ahead of each page's own main content, means it's now the *first* Livewire component in DOM order on every page. Three existing browser tests used a shortcut — `window.Livewire.first()` (`FarkleMobileLayoutTest.php`) or `document.querySelector("[wire\:id]")` (`YahtzeeRoomBrowserTest.php`, `FamilyFeudRoomBrowserTest.php`) — to reach "the" Livewire component and call an internal method (`set('bankedDiceThisTurn', ...)`, `call('onStateUpdated', ...)`, `call('onRoomStarting', ...)`) directly, all silently assuming there was only ever one. All three now instead resolved rooms-menu and threw (`PublicPropertyNotFoundException`/`MethodNotFoundException`) — one of which (Farkle's) hung the entire browser session indefinitely rather than failing fast, costing real debugging time before the actual cause (not a flaky test, a genuine regression) was found; the other two failed to update anything and coincidentally still passed their own assertions (a false-positive pass, not a hang) purely by accident of what state the page was already in. Fixed by anchoring each lookup to the actual target component instead: Farkle's via `document.getElementById('farkle-game').closest('[wire\:id]')`, Yahtzee's/Family Feud's via the `wire:name` attribute Livewire already renders on every component root (`[wire\:name="pages::yahtzee.play"]`) — more direct than the DOM-walk, and immune to ordering entirely.
- **Two more, same "the navbar is now everywhere" shape, found by an actual hung test run**: `loginAsAndVisit`-style test helpers (six call sites across `StatsTableMobileScrollTest.php`, `AppSidebarMobileMenuTest.php`, `GameLobbySwitchGameBrowserTest.php`, `ProfileDangerZoneDialogsTest.php`, `YahtzeeRoomBrowserTest.php`, `FamilyFeudRoomBrowserTest.php`) clicked the login form's submit button via the CSS selector `button[type=submit]` — ambiguous now that `<livewire:rooms-menu />`'s own "Join a Room" dialog (present, if hidden, on every page including `/login`) has a submit button of its own. Fixed by giving the actual login button a real `id="login-submit"` and switching all six call sites to it — safer than a text-based selector, since `/login` separately turned out to *also* have "Sign in" as substring/exact text in three different places (the `<x-panel heading="Sign in">` itself, the passkey button "Sign in with a passkey," and the form button), which a first attempt at fixing this with `click('Sign in')` collided with just as badly. Yahtzee's/Family Feud's own pre-existing `click('Join')` (their setup screens' old "Join" submit button) hit the identical ambiguity against the navbar dialog's own "Join" button — moot once Phase E deleted that old button along with the rest of the setup-screen room UI it belonged to, rather than patched.
- Verification discipline worth noting: every one of the collisions above was found by actually running the full test suite (not just the tests for whatever was newly written) and chasing down real hangs/failures rather than assuming "my new tests pass, so it's fine" — the six-file selector collision in particular would never have been caught by running only the room-system tests, since none of the *new* code was what broke.
- New tests: `tests/Feature/Models/RoomTest.php` (auto-host-membership, `addMember` idempotency/distinctness), `tests/Feature/RoomShowTest.php` (404, member list, host-only end/non-host leave, guest auto-provision, seated-vs-spectator link text), `tests/Browser/RoomShowDialogsTest.php` (both confirm dialogs' cancel/confirm paths, actually navigating), `tests/Feature/RoomsMenuTest.php` + `tests/Browser/RoomsMenuBrowserTest.php` (create/join, guest provisioning, `myRooms` scoping/expiry filtering, the dropdown's open/close and both dialogs end-to-end), one `{Game}HostedRoomsDropdownTest.php` browser test per game, and `tests/Browser/RoomResumeSeatTest.php` (the full #6 path: visit the room hub, leave to Home, come back, resume the same seat — not a fresh spectator view).

**Two real bugs found immediately after the above shipped, from actually using it.**
- **The room hub's "Start a new game" buttons had no way to bring anyone else along.** They were plain `<a href="{{ route($type->roomRoute(), ...) }}">` links — the host clicked one and landed in that game's own lobby just fine, but nothing told any *other* member sitting on the same room hub page that a game had even been chosen, let alone which one. There was, in effect, no way for a second player to ever actually join a game a host started from the hub. Root cause: unlike `<livewire:game-lobby>`'s own `switchGame()` (which broadcasts `RoomGameSwitched` specifically because "everyone but the host would be silently stranded" otherwise, per that event's own docblock), the hub's buttons were never wired to broadcast anything at all. Fixed by giving `pages::room.show` its own `startGame(GameType $type)` (host-only, resets `game_session_id` to `null` for the same reason `switchGame()` already does — a stale non-null id makes that game's `onRoomStarting()` skip creating a fresh session and silently reuse an old, finished one — then dispatches the *same* `RoomGameSwitched` event) and an `onGameSwitched()` listener mirroring `GameLobby`'s own, so every other member currently viewing the hub follows the host into the same lobby automatically. Reused the existing event/pattern rather than inventing a new one, since the semantics ("the host picked a game, everyone go there") are identical.
- **The navbar Rooms dropdown positioned itself off-screen on mobile.** `#rooms-menu-panel`'s `absolute right-0 top-full` assumed the toggle button always sits at the screen's right edge — true on desktop, but `.button-bar .right` wraps onto its own line below 900px, and the Rooms button (first item in that group) can land anywhere along that wrapped line depending on how much else is in `.right` (Sign In/Create an Account for a guest, say). A fixed `right-0` offset relative to a button that's no longer near the true right edge pushed the whole 256px panel partly off the *left* edge of the screen (confirmed live: a real `getBoundingClientRect()` returned a negative `left`). Fixed by computing the panel's position in JS at open time instead of relying on CSS alone — reads the toggle's actual `getBoundingClientRect()`, clamps the panel's `left` to stay within `[8px, viewportWidth - panelWidth - 8px]`, and sets `position: fixed` with that computed offset. Robust regardless of how `.button-bar` wraps at any given viewport width, not just a specific breakpoint guess.
- Regression tests: `tests/Feature/RoomShowTest.php` (host `startGame()` resets a stale `game_session_id` and redirects; non-host gets 403; `onGameSwitched()` redirects a non-host member exactly as a real broadcast would), `tests/Browser/RoomHubStartGameTest.php` (the host's own real click-through), and a new test in `tests/Browser/RoomsMenuBrowserTest.php` asserting the dropdown panel's real bounding rect stays within a 390px viewport.

**A third, related "member left behind" bug (found live while testing Best Match's own 3-player minimum, 2026-08-12): `RoomGameSwitched` only ever reaches whoever's actively viewing the room hub at the exact instant it's broadcast.** A member who loads (or reloads) the hub page even a few seconds *after* the host already picked a game — or after the host reached that game's own lobby via its "play in a room you host" shortcut on its bare play page instead of the hub's own `startGame()`, which never broadcasts anything at all — has no way to discover it: `activeGameType` only ever reflects a real, already-started `GameSession`, and nothing else records "the host is on {game}'s lobby, not started yet." This is exactly Best Match's own reported repro: host starts a room, one guest joins, host tries Best Match with only 2 people (see this file's "Best Match" section for that half of the fix), a third person joins the room *afterward* and has no path into the lobby the host is already sitting on. Not new to Best Match — every game has this gap — just far more likely to surface here because Best Match's player minimum means there's now a normal, everyday window where the host is waiting on the lobby screen for more people.
  - **Fixed with `Room::markPendingGame(GameType $type)`/`pendingGameType()`**, Cache-backed (not a new DB column — Room stays decoupled from any specific game, same reasoning as everywhere else in this section; same `Cache::put(..., now()->addMinutes(Room::LIFETIME_MINUTES))` shape `<livewire:game-lobby>`'s own ready-state cache already uses for equivalent ephemeral, non-authoritative state). Every `{game}.room` page's own `mount()` calls `markPendingGame()` when the *current* visitor is the host and no real session exists yet — covers both entry paths (the hub's `startGame()` and each game's own "hosted rooms" shortcut) with one line in each page, rather than needing to touch either of those call sites individually. `pendingGameType()` returns `null` the instant a real `GameSession` exists, so a stale marker from an abandoned lobby visit is simply never consulted rather than needing to be explicitly cleared — a single cache key per room (not per game), naturally overwritten if the host instead visits a *different* game's lobby.
  - `pages::room.show` gained a `pendingGameType` computed property and, when set (and `activeGameType` isn't), a "The host is setting up {label} — hasn't started yet" panel with a Join link into that game's `roomRoute()` — sitting in the same slot `activeGameType`'s own "Resume your seat/Watch" panel occupies, one or the other, never both.
  - Regression tests: four new cases in `tests/Feature/Models/RoomTest.php` (`markPendingGame`/`pendingGameType`'s own behavior, including the overwrite-not-stack and superseded-by-a-real-session cases), two in `tests/Feature/RoomShowTest.php` (the Join link appears/disappears correctly), and one in `tests/Feature/Games/AppleMatchRoomTest.php` tying it back to the actual reported scenario (visiting `pages::apple-match.room` as host marks the room pending).

**A room ending mid-game briefly flashed a raw 500/404 error over the game screen instead of just leaving (`docs/concerns.md`).** Root cause: `pages::room.show::endRoom()` deletes the `Room` row, but `RoomEnded` (broadcast right before the delete) is only ever listened for by `pages::room.show` itself — every actual gameplay `⚡play.blade.php` component (all six games) has its own `resolveRoom()` (`Room::where('code', $this->room)->firstOrFail()`), called from inside nearly every mutating action (`roll()`, `syncOnlineState()`, ...), which throws `ModelNotFoundException` the moment any of those run after the room's gone. Livewire's client had no error interceptor registered anywhere in this app, so its default `showHtmlModal()` behavior kicked in: the raw HTML error response gets dumped into a `<dialog>`/`<iframe>` overlay directly on top of the game — the exact "briefly flashed" symptom, since the very next user action (or the next broadcast-triggered re-render) typically replaces or dismisses it.
- **Fixed generally, not per-game**: a global `Livewire.interceptRequest()` in `resources/js/app.js` (registered inside a `livewire:init` listener, since `window.Livewire` isn't attached until Livewire's own script has run), added once rather than wiring a `RoomEnded` listener into all six `⚡play.blade.php` files individually. On `onError`, `preventDefault()` suppresses the raw-HTML modal and the browser is redirected to `/` — the same recovery `pages::room.show`'s own `onRoomEnded()` already uses for the one case it *can* detect directly. Matches the concern's own broader framing ("errors should not flash on the screen, especially when they do not affect anything") rather than only patching the one reported game.
- **Deliberately excludes status `422`** — Livewire's own validation-error status (confirmed against its documented error-rejection shape, which is `{status: 422, errors: {...}}` for a validation failure vs. `{status, body: '<html>...'}` for a real server error). Without this exclusion, an entirely ordinary validation failure (e.g. clearing a required field on `/profile`, or any future `#[Validate]`/`$this->validate()` form) would bounce the user away from the form they were actively filling in instead of re-rendering it inline with `$errors`/`@error` the way it's supposed to. Confirmed live both ways: a genuine 422 from `/profile`'s `save()` stays on `/profile` with the inline message visible; a genuine failure (room deleted out from under an in-progress Yahtzee action, or an unrelated `PublicPropertyNotFoundException` hit while probing this) redirects home with no modal.
- Test coverage: `tests/Browser/RoomEndedMidGameTest.php` (seeds an in-progress online Yahtzee room, deletes the `Room` row directly to isolate "an action fails after the fact" from whether the `RoomEnded` broadcast happens to arrive in time — same DB-seeded-state convention `YahtzeeRoomBrowserTest.php` already uses — then confirms no `#livewire-error` dialog appears and the browser lands on `/`) and `tests/Browser/LivewireValidationNotInterceptedTest.php` (forces a real 422 via `/profile`'s validation and confirms it stays on-page instead of redirecting).

**Optional guest display name (`docs/concerns.md`)**: `User::createGuest(?string $name = null)` already accepted a name (see the "Guests can join an online room without an account" entry above) — every one of its ~8 call sites just never passed one, so every guest was always literally named `"Guest"`. Rather than restructuring all 7 `mount()`-time auto-provision flows (each a bare page load with no chance to interrupt for input), the name field was added to the one place a visitor *deliberately* joins/hosts through this app's own UI: `<livewire:rooms-menu>`'s dropdown panel, one optional "Your name (optional)" input shared by both the Join and Create actions (rather than duplicating it into two separate dialogs), `@guest`-gated so it never shows to an already-logged-in account. `joinByCode()`/`createRoom()` pass it straight to `User::createGuest($this->guestName)` — empty still falls through to the existing `"Guest"` default exactly as before.
- **Remembered via a long-lived cookie, not just used once**: `rememberGuestName()` queues `Cookie::queue('guest_name', ..., 60 * 24 * 365)` whenever a name was actually given, and every one of the 7 `mount()`-time guest-provisioning call sites (`pages::room.show` plus all six `{game}.room` pages) now reads it back via `User::createGuest(request()->cookie('guest_name'))` instead of a bare `createGuest()`. This covers the case the dialog itself can't: a repeat guest who typed a name once, then later arrives cold on a direct/shared room URL (no dialog in that flow at all) still gets their remembered name instead of "Guest" again.
- Test coverage: `tests/Feature/RoomsMenuTest.php` (both actions use a supplied name over the default, the cookie is queued only when a name was actually given, both still default to "Guest" with nothing supplied) and a `tests/Feature/Games/YahtzeeRoomTest.php` case confirming a `guest_name` cookie is picked up on a direct room-URL visit (representative of the identical one-line change repeated across all six games).

**Advancing from one game to the next was confusing, especially for non-hosts (`docs/concerns.md`'s first item).** Investigated before changing anything: the pieces this concern assumed were missing (a way to see "the host picked a game" state, a "ready" step, a live join notification) mostly already existed — `Room::pendingGameType()`, `<livewire:game-lobby>`'s ready-up flow, `RoomGameSwitched` broadcasting. The actual gaps were narrower: "Back to room" on every `⚡play.blade.php` linked into the just-*played* game's own lobby (`{game}.room`), not the game-agnostic hub, so a straggler who finished late landed on a stale, game-specific page with no path to whatever the host had since switched to; and `onGameSwitched()` in both `pages::room.show` and `<livewire:game-lobby>` force-redirected anyone currently on that page the instant the host picked a *different* game, rather than letting them opt in. Fixed in three tiers, all reusing the existing `pendingGameType()`/broadcast machinery rather than adding new state:
- **"Back to room" now always routes to `pages::room.show`** (all six games), not each game's own lobby. This makes the hub the one canonical, truthful landing page — it already computes "what's happening in this room" straight from `Room.status`/`game_session_id`/`pendingGameType()`, not from whether a broadcast happened to be caught in time — so a player who finishes a game after the host has already moved on now always lands somewhere that reflects reality, with no dependency on real-time delivery at all.
- **The forced redirect on `RoomGameSwitched` became a Join prompt.** `pages::room.show::startGame()` and `<livewire:game-lobby>::switchGame()` now call `$room->markPendingGame($type)` synchronously, *before* broadcasting — previously that cache write only ever happened on the host's own next page load (each game's own `{game}.room::mount()`), which raced any other member's `onGameSwitched()` reacting to the same broadcast. Both `onGameSwitched()` handlers dropped their `$this->redirect(...)` call entirely (now near-empty methods that exist purely to trigger a re-render); Livewire's own listener-invocation re-render then recomputes `pendingGameType()` fresh from the now-already-written cache and shows the existing "host is setting up X — Join" banner instead of yanking the viewer there mid-thought. `<livewire:game-lobby>` gained its own equivalent banner ("The host switched to X — Join") via a new `getSwitchedGameTypeProperty()`, guarded against equaling `$currentGame` (defensive only — `switchGame()`'s own UI never offers the current game as a target).
- **A new global toast in `<livewire:rooms-menu>`** (mounted on every page via `layouts/app.blade.php`, so it's the one place that can reach a member regardless of what page they're actually on — mid-play in an unrelated game, browsing elsewhere entirely) — `getListeners()` now subscribes to `RoomGameSwitched`/`RoomGameStarting` on *every* room the account belongs to (`$this->myRooms`, already bounded by `notExpired()`), each pushing a dismissible `{id, message, href}` notification linking to the room hub. Needed both events to carry the room's `code` explicitly in their broadcast payload (`RoomGameSwitched::broadcastWith()` gained it; `RoomGameStarting` previously had no `broadcastWith()` override at all, silently defaulting to every public property including the full serialized `$room` model — replaced with an explicit minimal payload, safe because every existing listener for it, `onRoomStarted()`/`onGameStarting()`, is already parameterless) since a single method now has to disambiguate *which* of a user's several rooms a given event is for. Deliberately no auto-dismiss/fade animation — this app's whole design premise is no-motion/low-stim, so the toast is a static box with a manual Dismiss button.
- **Known, accepted overlap**: a member sitting on the room hub itself sees both the hub's own inline banner *and* the global toast for the same event, since `rooms-menu` renders on every page including the hub. Not worth special-casing `rooms-menu` to know what page it's on for a low-stakes casual-game app.
- **Rooms-menu dropdown row got a trailing `›` chevron** (`docs/concerns.md`'s own "maybe just an arrow" suggestion) so each room row visibly reads as a link rather than plain text.
- Regression tests updated for the new non-redirect behavior: `RoomShowTest.php`'s and `GameLobbySwitchGameTest.php`'s own `onGameSwitched()` cases now seed `markPendingGame()` first (standing in for the real broadcast timing) and assert `assertNoRedirect()` + the banner text instead of a redirect; `tests/Browser/FarkleBackToRoomTest.php` updated to expect `/room/{code}` instead of `/10000/room/{code}`. New: three cases in `tests/Feature/RoomsMenuTest.php` covering the toast (pushed with the right link, dismissible, ignored for a room the user doesn't belong to).

**Removed `<livewire:game-lobby>`'s own "switch to a different game" buttons entirely** (`docs/concerns.md`: a host shouldn't see links to play other games on a game's own setup page — that choice belongs on the room hub only). `game-lobby` had grown this via its own `switchGame()`/`getOtherGamesProperty()`, fully duplicating `pages::room.show::startGame()`/`$this->startableGames` above — both did the identical reset-`game_session_id`/`markPendingGame()`/broadcast `RoomGameSwitched`/redirect dance, just from two different pages. Deleted `switchGame()`, `getOtherGamesProperty()`, and the "or play something else in this room:" button block from `game-lobby`; `getSwitchedGameTypeProperty()`/`onGameSwitched()` and the "host switched to X — Join" banner both stayed as-is, since they already just react to `Room::pendingGameType()` regardless of *which* page's `startGame()`/`markPendingGame()` call set it — no longer game-lobby's own action specifically, but still fully correct now that `room.show` is the only caller.
- Test fallout: `tests/Browser/GameLobbySwitchGameBrowserTest.php` deleted outright — fully superseded by `tests/Browser/RoomHubStartGameTest.php`, which already covers the identical real-click-through against `room.show`'s own buttons. `tests/Feature/GameLobbySwitchGameTest.php` trimmed from 8 tests down to the one still-relevant case (a member sees the "switched" join prompt without being redirected, now triggered via `markPendingGame()` directly rather than a `switchGame()` call that no longer exists) — the rest (`otherGames` filtering, host-only gating, broadcast assertion, stale-`game_session_id` reset, repeated-switch stability) were exercising the now-deleted API and are already equivalently covered against `room.show::startGame()` in `tests/Feature/RoomShowTest.php`. The `GameType::roomRoute()` case-by-case mapping test moved into `tests/Unit/GameTypeTest.php` (tests the enum, not game-lobby, so it didn't belong in a game-lobby-specific file to begin with).

**Visiting a game's play page for a room whose active session belongs to a different game crashed with a 500, root-caused from a raw error in `docs/notes.md` and fixed (2026-08-17).** Every one of the five games that sync shared state via `GameSession.state` (Farkle, Yahtzee, Family Feud, Best Match, Clear the Deck) had the identical bug: `mount()` fetches `$room->gameSession` and unconditionally calls `hydrateFromState($session->state)` without ever checking that `$session->type` actually matches the page's own game. Reproduced live with a room switched to Boggle specifically (Boggle's own `GameSession` never populates `state` at all, only `settings.seed` — see `pages::boggle.room`), which threw `TypeError: ...hydrateFromState(): Argument #1 ($state) must be of type array, null given`; the same root cause would also have silently corrupt-read (not necessarily throw the identical error) any of the other four games' state shape if visited while a *different one* of those four was actually active. Reached in practice via a stale bookmark/browser tab left open to one game's play URL from before the host switches the room to a different game (`switchGame()`, see above), or by typing/pasting the wrong game's play URL with a `?room=` for a room actively playing something else.
- **Fixed identically in all five `mount()` methods**: immediately after resolving `$session`, `if ($session === null || $session->type !== GameType::X) { $this->redirect(route('room.show', ['code' => $roomModel->code])); return; }`, before `hydrateFromState()` is ever called — the same "always land somewhere truthful" principle the room-hub work above already established, rather than crashing. A missing session (`game_session_id` never set) is treated the same as a mismatched one, since both mean "no active game of my type here" from this page's perspective.
- Added one regression test per game to its own `tests/Feature/Games/{Game}RoomTest.php` (`AppleMatchRoomTest`, `ClearTheDeckRoomTest`, `FamilyFeudRoomTest`, `FarkleRoomTest`, `YahtzeeRoomTest`): seeds a room whose `game_session_id` points to a `GameSession` of a different (`Boggle`) type, then asserts the play page redirects to `room.show` instead of throwing.

**Boggle's "New Game" button did nothing after a room game, and a player stuck on a just-ended reveal screen had no way to learn the host had already started a new one elsewhere (user report, 2026-08-19).** Two related but distinct gaps, both fixed together.

- **The button itself wasn't broken, just always disabled for a shared session, with no explanation.** `resetGame()` is a deliberate no-op for any room/challenge game (a shared board is fixed for the whole session, not reroll-able per player), but the teleported button bar still rendered "New Game" `disabled` regardless — reading exactly like a broken click, not a correctly-unavailable one. Boggle was the one game with this shape at all; every other game (Farkle, see its own section) simply never renders a "New Game" button in room mode, showing "Back to room" instead. Fixed the same way: the button bar's `@unless ($ended) End Game @else New Game @endunless` swap (a single stable `display: contents` wrapper div, load-bearing — see `tests/Browser/BoggleNewGameTest.php`'s own docblock for the real teleport/`wire:click`-binding bugs that shape exists to avoid) gained two more branches ahead of the solo case: `$room !== null` → a real "Back to room" link (`route('room.show', ...)`), `$challenge !== null` → "Back home" (`route('boggle.lobby')`) — both previously only rendered further down, inside the reveal screen's own content, now removed from there so there's exactly one visible way back, not two. Regression: `tests/Browser/BoggleRoomBackToRoomTest.php`, a real click-through (not just markup) given this exact button bar's documented history.
- **The deeper gap: no room game notifies a player still viewing an already-superseded game that the room has moved on.** The only way back was ever "Back to room" itself — nothing pushed the news to whoever hadn't clicked it yet. Fixed generically, not per-game, with a new shared, embeddable Livewire SFC, `<livewire:room-new-game-notice>` (`resources/views/components/⚡room-new-game-notice.blade.php` — same "one game-agnostic component, every game drops it in" shape as `<livewire:game-lobby>`), dropped into all six `⚡play.blade.php` files identically (`:room-code="$room"`, `:current-game="GameType::X"`, `:my-game-session-id="$gameSessionId"` — every game already carries these two properties in the exact same shape, so this was a genuinely uniform one-line addition per file). It listens for `RoomGameStarting`/`RoomGameSwitched` on the room's presence channel and compares the room's *current* `game_session_id` against the session this page itself is showing (`$myGameSessionId`, captured once at the page's own `mount()`) — a mismatch means the room has moved on since this page loaded, whether to a fresh round of the same game or a switch to a different one, regardless of whether this visitor ever leaves the page to notice on their own.
- **A game without a way to actually spectate must never offer a "join" link into one already in progress.** Boggle has no `RoomSeat`/shared-live-board concept at all — every player's play-through is its own fully independent Livewire instance, so "joining" a Boggle game already in progress would silently just start a *fresh* playthrough, not watch the existing one, the opposite of what a "Watch" link should mean. New `GameType::supportsSpectating(): bool` (false only for Boggle; every seat-based room game already treats a seatless viewer as an implicit spectator — some via an explicit `$spectate` toggle, some just by gating every action behind `$mySeatIndex !== null` and rendering read-only otherwise, confirmed for each of Family Feud/Best Match specifically since neither has an explicit spectate toggle at all) gates both the new notice component (a spectate-capable game gets "The host started a new {label} game" + a Watch link; Boggle gets a plain "{label} is being played right now — check back to join the next game" with no link at all) and, for consistency, `pages::room.show`'s own pre-existing "Playing X right now" banner, which had the identical bug (always offered "Watch" to a seatless visitor regardless of game type).
- Test coverage: `tests/Unit/GameTypeTest.php` (`supportsSpectating()` for every case), `tests/Feature/RoomNewGameNoticeTest.php` (the component in isolation — no-op while in sync, the Watch-link branch, the wait-message branch, a cross-type switch, a finished room, and the live-broadcast-after-mount case via a direct `->call('onRoomChanged')`, the same "simulate what the broadcast would do" convention `RoomShowTest.php`'s own `onGameSwitched()` test already established for this app's test env, which runs with `BROADCAST_CONNECTION=null`), and a new case in `RoomShowTest.php` for the updated banner's no-spectating branch.

**Fixed the global `<livewire:rooms-menu>` toast (added above) only ever being visible while the site menu drawer happened to be open, plus its link target (`docs/concerns.md`).** Root cause: the toast renders `position: fixed`, but it's nested inside `<x-app-sidebar />`'s own `<aside class="app-sidebar">`, whose `.app-sidebar` rule is `display: none` while the drawer is closed — an ancestor's `display: none` un-renders its *entire* subtree regardless of what `position` a descendant declares, so the toast was invisible on every single page unless the drawer was already open, exactly defeating the point of a global notification.
