Fixed with a `@teleport('#room-notifications-anchor')` (the same Livewire mechanism `#game-actions` already uses) to a new anchor `<div>` in `layouts/app.blade.php`, placed as a sibling of `<x-app-sidebar />` rather than inside it — same "one stable `display: contents` wrapper root" shape every other `@teleport` in this app already uses, needed here too since the teleported content is conditional (`@if (count($notifications) > 0)`) and would otherwise have zero root elements to move whenever the list is empty.
- **Link targets fixed in the same pass.** Both `onGameSwitched()` and `onGameStarting()` previously always linked to `room.show` regardless of what was actually happening, requiring a second click through the room hub to reach the real game. `onGameSwitched()` now links straight to `$type->roomRoute()` (the game being set up); `onGameStarting()` now reads `$room->gameSession?->type` (already set by the time `RoomGameStarting` broadcasts — every `{game}.room`'s own `onRoomStarting()` persists it first) and links straight to `$type->playRoute()`, falling back to `room.show` only if the session type can't be resolved.
- New test (`tests/Browser/RoomsMenuBrowserTest.php`) drives the real `onGameSwitched()` action via `window.Livewire` (same technique `FamilyFeudRoomBrowserTest.php`'s `triggerFeudRoomStarting()` uses to stand in for a real presence broadcast, which this test environment's `BROADCAST_CONNECTION=null` can't deliver) and asserts the toast is actually `assertVisible()` — not just present in the DOM — with the drawer left closed the whole time; confirmed this fails against the pre-fix markup before treating it as real coverage. `tests/Feature/RoomsMenuTest.php`'s existing two link-target tests updated to assert the new destinations, plus a new case covering the `onGameStarting()` no-session fallback.

**Added a "resume your seat" link straight from each room-capable game's own home page card** (`docs/concerns.md` #7) — previously the only way back into a room you were already in was via the room hub, even if you knew perfectly well which game you were playing and just wanted back in. New embeddable Livewire SFC `<livewire:game-card-resume :type="GameType::X" />`, dropped into `welcome.blade.php`'s `.game-card-actions` for every `supportsRooms()` game (mirrors `<livewire:room-new-game-notice>`'s "one game-agnostic component, every game drops it in" shape), since `welcome.blade.php` itself stays a plain Blade view rather than needing a full Livewire-page conversion just for this.
- **Two distinct resumable states, not one.** `getResumableProperty()` walks the account's `RoomMember`s (same `notExpired()`-scoped query shape as `<livewire:rooms-menu>`'s own `$this->myRooms`) looking for a room whose currently relevant game matches `$type`: an *active* session (`$room->gameSession->type === $type`, plus an actual `RoomSeat` for seat-based games — Boggle has none, so room membership alone qualifies there, same reasoning as `GameType::supportsSpectating()`'s own docblock) renders "Resume your seat" straight into `playRoute()`; a still-*pending* lobby (`$room->pendingGameType() === $type`, host hasn't started yet) renders "Continue setup" into `roomRoute()` instead, so a member isn't left with nothing to click while waiting.
- **Accepted limitation, same shape as `<livewire:rooms-menu>`'s own**: an account seated in more than one room for the same game type only ever surfaces the first one found — rare enough in a casual-game app not to be worth a picker.
- **Real hang found and worked around while writing the browser test, not the feature itself.** The first attempt drove a real `Livewire::test('game-lobby', ...)`/`onRoomStarting()` start sequence (Farkle) from inside a `tests/Browser/*` file, the same pattern `tests/Feature/Games/FarkleRoomTest.php` already uses successfully — but combined with a real Playwright page in the same test, it reliably hung with zero output for 100s+ regardless of timeout, every time. Not root-caused further (a `RoomGameStarting::dispatch()` — `ShouldBroadcastNow` — interacting badly with this environment's amphp/revolt event loop is the leading suspect, but unconfirmed); worked around by switching the fixture to Boggle, hand-seeded directly via `GameSession::factory()->online()->create(['settings' => [...]])` with no `state` array and no event dispatch at all (Boggle never uses `GameSession.state`, unlike every other room game — see its own section below), matching the established "hand-build DB state, don't drive a real start flow, inside a Browser test" convention `tests/Browser/PunchlineRoomBrowserTest.php`/`AppleMatchRoomBrowserTest.php` already use for the same reason (two-live-things-at-once fragility in this Playwright setup).
- Test coverage: `tests/Feature/GameCardResumeTest.php` (9 cases: guest, no rooms, active+seated, active-but-unseated for a seat-based game, Boggle's seatless resume, pending lobby, mismatched type, expired room, a room-less game type) and `tests/Browser/GameCardResumeBrowserTest.php` (3 cases: a real click-through for both the active and pending states, plus a guest seeing neither link at all) — the latter specifically covering the "new conditionally-shown element" bug class `CLAUDE.md`'s own "Automated Browser Testing" section flags a Livewire-only test can't catch.

## Boggle

- `App\Services\Boggle\{BoardGenerator,Solver,Scorer}` — Livewire solo play, online multiplayer (`/boggle/room/{code}`), async challenge links (`/boggle/share/{gameSession}`).
- 32 tests across solo, rooms, and challenges.

Decisions and bugs along the way:
- Ported `legacy/boggle-shared.js` to the three service classes and copied `legacy/words.txt` to `storage/app/words.txt` (78,833 words, committed to git — it's not build output, the app reads it directly).
- `BoardGenerator::generate(int $size, ?int $seed = null)` uses `Random\Randomizer` + `Random\Engine\Mt19937` (PHP 8.2+) instead of global `mt_rand`/`Str::random()` state, so the **same seed always produces the same board** — added because online rooms (shared board) and async challenges (replay the same board later) both need this.
- `Solver` does **not** use a nested char-by-char trie — that structure hit ~86MB for the ~79k-word dictionary, uncomfortably close to PHP's 128MB CLI default, and combined with Xdebug's per-array overhead reliably crashed the test process with no output at all. Replaced with two **flat** hashmaps (`words`, `prefixes`, for DFS pruning) — same algorithm, ~31.5MB, faster to build, simpler code. Bound as a container **singleton** so the dictionary is parsed once per worker, not once per request. **Debugging gotcha:** running the full test suite with the old trie under this machine's default PHP CLI (Xdebug enabled by default via Homebrew) produced zero output and a nonzero exit code — no exception, nothing, because Xdebug's memory overhead exceeded the CLI's `memory_limit` so severely that even Collision's error handler couldn't allocate enough to report it. `XDEBUG_MODE=off` surfaces the real error cleanly; the flat-hashmap rewrite fixes the underlying problem either way. If a future test run mysteriously produces empty output with no error, suspect Xdebug + memory_limit before anything else.
- Livewire: `pages::boggle.lobby` (hosts the `boggle-setup` embeddable component), `pages::boggle.play` (tap-to-select word entry via `wire:click` per die plus Submit/Clear, later replaced with a drag gesture — see "Site-wide design system & chrome" below), `boggle-reveal` (post-game word list). Timer uses `wire:poll.1s` against a server-side `endsAt` unix timestamp (only when timed and not yet ended) rather than a client Alpine interval, to avoid clock drift and keep game-end authoritative on the server.
- Ending a game (timeout or manual) always records a `GameSession` + one `GameParticipant`, for both logged-in users (`user_id`) and guests (`guest_name: 'Guest'`).
- **Boggle online multiplayer**, on top of the Room/GameLobby infrastructure:
  - `pages::boggle.room` (route `/boggle/room/{code}`) hosts a host-only board-size/time-limit picker plus the nested `<livewire:game-lobby>`. `onRoomStarting()` (host path) generates the board, creates a `GameSession` (`mode: Online`), attaches it to the room, redirects the host into `pages::boggle.play`. `onRoomStarted()` (every player, via the broadcast) does the equivalent redirect once `game_session_id` is populated.
  - `pages::boggle.play` gained `#[Url] public ?string $room` — when present, `size`/`minutes`/`seed` are **overridden** from the room's `GameSession.settings` rather than trusted from the query string (a player shouldn't change their own board by hand-editing the URL); `endGame()` joins the existing `GameSession`; each `GameParticipant`'s `result` is computed by re-ranking all participants recorded so far against the top score every time someone finishes — deliberately simple/self-correcting rather than trying to detect "everyone's done" and finalize once.
  - Requires an account for **all** participants, not just the host — Reverb presence channels authenticate via the app's default guard before the channel callback even runs, so there's no way to admit an unauthenticated user to `room.{code}` at all. Solo play stays guest-friendly; online rooms don't (closed by the guest-account fix documented under "Room system" above, which covers *joining*, not hosting).
- **Boggle async challenge links**, built on the same "join an existing `GameSession`, rank once more than one participant has finished" logic as multiplayer: `pages::boggle.play` generalizes `isSharedSession` to cover `$room` **or** `#[Url] public ?int $challenge`, rather than duplicating logic. `GET /boggle/share/{gameSession}` is a plain redirect to `pages::boggle.play?...&challenge={id}`, not its own Livewire page. A plain solo game now also stores its `GameSession`'s id onto `$this->gameSessionId` once `endGame()` runs, so the reveal screen can build a `route('boggle.share', ...)` link. The "Copy" button uses a **delegated** `document.addEventListener('click', ...)` matching `[data-copy]`, since it only exists after a Livewire re-render (ending the game) rather than at initial page load.
- **Layout centering bugs (two, same root cause)**: a plain (non-flex) wrapper `<div>` doesn't auto-center a narrower child, even with `items-center` on the child itself. Fixed `pages::boggle.play`'s root element (`class="flex flex-col items-center w-full"`) and the room-code input (was `flex: 1`, stretched to fill the row and centering only the *text* within an oversized box; changed to a fixed width so input+Copy button sit together as one centered unit).
- **Shared-session (room or async challenge) reveal shows who found what, and scores only exclusive discoveries.** Previously every shared game showed the same single-player reveal, and scoring was raw per-word points with no way to know until reveal whether another participant found the same word too. `GameParticipant.stats['found_words']` now persists each participant's full word→points map (previously only the *count*), and `finalizeSharedSession()` recomputes every finished participant's score as the sum of only words *no one else in the session also found*, self-correcting on every finish. The reveal list colors each word by who found it: mine-only (`--accent`), shared by 2+ (`--bar`, "Both/All"), or one of up to four other individual participants (`--rival-1`..`--rival-4`, wrapping via `% 4` for a 5th+). Verified visually via a headless-browser render against the real board/dictionary, not just test assertions. Later extracted into its own `App\Services\Boggle\SharedSessionFinalizer` service class (see the commit-by-commit `docs/CHANGELOG.md` for that specific refactor).
- **Per-game Boggle stats on the profile**, scoped deliberately to Boggle only (the deep-dive metrics — longest word, per-length breakdown, % of board found — are word-game-specific; Yahtzee/Family Feud only get `PlayerStatsService`'s generic per-type summary on the dashboard, though see Yahtzee's own stats page below):
  - `GameParticipant.stats` (already a flexible JSON column, no migration needed) gained a `total_valid_words` key, written in `pages/boggle/⚡play.blade.php`'s `finishGame()` right next to `found_words` — it's just `count($this->allValidWords)`, already computed there via the Solver to render the reveal screen, so persisting it is free; without it, "% of the board found" can't be computed later without re-running the ~80k-word Solver per historical game per view. Everything else (words found, longest word, per-length histogram) is derived on the fly from `found_words` in `App\Services\Stats\BoggleStatsService`, not stored redundantly, so it stays correct for every already-played game.
  - New routes `profile.boggle` (`/profile/boggle`) and `profile.boggle.show` (`/profile/boggle/{gameParticipant}`), registered **before** the existing `/profile/{username}` public-profile wildcard route — otherwise that route's single `{username}` segment would swallow `/profile/boggle` as if "boggle" were someone's username. `profile.boggle.show` gates with `abort_unless($gameParticipant->user_id === Auth::id(), 404)` — 404, not 403, so another user's game id doesn't even confirm it exists.
  - `<x-player-stats>` gained a `linkable` prop: only when true does the Boggle card become an `<a>` to `profile.boggle` — public viewers of someone else's profile never get a link into what would otherwise resolve to *their own* Boggle stats, since `profile.boggle` has no username segment at all. (Later generalized into a `match` over game type once Yahtzee got its own stats page — see below.)
  - This stats display (per-type summary, recent games, the Boggle link) was initially added to `/profile` itself, then moved to `/dashboard` once it became clear `/profile` should stay edit-only and the dashboard already independently showed the same `PlayerStatsService` output — consolidated onto the dashboard rather than duplicated across both.
  - The "all games"/"recent games" lists were rebuilt as a real CSS grid (shared `grid-template-columns` across a header row and every data row, each row a single `<a>`/`<div>` with `display: contents` so its cells become direct grid items) instead of `flex flex-wrap justify-between`, which redistributed each row's items independently and made columns drift out of alignment row to row.
  - Added `tests/Unit/Stats/BoggleStatsServiceTest.php` and `tests/Feature/ProfileBoggleTest.php`.
  - **Win/loss "Result" column added to the "All games" table** (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #1). Reads `$participant->result` directly (same `GameResult::Win => text-accent, else text-text/65` color rule already used on the dashboard's recent-games list and the new `/profile/games` page) — shows `—` for solo/practice games, which never get a result (see the win-rate DEVLOG entry above for why). Adding the column widened the table past comfortable mobile width, so the container also picked up `overflow-x-auto` + a `min-w-[640px]` on the grid rather than letting columns squash — the same scroll-not-squash pattern the new `/profile/games` page launched with. This is **not** a full fix for `docs/TODO.md`'s separate "stats tables don't scroll on mobile" item, which still needs the other stats tables (Yahtzee's, Family Feud's) checked/fixed the same way.
  - **Win/loss also added to the individual game detail page** (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #1, the very next item after the table above). Yahtzee's and Family Feud's own `profile.{type}.show` pages already had a "Result" stat tile — always rendered, `—` when null, though for them `result` is never actually null (see the win-rate entry above). Boggle's own show page had no such tile at all. Added one, but **omitted entirely** rather than showing `—` for a solo game — a deliberate difference from the other two games' "always show the tile" pattern, matching the TODO's literal wording ("never" show it for solo play, not "show a placeholder").
  - **Stats tables now scroll horizontally on mobile instead of squashing** (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #1, completing the item Boggle's table only partly closed above). Applied the same `overflow-x-auto` container + `min-w-[...]` grid pattern to the three other grid-based multi-column tables that had it: Yahtzee's and Family Feud's own "All games" tables (`profile.yahtzee.index`/`profile.family-feud.index`), and `<x-player-stats>`'s shared "Recent games" list (used on both `/dashboard` and public profiles). Deliberately **not** applied to the narrower 2-column key-value grids (Yahtzee's "Best category scores," Family Feud's "Round-by-round" breakdown) — those already fit any screen width, so there's nothing to scroll. Also deliberately left alone: Yahtzee's in-game/`profile.yahtzee.show` scorecard `<table>` (`<x-yahtzee-scorecard-table>`) — that already has its own, more sophisticated mobile strategy (`single-seat-mobile`, hiding other players' columns via CSS rather than scrolling to them; see "Yahtzee" section above), a deliberate prior design decision rather than an oversight, and out of scope here. CSS-only changes like this can't be verified by a Feature/HTTP test (nothing renders real layout), so added `tests/Browser/StatsTableMobileScrollTest.php`: five tests, one per fixed table, each resizing to a real 375px viewport and asserting `scrollWidth > clientWidth` on the scroll container — proving the content actually overflows and is reachable by scrolling, not just clipped.
- **Mobile Safari auto-focus on the room-code field**: iOS Safari's OTP-autofill heuristic was auto-focusing the join-code input and popping the keyboard on `pages::boggle.lobby`, even on a genuinely fresh page load. Initially fixed with `autocomplete="off"` plus a JS blur-on-load guarded by a `userInteracted` flag; ultimately fixed more thoroughly by renaming the whole concept from "join code"/"room code" to **"join ID"/"room ID"** throughout — `joinCode` → `joinID`, `id="join-code"` → `id="join"`, placeholder "Room code" → "Room ID", and removing the `maxlength="6"` constraint — which stopped Safari's heuristic from pattern-matching the field as an OTP/code input at all. Mirrored identically into Yahtzee's own room-join UI once confirmed working (see Yahtzee's section below).
- **Real duplicate-`GameParticipant`-row bug found and fixed** (`docs/TODO.md`'s former "confirm the score math is correct" item). `mount()` on `pages::boggle.play` never checked whether the acting user already had a `GameParticipant` row for a shared (room or challenge) session — `$ended` defaults to `false` on every fresh Livewire mount regardless. Reloading the page right after a shared game ended therefore silently started a brand-new, fully replayable attempt (fresh timer, empty board state), and finishing *that* called `GameParticipant::create()` again, producing a second row for the same session/user. Confirmed against the app's own real dev database, not hypothetically: `game_session_id=9` had exactly this — two players, four rows, two of them (`0` words found, immediately superseded) clearly the stale pre-refresh attempt. Fixed two ways: `mount()` now looks up an existing `GameParticipant` for `(session, Auth::id())` on a shared session and restores `$ended`/`$score`/`$foundWords`/`$allValidWords` (re-running the `Solver` to rebuild the reveal screen) rather than letting the user start over; `finishGame()`'s persistence switched from `create()` to `updateOrCreate(['game_session_id', 'user_id'], ...)` for authenticated users specifically, so even a genuine race (e.g. two tabs) can't produce two rows for the same account. Deliberately **not** applied to guests — every guest participant in a session shares `user_id === null`, so there's no stable key to restore/upsert on without risking clobbering a *different* guest's row; a guest reload still starts a fresh attempt, same as before, consistent with how guest identity is already treated as ephemeral everywhere else in this app. Verified no other session in the real database has this pattern (queried across every game type, not just Boggle) before manually deleting the two confirmed-stale rows (ids `12`/`13`) and re-running the real `SharedSessionFinalizer::finalize()` against the now-correct 2-row session — the same logic the app already trusts elsewhere, not a one-off manual score edit. Regression-covered by three new tests in `tests/Feature/Games/BoggleChallengeTest.php` (remount restores `ended` instead of allowing a replay; finishing twice for the same account updates one row rather than duplicating; guests are unaffected).

**Ending a timed game early no longer records it (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #18, 2026-08-17, narrowed at the user's own direction).** The task's original framing (a separate "discard this game" flow, distinct from the existing End Game/New Game buttons) turned out to have a simpler, already-latent version of the same problem: `finishGame()` treated *every* `endGame()` call identically, whether triggered by the client-side countdown genuinely reaching zero or by clicking "End Game" seconds into a fresh timed game — both got recorded the same way, silently polluting stats with abandoned attempts.
- **The untimed-mode wrinkle, caught before implementing rather than after**: Boggle's setup screen has an explicit "Untimed" option (`minutes = 0`), and for that mode, clicking "End Game" *is* the only real finish condition — there's no timer to run out at all. A literal "any button click means not finished" reading would have meant untimed Boggle games could never be recorded, ever. Confirmed the intended behavior with the user directly before building anything (their own preference): only skip recording when End Game is clicked while a *timer is still running* — untimed games, and a timed game whose clock genuinely reached zero, both still count as real finishes.
- **Distinguished server-side with no new trigger/parameter needed**: `getRemainingSecondsProperty()` already existed (`max(0, $this->endsAt - now())`, used by the countdown's own JS) — since the client-side timer only ever calls `$wire.endGame()` once `remaining <= 0`, by the time that request reaches the server `remainingSeconds` has also already settled to `0`. So `finishGame()` just checks `! $this->isSharedSession && ($this->remainingSeconds ?? 0) > 0` right after computing `$allValidWords`: true only for a genuine early manual stop, false for a natural expiry or an untimed game (`remainingSeconds` is `null` there, coalesced to `0`). New `public bool $endedEarly` records which case happened, and `finishGame()` returns before ever touching `GameSession`/`GameParticipant` when it's true — no row of either kind gets written.
- **Deliberately solo-only** — an online room's End Game ends the shared session for every seat at once, a much bigger consequence than one player quitting their own local timer early, and no online-room case was reported. `! $this->isSharedSession` in the guard keeps room/challenge sessions' existing behavior completely untouched.
- **The reveal screen still shows everything** (found words, the full solved-word list) for an early-ended game — only the database write is skipped, not the player's own view of their round. Added a plain "Ended early with time still on the clock -- not saved to your stats." message so this is visible, truthful state rather than a silent gap someone might later wonder about (e.g. "why isn't my last game in my stats?").
- **A real, adjacent bug found and fixed while wiring this up**: `resetGame()` never reset `$gameSessionId` back to `null`. Harmless before this change — `finishGame()`'s non-shared branch always created a fresh `GameSession` and overwrote it before the reveal screen could ever read a stale value — but now that `finishGame()` can finish (`$ended = true`) *without* touching `gameSessionId` (the early-end path), a second game ended early after a first, *recorded* one would have shown a Copy/share-link input pointing at the **previous** game's `GameSession`, not the current (nonexistent) one. Fixed by resetting `gameSessionId` (and `endedEarly`) in `resetGame()` too. The reveal screen's own share-link block also gained an explicit `$gameSessionId !== null` guard (previously unconditional) — without it, an early-ended game would have thrown building `route('boggle.share', ['gameSession' => null])`, a required route parameter.
- Added `id="end-game-btn"` to the End Game button itself (previously unidentified) — needed once a browser test tried to click it and hung: "End Game" is *also* literal text inside the Rules dialog's own copy ("Press End Game any time to stop early..."), the exact ambiguous-text-click hang this app's own testing docs already warn about.
- Test coverage: three new `Livewire::test()` cases in `tests/Feature/Games/BoggleGameTest.php` (a timed game ended immediately isn't recorded; a timed game whose `endsAt` is traveled into the past *is* still recorded; `resetGame()` after an early end clears the stale state and a following untimed game records normally) and a new `tests/Browser/BoggleEndGameEarlyTest.php` (real click-through: the "not saved" message and no share link for an early-ended timed game; the share link and no message for a normal untimed one) — the conditional-Blade-visibility class of bug this app has repeatedly shipped broken before.

**"New Game" at the end of a solo Boggle game silently did nothing (user-reported, 2026-08-18) — a real, pre-existing bug, confirmed unrelated to the Task 18 work directly above it before chasing it further.** First ruled out the obvious suspect: a `git worktree` bisect against the commit *before* any of that session's changes reproduced the identical symptom, so this wasn't something the stats-exclusion work broke.
- **Root-caused with a raw `fetch`/XHR intercept, not guesswork** — clicking "New Game" fired **zero** network requests at all, not even a wrong/no-op one. That ruled out a server-side logic bug immediately (confirmed separately via `Livewire::test()` that `resetGame()` itself works perfectly server-side) and pointed at the client never even dispatching the click through Livewire in the first place.
- **The actual mechanism: `@teleport` compiles to Alpine's own `x-teleport` directive** (`vendor/livewire/livewire/src/Features/SupportTeleporting/SupportTeleporting.php` — `<template x-teleport="...">`), and reading Alpine's own morph implementation (`vendor/livewire/livewire/dist/livewire.js`) showed it has explicit, separate interop for teleported content: `if (from._x_teleport && to._x_teleport) { context.patch(from._x_teleport, to._x_teleport) }`, patching the teleported destination's content as its own nested step, distinct from the normal in-tree child morph. Boggle's own teleport block put the two conditionally-swapped `<button>` elements (End Game / New Game) *directly* as that patch target, with no stable wrapping element between them and the teleport boundary.
- **Two follow-up fix attempts, both dead ends, worth recording so they aren't retried**: (1) `wire:key` on each button (Livewire's own documented mechanism for telling a morph "these are different elements, replace me") had zero effect — still zero requests after clicking New Game. (2) Rendering *both* buttons unconditionally and toggling a `hidden` class instead of swapping which element exists seemed like the safe rewrite, but broke differently: confirmed live (dumping `#game-actions`' raw `innerHTML`) that only the **first** button ever appeared in the teleported output at all, on every render — Alpine's `x-teleport` only ever moves a template's *first root child* to the target, so the second sibling button was silently dropped entirely, not hidden.
- **The actual fix, and already the established pattern everywhere else in this app**: wrap the teleported content in a single, stable `<div style="display: contents;">`, with the real conditional content as its *children*. Checked every other game's own `@teleport('#game-actions')` block (Yahtzee, Farkle, Family Feud, Best Match, Clear the Deck) while fixing this — **all five already do exactly this**, unprompted; Boggle's was the one `@teleport` block in the entire app missing the wrapper, apparently never revisited since Boggle's End Game/New Game toggle was one of the earliest features built (see "Origins" above), before this convention was established for the games that followed. With the wrapper, teleport only ever has the one always-present `div` to move (no first-child-only ambiguity, no swap-vs-patch ambiguity), and the button toggle inside it goes through Livewire's ordinary, non-teleport-specific child morphing — the same mechanism every other conditional element in this app's components already relies on successfully.
- Confirmed fixed via the same fetch-intercept technique: clicking "New Game" now fires a real `livewire/update` request, and the button-bar correctly swaps back to "End Game" with a fresh board afterward.
- Regression coverage: new `tests/Browser/BoggleNewGameTest.php` (a real click-through: end a game, click New Game, confirm the End Game button and a fresh, non-reveal game state actually come back — not just that *something* renders).

**"Points from long words" stat added (`docs/concerns.md`, 2026-08-21).** Requested as a way to see how much of a game's score came from the harder, higher-value finds rather than a pile of short 3-4-letter words worth 1pt each. `BoggleStatsService::statsFor()` gained `longWordPoints`, summed directly from `found_words`' existing `word => points` map for every word over 4 letters (`mb_strlen($word) > 4`, i.e. 5+ letters — the same length `Scorer::pointsFor()` starts awarding 2pts instead of 1) rather than recomputed via `Scorer` — `found_words` already stores the exact points each word earned *for that participant*, and stays intact as every word the player found regardless of mode (see `SharedSessionFinalizer` below), so no separate scoring pass was needed. Surfaced in two places: a new tile on the per-game detail page (`pages::profile.boggle.show`) alongside Score/Words found, and a new "Most points from long words (5+ letters)" entry in `bestMetrics()` (the per-game index page's personal-bests row).

**Daily puzzles (`docs/TODO.md`'s Task 11, 2026-08-21) — see "Word Blocks" below for the shared `DailyPuzzle` seed-derivation service; this entry covers Boggle's own wiring, the larger half of the two games' work since Boggle had no resumable-in-progress persistence at all before this.**
- `#[Url] public ?string $dailyDate` (a `Y-m-d` string) drives a new `mountDaily()` branch in `mount()`, checked before the existing `$room`/`$challenge` handling. The seed comes from `DailyPuzzle::seedFor(GameType::Boggle, $date)`, not `$seed` — an explicit `$seed`/`$size`/`$minutes` query override is silently ignored for a daily, same defensive reasoning `isSharedSession` already applies to a room/challenge board (a player shouldn't be able to hand-edit the URL into an easier daily). **Every daily is forced untimed, size 4** (`$minutes = 0`, `$endsAt = null`) regardless of any override — deliberately, so there's nothing to persist/restore across a page reload mid-countdown, and so every player's daily is directly comparable (no size/time advantage).
- **Only *today's* date is locked to one signed-in play per day** — `findDailySession()`/`startDailySession()` mirror Word Blocks' `resumeInProgressGame()`/`startNewSession()` pattern (an early `GameSession` row created the moment the board is generated, not deferred to game-end), new `persistDailyState()` (mirrors Word Blocks' `persistState()`) called at the end of `submitWord()` after every scored word. A guest, or any date that isn't today (an archive entry), is instead treated exactly like an ordinary seeded puzzle — no early row, no lock, freely replayable every time, matching Wordle-style archive UX (no streak feature exists here to protect).
- `finishGame()`'s `isSharedSession` ? update : create branch became three-way, with an `elseif ($this->isDaily && $this->gameSessionId !== null)` arm updating the early daily row in place (`state` + `ended_at`) rather than creating a second row — same reasoning as the shared-session arm right above it. The existing "ended early, don't record" guard (`! $this->isSharedSession && ($this->remainingSeconds ?? 0) > 0`) needed no change: a daily is always untimed, so `remainingSeconds` is `null` there, coalescing to `0`.
- **Reveal screen**: the existing solo share-link box (`$room === null && $challenge === null && $gameSessionId !== null`) gained `&& ! $isDaily` — a daily's board is fixed/comparable across every player, so its own "share" story is "play today's daily" (the archive page), not a personal async challenge to this one board instance. In its place, `$isDaily && $dailyDate === today` renders a new `<x-daily-countdown>` component (plain Alpine `x-data`/`x-init`/`setInterval`, no `$wire` needed, dropped in wherever a "come back tomorrow" nudge is needed — reused identically by Word Blocks) counting down to the next UTC-midnight reset. The teleported button bar's `@else` "New Game" arm gained a matching `@elseif ($isDaily)`, linking to the new `/daily-puzzles` archive page instead (a daily isn't reroll-able, same reasoning already applied to the room/challenge arms next to it) — `resetGame()` itself also gained a defensive `|| $this->isDaily` no-op guard, even though the UI never offers the button for a daily.
- Regression risk called out explicitly during planning and covered by a dedicated test: with dailies added, a signed-in user can have *two* legitimately separate in-progress rows at once (their ordinary random game — Boggle has never had one of these persist before now — and today's daily). `findDailySession()`/`startDailySession()` are scoped by `is_daily = true` specifically so they can never collide with anything else.
- New `App\Notifications\GameChallengeReceived` + `App\Services\Social\GameChallengeService` (queued mail, modeled directly on `FriendRequestReceived`/`FriendService::sendRequest()`, including the same guest-recipient guard) power a **"Challenge a Friend" button** on the reveal screen, `@auth`-gated, via a new shared `<livewire:challenge-friend>` component (a friend-picker `<x-dialog>`, `wire:ignore.self` for the same "an action re-render shouldn't silently re-close the dialog" reason `join-room-dialog` already needed). Boggle's own challenge link is deliberately its **existing** `route('boggle.share', ...)` shared/ranked-session link, not a second unranked one — the friend joins the same `GameSession` and gets ranked against the sender exactly like the manual copy-link flow already did; only the delivery (a real email vs. copy-paste) is new. `GameType::isChallengeable()` (`true` for Boggle and Word Blocks today) is the extensibility hook for whichever future puzzle game gets a shareable link next.
- New migration adds `is_daily` (bool, default `false`) and `daily_date` (plain string column, deliberately **not** cast to `date` on the model — Eloquent's `date` cast reformats to a full datetime on *write*, only truncating on *read*, which drifts from a bare `toDateString()` comparison under sqlite's dynamic typing; a real `DATE` column under MariaDB would happen to truncate on its own, but the mismatch would still bite the moment sqlite-backed tests ran, so the model just treats it as a plain `Y-m-d` string everywhere instead) plus a composite `(user_id, type, is_daily, daily_date)` index, shared with Word Blocks on the same `game_sessions` table.
- New `/daily-puzzles` route (`pages::daily-puzzles`), one shared page for both games rather than one per game — a plain 30-row date list (today back 29 days), each row linking both games' own `?dailyDate=...` param. No auth middleware (guests can browse and play the archive, same as any random puzzle) and no new DB table or scheduled job — every day's puzzle is derived on the fly by `DailyPuzzle::seedFor()`, so the "archive" is pure date math, zero persisted rows.
- Tests: `tests/Feature/Games/BoggleDailyTest.php` (deterministic board for a date; forced untimed/size-4 regardless of overrides; early row created and tagged `is_daily` for a signed-in user; a finished daily restores read-only on reload; an in-progress daily resumes found words/score; a past date is always freely replayable with no lock; the New Game button links to the archive instead), `tests/Feature/Games/BoggleChallengeFriendTest.php` (button visibility, notification recipient/link/guest-exclusion), `tests/Browser/BoggleDailyResumeTest.php` (a real login + reload confirms the locked "already done" state actually survives a browser reload, not just an in-memory Livewire assertion), `tests/Browser/BoggleChallengeFriendDialogTest.php` (a real click-through: open the dialog, send, see the confirmation).

## Yahtzee

- `App\Services\Yahtzee\{DiceRoller,Scorer,GameEngine}` + Livewire, pass-and-play only at first (online mode added later — see "Online rooms for Yahtzee" below).

Decisions and bugs, deliberate deviations from the original plan's literal wording:
- `App\Services\Yahtzee\{DiceRoller,Scorer,GameEngine}` split by responsibility: `DiceRoller` is `initial()`/`reroll()` (server-side `random_int`); `Scorer` is the pure per-category calculator (mirrors legacy's `calcScore()`, told `$jokerBonusEligible` rather than working it out itself); `GameEngine` orchestrates the Joker rule / Yahtzee bonus chip (which need a player's other scores) and owns the aggregate math. New `App\Enums\YahtzeeCategory` (13 cases) replaces legacy's plain-object category arrays.
- Livewire: single full-page `pages::yahtzee.play`, mirroring legacy's single-page setup-then-game-then-reveal structure.
- Real bug caught before shipping: `GameEngine`'s helper methods and per-cell `categoryScore()`/`isPickable()` calls are invoked *directly from the Blade template*, not via `wire:click`. Livewire's automatic method-dependency-injection only applies to the latter — a plain method call from a render has no framework layer to inject anything, so an initial version with `GameEngine $engine` as a method parameter would have fatal-errored the moment the scorecard rendered. Fixed by resolving `GameEngine` once via a `boot(GameEngine $engine)` hook into a private property instead.

**Yahtzee undo + "which player/team is you" attribution.** Reported bug: a logged-in Yahtzee game never showed up in "recent games." By design, not a bug — Yahtzee/Family Feud were single-device pass-and-play games where every local player/team was just a typed name (`guest_name`), never `user_id`, since there was normally no way to know which local player was the account holder (Boggle always has a real, if throwaway, account per player, which is why only Boggle ever showed up). This pass shipped two small, independent pieces:
- **Yahtzee undo**: reinstated legacy's snapshot/restore undo (`pushHistory()`/`undo()`), deliberately dropped when Yahtzee was first ported. A public `array $history` property (must be public — Livewire only persists public properties across requests) snapshots `dice`/`kept`/`rollsLeft`/`players`/`currentPlayer` right before every `roll()` and `scoreCategory()`, mirroring exactly which two actions legacy's `pushHistory()` guarded. Not usable once `$gameOver` — `endGame()` already persists a `GameSession`/`GameParticipant` the moment the last category is scored, and unwinding that DB write was out of scope. Redo was never surfaced in legacy's own UI either and stays out here too.
- **"Which player/team is you?"**: an optional setup-time picker (`meIndex` for Yahtzee), shown only `@auth`, using the existing `.option`/`.option-group` button-group markup verbatim. Whichever local player is picked gets `user_id`/`guest_name` swapped the same way Boggle's own solo games already do — every other participant in that game stays guest-only, avoiding the double-counting trap of tagging *every* local player as "you" (which would skew win-rate/avg-score for any multi-player local game). No migration needed and no changes needed to `PlayerStatsService` — it already queries by `user_id`.

**Yahtzee sizing: the full scorecard now fits one screen on desktop without scrolling.** Reported as a TODO ("Yahtzee should all fit on one screen on desktop"); the page already had CSS aimed squarely at this (`body.scrollable.yahtzee-play.in-game { height: 100dvh; }`, `#yahtzee-game`'s flex/`min-height:0` chain, `.card-wrap`'s `overflow: auto` safety net) but none of it actually worked. Two real, independent bugs, found by measuring layout via a scripted headless-Chrome (CDP) session rather than eyeballing screenshots:
- **The `in-game` class never reached the live `<body>`, ever, even on first load.** `<x-slot:bodyClass>yahtzee-play{{ $started ? ' in-game' : '' }}</x-slot>` is a layout-level slot, evaluated exactly once at the page's first render — and `$started` is always `false` at that moment (the setup screen is what mounts first), so `in-game` was never in the server-rendered HTML to begin with. Worse, `startGame()`'s own Livewire round trip only re-renders and morphs the component's *own* root element, never touching `<body>` in the surrounding layout — the same bug class documented for the button-bar's `@teleport` fix (see "Site-wide design system & chrome" below), and already fixed once before for Boggle's own `in-game` toggle. Yahtzee just hadn't gotten that same treatment. Fixed identically: an `@script` block calling `document.body.classList.toggle('in-game', $wire.started)` plus a `$wire.$watch('started', ...)` for every later Livewire update; removed the now-provably-dead `$started ? ' in-game' : ''` from the bodyClass slot entirely rather than leaving inert markup.
- **The root `<div>` wrapping the whole component broke the flex chain from `<body>` down to `#yahtzee-game`.** `#yahtzee-game { flex: 1; min-height: 0; }` only means something if its *parent* is a flex container sized by the page — but that parent was a bare `<div>` with no flex classes at all, so it just sized to its content's natural height (auto), and `#yahtzee-game`'s own `flex: 1` had nothing to grow or shrink against. Same underlying lesson as Boggle's own centering bugs (a plain wrapper `<div>` doesn't participate in a flex layout it isn't itself part of). Fixed by giving that root `<div>` `class="flex flex-col items-center w-full flex-1 min-h-0"`, matching the pattern Boggle's own play page root already uses.
- With both of those actually wired up, `<body>` correctly clipped to `100dvh` — but `.card-wrap` (holding the 17-row table) still needed its own internal scrollbar, because cell font-size/padding were sized off raw viewport `vh`, entirely disconnected from how much vertical room the dice area above it actually used, or from the fixed row count. Fixed by giving `.card-wrap` `container-type: size` and switching `table.scorecard th`/`td`'s font-size/padding to `cqh` (a percentage of `.card-wrap`'s own resolved height) instead of `vh` — the 17 rows now always divide up whatever space is actually left, self-adjusting to any dice-area height or viewport size without referencing the row count or viewport height directly.
- Verified across 2 and 8 players (min/max) at four viewport heights (720/768/900/1080, 1440/1366/1280/1920 wide) via the same scripted CDP session: `document.body.scrollHeight` exactly equals `window.innerHeight` and `.card-wrap`'s `scrollHeight` exactly equals its `clientHeight` in every case — zero pixels of overflow. No test coverage exists (or was added) for pure CSS layout fit, which isn't something Pest can assert on — this class of bug is exactly what the new Pest browser-testing setup (see the bottom of this file, and `CLAUDE.md`) is meant to start catching automatically going forward.

**Online rooms for Yahtzee** (the multi-session item scoped out in `docs/TODO.md`; Family Feud's own follow-up is what remains there now). Reused `Room`/`RoomStatus`/`routes/channels.php`/`⚡game-lobby.blade.php`/`RoomGameStarting` entirely as-is — all genuinely game-agnostic already (see "Room system" above) — and added the shared infrastructure this app didn't have yet:
- **`GameSession.state`** (new nullable `json` column): the persisted, server-authoritative blob for an in-progress online game (`seats`, `currentSeat`, `dice`, `kept`, `rollsLeft`, `gameOver`) — broadcast via a new, deliberately game-agnostic `App\Events\RoomGameStateUpdated` (`ShouldBroadcastNow`, full state each time rather than a delta, matching this app's preference for simplicity over premature optimization) any time a seat successfully rolls/keeps/scores.
- **`RoomSeat`** (new model/table/factory): `room_id` + `seat_index` + `user_id`, unique on both pairs — what lets a reloading phone recover "you are seat 2" from `Auth::id()` alone, independent of ephemeral presence-channel membership. Deliberately minimal (no `team_index`/`is_captain`) since Yahtzee has no teams; Family Feud's own follow-up adds those when it actually needs them.
- New `pages::yahtzee.room` (mirroring `pages::boggle.room`), consuming `game-lobby`'s `$players` payload (see "Room system" above) to seat-assign every ready player at start time, in join order.
- **Turn authorization**: `roll()`/`toggleKeep()`/`scoreCategory()` on `pages::yahtzee.play` each gained `abort_unless($this->room === null || $this->mySeatIndex === $this->currentPlayer, 403)` — local pass-and-play (`$room === null`) completely untouched by this. `mySeatIndex` (null for a spectator/host connection) is looked up from `RoomSeat` at `mount()`, not guessed. **Bug found and fixed**: `isPickable()` initially only checked `$playerIndex === $this->currentPlayer` — i.e. whose *column* this is, not who's actually looking at it. Any other connected seat viewing the current player's column got a live, clickable `wire:click="scoreCategory(...)"` wired onto that cell, which then 403'd server-side the moment they actually tapped it, instead of simply never being clickable in the first place. Fixed by adding `&& $this->isMyTurn` to `isPickable()`'s conditions.
- **Broadcast payload binding gotcha**: local Livewire dispatches (`$this->dispatch(...)`) bind named args to listener parameters by name, but Echo/broadcast custom events deliver their entire `broadcastWith()` payload as **one positional argument** — an initial `onStateUpdated(...)` written to expect named args silently failed to update anything. Fixed to match the existing `onReadyToggled(array $event)` pattern already used elsewhere in this codebase: `onStateUpdated(array $event)` reads `$event['state']` itself.
- **Undo intentionally isn't supported online** (hidden in Blade, `undo()` early-returns when `$room !== null`) — rewinding shared state other seats may have already seen broadcast is a real, unsolved design problem (whose view wins?) with no user request driving it, so it was cut rather than half-solved.
- **Reconnect / mid-game recovery**: `mount()`, when `$room !== null`, resolves the `Room`, loads its `GameSession`, and hydrates `$players`/`$dice`/`$kept`/`$rollsLeft`/`$currentPlayer`/`$gameOver`/`$started = true` straight from `GameSession->state` — a reload recovers mid-game state instead of re-running local setup screen from scratch. Combined with the lobby's own ready-state persistence fix (see "Room system" above), a phone that loses connection at any point — pre-game or mid-game — can reload and land back exactly where it was.
- **Spectator/host view, and "watch only" for a seated player on a second device**: whenever a connected account occupies no seat in the room (`mySeatIndex === null`), or a seated player explicitly opts into a `#[Url] public bool $spectate` flag (a per-device URL toggle, not account-wide — deliberately so switching it on a casting device doesn't also switch it on the device the same account is actually playing from), the view becomes read-only: dice values and kept/selected highlighting are still shown live (a spectator benefits from watching the roll), but clicking a die to keep it and the Roll button itself are hidden outright rather than merely disabled — a disabled-but-visible Roll button invites clicking it anyway, the exact class of bug `isPickable()` just got fixed for above. A "Player view"/"Watch only" toggle button (teleported into `#game-actions`, shown only to a seated player) flips `$spectate` for the local device only.
- **Mobile scoreboard drawer**: a "Scores" button (`id="scores-btn"`, teleported into `#game-actions`, online games only, desktop-hidden via CSS since every column already fits on the main desktop scorecard) opens a full-screen `<dialog class="scoreboard-dialog">` showing the same scorecard read-only for every player, reusing a newly-extracted `<x-yahtzee-scorecard-table>` Blade component — so a group playing purely on their own phones, with no separate host/TV device, can still check the shared board on demand. That extraction is a plain Blade component (not a `@include`, which this codebase had never used and turned out not to propagate `$this` into the included view the way the surrounding Livewire single-file component does) — `$livewire` is passed in explicitly as a prop instead, sidestepping the ambiguity entirely.
  - **`dialog:not([open]) { display: none }` UA-default bug**: the drawer's own `dialog.scoreboard-dialog { display: flex; ...; inset: 0; }` rule set `display` unconditionally, which — since author styles always beat the browser's built-in UA stylesheet regardless of specificity — silently overrode the browser default that hides a `<dialog>` without the `open` attribute. The *closed* dialog was therefore a permanent, invisible-but-present full-viewport `position: absolute; inset: 0` overlay for every single visitor, on every page load, intercepting layout (and, given a stray click, input) the entire time. Confirmed live via `getComputedStyle`/`hasAttribute('open')`. Fixed by scoping the whole ruleset to `dialog.scoreboard-dialog[open]` — `.rules-dialog` never had this bug only because it happens to not set `display` at all, leaving the UA default intact.
  - **Closing on unrelated re-renders**: the native `open` attribute is untracked DOM state; any Livewire re-render (triggered by *any* broadcast, including from other players' actions) morphs the tree back to server-rendered markup (which never has `open` on it), silently closing the drawer out from under whoever had it open, mid-read. Fixed with `wire:ignore.self` on the `<dialog>` — `.self`, not a bare `wire:ignore`, so the scorecard *inside* still updates live while the drawer's open; only the dialog element's own open/closed attribute is left untouched by morphing. Verified live: opened the drawer, simulated an incoming broadcast (`wire.call('onStateUpdated', {state: newState})`), confirmed `dialog.hasAttribute('open')` stayed `true` while the displayed score updated underneath, and confirmed the close (×) button still worked afterward.
  - **Equal-width columns, both mobile and desktop**: per-cell `width`/`max-width` turned out not to be reliably binding under `table-layout: auto` (two columns both declared `width: 10ch` still rendered at visibly different widths) — fixed with a `<colgroup>`/`<col>` per column instead, which *is* binding, but only once `table-layout: fixed` is set **and** the table's own `width` is an explicit, definite value rather than `auto` (confirmed live: `fixed` plus an auto table width still let a longer name's column render wider than a shorter one's). The definite width is computed from CSS custom properties, `--scorecard-category-width`/`--scorecard-player-width` (`:root`-level, so `<col>`'s widths and the table's own total width can never drift out of sync) times a `--player-count` value set inline per-render by `<x-yahtzee-scorecard-table>`.
  - **Mobile single-seat narrowing broke desktop, then broke itself**: on mobile, a seated player's own phone hides every other seat's column (`not-mine`, driven by `mySeatIndex`) since the drawer above already covers "see everyone." Collapsing just the *cells* (`display: none`) left each hidden column's `<col>` still contributing its share to `--player-count`'s total width, reserving a dead visual gap where they used to be. Collapsing the `<col>` itself (`width: 0`) instead of the cell fixed the gap — but combining `display: none` on the cell **and** `width: 0` on its `<col>` simultaneously turned out to be actively broken in a different way: under `table-layout: fixed`, Chromium then rendered the *next* (still-visible, "mine") column's cells at zero width too, even though that column's own `<col>` correctly still specified its real width — confirmed via precise `getBoundingClientRect()` measurements and a live diagnostic test reverting the cell's `display` back to its default. The actual, final fix was to remove `display: none` from the cells entirely and collapse *only* the `<col>` — the existing `overflow: hidden` on non-category cells already makes a zero-width cell show nothing, with no separate `display` override needed or safe to add.
  - **Desktop category labels truncating**: the `18ch` category-column width, sized against the *mobile* font-size, truncated labels like "Bonus (63+ = 35)"/"Upper Subtotal" once the responsive font-size clamp reached its own max (28px), which only happens once `.card-wrap` has real desktop-height room to grow into — confirmed by forcing `font-size: 28px !important` via an injected style and checking `scrollWidth > clientWidth`. Fixed by widening both `--scorecard-category-width`/`--scorecard-player-width` (18ch/10ch mobile → 22ch/16ch) inside a `@media (min-width: 900px)` override of the `:root` custom properties above.
  - **Row labels cut off on mobile, and not sticky**: the category column was getting squeezed hard enough on a real phone width to visibly cut off labels, competing with the (now fixed-width) player column for room. Fixed by shrinking and un-bolding that column's font specifically on mobile (`font-size: clamp(9px, 3.2cqh, 20px); font-weight: normal;`), and making it `position: sticky; left: 0;` (against `.card-wrap`'s own `overflow: auto` scrolling ancestor) so the row you're looking at stays identifiable while scrolling horizontally through more players than fit on screen at once.
  - **Roll button and dice sharing one row; smaller dice**: `.dice-area` used to `flex-wrap: wrap`, and `.row`'s own width (`min(94vw, 64vh, 1200px)`) claimed nearly the full available width, leaving no room for Roll/Undo beside it — they always landed on their own row below the dice. Switched `.dice-area` to `flex-wrap: nowrap` and meaningfully narrowed `.row` (`min(56vw, 42vh, 720px)`) and its die font-size (`clamp(18px, 5vmin, 72px)`, down from `clamp(26px, 8vmin, 120px)`) so Roll/Undo fit on the same row as the dice — freeing the vertical space a second controls row used to cost for `.card-wrap` to grow into instead, making the scorecard itself taller and each category cell easier to tap precisely.
- **Ending the game**: reuses the existing `endGame()`/participant-creation logic as-is, with one simplification specific to online mode — each seat's `RoomSeat->user_id` is always a real (if possibly guest) account, so `GameParticipant` attribution no longer needs the local mode's `meIndex`-guessing; every seat writes `user_id` directly. No separate `RoomGameEnded` broadcast is needed (unlike Boggle) — Yahtzee's shared turn state means every player already learns "game over" from the same final `RoomGameStateUpdated` (`gameOver: true`) that ended the last turn.
- **Known, deliberately accepted limitation**: seats are bound to `user_id`, not to a device/session, so the same account logged in on two devices at once will show interactive controls on *both* during that seat's turn unless the casting device's user manually flips its own `$spectate` toggle (see above) — solving this automatically would need session/device-scoped seat binding, a bigger change with no separate request driving it.
- **Join-ID rename mirrored from Boggle**: once the Boggle mobile-Safari autofocus fix (see the Boggle section above) was confirmed working via the "join code"→"join ID" rename, the identical rename (`joinCode`→`joinID`, `id="join-code"`→`id="join"`, "Room code"→"Room ID", `maxlength` removed) was mirrored into Yahtzee's own room-join UI.
- **Yahtzee stats page**, mirroring Boggle's own (`profile.boggle`/`BoggleStatsService`): new `profile.yahtzee`/`profile.yahtzee.show` routes and `App\Services\Stats\YahtzeeStatsService` (constructor-injects the existing pure `GameEngine` rather than re-deriving upper subtotal/bonus math). Needed `endGame()` to start persisting each participant's full per-category `scores` array in `stats` (previously only `yahtzee_bonus_count` was kept) — free, since every category's final score is already computed in memory right there, same reasoning as Boggle's own `total_valid_words`. `<x-player-stats>`'s hardcoded "only Boggle links anywhere" check became a small `match` over game type instead. Multi-account attribution for a shared online game falls straight out of the `RoomSeat -> user_id` design above — every distinct logged-in account gets its own correctly attributed `GameParticipant` row already, no separate work needed.
- Added `tests/Feature/Games/YahtzeeRoomTest.php`, `tests/Unit/Stats/YahtzeeStatsServiceTest.php`, `tests/Feature/ProfileYahtzeeTest.php`, and 2 new tests in `tests/Feature/GameLobbyTest.php` (the ready-state cache behavior). Full `php artisan test` green throughout, including the existing `BoggleRoomTest`/`YahtzeeGameTest` suites (confirming the `game-lobby` dispatch-payload change and every online-mode branch are additive, not disruptive, to what already worked).
- **Verification-process correction, worth keeping visible**: an early pass through this feature was reported as working based only on Pest assertions (which invoke Livewire component methods directly and never render real DOM/CSS) — the actual live views were wrong in ways no test caught (no roll button rendered for anyone, mobile showed every player instead of one, the player view didn't show dice at all). Every fix listed above under "spectator/host view," the dialog bugs, and the column-width bugs was instead confirmed against a real running browser (screenshots, `getComputedStyle`, `getBoundingClientRect`, simulated broadcasts via `wire.call(...)`) before being called done. This is now a standing practice for any UI/CSS change in this app, not just this feature — see the browser-testing section at the end of this file and `CLAUDE.md`'s own testing rules.

**Two reported watch-only/mobile-drawer gaps, from `docs/TODO.md`.**
- **Watch-only leaked live possible scores for the active seat.** `categoryScore()`'s only gate against showing a computed possible score was `isPickable()`, which checks `isMyTurn` — deliberately independent of the `$spectate` flag (see its own docblock above), since `isMyTurn` still has to say "yes" for `roll()`/`toggleKeep()` to keep working from a casting device. That meant switching a device to "Watch only" mid-turn left every open category in the active seat's own column still showing the live number they'd get for picking it right now, rather than blank like any other unfilled category ought to look from a read-only view. The click-ability half of this (`wire:click`/`.pickable`) was already correctly gated on `$readOnly` — only the displayed *text* wasn't. Fixed by adding an explicit `$this->isSpectator ||` check to `categoryScore()` itself, ahead of the `isPickable()` call, so the display gate no longer piggybacks on a property that was never meant to encode "is this device read-only."
- **Mobile Scores drawer showed only the scorecard, no dice or roll status.** The drawer (`#scoreboard-drawer`) exists specifically because a seated player's own phone only shows their own column (see "single-seat narrowing" above) — but it only ever rendered the read-only `<x-yahtzee-scorecard-table>`, with no way to see whose turn it currently is or what's been rolled without closing the drawer first. Fixed by rendering the same `statusText`/dice-row markup the main view already uses (no `wire:click`/Roll/Undo, matching the drawer's existing read-only intent) above the scorecard inside the dialog. No new "round counter" was added — there's no such concept anywhere in `GameEngine`/`GameSession.state` today, and `statusText`'s existing "`{name}'s turn — Roll N of 3`" line already conveys the same "what's currently happening" information the request was after. Deliberately shrunk the dice specifically inside the drawer (a new `.drawer-dice-area` CSS class, smaller `.row` width and `.number` font-size than the main view's) rather than the main in-game view's own dice, since the drawer now has a header + status line competing for the same full-screen-dialog height the main view doesn't have to share; the scorecard below needed no explicit size changes — its existing `cqh`-based sizing (see "Equal-width columns" above) already reflows to fill whatever's left.
- Both covered with regression tests rather than only manual verification: a `tests/Feature/Games/YahtzeeRoomTest.php` case asserting `categoryScore()` returns `null` (not the live number) the instant `spectate` flips true for the current seat, and reverts once flipped back; and a `tests/Browser/YahtzeeRoomBrowserTest.php` case asserting the roll-status text and `.dice-area` are visible inside the opened drawer, matching the file's existing single-real-browser-page-per-test convention.

**Mobile single-seat narrowing and the Scores drawer, removed; watch-only's own live-preview hiding, deliberately reversed (`docs/concerns.md`, superseding both entries directly above).** Two follow-up requests intentionally undid design decisions from the section just above, once actual usage showed the opposite was wanted:
- **"Show scores for all players on the main screen, no separate panel."** The main view always renders every player's column now, on every viewport — `<x-yahtzee-scorecard-table>` dropped its `mySeatIndex` prop entirely (along with the `single-seat-mobile`/`not-mine` CSS classes and `<col>`-collapsing it drove), and `⚡play.blade.php` dropped `#scores-btn` and the `#scoreboard-drawer` dialog outright, relying on `.card-wrap`'s pre-existing `overflow: auto` + sticky category column instead of hiding columns to fit. `resources/css/app.css` lost the whole `single-seat-mobile`/`scoreboard-dialog`/`drawer-dice-area` block (~140 lines) as a result, along with the two `#scores-btn`-specific fullscreen/chrome-hidden exceptions that existed only to keep that drawer's trigger reachable in fullscreen.
- **"Show other players' options and possible scores during their turn, colored differently from recorded scores."** `isPickable()` split into `isAvailable()` (open category, current player's column, full roll — drives display, for *any* viewer) and a thinner `isPickable()` (`isAvailable() && isMyTurn` — drives actual clickability only). `categoryScore()` now gates on `isAvailable()` instead of `isPickable()`/`isSpectator`, so a live preview number shows to every viewer during the current player's turn, not just that player. **This is a deliberate, direct reversal of "watch-only leaked live possible scores for the active seat"** two entries above — that fix hid the number from any non-acting viewer; this request asks for the opposite, so the earlier "leak" is now the intended behavior. `.score-cell.available` (not `.pickable`) now carries the accent color/background that used to signal "you can tap this" — `.pickable` itself was narrowed to just `cursor: pointer` + hover, layered on top for the subset of cells that are both available *and* actually clickable by this viewer.
- Test coverage: the "watch-only... leaked" Feature test was rewritten to assert the opposite (`tests/Feature/Games/YahtzeeRoomTest.php`), plus a new case for a true spectator (no seat at all, not just `$spectate`-toggled); the drawer-specific browser tests in `tests/Browser/YahtzeeRoomBrowserTest.php` were replaced with one asserting every seat's column renders on the main view and one confirming the scorecard actually scrolls horizontally (`scrollWidth > clientWidth`, then a real `scrollLeft` move) at a 6-player width too narrow to fit them all at once.

**Yahtzee undo: redesigned to be opt-in, room-capable, and roll-only, with a confirm step replacing its old ability to also undo a score (`docs/concerns.md`).** Undo already existed (see "Undo functionality" above) but was local-pass-and-play-only, always on, and — because `scoreCategory()` used to call `pushHistory()` too and nothing ever cleared `$history` at a turn boundary — a sequence of undos could actually step back across a turn boundary and undo a score, which this request explicitly wants impossible now.
- **Opt-in `undoEnabled`, chosen at setup, off by default.** `⚡play.blade.php` gained the toggle on its existing local setup screen (an `.option-group`, same pattern as `$meIndex`'s own). Yahtzee's room lobby (`⚡room.blade.php`) never had *any* pre-start settings step before this — unlike Boggle's own room page, which already sets `$size`/`$minutes` on itself and bakes them into `GameSession.settings` before `<livewire:game-lobby>` — so this added that same pattern to Yahtzee for the first time: a host-only `$undoEnabled` toggle rendered above the lobby, baked into `'settings' => ['player_count' => ..., 'undo_enabled' => $this->undoEnabled]` in `onRoomStarting()`. `⚡play.blade.php`'s `mount()` reads it back once (`$session->settings['undo_enabled'] ?? false`) for the online path. **Bug caught immediately by an actual page load, not the Feature test suite**: the new `@if ($this->isHost)` in the room Blade referenced a computed property that had simply never existed on this component before (Yahtzee's room page never needed host-only branching until now) — `Livewire\Exceptions\PropertyNotFoundException`, invisible to `Livewire::test()`-style Feature tests (which never render the real page), only surfaced by actually visiting `/yahtzee/room/{code}`. Fixed by adding the same `getIsHostProperty()` every other room page already has.
- **Roll-only, current-turn-only, by construction, not convention.** `scoreCategory()` no longer calls `pushHistory()` at all — only `roll()` still snapshots. `startTurn()` now clears `$this->history = []` at the start of every turn, so a stale snapshot from the previous player's turn can never be popped into the new one. `undo()` itself changed from an outright `$room !== null` block to the same `abort_unless(...$mySeatIndex === $this->currentPlayer, 403)` shape every other mutating action already uses, gated on `$undoEnabled` instead of local-only, and calls `syncOnlineStateIfNeeded()` so a rewind broadcasts.
- **A confirm step replaces undo's old ability to fix a bad score pick.** New `$pendingCategory`, `requestScore()` (does the same `isPickable()` validation `scoreCategory()` already did, but only records which category was tapped) and `cancelScore()`. The scorecard's `wire:click` now calls `requestScore()` instead of `scoreCategory()` directly; a new `<x-confirm-dialog id="score-confirm-dialog">` (reusing the same component `pages::room.show` already uses for End Room/Leave Room) shows the pending category's label and live score (`pendingScoreMessage()`), with Confirm calling the real `scoreCategory()` and Cancel/Escape/backdrop-click all routed through one `close` event listener calling `cancelScore()`.
- **Two real Blade-compilation bugs found the hard way, both from the same root cause: Livewire's `@script`/`<x-...>` compilation scans the raw template text for those literal markers, not just real Blade directives/tags — even inside a `{{-- --}}` comment or a JS `//` comment.** A doc-comment mentioning "the `@script` block below" made Livewire's script-relocation pass think a *second* `@script` region started there, and a separate comment mentioning "`<x-dialog>`'s own `.js-dialog` handling" made Blade's component-tag compiler try to compile that prose as a real, unclosed component tag. Both corrupted the compiled output into something PHP couldn't parse (`ParseError: unexpected end of file, expecting elseif or else or endif`) — invisible to `Livewire::test()`-based Feature tests in this instance too, since the view happened to already be compiled from before the change in one of the runs; only a fresh `$this->get('/yahtzee')` after clearing `storage/framework/views` surfaced it reliably. Root-caused (not just guessed at) by lexing the compiled output with PHP's own `token_get_all()` and counting `T_IF`/`T_ENDIF` depth per line to find exactly where the nesting broke, rather than re-reading the source by eye. Fixed by rewording both comments to avoid the literal `@script`/`<x-...>` substrings — worth remembering for any future comment in this codebase that needs to *talk about* either mechanism by name.
- **A second, unrelated bug found the same way: the dialog's own open/close watcher never fired at all**, because the `@script` block that registers it runs exactly once, at initial mount — which happens on the local setup screen, before `$started` is true and before `#score-confirm-dialog` exists in the DOM at all. A `document.getElementById('score-confirm-dialog')` guard around the `$wire.$watch(...)` registration evaluated `null` at that moment and silently skipped registering the watcher forever, even after `startGame()` later added the dialog to the DOM. Confirmed live (a manually re-registered `$watch` still never fired) before concluding this, not assumed. Fixed by moving the `getElementById` lookup *inside* the watcher callback (so it re-resolves fresh each time `$pendingCategory` changes, by which point `$started` is guaranteed true) and switching the `close`-event listener to a capture-phase `document`-level delegate (dialog `close` events don't bubble, but capture-phase listeners still see them on the way down) instead of binding directly to an element that doesn't exist yet at script-registration time.
- Test coverage: `tests/Feature/Games/YahtzeeGameTest.php` (undo off by default and a no-op until enabled; undo working normally within a turn once enabled; undo unable to reach a scored category or cross a turn boundary even when enabled; the two-step `requestScore()`/`scoreCategory()` flow, including that the pending category isn't scored until confirmed, and `cancelScore()`), `tests/Feature/Games/YahtzeeRoomTest.php` (the toggle defaulting off and landing in `GameSession.settings`, undo working online for the current seat and broadcasting, 403 for a non-current seat even with undo enabled), and `tests/Browser/YahtzeeLocalPlayTest.php`/`YahtzeeRoomBrowserTest.php` updated so their existing "click a pickable cell to score" steps also click the new confirm dialog's button, plus direct assertions that the dialog actually opens on a real click and confirming actually commits the score in the rendered DOM.

**Sticky category column covering the current-player column on mobile (`docs/concerns.md`).** Reported: "you cannot see the current player on the left of the score grid because it's covered by the score labels, and it won't scroll that way." Root-caused live rather than guessed at: with enough players (6+) that `table.scorecard`'s computed width exceeds `.card-wrap`'s, `.card-wrap`'s `align-items: center` (missing the `safe` keyword its own `justify-content: safe center` already uses on the other axis) let the browser center the overflowing table such that its left edge rendered off-screen *at `scrollLeft: 0`*, with no further-left scroll position available to reach it — confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` at 8 players/390px wide: the table started at `x: -221` while `.card-wrap.scrollLeft` was still `0`. That pushed `table.scorecard th.category`/`td.category` (already `position: sticky; left: 0`, pinned against `.card-wrap`'s own `overflow: auto`, see "Equal-width columns" above) to visibly overlap the first player's column instead of sitting beside it — hiding whichever player that happened to be, most often the one the "current player" complaint was about, and unreachable by scrolling since sticky-by-design never scrolls away. Fixed with `align-items: safe center` — falls back to start-alignment once content actually overflows, so the table's own left edge (and the sticky column pinned to it) stays reachable at `scrollLeft: 0` instead of clipped off-screen unreachably. Verified live via the same `getBoundingClientRect()` measurements pre/post fix (category column and first player column no longer overlap) and covered by a new test in `tests/Browser/YahtzeeMobileTest.php`.

**Scorecard needed horizontal scrolling to see every player on a tablet/TV-width spectator viewport too, not just mobile (`docs/concerns.md`'s Clear the Deck spectator item, generalized).** The scorecard's *row height* already scales via `cqh` (see "Equal-width columns" above), so it never needed vertical scrolling regardless of player count — but its *column width* (`--scorecard-category-width`/`--scorecard-player-width`) was a flat `ch` floor per player, completely independent of how many players there actually were. Confirmed live at 8 players/1024px-wide: `.card-wrap`'s existing `overflow: auto` was forced into an actual horizontal scrollbar. This is real and *intentional* on mobile (`tests/Browser/YahtzeeRoomBrowserTest.php` already has a passing test asserting a narrow phone scrolls horizontally through too many players, deliberately unchanged by this fix) — but a TV cast or a tablet has no way to reach the cut-off columns the same way a phone's touch scroll does.
- **First attempt didn't work, worth recording so it isn't retried:** capping `table.scorecard`'s own `width` property (`min(calc(...), 100%)`) did nothing at all — confirmed live via `getComputedStyle`/`getBoundingClientRect` that the table still rendered at its full uncapped width regardless. Root cause: under `table-layout: fixed`, the table's *rendered* width is the greater of its own specified width and the sum of its `<col>` widths (CSS2.1 §17.5.2) — capping the table's own width property doesn't shrink it below what its columns already demand, so the columns themselves have to be what shrinks.
- **Second attempt also failed, different reason, also worth recording:** moved the shrinking logic into `--scorecard-category-width`/`--scorecard-player-width` themselves (`min(16ch, calc(66cqw / var(--player-count)))`), but declared at `:root` alongside their existing plain-`ch` desktop override — and `getComputedStyle` showed the custom property resolving to *empty*, not a length. `--player-count` is only ever set via an inline `style="--player-count: ..."` on `table.scorecard` itself (`yahtzee-scorecard-table.blade.php`) — `:root` is an *ancestor* of that element, and custom-property inheritance only flows downward, so `var(--player-count)` genuinely had nothing to resolve to when the substitution was attempted at `:root`'s own scope.
- **Fixed by declaring the override directly on `table.scorecard`** (a new `@media (min-width: 900px) { table.scorecard { --scorecard-category-width: ...; --scorecard-player-width: ...; } }`, alongside — not replacing — the existing `:root` block, which still supplies the mobile default and now also the desktop *fallback* shape) — the one place `--player-count` is guaranteed already in scope. `col.player-col`/`col.category-col` (descendants of `table.scorecard` via `<colgroup>`) inherit the override correctly from there. Constants (`19cqw` for category, `75cqw` total divided by player count) tuned empirically against real screenshots, same discipline as Clear the Deck's own spectator sizing above — an initial pass left the category column too narrow to fit "Bonus (63+ = 35)" without truncating (a regression from the *existing*, deliberately-tuned desktop behavior from "Desktop category labels truncating" above, caught by comparing before/after screenshots side by side, not just checking that scrolling was gone), fixed by giving category a larger, mostly player-count-independent cqw share since there's only ever one of it, unlike the N player columns actually under space pressure.
- `min(ch, cqw share)` means a small room is completely unaffected: at 2-3 players, the cqw share is far wider than the ch floor, so the plain `ch` value still wins, identical to before this change.
- **Known remaining gap, not fixed here:** a very short *height* viewport (tested down to 1920×750, simulating an aspect-ratio change like AirPlay mirroring to a wider-but-shorter display) can still need vertical scrolling even after this fix — `table.scorecard`'s row-height `cqh` scaling has a `10px` font-size floor (`clamp(10px, 3.9cqh, 28px)`), and 17 rows don't fit some very short viewports even at that floor, since the dice area above `.card-wrap` still claims a large, currently-fixed share of the vertical space regardless of how little is left over. Confirmed this isn't something the width fix above caused or could fix — it's a pre-existing height-budget limit, unrelated to player count/column width. Logged in `docs/TODO.md` rather than attempted in the same pass.
- Regression coverage: a new case in `tests/Browser/YahtzeeRoomBrowserTest.php` (a real 8-player game, zero horizontal `.card-wrap` overflow at a tightest-case tablet viewport) alongside the existing mobile-scroll test, which stays green.

**The short-viewport gap above (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #14) turned out not to be the row-height floor at all — root-caused instead of just tuning constants harder (2026-08-17).** The floor was the visible symptom, but measuring live at 1920×750 with 8 players showed `.dice-area` itself consuming 367px, far more than a 5-die row should need at that width. Traced to `.yahtzee-play .row`'s `flex: 1 1 auto` — its `width: min(56vw, 42vh, 720px)` is only a flex-basis, a *starting* size, and a positive `flex-grow` happily inflates it past that cap to fill whatever's left over in `.dice-area`'s full-width flex row (the identical class of bug already documented above for `table.scorecard`'s own `width` property under `table-layout: fixed` — a size property alone doesn't cap rendered size once another mechanism, there `<col>`, here `flex-grow`, is what actually wins).
- **Confirmed this wasn't short-viewport-specific at all** — checked a perfectly ordinary 1920×1080 desktop viewport next, and `.row` rendered **1860px** wide against its intended ~454px cap there too. Since dice are `aspect-ratio: 1/1`, inflating the grid's width inflates its height by the same factor, so this had likely been silently oversizing the dice grid (and eating vertical room the scorecard could have used) on every sufficiently wide desktop screen this feature has ever shipped to — just never surfaced as a *bug report* on a tall screen, since there was enough spare height there to absorb it without an actual scrollbar.
- **Fixed with `flex: 0 1 auto`** — `flex-shrink: 1`/`min-width: 0` both stay (still needed so a narrow mobile `.dice-area` can shrink `.row` below its own basis to share a row with Roll/Undo, per the comment directly above this rule), only `flex-grow` changes from `1` to `0`. This is a real correctness fix, not a narrow patch scoped to the reported viewport — verified `.row` now lands exactly on its intended `min(56vw, 42vh, 720px)` value at 1920×1080 (453.6px, matching `42vh` exactly) *and* at the original 1920×750 report (`.card-wrap`'s `scrollHeight`/`clientHeight` now equal, zero overflow) *and* at 2560×1080 ultrawide, without touching `table.scorecard`'s own cqh/floor logic at all — the actual vertical budget available to the scorecard just grew enough that the existing floor was never the binding constraint in the first place.
