- **`.farkle-play .row` is a separate selector with the identical `flex: 1 1 auto`/`aspect-ratio: 1/1` shape, and was deliberately left alone here rather than fixed alongside it** — Farkle's landscape-layout work (see that section below) already documented needing `flex: 1 1 auto` specifically for its own narrow `.farkle-dice-column` layout, so blindly copying this exact fix over risks breaking that without the same live verification. Farkle almost certainly has the analogous over-grow effect on its own desktop/spectator dice row (same class, same construct, same `width:100%` `.dice-area` ancestor) — flagged for whoever picks up `docs/TODO.md`'s Farkle TV-scoreboard-legibility item to check while already looking at that area, rather than assumed fixed by this change.
- Regression coverage: a new case in `tests/Browser/YahtzeeRoomBrowserTest.php` (a real 8-player game at 1920×750, zero vertical `.card-wrap` overflow) alongside the existing tablet-width horizontal-scroll test; full existing Yahtzee Feature/Browser suite re-run green to confirm the flex-shrink/mobile-sharing behavior the change deliberately preserved is in fact unaffected.

## Family Feud

- Question seeder (32 questions), `JudgeService` (OpenRouter/Claude Haiku), single shared-screen Livewire game (no separate host/display split — see below).

Decisions, deliberate deviations from the original plan's literal wording (ported from `legacy/family-feud.html` / `legacy/netlify/functions/game.js` / `legacy/family-feud-spec.md`):
- **No `FamilyFeudHost`/`FamilyFeudDisplay` split, no Reverb.** The original plan called for two components connected over Reverb, but the legacy spec's core design decision is explicit: *"The game works on one screen only because an LLM is the judge, not a person... there is no hidden information on the screen, so a single shared view is correct. Do not build a separate host and board view."* Built as one Livewire page (`pages::family-feud.play`), same pattern as Yahtzee's pass-and-play. (Family Feud's own online-room follow-up, reusing the seat/state infrastructure Yahtzee proved out above, is the one piece of the original combined scope still deferred — see `docs/TODO.md`.)
- **No queued job.** The plan called for a queued `JudgeFamilyFeudAnswer` job "so the UI doesn't block," but there's no UI to keep unblocked in the first place if it's one synchronous screen — the legacy version's own `fetch()` call is itself a blocking round-trip from the browser's perspective. `wire:loading` on the submit button/input gives the "Checking..." state for free. `JudgeService::judge()` uses a 15s HTTP timeout.
- **Security-critical port hazard, not a deviation:** the legacy security model works by never sending the answer bank to the browser at all (it lives only in the Netlify function). A naive Livewire port would break this much more severely — every Livewire public property gets serialized into the page's `wire:snapshot` HTML on every request, so holding `$this->questions` as full rows would leak every hidden answer to anyone opening dev tools, on every request. Fixed by **never storing `answers` as a public property at all** — `$this->questions` only ever holds the client-safe shape (`FamilyFeudQuestion::toSafeArray()` → `{id, prompt, answerCount}`), and every action that needs real answers re-fetches the question fresh from the database inside that action. Confirmed by a `not->toHaveKey('answers')` test assertion.
- Team names use `guest_name` on `GameParticipant` (never `user_id`) — the same attribution gap Yahtzee had before its own "which player is you" picker (see Yahtzee's section above); Family Feud's own follow-up will need the equivalent for teams/captains.
- Initially kept `wire:confirm` (native browser `confirm()`) for Skip Question/End Game/Reveal confirmations as the simplest option for v1, even though the legacy spec calls out native browser dialogs as an accessibility violation ("bright OS-default modal"). Later replaced with the reusable `<x-confirm-dialog>` component — see "Low-stim confirm dialogs" under "Site-wide design system & chrome" below.
- Test-writing gotcha: `Http::fake()` calls don't replace earlier registrations for the same URL pattern — the *first*-registered stub keeps matching first, so simulating "judge fails, then a retry succeeds" needs `Http::fake(['url' => Http::sequence()->push(...)->push(...)])` in one call, not two separate `Http::fake()` calls.
- Verified `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` with one real (tiny-cost) call through the deployed docker stack via `php artisan tinker` — "sun cream" correctly matched to a "Sunscreen" answer.

**Online rooms for Family Feud** (the last piece of the original combined-with-Yahtzee scope, deferred in `docs/TODO.md` until now). Reused `Room`/`RoomStatus`/`routes/channels.php`/`⚡game-lobby.blade.php`/`RoomGameStarting`/`GameSession.state`/`RoomGameStateUpdated` entirely as-is — the last two events' own docblocks were already written expecting this — and added the parts that are genuinely Feud-specific: teams with multiple players each, a captain-gated steal, and a within-team rotating turn pointer.
- **Team/captain assignment defaults to alternating by join order, but the host gets an explicit assignment screen to override it before the round starts.** Initially shipped fully automatic with no UI at all (even seat index → team 0, odd → team 1, first seat on each team captain, mirroring Yahtzee's "seat index = join order" simplicity) — revised almost immediately after a follow-up request for host control over who's on which team. `pages::family-feud.room` gained a whole intermediate step: `game-lobby`'s "Start Game" click still dispatches the same local `room-game-starting` event, but `onRoomStarting()` no longer creates the `GameSession`/`RoomSeat` rows itself — it only snapshots the ready players into `$readyPlayers` (with the alternating split as the *starting* `$teamAssignments`/`$captainAssignments`) and flips `$assigningTeams = true`, rendering a new screen with a "Move to {other team}" and "Make Captain" button per player. `startRound()` (host-only, gated on `canStartRound` — both teams non-empty) is what now actually builds `teamSeats`/creates the `GameSession`/creates `RoomSeat` rows/broadcasts `RoomGameStarting`/redirects, using whatever the host's own `$teamAssignments`/`$captainAssignments` ended up as rather than the untouched default. `moveToTeam()` always strips the moved player's own captaincy (an explicit "Make Captain" tap is required on their new team, never automatic) and calls `ensureEachTeamHasACaptain()`, which promotes a team's first remaining member if a move just left it captain-less — a non-empty team can never end up with zero captains through the UI. Only the host's own connection ever sees `$assigningTeams = true` — `'room-game-starting'` is a local (non-broadcast) Livewire dispatch that only reaches the request that triggered it, and `game-lobby::startGame()` already aborts for a non-host caller — so every other connected player just keeps looking at the ordinary lobby, unaware anything happened, until `RoomGameStarting` actually broadcasts once the host confirms. `room_seats` gained `team_index` (nullable unsignedInteger) and `is_captain` (boolean, default false) — nullable/defaulted specifically so Yahtzee's own existing `RoomSeat::create()` call, which sets neither, keeps working completely unmodified. **Accepted limitation:** a host who reloads mid-assignment loses their in-progress picks and lands back on the plain lobby (`$assigningTeams`/`$readyPlayers`/etc. are plain Livewire component properties, not persisted anywhere, so a real page reload — as opposed to a normal same-tab Livewire round trip, which persists them fine — remounts fresh) — not worth persisting a draft-state screen to the database for.
- **Turn authority, all newly added — `submitGuess()`/`retryGuess()`/`skipQuestion()`/`swapControl()`/`revealManually()`/`awardManual()`/`nextRound()`/`nextRoundSummary()`/`endGame()` had zero authorization at all before this**, exactly as `docs/TODO.md` flagged for `submitGuess()` specifically. During the `Play` phase, only the controlling team's current turn-pointer seat may guess/skip/swap; during `Steal`, only the *stealing* team's captain may act, regardless of the turn pointer (a captain-only action never advances it). `RoundEnd`/`RoundSummary`/`Manual`/`GameEnd`-phase actions are gated to "any seated player" instead of a specific turn-holder, since those are shared "we're ready to continue" decisions, not guessing turns.
- **The within-team turn pointer** (`teamTurnPointer`, one index per team into that team's own `teamSeats` list) rotates by one, wrapping, every time the controlling team submits a *resolved* guess during `Play` — captured via `judgeGuess()` (the single private method both `submitGuess()` and `retryGuess()` already routed through), which is also where the pointer-advance and `syncOnlineStateIfNeeded()` call both live now. A judge-unreachable failure (`pendingGuess`) does **not** rotate the pointer or sync online state — that failure and its Retry option are deliberately local-device-only state, same reasoning as keeping `guessInput`/`turnOptionsOpen` unsynced.
- **The judge call stays exactly where it was: synchronous, in-request, inside whichever seat's own guess triggered it.** No queue was added — matches both local pass-and-play's existing behavior and the TODO's own explicit instruction. `RoomGameStateUpdated` broadcasts the result once it resolves, same as every other mutating action.
- **`GameParticipant` attribution is one row per seated user, not one row per team** — a real improvement over local mode, not just a port of it. Local pass-and-play still needs `$meTeamIndex`'s guessing trick (there's no way to know which local team is the account holder in general), but online, every seat's `user_id` is known for certain, so every real account that played gets its own correctly attributed row (same reasoning as Yahtzee dropping `meIndex` for its own online mode). Each row also gets a new `stats` payload (`team_index`, `is_captain`, `round_history` — that participant's own team's points per round only) on **both** the local and online `endGame()` branches, added as part of the stats-page work below since it was free to compute right there either way.
- `pages::family-feud.room`'s `onRoomStarting()` silently declines to enter the team-assignment screen at all if the question bank is empty (mirrors `startGame()`'s own local guard against the exact same fresh/unseeded-deployment crash) rather than trying to message an error back through the lobby's own start-game event, which has no return path. A team ending up with zero seated members — the scenario `endGame()`'s per-seat `GameParticipant` loop would otherwise have to handle specially — can no longer actually happen: `canStartRound` (both teams non-empty) blocks `startRound()` entirely, so this is enforced at the assignment screen rather than tolerated at game-end.
- **Host-only room settings** (rounds + two team names) live on the new `pages::family-feud.room` page, mirroring Boggle's room page (host-only board-size/time picker) rather than Yahtzee's (which has nothing to configure pre-start at all).
- Found and fixed one real test-writing bug of our own while writing the browser test suite: an early version of "the acting seat sees the guess form, the other seat sees a waiting message" held two `visit()` pages open in one test, with a content assertion running on the first before the second was ever created — exactly the specific pattern `docs/DEVLOG.md`'s own "Automated browser testing" section already documents as a reliable 30-60s hang in this Playwright setup. Split into two separate one-page-per-test cases (mirroring `YahtzeeRoomBrowserTest`'s existing convention) rather than fighting it.
- Added `tests/Feature/Games/FamilyFeudRoomTest.php` (17 tests: host/join flows, the team-assignment screen's default split/moves/captain-reassignment/`canStartRound` gating, that confirming persists the host's *customized* teams rather than the untouched default, turn-pointer-only and captain-only-during-Steal authorization — including that a non-captain teammate on the stealing team is still forbidden, not just the controlling team — pointer rotation, reload rehydration, per-seat `GameParticipant` attribution) and `tests/Browser/FamilyFeudRoomBrowserTest.php` (7 tests, one real browser page per test with the rest of the room DB-seeded, same convention as `YahtzeeRoomBrowserTest`) — covering exactly the `@teleport`/conditional-visibility bug class this app has repeatedly shipped broken before (see "Site-wide design system & chrome" below), since this pass adds several new conditionally-shown elements (the guess form vs. a waiting message, the Reveal/End Game button-bar buttons hidden from spectators, and now the team-assignment screen's own real-DOM Start Round enabled/disabled state). The two new team-assignment browser tests drive `onRoomStarting()` directly via `window.Livewire` (the same JS-injection pattern `YahtzeeRoomBrowserTest` uses for `onStateUpdated()`) to stand in for `game-lobby`'s own presence-broadcast-dependent "Start Game" dispatch, which this test environment's `BROADCAST_CONNECTION=null` can't otherwise trigger for real.

**Family Feud stats pages**, added alongside the online-rooms work and mirroring `profile.yahtzee`/`YahtzeeStatsService` (Boggle's own deep-dive metrics are word-game-specific and don't apply here). New `App\Services\Stats\FamilyFeudStatsService` reads `team_index`/`is_captain`/`round_history` back out of each `GameParticipant.stats` (see above); `bestMetrics()` surfaces "Highest score" and "Biggest single round" (the latter derived from each game's own `round_history` max, omitted entirely if no game ever scored above zero in a round — same "omit rather than show a misleading zero" rule `YahtzeeStatsService::bestMetrics()` already follows). New `profile.family-feud`/`profile.family-feud.show` routes (registered before the `/profile/{username}` wildcard, same reasoning already given for `/profile/boggle` and `/profile/yahtzee`) and two new Blade pages adapted directly from `pages::profile.yahtzee`'s own two files — the show page adds a "Captain" badge when `stats['is_captain']` is true and a round-by-round breakdown table instead of Yahtzee's per-category scorecard, since Family Feud has no per-category concept. `<x-player-stats>`'s `match ($stat['type'])` gained a `GameType::FamilyFeud => 'profile.family-feud'` arm (and, with all three `GameType` cases now covered, the `default => null` fallback was dropped in favor of an exhaustive match). Added `tests/Unit/Stats/FamilyFeudStatsServiceTest.php` and `tests/Feature/ProfileFamilyFeudTest.php`, mirroring the equivalent Yahtzee test files.

**Fixed a real stale-computed-property bug (2026-08-05), found while auditing for the same class of bug uncovered fixing Farkle** (see "Farkle" below for the mechanism in full). `submitGuess()`, `retryGuess()`, `skipQuestion()`, and `swapControl()` all read `$this->isMyTurn` as their own guard *before* mutating `$phase`/`$controllingTeam`/`$teamTurnPointer` — exactly the state `getIsMyTurnProperty()` depends on — caching the pre-mutation answer thanks to Livewire's legacy `getXxxProperty()` memoization. Concretely: right after tapping "Swap Team," the tapper's own screen kept showing the guess form instead of switching to "Waiting for {other team}...", since `isMyTurn` was cached `true` from before `$controllingTeam` flipped. Fixed the same way as Farkle: `getIsMyTurnProperty()` now delegates to a private, uncached `isMyTurnValue()`, and all four actions call that private version instead of the memoized public one. Added a regression test (`tests/Feature/Games/FamilyFeudRoomTest.php`) asserting the raw rendered HTML after `swapControl()` — confirmed it actually fails against the pre-fix code (`git stash`d the fix, reran, watched it fail with the exact stale-`true` symptom, then restored it) before treating it as real coverage.

**Added a host-only manual "End Game" action for online Yahtzee rooms** (docs/concerns.md) — Yahtzee had no way to stop a room game early before this; `endGame()` only ever ran once every player filled every category. `endGameManually()`/`getCanEndGameManuallyProperty()` mirror Farkle's own shape exactly (see "Farkle" below), gated to `$this->isHost` and to `$this->room !== null` — unlike Farkle, this doesn't extend to local pass-and-play, since that mode has no manual-quit concept today and wasn't part of the request. Reuses `endGame()` unchanged: every still-open category is simply absent from a player's own `totalScore()` sum, identical to a natural finish with fewer categories filled.

## Farkle

`docs/TODO.md`'s former #16 ("Add Farkle") started as a spec skeleton (`docs/specs/farkle.md`) with five open design questions flagged inline — scoring variant, target score/win condition, player count/mode, hot dice, turn UI shape. Answered by the user directly (2026-08-05) rather than via a proposal/approval round like the game-renaming task: standard scoring with a 500-point minimum entry and hot dice, a 10,000-point default target with a per-game-configurable "with final round" toggle (defaulting on), a host/local-player-gated manual End Game action, and both pass-and-play *and* online rooms at launch (unlike Yahtzee, which shipped pass-and-play-only originally and needed a whole later phase to add rooms — Farkle could build directly against the now-decoupled, generic room system from day one, see "Room system" above). The answered spec was committed on its own before implementation started, same "record the decision, then build" pattern used for the Boggle win-rate scoping decision earlier in this project.

- **Architecture mirrors Yahtzee's file layout deliberately** (`docs/specs/farkle.md`'s own section 1 calls this out): `App\Services\Farkle\{DiceRoller,Scorer,GameEngine}` alongside `App\Services\Yahtzee`'s equivalents, a single `pages::farkle.play` page handling both local setup and the actual game (no separate lobby page, matching Yahtzee not Boggle), `pages::farkle.room` for the online-room seat/start screen, `App\Services\Stats\FarkleStatsService`, and `pages::profile.farkle.{index,show}`. `GameType::Farkle` (`'farkle'`) and `roomRoute()` → `'farkle.room'` slot in as a fourth case everywhere those enums are already exhaustively matched (`<x-player-stats>`, `pages::profile.games.index`'s `showRoute()`) — both needed a new match arm, found by grepping for every existing `GameType::Boggle =>` site rather than guessing.
- **Scoring is deliberately two small, pure classes, not one.** `Scorer::scoreSelection(list<int> $values): array{score, valid}` scores *only* the dice a player actually selected out of one roll — every face with count ≥3 scores as a group, and any leftover single 1s/5s score individually (100/50 each) on top. Critically, a selection containing so much as one non-scoring die (e.g. three 4s plus one lone 2) comes back `valid: false` entirely, not "worth less" — Farkle's real rule is that you can only set aside dice that actually score, so an invalid pick has to be rejected outright rather than partially honored. `Scorer::hasAnyScoringDice(list<int> $values): bool` is the separate, simpler "does this *whole* fresh roll have anything scoring at all" check that decides an immediate bust. `GameEngine` is a thin wrapper around `Scorer` (delegating its methods, plus `meetsMinimumEntry(bool $hasQualified, int $projectedTurnScore): bool`) purely to match the existing `App\Services\{Game}\GameEngine` DI pattern (`boot(GameEngine $engine)` on the Livewire component, injected into the stats service) — there was no Yahtzee-style extra bonus-rule bookkeeping that actually needed a second layer here, unlike Yahtzee's Joker rule. **Scoring table corrected 2026-08-05** (see the dedicated entry below) after user testing found the original "doubling" rule (four 4s = 800) was wrong.
- **Turn state machine, three actions, no separate "confirm selection" step.** `roll()` is only ever valid at the true start of a turn (`$dice === []`, `$heldDiceCount === 0`); from then on, `toggleSelect(int $index)` flips a die in the currently-shown roll, and the turn is resolved by exactly one of two actions, both of which first validate *and apply* whatever's currently selected: `bank()` (adds the turn's running total to the player's banked score and ends the turn) or `rollAgain()` (folds the selection into `$turnScore`, then immediately rolls the remaining dice for real — matching the spec's literal wording, "Roll Again re-rolls the remaining dice," rather than requiring a separate follow-up Roll click). A bust is detected inside the same roll-producing code path `roll()` and `rollAgain()` both call through (`performRoll()`): if the fresh roll has no scoring dice at all, the turn ends immediately, `$turnScore` (everything accumulated *this turn*, not previously banked turns) is lost, and play advances to the next player — same code path handles this whether it's the very first roll of a turn or a follow-up from `rollAgain()`.
- **Hot dice is a `$heldDiceCount` reset, not a separate flag.** `$heldDiceCount` (how many of the 6 dice are already set aside this turn) increments every time a selection is applied; the moment it reaches 6, it resets to 0 (not decremented — a fresh full set) and the player's `hotDiceCount` stat ticks up, but `$turnScore` itself is untouched, so the next roll is a genuine fresh 6-dice roll with the accumulated total still on the line.
- **A real bug caught by testing, not by inspection: the triggering player's own turn-advance double-decremented `finalRoundRemaining`.** `bankTurnScore()` sets `finalRoundActive = true` and `finalRoundRemaining = count($players) - 1` the instant a bank crosses the target, then falls through to the same `advanceTurn()` every other bank/bust goes through — which *also* unconditionally decremented `finalRoundRemaining` whenever `finalRoundActive` was true, immediately undoing half of what was just set, one call later, in the same request. Caught by `tests/Feature/Games/FarkleWinConditionTest.php` asserting `finalRoundRemaining === 2` for a 3-player game right after the trigger and getting `1` instead. Fixed with an explicit `$justTriggeredFinalRound` flag threaded through to `advanceTurn(bool $skipFinalRoundDecrement = false)`, so the triggering bank's own fall-through advance is exempted from the countdown — it's the *other* players' turns being counted down, not the trigger's own.
- **Two more real bugs, same root cause (an incomplete turn-end reset), caught the same way.** `heldDiceCount` was reset to 0 on a bust (inside `performRoll()`) but never on a successful `bank()` — so banking after setting aside, say, 2 of 6 dice silently started the *next* player's turn already 2 dice short, since `performRoll()` computes how many dice to roll as `DICE_COUNT - $heldDiceCount`. Same gap existed for `finishGame()`'s own manual-end-game reset. Both fixed by making `advanceTurn()` (not each caller individually) the single authoritative place a fresh turn's state gets reset, via a call to the existing `startTurn()` right before picking the next player — and adding the missing `heldDiceCount = 0` to `finishGame()`'s own reset alongside `dice`/`selected`/`turnScore`, which it already had.
- **Real dice can't be forced through `roll()`/`rollAgain()`'s own `DiceRoller` call without mocking it, and mocking turned out to actively break other, unrelated tests.** First attempt used `$this->mock(DiceRoller::class)->shouldReceive('roll')->andReturn(...)` to force a specific bust for one test — worked in isolation, then intermittently made *later, unrelated* tests in the same file fail with real rolls coming back busted too. Root cause: this suite's app container is reused across tests within a process (a real, confirmed behavior in this environment, not just a theoretical Laravel testing optimization), so a `$this->mock()`-bound singleton instance leaks past the test that created it. This codebase has zero prior precedent for mocking a game's randomness (Yahtzee/Boggle/Family Feud tests all just call the real action and pin down specific values afterward via `->set('dice', ...)`), so the fix was to stop trying to force *specific* rolls and instead either (a) skip the real `roll()` call entirely and `->set('dice', ...)` directly when the test only cares about turn logic given some dice, since every action only ever reads current `$dice`/`$selected` state, never how it got there, or (b) for tests that specifically need to observe a real bust, shrink the odds down to a single die (`->set('heldDiceCount', 5)` first) and retry a capped number of fresh component instances until one is observed (a lone die busts ~67% of the time, so this resolves in 1-2 tries almost always). A related, subtler version of the same mistake got caught along the way: several tests called the real `->call('roll')` immediately before overriding `$dice` via `->set(...)` "just to get a realistic array shape first" — harmless for Yahtzee (whose `roll()` never branches on the values rolled) but not for Farkle, where that real roll has a small-but-nonzero chance of legitimately busting *before* the override ever takes effect, silently advancing the turn, incrementing `farkleCount`, and (in the online-room tests) even flipping `$mySeatIndex === $currentPlayer` false out from under a subsequent action, throwing a genuine 403 the test never expected. Removed every such pointless real `roll()` call once the pattern was recognized.
- **Manual "End Game," gated by the same room/local split as Family Feud's own `endGame()`** — online, restricted to `Auth::id() === $room->host_user_id`; in local pass-and-play (`$room === null`, no host concept), any player. Reuses the same `<x-confirm-dialog>` component Family Feud's own manual end-game action introduced (see "Low-stim confirm dialogs" — not repeated here since it's the identical component, just a new call site).
- **CSS is a near-exact copy of the `.yahtzee-play` block** (`resources/css/app.css`), sized for 6 dice instead of 5 (`grid-template-columns: repeat(6, 1fr)`) and using `.die.selected` (this turn's chosen-but-not-yet-banked dice) instead of Yahtzee's `.die.kept`, reusing the same `--die-kept` color variable for the highlight — no new design-system decisions needed, the existing dice-game visual language already covered this.
- Homepage card, sidebar link (`app-sidebar.blade.php`), and `<x-player-stats>` wiring added the same way every prior game's did. `Farkle`'s own display name needed no rename treatment at launch the way Boggle/Yahtzee/Family Feud did (see "Game renaming" above) — this was revisited later once the trademark concern was actually raised, see below.
- Added `tests/Unit/Farkle/{Scorer,DiceRoller,GameEngine}Test.php` (pure scoring logic), `tests/Feature/Games/{FarkleGame,FarkleRoom}Test.php` (turn actions, minimum-entry gating, hot dice, busts, seat/host authorization, a full online game writing distinct `GameParticipant` rows — mirroring `YahtzeeGameTest`/`YahtzeeRoomTest`'s own coverage), `tests/Feature/Games/FarkleWinConditionTest.php` (target-crossing, the final-round countdown, ties, manual early end — kept separate from the general game test since the win-condition bug above made this worth isolating), `tests/Feature/ProfileFarkleTest.php`, `tests/Unit/Stats/FarkleStatsServiceTest.php`, and `tests/Browser/FarkleLocalPlayTest.php` (a real multi-turn browser run reading actual rendered die values back out of the DOM via `$page->script(...)` to decide which to click, plus the End Game confirm-dialog flow).

**Post-launch fixes from real play (`docs/concerns.md`, 2026-08-05).** Farkle shipped, then the user actually played it and found a real scoring-rule error plus several UX rough edges — all fixed the same day, `docs/concerns.md` cleared out afterward per its own purpose (a scratch inbox, not a durable doc).

- **Scoring table was wrong and got corrected.** The shipped "four/five/six of a kind doubles the three-of-a-kind value" rule (four 4s = 800, six 4s = 3200) was never what the user actually wanted — the real rule is flat values regardless of face (four of a kind = 1000, five = 2000, six = 3000), *plus* three new full-roll-only patterns that didn't exist before at all: a straight (1-2-3-4-5-6) = 1500, three pairs = 1500, two separate triplets = 2500. `Scorer::scoreSelection()` now checks for the three special 6-dice patterns first (`scoreSixDiceSpecial()`, matching on the sorted group-size shape of the roll: `[1,1,1,1,1,1]` = straight, `[2,2,2]` = three pairs, `[3,3]` = two triplets), then falls back to a new `bestScoreForFace(int $face, int $count): int` helper for the normal per-face case — which doesn't just apply one fixed tier, it computes every tier the count qualifies for (three/four/five/six-of-a-kind, each optionally topped up with leftover 1/5 singles) and returns whichever scores highest. This matters concretely: four 1s scores 1100 (three 1s = 1000, plus one leftover single 1 = 100) rather than the flat four-of-a-kind value of 1000, since 1100 is strictly better and there's no reason to ever pick the worse interpretation. `hasAnyScoringDice()` also had to learn about the three special patterns — three pairs of non-1/5 faces (e.g. 2-2-3-3-4-4) has no lone 1/5 and no 3+-of-a-kind group, so without this it read as a false bust.
- **Bank now auto-includes every scoring die in the roll, not just manually clicked ones.** The user's report ("if there are scoring dice they did not click, those should be added up and banked too") and a separate-sounding report ("the Bank amount... is wrong") turned out to be the same root cause: `bank()` and its button label both only ever summed `$selected` dice. New `Scorer::scoreMaximal(list<int> $values): int` (and `GameEngine::scoreMaximal()`) computes the best achievable score from an *entire* roll regardless of what's clicked, silently treating non-scoring dice as "worth 0, left out" rather than invalidating anything (unlike `scoreSelection()`, which still enforces "no dead dice in a manual selection" for `rollAgain()`, which keeps requiring an explicit, valid selection — only `bank()`'s behavior changed). The Blade button label (`Bank {{ $this->bankableAmount }}`) and the actual `bank()` action both call this exact same method, so the number shown can never disagree with what actually gets credited.
- **A second, more subtle bug found *while fixing the first one*: Livewire's legacy `getXxxProperty()` convention memoizes each computed property's value the first time it's read within a request** (`vendor/livewire/livewire/src/Features/SupportComputed/SupportLegacyComputedPropertySyntax.php`'s `getComputedProperty()`, a `store($target)->find('computedProperties', ...)` cache keyed by property name). `rollAgain()`/`bank()` used to read `$this->canRollAgain`/`$this->canBank` as their own guard, *before* mutating `$dice`/`$selected`/`$currentPlayer` — caching the pre-mutation answer — and Blade's later render of the same property in the same request silently reused that stale cached value instead of recomputing. Concretely: after a real `rollAgain()` roll (nothing newly selected on the fresh dice), the server-rendered HTML for `#rollAgainBtn` had *no* `disabled` attribute at all, confirmed by inspecting the raw Livewire response HTML directly (not just the browser's DOM, to rule out a client-side morph issue) — the button was genuinely clickable with an empty selection. Fixed by splitting every affected property into a private, uncached `xValue()` method that actions call internally, and a public `getXProperty()` that just delegates to it for Blade — since Blade's render is then the *only* place the cache gets populated per request, it's always fresh. Applied to `selectionScore`, `canRollAgain`, `bankableAmount`, `canBank`, `isSpectator`, and `isMyTurn` (the last two because they're read *inside* the other private helpers, so they needed the same treatment to avoid the exact same staleness one level down). Added `tests/Feature/Games/FarkleGameTest.php`'s `'canRollAgain and selectionScore correctly reflect the fresh roll after rollAgain...'` test, which asserts against the raw rendered HTML (`assertSeeHtml(...disabled)`) specifically because that's the only way to catch this class of bug — asserting the Livewire property alone doesn't distinguish "computed correctly" from "cached correctly."
  - A quick audit found the *same* memoization pattern already live in Family Feud (`submitGuess()`/`retryGuess()`/`skipQuestion()`/`swapControl()` all read `$this->isMyTurn` before mutating `$phase`/`$controllingTeam`/`$teamTurnPointer`, the exact state it depends on) — fixed the same day, same technique, see the "Family Feud" section above. Yahtzee and Boggle were both audited clean: neither reads a computed getter as an action guard before mutating what it depends on.
- **UI legibility fixes**, all from direct user feedback on the shipped version: dice locked in earlier this same turn now render in a separate, bordered `.farkle-banked-row` instead of just disappearing the moment they're set aside (tracked via a new `$bankedDiceThisTurn` array, appended to whenever a selection is applied, reset at the start of each turn); the target score ("Playing to 10,000") and a "Final Round!" banner (once triggered) are now always visible during play, not just implied by context; a Farkle bust message gets its own distinct, bolder, warm-colored styling (`.farkle-message-bust`) instead of blending in with a normal "X banked Y" message; the message line itself moved into an always-rendered, fixed-min-height wrapper (`.farkle-message-slot`) instead of being conditionally added/removed from the DOM, which is what was actually causing the reported page jump every time a message appeared or disappeared; and once the game ends, the dice/Roll-Again/Bank controls disappear entirely (`@unless ($gameOver)`) and the scoreboard renders larger (`.farkle-final-scores`) instead of staying at its small in-game size.
- **Debugging false alarm, worth remembering:** while verifying the End Game confirm dialog in a real browser, ad-hoc `vendor/bin/pest --agent='...'` snippets repeatedly showed a genuine 500 error / silently-no-op'd click on that flow. Root cause was entirely in the *debugging method*, not the app: a raw `--agent` snippet's ephemeral SQLite `:memory:` database never runs migrations (`RefreshDatabase` only applies inside a real test file's `uses(RefreshDatabase::class)`), so `finishGame()`'s own `GameSession::create(...)` genuinely failed with "no such table: game_sessions" every time — silently swallowed client-side as "the click did nothing," since Livewire's AJAX response failed too. The already-existing `tests/Browser/FarkleLocalPlayTest.php` (a real test file, real migrations) had been passing the entire time. Confirmed by writing one more real (if throwaway) test file instead of another `--agent` one-liner — worth reaching for first next time a `--agent` browser probe shows behavior a real test file disagrees with.

**"Back to room" button did nothing on the game-over screen, in all four games (`docs/concerns.md`, 2026-08-06).** Genuinely puzzling to diagnose from reading the code alone — the button is a plain `<a href="{{ route(...) }}"><button>...</button></a>` teleported into `#game-actions`, the exact same pattern the always-working Home button uses, with no visible overlay, no disabled state, correct `href`. Root-caused by scripting a real Playwright session (per `CLAUDE.md`'s browser-testing rules): even a raw, non-Playwright `element.click()` on the actual DOM node didn't navigate, and visiting the target URL (`/farkle/room/{code}`) *directly* landed right back on the play page. The button was never broken at all — clicking it worked exactly as coded, and `pages::farkle.room`'s own `redirectIfAlreadyStarted()` (`Room.status === Active && game_session_id !== null`) then immediately redirected straight back, because **nothing had ever set those two fields to anything else once a game ends** — `Room.status`/`game_session_id` are only ever touched by `startGame()`/`switchGame()` (both set `status: Active` right before creating a fresh session), never by any game's own `finishGame()`/`endGame()`. `RoomStatus::Finished` had existed in the enum since the room system was first built but was never actually assigned anywhere — `redirectIfAlreadyStarted()` (written for a genuinely different case, "a reconnecting mid-game player whose device dropped") had no way to distinguish "still being played" from "already over." Fixed by having each game's own online-mode end-game action (`Farkle::finishGame()`, `Yahtzee::endGame()`, `FamilyFeud::endGame()`, `Boggle::finishGame()` — guarded there on `$this->room !== null`, not `$this->isSharedSession`, since that flag also covers Boggle's unrelated async-challenge-link mode) mark `Room.status = RoomStatus::Finished` right alongside the existing `GameSession->update(['ended_at' => now()])`. `game-lobby`'s replay/switch logic needed no changes — `startGame()`/`switchGame()` already unconditionally set `status: Active` regardless of what it was before, so replaying out of `Finished` works the same as out of `Active`. Regression-covered by `tests/Browser/FarkleBackToRoomTest.php` (the real end-to-end click, proven live) plus one added assertion each in `BoggleRoomTest.php`/`YahtzeeRoomTest.php`/`FamilyFeudRoomTest.php`'s existing full-game tests (room ends up `Finished`, and the room page renders instead of redirecting away).

**Four more mobile/desktop layout bugs from real play (`docs/concerns.md`, 2026-08-06).** All confirmed live via scripted Playwright sessions (real `getComputedStyle`/`getBoundingClientRect`/`scrollHeight` measurements) before being called fixed, not just eyeballed — the exact discipline `CLAUDE.md`'s browser-testing section asks for. Regression-covered by `tests/Browser/FarkleMobileLayoutTest.php`.
- **Scores unreachable on a short mobile viewport.** `.farkle-scoreboard`'s own `overflow-hidden` (there for the `rounded-lg` corner-clipping trick) combined with `#farkle-game`'s `flex:1; min-height:0` chain: a flex item with non-`visible` overflow resolves its automatic minimum size to 0 instead of its content size, so on a short viewport the flex container was free to squeeze the scoreboard down as far as it liked and the excess rows were silently clipped, with the outer document itself reporting zero overflow (`scrollHeight === clientHeight`) — nothing to scroll anywhere. Fixed by giving the scoreboard its own `overflow-y: auto` (keeping `overflow-x: hidden` so the rounded corners still clip sideways) plus a `min-height: min(160px, 25vh)` floor so it isn't squeezed to near-nothing before it even gets a chance to scroll.
- **Banked dice wrapping to a second row, pushing the roll row down.** `.farkle-banked-row` was `flex-wrap: wrap`; a long turn (hot dice can push well past 6 banked dice) wrapped onto a second line, growing the row's own height and shoving the active dice/Roll button further down the page. Changed to `flex-wrap: nowrap` + `overflow-x: auto` — always exactly one row, scrolling sideways instead of growing vertically.
- **Dice squeezed illegibly small in narrow portrait, read as "overlapping the roll button."** The shared `.dice-area { flex-wrap: nowrap }` rule (deliberately nowrap for Yahtzee's benefit, see its own comment) left the six-die grid and Farkle's `min-width: 260px` `.controls` (room for both Roll Again *and* Bank, wider than Yahtzee's own Roll/Undo pair) fighting for the same too-narrow row on a phone in portrait — confirmed live via a real 390×844 screenshot showing the dice shrunk to illegible dots rather than literally stacked on the button. Fixed with a Farkle-scoped `@media (max-width: 899px) and (orientation: portrait)` override wrapping `.dice-area` onto two rows (dice, then controls) — the same "protect Yahtzee's layout, carve out Farkle's own exception" approach `.farkle-play #rollBtn`'s reserved min-width already used elsewhere in this file.
- **Live in-game scoreboard font too small on desktop.** Unlike `.farkle-final-scores` (game-over only, already had a real `clamp()`), the live scoreboard used plain Tailwind `text-sm` unconditionally. Added a `@media (min-width: 900px)` override bumping it to 18px while in progress.

**"Farkle" renamed to "10,000" (`docs/concerns.md`, 2026-08-06)** — belated version of the same trademark-avoidance pass "Game renaming" above did for Boggle/Yahtzee/Family Feud at launch; skipped for Farkle at the time since it wasn't flagged as a live concern yet. Same rules as before: user-facing copy only (`GameType::Farkle->label()`, page titles/headings, homepage card, rules text, in-game messages, profile stats headings/empty-state/column labels), routes/enum case name+value/PHP namespace (`App\Services\Farkle\*`)/CSS classes (`.farkle-play`, `.farkle-banked-row`, ...)/element ids (`#farkle-game`)/array keys (`farkleCount`)/test code left untouched. One wrinkle the prior three renames didn't have: "Farkle" is also the branded game's own term for a scoreless roll (rules text said "is a Farkle," the in-game message said "rolled a Farkle"), not just the product name — both instances renamed to "bust" instead, matching the vocabulary the code already used internally (`messageType === 'bust'`, `.farkle-message-bust`), so no second candidate name needed to be picked. The "Farkles" stat column/tile (count of busts, on both `profile.farkle.index` and `.show`) renamed to "Busts" for the same reason. Verified complete via the same `grep -rniE 'farkle' resources/views/` sweep the original renames used — every remaining hit is a route name, PHP class/namespace, CSS class/id, array key, or comment.

**Five more layout reports, some revisiting earlier fixes (`docs/concerns.md`, 2026-08-11).** Regression-covered by `tests/Browser/FarkleMobileLayoutTest.php`.
- **Banked dice moved below the main dice row, and split into one row per roll.** The 2026-08-06 fix (see above) kept `.farkle-banked-row` as a single `nowrap` + horizontally-scrolling row *above* `.dice-area`, specifically to avoid a wrapping row growing tall and pushing the Roll button down. Once the user actually played more than a few turns, that read backwards (already-locked-in dice appearing before the roll they came from) and the horizontal scroll cut dice off on mobile with no visible affordance to reach them. Since the row now renders *below* `.dice-area`/`.controls` instead of above, the original vertical-growth concern no longer applies — nothing above it can be pushed down. New `getBankedDiceRowsProperty()` (`array_chunk($this->bankedDiceThisTurn, self::DICE_COUNT)`) turns the flat array into one row per roll (a hot-dice turn spanning, say, 14 dice now renders as three stacked rows: 6, 6, 2), each capped at `DICE_COUNT` so no single row can overflow its own width and need to scroll. `.farkle-banked-row`'s CSS is otherwise unchanged (still small, muted, `border-color: var(--accent)`); a new `.farkle-banked-rows` wraps them in a column with the same width as `.dice-area .row`. The portrait-mobile override that widens `.farkle-play .row` to `min(92vw, 480px)` (see the "squeezed illegibly small" fix above) needed the identical override added for `.farkle-banked-rows` — the desktop-oriented default width was too narrow to fit a full row of 6 smaller banked dice on a real phone, which was the mobile-cutoff report's actual root cause. **CSS ordering gotcha hit while wiring this up:** the portrait override and the unconditional base rule for `.farkle-banked-rows` have identical specificity (one class each), so whichever is later in the file always wins regardless of the media query matching — the override was initially placed *before* the unconditional base rule in source order and silently did nothing (confirmed live: computed width stayed at the desktop default at a 390px viewport). Fixed by moving the base rule earlier, matching how `.farkle-play .row`'s own base-then-portrait-override pair is already ordered just above it in the file.
- **Desktop scoreboard font size, round two.** The 2026-08-06 fix bumped the live (in-progress) scoreboard from Tailwind's 14px `text-sm` to 18px on desktop; reported still too small. Raised to 22px, matching `.farkle-final-scores`' own existing `clamp()` max, so the live and final scoreboards now read at the same size instead of introducing a third number.
- **Landscape mobile: content hidden under the camera cutout.** Root cause was site-wide, not Farkle-specific: `<meta name="viewport" ... viewport-fit=cover>` (already present) makes `env(safe-area-inset-*)` populate on a notched device, but `body`, `.button-bar`, and `.site-footer` only ever consumed `safe-area-inset-top`/`-bottom` — left/right were always a plain `16px`/`1rem`, which a landscape camera cutout (on either side edge) sits underneath. Added `max(16px, env(safe-area-inset-left/right, 0px))`-style padding to all three. Can't be verified in this environment's headless Chromium the way the other fixes were screenshotted/measured live — Chromium has no way to simulate a device's actual safe-area-inset values from a test, so `env()` always resolves to its `0px` fallback here. Confirmed only that the formula doesn't regress the non-notch case (computed padding is still exactly `16px`/`1rem` with a zero inset); real verification needs a physical notched device.
- **Navbar buttons crowded/off-screen in an online-room Farkle game.** An in-progress room game as host stacks Rooms + Scores + Watch only + End Game + the fullscreen toggle all in `.button-bar .right` at once, alongside Home + Rules in `.left` — confirmed live (`tests/Browser/FarkleMobileLayoutTest.php`'s new crowded-navbar test) that at 375px wide this pushed the fullscreen toggle and End Game button past the visible edge entirely, with nothing to scroll to reach them. Fixed by forcing `.right` onto its own full-width line below `.left` once `.many-buttons` is set below the existing 480px breakpoint (`flex: 1 1 100%` — deterministic, not dependent on whether it happens to overflow) and letting `.right`'s own buttons wrap onto a second line within that if even the full bar width isn't enough, plus a small `margin-left` before `#end-game-trigger` (shared id with Family Feud) so it doesn't sit flush against its neighbors. Two follow-on bugs found while verifying this live, both only visible in a real screenshot, not from reading the CSS: (1) the fixed-position bar's real height now varies (one line vs. two), but the page's own reserved top padding was still a fixed `clamp(80px, 10vmin, 96px)` — the page's content rendered starting underneath the now-taller bar. Added a `body.scrollable:has(.button-bar.many-buttons)` override at the same breakpoint, sized from the real measured bar height (108px at both 375px and 320px wide) with a real margin above it, not guessed. (2) `.button-bar`'s default flex `align-items: stretch` made `.left` match `.right`'s new taller height whenever `.right` wrapped, and — because Home is wrapped in an `<a>` while `#rules-btn` ("?") is a direct `<button>` flex item — only the Rules button visibly stretched into a tall, narrow rectangle rather than both looking the same. Fixed with `align-items: flex-start` on `.button-bar`.
- **A false alarm while regression-testing the landscape fixes below, worth recording so it isn't re-investigated from scratch.** An online-room game at 667px wide initially measured the scoreboard at a squeezed ~98px, vs. ~136px for local pass-and-play at the same size — looked like a real, separate crowded-navbar bug at a wider breakpoint than the fix above covers. It wasn't: `body`'s `padding-top` genuinely transitions on load (the existing `transition: padding-top 0.4s ease !important` rule, real and load-bearing elsewhere — see "Fullscreen / chrome toggle" below), and the very first measurement landed mid-transition. Timed samples 0.1s apart confirmed it: `95.3px → 86.8px → 80.2px → 80px` over ~0.4s, settling at the correct, un-squeezed value — the same class of premature-measurement mistake `CLAUDE.md` already documents for ad hoc `--agent` probes, just hit here inside a real test instead. `tests/Browser/FarkleMobileLayoutTest.php`'s own landscape scoreboard-height test uses local pass-and-play specifically to sidestep this (a room's extra button-bar buttons make the transition's starting point more visible), not because room mode has a real bug — worth remembering if a similar "looks squeezed at first measurement" report shows up again: wait for the transition before concluding anything.

**Two landscape-mobile reports, both from the same root cause (`docs/concerns.md`, 2026-08-11).** A short landscape viewport (as little as ~320px tall) forces target line/status/dice/banked-dice rows/scoreboard into one stacked column with no vertical room to spare — the opposite problem from every prior mobile fix above, which all had width to protect and height to spare. Regression-covered by two new tests in `tests/Browser/FarkleMobileLayoutTest.php`.
- **"Roll Again" covering the last die, and the scoreboard unreachable because "the page does not scroll."** Both are the same underlying issue as the earlier "squeezed illegibly small" portrait fix (see above) — a too-narrow row forcing dice and controls to fight for space — just triggered by short *height* instead of narrow *width*, and with no landscape-specific handling to catch it (Farkle had a `orientation: portrait` override, but none for `orientation: landscape`). Root-caused live rather than guessed: several synthetic viewport sizes (568×320 up to 844×390) never reproduced literal pixel-level overlap in this environment, but the scoreboard's own `clientHeight` was confirmed squeezed to as little as 90px (`min-height: min(160px, 25vh)`'s floor, `25vh` of a 375px-tall viewport) against real content overflow, and the underlying "controls sharing a row with the dice" pattern is exactly what already broke once before in portrait — so the fix generalizes that proven pattern rather than chasing an unreproducible exact trigger.
- **Fixed with a dedicated landscape layout, mirroring Boggle's own `#board-area`.** Boggle already solves this identical class of problem (short height, spare width) by going row-oriented in `@media (max-width: 899px) and (orientation: landscape)`, putting the board and word list side by side instead of stacked. Farkle's Blade template gained two new wrapper divs — `.farkle-game-area` (around the dice column + scoreboard) and `.farkle-dice-column` (around `.dice-area` + `.farkle-banked-rows`) — both `display: contents` everywhere except this new landscape query, so they're invisible to layout (and to every existing test) outside it. Inside the query, `.farkle-game-area` becomes a real `flex-direction: row` container; the scoreboard, as its other flex item, picks up the container's default `align-items: stretch` and fills roughly the *full* available height for free, no extra height rule needed, instead of the ~90px sliver it had before. `.dice-area` also gets `flex-wrap: wrap` (same trick the portrait fix uses) so Roll Again/Bank always render below the dice rather than squeezed beside them.
- **Two more bugs found only by measuring live, not by reading the CSS, while wiring this up:** (1) `.farkle-play .row`'s shared base rule includes `flex: 1 1 auto` (needed so it can grow to fill Yahtzee's/desktop Farkle's own unconstrained row) — inside the new narrow `.farkle-dice-column`, with nothing else capping `.dice-area`'s own width, that `flex-grow` had nothing to stop it and the row measured ~400px wide against a ~140px intended width, nowhere close to matching `.controls` on the line below. Fixed by pinning `.farkle-play .row` to `flex: 0 0 auto` in this query, and giving `.dice-area` itself the same explicit width so there's no ambiguity for `.controls`' own `flex-basis: 100%` to resolve against. (2) An initial `min(44vh, 42vw, 360px)` width formula shrank to as little as ~140px on the shortest tested viewport (320px tall) — nowhere near enough for the two-button Roll Again + Bank row (each `min-width: 120px`, plus gap, ~260px real minimum), and the buttons overflowed their own line asymmetrically (`justify-content: center` on a too-narrow container centers the *overflow* too, pushing part of the Roll Again button to a negative `left`, off the visible edge). Fixed with `clamp(260px, 46vw, 380px)` — the `260px` floor, not just the preferred/max, is what actually matters here.

**`farkle` scrubbed from URL paths too (`docs/concerns.md`, 2026-08-12).** The 2026-08-06 rename (see above) deliberately left routes untouched, reasoning that "in code isn't important." Revisited: URL paths are user-visible (typed, bookmarked, shared) in a way the rest of "in code" isn't, so this closes that gap specifically for the path segment. `/farkle` → `/10000`, `/farkle/room/{code}` → `/10000/room/{code}`, `/profile/farkle` → `/profile/10000`, `/profile/farkle/{gameParticipant}` → `/profile/10000/{gameParticipant}`, all in `routes/web.php`. Route *names* (`farkle.play`, `farkle.room`, `profile.farkle`, `profile.farkle.show`), the `pages::farkle.*` Livewire view namespace, `GameType::Farkle`, CSS classes/ids, and array keys are all still untouched — every other caller goes through `route()` by name, so none of them needed to change. Only literal path strings did: `routes/web.php` itself and every test hardcoding `/farkle`/`/profile/farkle` rather than using `route()`.

**"busted" → "Farkled" request skipped, not implemented (`docs/concerns.md`, 2026-08-12).** A separate `docs/concerns.md` item asked to change the in-game bust message, rules text, and stats-page labels from "bust"/"Busts" back to "Farkled"/"got a Farkle"/"Farkles". Flagged to the user before implementing: this directly reverses the "Farkle" renamed to "10,000" trademark-avoidance decision from 2026-08-06 above, which specifically chose "bust" *instead of* "Farkle" for this exact vocabulary for that reason. User chose to skip it and keep the existing "bust" terminology rather than reintroduce the trademarked term into user-facing copy. No code changed for this item; noted here so the request isn't silently lost and doesn't get re-attempted without the same trade-off being considered again.

**Watch-only scoreboard needed horizontal/internal scrolling with a realistic player count (`docs/concerns.md`'s Clear the Deck spectator item, generalized).** `.farkle-scoreboard` is a single-column name/score list, shared by seated players and spectators alike, with a fixed `min-height: min(160px, 25vh)` and `overflow-y-auto` -- reasonable for a seated player's own device (scrolling to see the rest of the field is already a normal interaction there), but confirmed live via a seeded 8-player room that it cut off the bottom 2-3 players entirely on a TV/tablet-width viewport with nothing to scroll it with. Fixed narrowly, only for `$this->isSpectator && ! $gameOver`: a new `.farkle-spectator-grid` (CSS `display:grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(140px, 1fr))`) replaces the single column, and `container-type: size` on that same grid lets `.farkle-spectator-name`/`.farkle-spectator-score` size themselves in `cqh` against the grid's own actual rendered height -- the identical technique Yahtzee's `table.scorecard` cells already use (see that section below) to guarantee everything fits without the component needing to know the player count in advance. The seated-player and game-over scoreboards are both completely untouched (separate `@else` branch, unchanged markup/CSS) -- this only replaces the spectator-and-still-playing case, the one actually reported.
- Player-count-independent by design, not tuned to 8: `auto-fit` picks however many columns fit the actual width, and `cqh`-based font-size shrinks to whatever height is actually available, so this isn't a fixed-N-columns hack that would need revisiting the next time someone hosts a bigger room.
- Regression coverage: `tests/Browser/FarkleSpectatorScoreboardTest.php` (a real 8-player game, every name visible, zero `scrollWidth`/`scrollHeight` overflow at a tightest-case tablet viewport).

Yahtzee's own scorecard had the identical bug, one layer deeper -- see the Yahtzee section below for the full fix. Family Feud and Best Match were checked too but didn't show the same pattern -- see the Clear the Deck section above for that summary.

**The live (non-spectator) scoreboard's font stayed at the old, smaller 22px ceiling on a TV, even after the dedicated spectator scoreboard above got bumped up to 36px for the identical reason (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #17, 2026-08-17).** A seated player actively playing (not toggled to "Watch only") on a device that's itself screen-mirrored/cast to a TV only ever saw the live scoreboard's own `@media (min-width: 900px)` flat `22px` rule, never the spectator-only fix above — an inconsistency more than a fresh discovery, since that prior fix is itself direct evidence 22px reads too small on an actual TV in this exact app.
- **Bumped both the live scoreboard and `.farkle-final-scores` together, not just the live one** — the two have always intentionally shared the same font size (see the live scoreboard's own comment, "raised to match `.farkle-final-scores`' own 22px... rather than picking a new number"), and a game-over screen is just as plausible to be sitting on a TV as a live one, so breaking that parity to fix only half of it would have reintroduced a smaller, inconsistent version of the same problem. Both now `clamp(18px/16px, 3.2vh, 32px)` (raised from `2.4vh`/flat `22px`) — confirmed live at a real 1920×1080 TV resolution that this actually reaches the new 32px ceiling, not just "bigger than before" at an arbitrary desktop size.
- Regression: a new case in `tests/Browser/FarkleMobileLayoutTest.php` asserting the exact 32px value at 1920×1080, alongside the existing "greater than 18px" desktop case (kept as-is, deliberately not pinned to an exact number so it doesn't churn on a future re-tune).

**Spicy variant + High Stakes variant shipped (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #23, 2026-08-19).** Two new independent, host-configurable toggles (`docs/specs/farkle.md`'s new §6), following the same spec-first process Clear the Deck established: rules as given were reviewed for edge cases, some resolved by direct application of the given rules, some needed the user's own clarification before building (see the spec's §6.5 for exactly what was asked and answered), and the spec was marked ready-to-build-from before any code changed.

- **Per-die identity, not just per-die value, had to be introduced from scratch.** Every prior piece of Farkle state only ever tracked *how many* dice were left to roll (`$heldDiceCount`); Spicy's "one specific physical die's red face is always the same number" rule needed to know *which* dice specifically. Rather than randomizing a die-to-label mapping, the spec picked the simplest equivalent (`docs/specs/farkle.md` §6.1): label the six conceptual dice 1-6, and die *N*'s red face is the value *N* — statistically identical to any other fixed 1-to-1 mapping since real dice are interchangeable anyway. `DiceRoller::rollSlots(list<int> $slots): array{values, spicy}` rolls exactly the still-live labels and flags each `value === slot`. A new `$activeDieSlots` property (labels not yet held this turn) is now maintained in lockstep with `$heldDiceCount` everywhere the latter changes (`startTurn()`, `applySelection()`'s hot-dice reset, `bank()`) — and, notably, **always maintained even in a plain non-Spicy game**, not just when the toggle is on: `performRoll()` now always calls `rollSlots()` (never the old plain `roll()`), and simply discards the spicy flags when `!$spicyEnabled`. This turned out to simplify the implementation more than gating it would have — one roll code path instead of two, with `Scorer`'s own spicy parameter already designed to be a no-op when empty/all-false.
- **`Scorer::scoreSelection()`/`scoreMaximal()` gained an optional parallel `list<bool> $spicy` parameter, fully backward compatible** — every pre-existing call site and test in this codebase still passes with it omitted entirely (confirmed: the full pre-existing Farkle suite ran unchanged and green before any new spicy-specific test was added). Doubling math (`bestScoreForFace()`, `scoreSixDiceSpecial()`) required working out two non-obvious cases rather than just multiplying a whole selection's total: (1) below 3-of-a-kind, each scoring 1/5 is its own separate single-die combination, not one group, so a spicy die there adds one extra `$singleValue` rather than doubling the whole lump (`[1, spicy 1]` = 300, not 400 — caught by a first-draft test expectation that was itself wrong until worked through by hand); (2) at 3-of-a-kind and above, applying the *same* uniform `2 ** $spicyCount` multiplier to every candidate decomposition *before* `max()` is proven exactly equivalent to doubling only the winning candidate's own combination, because scalar multiplication never changes which candidate is the max, and the winning candidate always already uses every one of that face's dice as a single flat group (the whole reason `bestScoreForFace()` exists in the first place) — meaning no per-die placement search was needed despite the temptation to think one was. Full reasoning is in `Scorer::bestScoreForFace()`'s own docblock.
- **A second Scorer method, `nonScoringIndices()`, was needed for a subtler reason than doubling: `bank()` never asks the player to select anything.** High Stakes needs to know exactly which dice from the *final* roll didn't contribute to the auto-included bank (so they can be handed to the next player as "non-frozen") — but since Bank auto-includes every scoring die in the current roll regardless of what's clicked (an existing, pre-Spicy behavior), there was no selection state to read `$activeDieSlots` removal from the way `applySelection()`/`rollAgain()` already does. `nonScoringIndices()` recovers that split after the fact by reusing the exact same "does this face's count clear 3, or is it a lone 1/5" logic `scoreMaximal()` already computes internally. This was caught as a real gap while writing `FarkleSpicyTest.php`, not anticipated in the spec: an early version of `bank()` left `$activeDieSlots` completely untouched by the final roll, so `lastBankInheritance`'s `remainingSlots` would have silently reported the *pre-roll* slot count instead of "however many didn't score in the roll that just got banked" — exactly the "if they bank four dice, you start with... the two remaining" scenario the user described when clarifying the rule.
- **High Stakes' exact semantics needed the user's own clarification mid-build, not just spec review** (see `docs/specs/farkle.md` §6.5's own account) — three real ambiguities in the rules-as-given: whether the next player rerolls the leftover dice fresh or reuses the prior literal values (rerolls fresh, count only), whether the inherited amount is the *whole* banked turn total or just the leftover dice's own value (the whole amount — "the amount they banked becomes your starting amount"), and whether it can ever trigger off a bust (never — only ever a Bank, confirmed by the rule text's own phrasing only ever contrasting two banking sub-cases). Modeled as `$lastBankInheritance: null|array{turnScore, remainingSlots}`, set by `bankTurnScore()` only when `$highStakesEnabled`, offered once to the very next turn's opening roll (`rollTakeOver()`, a sibling to `roll()`), and cleared unconditionally the instant that next roll happens regardless of which was chosen — `roll()` (declining) clears it too, not just `rollTakeOver()` (accepting), so it can never be offered a second time.
- **Instant win (all six dice spicy in one roll) turned out to be a structural consequence of the labeling scheme, not a separate mechanic to detect.** Since a die is only ever spicy when it shows its own label, and the six labels are exactly 1-6, "all six spicy" can only physically happen when the roll's values are `{1,2,3,4,5,6}` arranged so each die lands on its own label — a specific arrangement of the already-existing straight pattern, not a new roll shape. `GameEngine::isInstantWin(list<bool> $spicy): bool` just checks `count($spicy) === 6 && !in_array(false, $spicy, true)`. `finishGame()` gained an optional `$forcedWinnerIndex` parameter for this path — needed its own bug fix mid-build: the initial version determined each player's `GameResult` by comparing `bankedScore` to `$topScore`, which is wrong for a forced win whenever another player's `bankedScore` *happens* to numerically equal the winner's (unrelated coincidence, not the actual win condition) — fixed by checking the forced winner's *seat index* directly rather than falling through to the score-comparison branches at all.
- **Testing an event with a real-world probability of (1/6)^6 needed a different technique than every other Farkle test's "retry a real roll a capped number of times" pattern** (which works fine for a single die's ~1-in-6 bust odds, hopeless at this scale). `FarkleSpicyTest.php`'s instant-win test forces `$dice`/`$dieSpicy` directly (as every other spicy-scoring test here does) and invokes the private `winInstantly()` via `ReflectionMethod` rather than a real roll — with one gotcha specific to that approach, worth remembering for any future reflection-based Livewire test: `assertSee()` re-renders through Livewire's own request/snapshot cycle and does *not* pick up a reflection-mutated property (it still rendered the pre-mutation dice/controls UI even though `->get('gameOver')` correctly read `true`), so assertions after a reflection call have to read the live instance directly (`$component->get(...)`/`$component->instance()`) rather than trigger a fresh render.
- **CSS is a single new rule, `.die.spicy`, reusing the existing `--warn` token** (already this app's one "pay attention" color — `.farkle-message-bust`, `.farkle-final-round-banner`) rather than introducing a new bright red, keeping it consistent with the low-stim design system's existing muted palette and no-flashing/no-bouncing rule.
- Setup/room toggles follow the exact existing Target Score/Final Round button-pair pattern (`pages::farkle.play`'s setup screen and `pages::farkle.room`'s host-only panel, both updated identically). Toggling Spicy resets `$targetScore` to that mode's own default (10,000/25,000) the same way `setPlayerCount()` already re-pads `$playerNames` on change, rather than leaving a stale prior selection in place.
- Added `tests/Feature/Games/FarkleSpicyTest.php` (doubling on bank, the variant being fully inert when off even if `$dieSpicy` is somehow set, instant win, and every High Stakes case: offered only after a Bank, take-over starting turn score at the full inherited amount, declining forfeiting it, and the "banked with all six already used" carve-out falling back to a fresh six) and `tests/Browser/FarkleSpicyDieTest.php` (the real rendered border color differing on a spicy die — CSS a Livewire-level test can't see, per `CLAUDE.md`'s browser-testing rules — plus the setup toggles and Spicy's target-score-option swap). Existing `Unit/Farkle/{Scorer,DiceRoller,GameEngine}Test.php` and `Feature/Games/FarkleGameTest.php` gained spicy-specific cases and updates (two pre-existing tests that forced `->set('heldDiceCount', 5)` to pin a single-die reroll needed a matching `->set('activeDieSlots', [1])`, since `performRoll()` now sources the roll count from `$activeDieSlots`' length instead of `$heldDiceCount` arithmetic).
- **A confirmed, but out-of-scope-here, follow-on finding**: while verifying this, `.farkle-play .row` (the dice grid, Farkle's own equivalent of `.yahtzee-play .row`) measured 1600px wide at 1920×1080 against an intended `min(56vw, 42vh, 720px)` ≈ 454px cap — the identical `flex: 1 1 auto` over-grow bug just fixed for Yahtzee (see that section above). Not fixed here: Farkle's landscape-layout work already documented a real, verified need for `flex: 1 1 auto` in a different, narrower context (`.farkle-dice-column`), so blindly copying Yahtzee's `flex: 0 1 auto` swap risks quietly breaking that without the same live-viewport verification Yahtzee's own fix got. Filed as `docs/TODO.md`'s new #20 rather than silently dropped.

**`.farkle-play .row`'s wide-desktop over-grow bug fixed, matching Yahtzee's own (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #20, 2026-08-18).** The base rule's `flex: 1 1 auto` swapped to `flex: 0 1 auto`, the identical fix already applied to `.yahtzee-play .row`. Confirmed the concern raised when this was filed — that Farkle's landscape layout had documented a real need for `flex: 1 1 auto` inside `.farkle-dice-column` — didn't actually apply to this base-rule change: the landscape media query already overrides `.farkle-play .row` to its own explicit `flex: 0 0 auto` and width (see the entry above), so it never inherits the base rule's flex value in the first place, and the portrait override only ever changes `width`, not `flex-grow`, so it's covered too (its row was never sharing a line with anything else to grow into). Verified live at all three cases per the task's own requirement: 1920×1080 desktop (new test asserting `.row`'s width stays under 500px, well below the pre-fix ~1600px blowout and close to the intended ~454px cap), plus the existing portrait (390×844) and landscape (568×320) tests in `tests/Browser/FarkleMobileLayoutTest.php` re-run clean with no changes needed.

**Three-of-a-kind 1s corrected from a flat 1000 to 300 (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #22, 2026-08-18).** `bestScoreForFace()`'s special case (`$face === 1 ? 1000 : $face * 100`) is gone — three 1s now just continues the same 100-per-die value one/two 1s already use (`3 * 100`), so there's no special three-1s bonus at all anymore, matching how 2/3/4/6 have never had one. A real, non-obvious side effect worth recording: this also silently changed what wins for **four** 1s. Before, decomposing four 1s as "three 1s (1000) + one leftover single (100) = 1100" beat the flat four-of-a-kind value (1000), so that's what four 1s scored. With three 1s now worth only 300, that same decomposition is 300 + 100 = 400 — below the flat 1000 — so four 1s now scores the flat four-of-a-kind value like every other face already does, not a face-specific bonus. `bestScoreForFace()` needed no logic changes for this (`max($candidates)` already picks whichever's higher automatically); only its own docblock and `scoreSelection()`'s did, since both referenced the now-wrong 1100 example.
- Updated every place that asserted or described the old value: `tests/Unit/Farkle/{Scorer,GameEngine}Test.php`, the in-game rules text (`pages::farkle.play`), and `docs/specs/farkle.md`'s own scoring section (kept in sync with the actual implementation the same way its 2026-08-05 correction already was, rather than left as a stale historical record).
- **One test helper broke in a way worth flagging, not just silently patched**: `tests/Feature/Games/FarkleWinConditionTest.php`'s `bankThreeOnes()` used `[1, 1, 1, 2, 3, 4]` specifically because three 1s (1000) cleared the game's 500-point minimum-entry bar — at the new 300 value it wouldn't have. Renamed to `bankFourSixes()` and switched to `[6, 6, 6, 6, 2, 3]` (four 6s = 1000 flat), preserving every existing assertion (`bankedScore` still ends up exactly 1000) without needing to touch the test's actual expectations, just the dice combination generating them. Both live-verified at a realistic max room size on a short, wide TV-cast-style viewport (1920×750) rather than left as an assumption:
- **Best Match**: an 8-seat room (`Room::factory()`'s own `max_players` default), spectator view. Confirmed zero `document.body` overflow — its per-seat score-chip row (`⚡play.blade.php`'s `@foreach ($seats as $seat)`) is a plain wrapping flex row, and the page has no `.in-game { height: 100dvh }` clip the way Clear the Deck/Farkle/Yahtzee's own in-game views do, so it was never structurally exposed to the "TV can't scroll to reach the rest" failure mode those three needed dedicated fixes for — it just grows and wraps onto more rows within the page's own natural (unclipped) height, which comfortably fit at this size.
- **Family Feud**: a full 4v4 (8-seat) room, spectator view. Confirmed zero overflow — its in-game scoreboard (`⚡play.blade.php`) only ever renders two fixed team-name/score cards, never a per-player list, so team roster size has no way to affect its layout at all; same "no `.in-game` height clip" structural point as Best Match above.
- Regression coverage: new cases in `tests/Browser/AppleMatchRoomBrowserTest.php` and `tests/Browser/FamilyFeudRoomBrowserTest.php`, each seeding a full-size room and asserting `document.body.scrollHeight <= window.innerHeight` at the 1920×750 viewport.

## Best Match

A new card-matching game (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #1), an "Apples to Apples"-style reinterpretation shipping under the non-trademarked display name **"Best Match"** — chosen collaboratively the same way as Boggle/Yahtzee/Family Feud (see "Game renaming" below): proposed alongside "Judge's Pick"/"Wild Pairing," user picked "Best Match" for being the most literal description of the mechanic. Internal identifiers (`GameType::AppleMatch`, `apple_match`/`apple-match` routes/DB values) were deliberately left as-is, same rationale as every other game's own internal name. Full build spec: `docs/specs/apple-match.md`.

- **Online rooms only, no pass-and-play mode** — the one deliberate scope decision every other game in this app didn't have to make. A private hand is the whole point of this game; pass-and-play can only fake that via an honor-system "don't peek," which nothing else here relies on. `pages::apple-match.play`'s own bare (no `?room=`) state reflects this directly: instead of every other game's own local-setup form, it shows only a "play in a room you host" list (when hosting any) plus a plain pointer to the navbar's Rooms menu — there's no "Start Game" button to click locally because there's nothing to start.
- **Cards are grouped under a `apple_match_decks` row from day one, even though v1 only ever seeds one.** This is forward-compat for a still-deferred idea (`docs/notes.md`, 2026-08-12): multiple default card sets, user-authored decks, and AI-assisted card generation for a theme. None of that is built — no editor, no AI call, no deck picker — but `apple_match_prompts`/`apple_match_cards` are FK'd to a deck (`owner_user_id` nullable = system deck, `source`/`generation_prompt` columns unused but present) specifically so adding that feature later is new UI/policy/service work, not a schema rework touching every card/deal query. `AppleMatchCardSeeder` creates exactly one system deck ("Classic") and attaches ~111 green prompt cards / ~203 red answer cards to it (both hand-written for this app, not ported from any real card set — the trademark concern here is the game's name, not needing literal Mattel/Out of the Box Games card text, so writing new content also sidesteps any question about that).
- **The private-per-seat-view pattern, set here first for Clear the Deck (`docs/TODO.md` #1) to reuse.** Two distinct kinds of "secret" exist in a round: each seat's own hand (safe — a Livewire component instance is already per-user/per-request, same as every other game's own state), and which seat submitted which card (not safe — this is the one that actually mattered). The stronger version of Family Feud's own answer-bank hazard applies here: it's not enough to keep secret data out of a rendered Blade element, or even out of a public property Blade never touches — `RoomGameStateUpdated`'s broadcast payload reaches every connected client's own browser over the websocket, inspectable in devtools regardless of what any single component chooses to render. So round attribution (`cardId => seatIndex`) never touches `$state` at all — it's written to `Cache::put("apple-match:{sessionId}:round:{round}:attribution", ..., now()->addHours(3))` (same 3-hour TTL as `<livewire:game-lobby>`'s own ready-state cache) inside `submitCard()`, and read back exactly once, fresh, inside `pickWinner()` — never assigned to `$this->anything` beforehand. What *is* broadcast once every non-judge seat has submitted is a shuffled, unattributed `anonymizedSubmissions` list (card text only), which is what every seat (judge and onlookers alike) actually sees revealed — matching the physical game, where nobody but the acting judge ever learns who played which card.
- **Hands live on `room_seats.hand` (nullable JSON), bolted on the same way Family Feud's own `team_index`/`is_captain` were** — nullable/defaulted so every other game's existing `RoomSeat::create()` calls keep working unmodified. Dealing/replenishing uses plain `inRandomOrder()`/`whereNotIn()` queries at the moment of dealing (mirroring Family Feud's own `skipQuestion()`), not a pre-shuffled draw-pile/discard-pile array as the build spec originally sketched — simpler, no separate reshuffle bookkeeping needed, and consistent with how every other game in this app actually deals cards/questions. Falls back to allowing card reuse if the deck's own card count is ever smaller than a long game needs, rather than leaving a hand short.
- **No separate `App\Services\AppleMatch\*` deck/round-engine class, despite the build spec's own "Deliverables" section calling for one.** Deliberate deviation, decided during implementation once it became clear dealing/replenishing here is just simple Eloquent queries, not complex reusable math the way Yahtzee's `DiceRoller`/`GameEngine` or Boggle's `Solver`/`Scorer` are — Family Feud has the closest-shaped problem (question/answer selection) and keeps that logic directly in its own Livewire component rather than a separate service, so this follows that precedent instead of the spec's own speculative one.
- **Judge rotation is a plain round-robin over `seat_index`**, advanced by whichever seated player clicks "Next Round" (a shared "we're ready to continue" action, not a turn — same reasoning as Family Feud's own `nextRound()`/`nextRoundSummary()`). `isJudge`/`canSubmit` both follow the private-uncached-method-backing-a-public-getter split proactively, not reactively — `submitCard()`/`pickWinner()` both mutate state their own guard depends on in the same request, the exact shape that caused the real stale-computed-property bugs documented in Farkle's and Family Feud's own DEVLOG entries (2026-08-05) — applied here from the start rather than found and fixed later a third time.
- **Minimum 3 seated players, enforced by `onRoomStarting()` without touching the shared `<livewire:game-lobby>` component** (no `minPlayers` prop added there) — no maximum enforced, matching every other game's existing convention (`rooms.max_players` stays an unused DB default of 8, same as it already was everywhere else).
