- **Real bug, found in live play the same day: starting with only 2 players silently did nothing.** The first version of the player-count guard above copied Family Feud's own "no message-passing path back into the lobby, just decline" shape for its empty-question-bank case — but that case is a misconfigured-deployment edge that basically never happens in real use, while "only 2 people have joined so far" is Best Match's single most ordinary starting state. `<livewire:game-lobby>`'s "Start Game" button has no way to know about a game-specific minimum (it only ever checks `allReady`, i.e. "everyone present has readied up"), so it stayed fully clickable — the host clicked it, and the room just sat there with no feedback at all, looking broken rather than explained. Fixed by adding `$startError` (a plain, un-persisted property on `pages::apple-match.room` itself, set inside `onRoomStarting()`, rendered right above `<livewire:game-lobby>`) instead of modifying the shared component — `'room-game-starting'` is a local, non-broadcast Livewire dispatch reaching only the host's own request, so setting it there is visible on that same response with no extra plumbing. Also surfaces the same way if the card deck hasn't been seeded yet, mirroring the message quality (if not the delivery mechanism) `startGame()`'s own local-mode guard already gives for that case.
- Win condition is a plain "first to N green cards," N host-configurable (default 4, range 3–10) at room setup — no Farkle-style "final round" complication, since there's no shared numeric total that could still change hands after the triggering win.
- `App\Services\Stats\AppleMatchStatsService` follows the established `statsFor()`/`gamesForUser()`/`bestMetrics()` shape exactly (`green_cards_won`, `rounds_judged` per participant; "Most green cards won"/"Most rounds judged" as the two best-metric tiles, the second omitted when nobody's ever judged a round, same "omit rather than a misleading zero" rule every other stats service already follows).
- Test coverage: `tests/Feature/Games/AppleMatchRoomTest.php` (dealing, the 3-player minimum, a full submit → judge → next-round cycle including hand replenishment, judge-can't-submit/non-judge-can't-pick/no-double-submit authorization, an explicit assertion that round attribution never appears in `GameSession.state`, and a full game-to-completion test verifying `GameParticipant` rows), `tests/Unit/Stats/AppleMatchStatsServiceTest.php`, `tests/Feature/ProfileAppleMatchTest.php`, and `tests/Browser/AppleMatchRoomBrowserTest.php` (submitter sees and can play their own hand, judge sees a waiting count instead of a hand, spectator sees neither) — the last one exists specifically because this game's phase-gated visibility (hand vs. waiting message vs. judge controls) is exactly the CSS/conditional-visibility bug class `CLAUDE.md`'s "Automated Browser Testing" section warns a Livewire-only test can't catch.
- **Round-reveal styling for non-judge players** (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #6). The judge's own view of a round's submissions already rendered each one as a real `<button>` (getting this app's global button styling for free); onlookers saw the identical list as bare `<span class="status-line">` text with no visual separation between entries. Restyled to a vertical stack of card-styled boxes (`bg-surface`/`border-surface-border`/`rounded-lg`, the same classes the score badges just above it on the same page already use), non-interactive since only the judge can act on them.
- **Added a host-only manual "End Game" action** (docs/concerns.md) — previously the only way to end a game was some seat reaching the room's target green-card count. `endGameManually()`/`getCanEndGameManuallyProperty()` mirror Farkle's own shape; picks a winner the same way `getWinnerNameProperty()` already did (highest current score via `max()`/`array_search()`) rather than inventing a separate "no winner yet" path, since `finishGame()` always needs exactly one `winningSeatIndex` regardless of whether the stop was early or on-target.

## Clear the Deck

A turn-based, multi-deck shedding card game (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #1), the first game in this app with **two different kinds of hidden information at once**: a private hand (like every other online-room game) and face-down table cards whose identity is secret even from their own owner until played. Full build spec, including every resolved design decision below: `docs/specs/clear-the-deck.md`.

- **Design decisions resolved collaboratively before any code was written**, the same "propose, get approval" process as Boggle/Yahtzee/Family Feud's renames (see "Game renaming" below): fixed round count (host-configurable, 1–10, default 1, lowest cumulative score wins) rather than a target-score ceiling; a "Clear the Deck" set can be formed from any combination of the acting player's own hand, face-up, and face-down cards with no minimum required from hand; a wild Ten always clears the deck, with no "play it as an ordinary card" path; no AFK/turn timer for v1; and the name itself was kept as-is (a generic mechanic description, not a specific trademarked product's name, unlike Boggle/Yahtzee/Family Feud/Apples-to-Apples). Two further implementation-level details were decided independently rather than re-surveying the user a second time — narrow enough to fall under "pick the simplest faithful digital equivalent" rather than a design choice: a face-down flip is validated atomically as part of a whole play attempt (not a stateful reveal-then-decide interaction), and Ten's 25-point scoring penalty applies wherever it remains at round end (hand, face-up, or face-down), not hand-only.
- **Cards are plain integers 1–13 (Ace low, King high) — no card-content table at all**, unlike Best Match's `apple_match_cards`. "Suits are not used in this game" (the original rules) means a virtual deck is just `ceil(20n/13)` repeated 1–13 sets, shuffled, first `20n` taken for `n` seats — no seeder, no factory for card content, nothing to seed before a room can start (a real operational difference from Best Match, whose own room start fails outright if its deck was never seeded).
- **Face-up/face-down table cards are unordered bags per seat, not per-slot pairs.** The physical dealing description (4 face-down, then 4 face-up "on top of each down card") implies a 1:1 slot pairing that determines exactly which face-down card a given face-up card covers. Since neither card's identity is ever tied to anything else once dealt, the covering rule reduces to a pure count — `max(0, count(table_face_down) - count(table_face_up))` uncovered — mathematically equivalent to strict per-slot covering as long as specific pairing never matters (it doesn't here), and it means slot indices never need to be stored, transmitted, or reasoned about at all. Two new nullable JSON columns on `room_seats` (`table_face_up`, `table_face_down`), alongside the existing generic `hand` column reused from Best Match (now holding plain integers instead of card ids).
- **The app's first "secret from its own owner" state, handled by never resolving it into any Livewire property until the one action that needs it.** `table_face_down` is never broadcast and never assigned to a public property (own or otherwise) — a flip is specified as a *count*, not a specific card (since the cards are fungible/unknown, "flip 2" is equivalent to "the first 2 elements" of the seat's own bag), resolved fresh from the database inside `playCards()` and validated as part of the whole attempted play. If the combined values (hand + face-up + however the face-down cards turn out) don't all match, the entire attempt is rejected with **no state change and nothing revealed** — the same as if the flip never happened. This is the harder version of the "private per-seat view" pattern Best Match established (`docs/DEVLOG.md`'s own Best Match section, "never assign secret state to a rendered property, resolve it fresh from the DB" — this game's table cards are secret even from the one client that would normally be trusted with them).
- **Turn resolution is four cases, checked in order**: a wild Ten always clears (bypassing the value comparison entirely); a completed 4+ run (spanning this play plus however much of the same value already sits atop the center stack) clears; playing higher than the stack's current top forces the entire stack plus the attempted cards into the acting seat's own hand; anything else (a legal equal-or-lesser play, no set) joins the stack and passes the turn. The first three all grant the same player an extra turn — the *only* way a turn passes to the next seat is an unremarkable legal play that doesn't complete a set. "Going out" (hand and both table bags all empty) is checked only after the fourth case, since the first three always leave the acting seat holding cards.
- **No separate `App\Services\ClearTheDeck\*` engine or dealer class**, same precedent Best Match set (dealing/replenishing is plain Eloquent queries, not complex reusable math) — `pages::clear-the-deck.room`'s `onRoomStarting()` and `pages::clear-the-deck.play`'s `nextRound()` each have their own private `dealSeats()`, deliberately duplicated rather than shared, since the two call sites (initial deal vs. between-round redeal) are similar in shape but not identical enough to be worth a shared abstraction for ~15 lines.
- **Scoring is inverted from every other game in this app: lower is better.** `App\Services\Stats\ClearTheDeckStatsService::bestMetrics()` sorts its headline metric *ascending* ("Fewest points"), the one deliberate deviation from the `statsFor()`/`gamesForUser()`/`bestMetrics()` shape every other stats service follows. Ties are possible at game end (unlike this app's other "first to N" games) since this is cumulative scoring across a fixed round count — `GameResult::Draw` is used for every seat tied at the lowest total, not just a single arbitrary winner.
- **Fixed two real bugs found while wiring up the new `GameType::ClearTheDeck` case, both pre-existing and both in exhaustive `match` expressions over `GameType` that were never updated when Best Match shipped**: `pages::profile.games.index`'s `showRoute()` had no `GameType::AppleMatch` arm at all (a `\UnhandledMatchError` waiting to happen the first time any user's "All Games" page tried to render a Best Match row), and `<x-player-stats>`'s own route-lookup match had the same gap. Both fixed in the same pass as adding the new `ClearTheDeck` arm, since leaving a known latent crash in a `match` this change was already touching would have been irresponsible to walk past.
- Test coverage: `tests/Feature/Games/ClearTheDeckRoomTest.php` (dealing shape, the 3–10 player range, all four turn-resolution cases including the wild-Ten-overrides-pickup interaction, a rejected mismatched face-down flip causing no state change, going-out ending a round immediately with correct Ten=25 scoring, `nextRound()`'s redeal/dealer-rotation, game end with both a clean winner and a tied-lowest Draw case, and an explicit assertion that `table_face_down` values never appear in broadcast `GameSession.state`), `tests/Unit/Stats/ClearTheDeckStatsServiceTest.php`, `tests/Feature/ProfileClearTheDeckTest.php`, and `tests/Browser/ClearTheDeckRoomBrowserTest.php` (active player vs. waiting vs. spectator visibility) — the same phase-gated-visibility rationale as Best Match's own browser test.
- **Playing-card visuals, imported from a Claude Design mockup** (`claude.ai/design`, project "Clear the Deck game screens") via the `claude_design` MCP tool, replacing the initial plain-button/text UI with an actual card/tableau/pile look. New `<x-clear-the-deck-card>` component (a `<div>`, not a `<button>` — same reasoning as Farkle's own `.die`: this app's global `button` base styling, `@layer base`, would otherwise have to be fought rather than just not applied) renders a real playing-card face (digits 1–10, `J`/`Q`/`K` for 11–13 — a more compact label than the prose `cardLabel()` used in server-generated messages like "played the Wild Ten," which stays spelled out) sized by a single `--ctd-card-w` custom property, so one component serves the hand, the table columns, and the fanned center-pile display at three different sizes via `calc()` rather than three near-duplicate components. Table columns are rendered by a new `columnsFor()` helper that reconstructs up to 4 face-down/face-up "columns" for *display only* from the aggregate-bag counts described above — a presentation-layer illusion of per-slot pairing over a model that (correctly) doesn't track it, not a change to the underlying data. The face-down-flip stepper was replaced with direct tap-to-select on the card-back tiles themselves.
- **Real bug hit while implementing the mockup import: a positioned wrapper with no reserved size.** `.ctd-pile-wrap`/`.ctd-column` compute their own width/height from `calc(var(--ctd-card-w) * ...)`, but `--ctd-card-w` was only ever being set inline on each *inner* `<x-clear-the-deck-card>` — custom properties don't cascade upward, so the wrapper's own `calc()` had nothing to resolve, collapsed to an invalid/zero size, and stopped reserving space in the flow even though its absolutely-positioned children still rendered at full size — the pile visibly overlapped the Submit button and the tableau below it. Fixed by also setting `--ctd-card-w` on each wrapper (`.ctd-tableau`, `.ctd-pile-wrap`) directly, letting it cascade down to every card and column inside instead of relying on a sibling-level property that was never actually shared upward. Caught by rendering a real seeded room state through a throwaway Pest browser test and reading back the actual screenshot — this exact "the code looks right, the DOM proves it's right, but the layout is still broken" class of bug is precisely why `CLAUDE.md`'s "Automated Browser Testing" section exists.
- **"(Tens)" clarifier added to the display name** (`docs/concerns.md`): "Clear the Deck" alone reads as a generic shedding-game description, not a specific game someone could name to a friend, so `GameType::label()` and every hardcoded literal that didn't already route through it (home page card, both the play/room pages' titles/headings/status text, both profile pages' titles/headings/empty state) became `"Clear the Deck (Tens)"`. `label()` being the one central source for most call sites (sidebar, player-stats, game-lobby switcher, room hub) meant only ~14 scattered literals needed a manual pass on top of that one enum change.
- Also required `npm run build` to pick up the new `resources/css/app.css` rules — a plain `git`-tracked source edit alone doesn't reach the page, the same gotcha `CLAUDE.md`'s "Frontend Bundling" note flags.
- **Follow-up discrepancy pass, comparing real screenshots of both the mockup and the shipped page side by side.** The first import round still diverged from the mockup in several ways the user caught by eye: the Submit button was stacked below the pile instead of beside it in the same row; opponent tableaus shrank in width as cards were played instead of staying a fixed 4-column width (`columnsFor()` now pads its output to always return exactly 4 entries — a `type: 'empty'` column renders nothing but still reserves `--ctd-card-w` of space, matching the mockup's own `TableauColumn`, which always renders its wrapper div regardless of content); section labels ("On the table," "Your columns," "Your hand") read as plain body text instead of a bold `var(--bar)`-colored heading; opponent names weren't bold; and the mobile hand grid reflowed with the same fluid `clamp()` card size as desktop instead of the mockup's fixed, larger 4-column grid (new `.ctd-hand-grid` class, grid on mobile via `@media (max-width: 899px)` — this app's existing mobile breakpoint — flex-wrap above it; raised the card-size clamp's minimum from 46px to 58px so mobile cards read closer to the mockup's own fixed mobile size without a second breakpoint-specific value). Also removed two elements the mockup never had at all (the "Round X of Y · dealing" status line and the last-action message) and trimmed the opponent line down to hand count only, dropping the score display that had no mockup counterpart — per explicit user direction, kept as a hand count rather than dropped entirely, since "the number of cards in hand must remain visible" is a real rule requirement, not just something the mockup happened to omit. One item was explicitly rejected rather than fixed: auto-highlighting any card matching the pile's current value (present in the mockup) was deliberately left out at the user's request.
- **Real bug found via user testing during this pass, unrelated to the mockup itself: tapping one face-down card selected every uncovered face-down card at once.** `toggleFaceDownSelection()`'s original "range select" design (clicking tile K set a scalar `selectedFaceDownCount` through K) was meant to read as "pick how many," but in practice just looked broken — and made it functionally very hard to combine a face-down flip with specific hand/face-up cards in the same play, since flipping more face-down cards than intended usually broke the value-match check. Replaced with `selectedFaceDownColumns` (a set of toggled display-indices, exactly the same independent per-tile toggle `toggleHandCard()`/`toggleFaceUpCard()` already used) — each face-down tile now selects only itself. Combining hand + face-up + face-down cards in one play already worked server-side (`playCards()` always validated all three sources together); the bug just made it look like it didn't. Covered by two new tests: one asserting a single tile toggle only selects that tile, and one driving a real combined hand+face-up+face-down play through to a successful `centerStack` update.
- **Reworked the face-down flip flow from fully blind/atomic into "reveal, then optionally add more, then confirm"** (`docs/specs/clear-the-deck.md` section 3's own "Revised after real play" note) after the user reported actually playing a game with a face-down 2, a face-up 2, and a 2 in hand, flipping the face-down 2 blind, and getting no chance to add the other two — exactly the deliberate atomic-validation simplification this spec had originally chosen on its own, without re-confirming with the user, and it read as broken in practice rather than as the intended design. New `$pendingFlipValue` property on `pages::clear-the-deck.play`: once a submitted play includes a face-down flip that resolves to a real, valid value *and* there's at least one other matching card still available in hand/face-up (checked via `array_diff` against what's already selected), the play pauses instead of committing — the acting player alone sees a "You flipped a {label}! Tap any more {label}s to add them — optional — then Confirm" message, the Submit button relabels to "Confirm," and `toggleHandCard()`/`toggleFaceUpCard()` both gained a guard rejecting taps on anything that doesn't match `$pendingFlipValue` while pending (the template renders those as `<x-clear-the-deck-card disabled>`, dimmed and non-interactive, rather than just silently not responding). Untoggling the flipped face-down tile itself while pending backs out of the whole reveal and returns to normal selection — the one addition beyond what was explicitly asked for, since a pending state with no way out otherwise seemed like an obvious follow-up gap. A flip with nothing else to offer, or a second `playCards()` call once already pending, both still resolve in the original one-step atomic way. Six new tests cover entering the pending state without committing, the disabled-card no-op, confirming a combined play, backing out, and confirming with nothing added. Also surfaced (and fixed) a test-fixture gap while writing these: `setSeatCards()` only ever wrote to `RoomSeat` directly, never to `GameSession.state.seatTables`, so `toggleFaceUpCard()`'s own guard — reading `getMyTableFaceUpProperty()`, sourced from broadcast state — saw stale data in tests even though real gameplay always keeps that in sync via `syncOnlineState()`'s `refreshSeatTables()`. Fixed in the fixture, not the app.
- **A TV-cast/screen-share spectator view, mirroring Yahtzee's own "Watch only" toggle** (the user explicitly named it as the model to follow). Added the identical `#[Url] public bool $spectate` property, and widened `getIsSpectatorProperty()` from "unseated connection only" to `$mySeatIndex === null || $spectate`, plus a teleported "Watch only"/"Player view" toggle button (shown only to a seated player) — copied from `pages::yahtzee.play` almost verbatim. `isMyTurnValue()`/`getIsMyTurnProperty()` deliberately stayed untouched (independent of `$spectate`), same reasoning as Yahtzee's own: `playCards()`'s `abort_unless(isMyTurnValue(), 403)` has to keep saying "yes" for a casting device to remain authoritative if the same account is genuinely mid-turn, even while that particular device displays read-only.
- **New spectator layout, not just a re-gated version of the existing one.** Previously, "opponents" (everyone except `$mySeatIndex`) rendered small (`$oppSize`, sized to share the screen with the seated player's own larger "Your columns"/hand below) — fine for a real spectator by accident, since excluding a `null` seat index excludes nobody, but wrong for a self-toggled seated player (their own tableau would vanish from view entirely) and needlessly cramped for a dedicated cast screen. `$this->isSpectator` now branches to a new `.ctd-spectator-grid`: every seat (including the toggling player's own, if seated) at a new, larger `$spectatorSize` (`clamp(50px, 9vw, 110px)`, bigger than `$oppSize` since nothing else competes for vertical room once "Your columns"/hand/Submit are hidden), flex-wrapping onto multiple rows instead of the playing view's single-row horizontal scroll (a TV/shared screen can't scroll, but has room to wrap). The seat whose turn it currently is gets an outlined/background-highlighted wrapper (`.ctd-spectator-seat-active`, the same accent-border-plus-`--die-kept`-background pairing a selected card already uses) and its name recolors to `--accent`, so it reads at a glance from across a room. "Your columns," the hand, and the Submit button are all now also explicitly gated on `! $this->isSpectator` (previously only `$mySeatIndex !== null`/`$this->isMyTurn`), closing the gap where a seated player toggling Watch only mid-own-turn would otherwise have kept seeing their hand and a live Submit button despite the toggle.
- **Two Blade-compilation footguns from the Yahtzee undo/confirm-dialog work (see that section above) were checked for here proactively, not rediscovered the hard way** — every new comment was re-read for a literal `@script`/`<x-...>`-shaped substring before landing, since both are textually scanned by Blade/Livewire's compiler even inside a `{{-- --}}` or JS comment.
- Test coverage: `tests/Feature/Games/ClearTheDeckRoomTest.php` (an unseated visitor always spectates with nothing to toggle; a seated player's `isSpectator`/`isMyTurn` before, during, and after toggling `$spectate` mid-own-turn) and `tests/Browser/ClearTheDeckRoomBrowserTest.php` (every seat's name actually renders for a real spectator with no hand/Submit in the DOM, exactly one seat wrapper carries the active-turn class and it's the right one, and a seated player's Watch-only/Player-view round trip actually shows/hides the real hand and `#play-cards` button, not just the underlying property).

**Spectator grid rework, from "wraps 2-per-row and needs several screens of scrolling" to "always fits one screen, desktop through TV" (`docs/concerns.md`'s Clear the Deck item).** Confirmed live via a seeded 10-player room (the game's own max) screenshotted at 5 realistic viewports before touching anything: `$spectatorSize`'s old `clamp(50px, 9vw, 110px)` picked a card size from viewport *width* alone, with no idea how many seats had to share a row, so at 10 players it only fit 2 tableaus per row and the page ran several screens tall.
- **Root fix: `$spectatorCols`/`$spectatorRows` (PHP, capped at 5 columns -- so 3-10 seats is always 1-2 rows) size `$spectatorSize` from *both* axes at once** via CSS `min()`: a vw-based share (fits `$spectatorCols` tableaus across) and a vh-based share (fits `$spectatorRows` rows down), so whichever axis a given display is actually short on is the one that ends up binding -- confirmed this matters for real: a normal desktop/tablet is width-bound, but a very short 1920x750 "cast" viewport (this app's own screenshot testing skill flagged AirPlay/screen-mirroring as changing the aspect ratio substantially, not just scaling proportionally) is height-bound instead, and only the min() approach handles both without picking one axis and guessing. `.ctd-spectator-grid` switched from `flex-wrap` (content-sized, which was the actual root cause -- it had no way to know 5 columns were intended) to a real CSS grid with `grid-template-columns: repeat($spectatorCols, auto)` set inline per-request.
- **Constants tuned empirically, not derived analytically** -- an early pass computed the width/height budget from `.ctd-tableau`'s own `4.54 * cardWidth` formula and the grid's own gap, undershot by ~20% in practice (cell padding/gap/page-margin overhead this codebase's other clamp() values are *also* always tuned against real screenshots, not hand-derived), then was intentionally pushed back up once every tested viewport showed consistent unused headroom. Verified at 3, 6, and 10 seats (min/mid/max) across 8 viewports total, including 3 deliberate aspect-ratio stress cases (a very short 1920x750, an ultrawide 2560x1080, and a squarish 1024x900) specifically for the AirPlay-changes-the-aspect-ratio concern -- all fit with no scrolling.
- **The pile ("On the table") now renders *above* the seat grid in spectator view specifically** (`docs/concerns.md`: "table cards should be at the top, not the bottom") -- the playing view's own order (opponents row, then pile, then your own columns) is unchanged, since a seated player already has their own hand/columns to read in sequence below the pile; a spectator has neither, so the shared table state is what should be visible first rather than after scrolling past every seat. Implemented as a second, near-identical `.ctd-pile-row`/`.ctd-pile-row-spectator` copy in the spectator branch (duplicated markup, not a shared partial -- ~15 lines, and this codebase doesn't reach for `@include` partials for a single-page-scoped repeat like this) with a much tighter margin, since it's now competing with a whole grid for the same fixed vertical budget instead of being the only thing below a single opponents row.

**Added a host-only manual "End Game" action** (docs/concerns.md) -- previously the only way to end a game was reaching the room's configured round target. `endGameManually()`/`getCanEndGameManuallyProperty()` mirror Farkle's own shape; reuses `finishGame()` unchanged (it already just scores from whatever `$this->scores` holds for *completed* rounds), so ending early simply stops counting further rounds rather than needing a separate scoring path. Left phase-agnostic (works from `Playing` too, not just between rounds) since there's no partial-round score at risk -- an in-progress round's unscored cards just don't count, the same as any other round that never got played.
- **`.ctd-opponent-name`/`.ctd-section-label`'s own `font-size`/`letter-spacing` had been silently broken since the spectator view first shipped** -- `var(--text-label)`/`var(--tracking-label)` were never actually defined anywhere in `app.css` (confirmed via a repo-wide grep). An unresolved `var()` with no fallback on an inherited property doesn't error or use any sensible default; it just falls back to whatever's inherited, which for these was the page's plain, un-styled 16px. Replaced with real `clamp()` values. Separately, spectator names/hand-counts got their own new, deliberately larger classes (`.ctd-spectator-name`, `.ctd-spectator-hand`, replacing a literal Tailwind `text-xs` for the hand count) than the in-hand playing view's own `.ctd-opponent-name` -- matching `docs/concerns.md`'s explicit "names and hand size font are so small they are hard to read" when casting, which the dead-variable bug had been silently causing.
- Regression coverage: two new cases in `tests/Browser/ClearTheDeckRoomBrowserTest.php` (a real 10-player spectator grid has zero `scrollWidth`/`scrollHeight` overflow at a tightest-case tablet viewport; the pile row's DOM position precedes the spectator grid's).

**Generalized the same no-scrolling-while-spectating fix to every other game with a central spectator view (`docs/concerns.md`'s own explicit ask, "this needs to be true for all games").** Investigated Farkle, Yahtzee, Family Feud, and Best Match; found and fixed the same underlying bug class in the first two (see their own sections below for each fix's full detail) -- Family Feud (2-team structure, not an N-seat grid) and Best Match (turn/round-based reveal, no permanent "everyone visible at once" panel) didn't show the same `overflow-y-auto`-scrollable-panel smell on inspection, though neither got the same live-screenshot verification the other three did, so that's a real gap rather than a confirmed clean bill of health -- noted in `docs/TODO.md`.

**The spectator view's current-player highlight was too washed-out to read on a TV (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #16, 2026-08-17).** `.ctd-spectator-seat-active`/`.ctd-opponent-name-active` reused the shared `--accent` token (a moderate, deliberately muted blue -- this app's whole design ethos is low-saturation, see "Site-wide design system & chrome" below) as a thin 2px border plus a barely-lighter background -- fine up close on a monitor, but confirmed via a real screenshot that both the hue *and* lightness contrast against the dark card background are too subtle to read at a glance from across a room, and a 2px border's own visual weight shrinks further with viewing distance regardless of its color.
- **Checked `--accent`'s blast radius before touching anything, per the task's own instruction**: it's used site-wide (a selected Boggle die/order badge, a found word, `option.active`, the controlling Family Feud team, a won game result, plus Clear the Deck's own in-hand `.ctd-card-selected`), so bumping it directly would have changed all of those too, including on ordinary desktop/mobile views this concern was never about. Introduced a new, dedicated `--ctd-active` token instead (`:root`/`.dark`, alongside `--accent`/`--rival-*`), scoped to this one spectator-highlight use only -- darker and more saturated than `--accent` in both themes for stronger simultaneous lightness *and* hue contrast against `--die`/`--bg` (the pairing that actually reads at a distance, not chroma alone), without pushing the shared accent color further from the app's own muted design language anywhere else. Border width also bumped from 2px to 3px -- a border's own weight matters as much as its color once viewed from across a room.
- **A second, color-independent signal was added on top, not instead of the color fix**: a "▸" marker now prefixes the active player's name in `⚡play.blade.php` itself (not a CSS `::before`, so it's real text) -- addresses the same complaint a different way, since a shape-based cue still reads correctly under a poorly color-calibrated TV or for a colorblind viewer, neither of which any color choice alone can fix. Verified together via a real screenshot: the active seat's outline is now clearly visible against the dark background at a glance, not just technically higher-contrast on paper.
- Updated `tests/Browser/ClearTheDeckRoomBrowserTest.php`'s existing active-seat assertion to expect the `▸ ` prefix, and added a companion assertion that no *inactive* seat's name carries it.

**A forced pickup now ends the turn immediately instead of granting an extra one (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #21, 2026-08-18).** A deliberate rule change, not a bug fix — the original design ("Turn resolution is four cases," above) grouped a pickup with the wild-Ten and completed-run cases as one of three outcomes granting the same player an extra turn; only an unremarkable equal-or-lesser play passed the turn. Requested reversal of just the pickup case, leaving the other two untouched.
- `resolvePlay()`'s pickup branch (`playCards()` in `pages::clear-the-deck.play`) now calls a new `finishTurnAdvancingToNextSeat()` instead of `finishTurnWithExtraTurn()` — extracted from what the ordinary-play branch (case 4) already did inline (increment `turnSeatIndex`, check `hasGoneOut()`, sync), since both cases now need the identical turn-advance logic rather than duplicating it. `finishTurnWithExtraTurn()` itself is unchanged and still used by the Wild Ten/completed-run branches.
- `hasGoneOut()`'s check inside the shared method can never actually fire for a pickup specifically (picking up the stack always *adds* cards to the acting seat's hand, so it can never be empty right after) — kept anyway for consistency with the identical call the ordinary-play branch already needed, not because that path can reach it.
- Updated everywhere the old behavior was described: `docs/specs/clear-the-deck.md`'s turn-resolution section 4 (kept as a record of what shipped first, with a note explaining the 2026-08-18 reversal, rather than silently rewritten — the appendix's own verbatim-original-rules text was correctly left untouched, since it was never about this app's specific implementation choice), the in-game rules text (`pages::clear-the-deck.play`), and `tests/Feature/Games/ClearTheDeckRoomTest.php`'s existing pickup test (renamed, and its `turnSeatIndex` assertion changed from staying on the acting seat to advancing to the next one) — updated in place rather than left alongside a new, contradicting test, per the task's own instruction.

## Word Blocks

A solo, untimed word-forming puzzle (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #8), ships under the
display name **"Word Blocks"** — "Quartiles" is NYT Games' trademarked name for this
exact mechanic, same rename treatment as Boggle/Yahtzee/Family Feud/Apples-to-Apples
(see "Game renaming" below). Full build spec, including every resolved design decision:
`docs/specs/quartiles.md`.

- **The one genuinely new problem this game posed: puzzle generation from scratch.**
  Every other game in this app either has a fixed physical ruleset (cards, dice) or, for
  Boggle, a real physical dice set to digitize — this one needed 20 letter-fragment tiles
  built from nothing, 5 of which had to form a hidden "find all of these" bonus group.
  Asked the user directly rather than guessing at generation strategy; two decisions came
  back: **algorithmic, not AI-curated** (pick 5 random 8–16 letter words from the same
  dictionary Boggle's `Solver` already loads, split each into 4 random-length 2–4 letter
  fragments, shuffle into the grid — no new LLM-call infrastructure, matching what the
  user proposed directly), and the display-name rename above.
- `App\Services\Quartiles\PuzzleGenerator` mirrors `Boggle\BoardGenerator`'s seeded
  `Randomizer`/`Mt19937` pattern (`#[Url] $seed` on the play page, unused by any UI yet
  but free reproducibility — positions this for Task 11's future "puzzle codes" work).
  `App\Services\Quartiles\WordFinder` is the harder new piece: since tile selection has
  no spatial-adjacency constraint (unlike Boggle, any subset of 1–4 tiles in any order is
  a legal attempt), "every possible word" is a subset/permutation search (≤123,520
  combinations for 20 tiles) rather than a graph walk — reuses `Boggle\Solver::
  isValidWord()` directly as the leaf dictionary check rather than loading a second
  ~79k-word dictionary, and is only ever run once, at End Game, same "expensive, so cache
  it" precedent as Boggle's own `solve()` call in `finishGame()`.
- **A "Quad" (the 40-point-bonus 4-tile group) is matched by tile-id *set*, not word
  text.** Submitting exactly a designated group's 4 tile ids (any order) marks it found;
  a *different* 4-tile combination that happens to also spell a valid dictionary word
  (entirely possible once 20 real dictionary fragments share a grid) still scores the
  normal 8 points but isn't a Quad — doesn't relocate, recolor, or count toward 5/5. This
  is the only way "found all 5" can mean something specific rather than "found five
  4-tile words." Covered explicitly in `tests/Feature/Games/QuartilesGameTest.php` (a
  word spelled from a different 4-tile combination than its own designated group scores
  but doesn't complete that group).
- **Found-group tiles relocate into their own row and recolor, remaining tiles reshuffle
  — the one CSS-driven behavior here** (`docs/CLAUDE.md`'s "Automated Browser Testing"
  section) a `Livewire::test()` can't actually see, since the underlying `$foundGroups`/
  `$unfoundOrder` arrays changing server-side isn't the same as confirming a found
  group's tiles genuinely move to a distinct DOM position with a different class in a
  real render. `tests/Browser/QuartilesFoundQuartileTest.php` covers it against a
  hand-verified seed (`PuzzleGenerator::generate(1)` puts "dingiest" at group 0, tiles
  0–3) rather than trying to assert against random puzzle content.
- **Real trademark leak, caught by a `grep` sweep, not proactively.** The rename above
  covers the game's own *name*, but a first implementation pass still said "Quartile(s)"
  verbatim throughout rules copy, the HUD tile, the found-group label, the reveal
  screen's answer list, both profile pages' stat labels, and the homepage card
  description — exactly the class of leak `docs/DEVLOG.md`'s own "Game renaming" section
  says to avoid ("what a user can see," not just the top-level name). Caught via `grep -rn
  "Quartile" resources/views/` after visually reviewing a live screenshot and noticing the
  homepage card's own copy still used the word. Replaced everywhere visible with **"Quad"**
  (internal identifiers — `GameType::Quartiles`, `App\Services\Quartiles\*`, `quartiles`/
  `profile.quartiles` route names — deliberately kept, same "not user-facing" rule every
  other renamed game already follows). `tests/Feature/Games/QuartilesGameTest.php` and
  `tests/Feature/WelcomePageTest.php` both gained an explicit `assertDontSee('Quartiles')`
  regression guard rather than just fixing the visible instances and moving on.
- No online-room mode at all (not even opted out of the way Best Match/Clear the Deck
  lack pass-and-play) — `GameType::supportsRooms()` is `false` only for `Quartiles`,
  filtering it out of `<livewire:game-lobby>`'s "switch game" list and `pages::room.show`'s
  "start a game" list, the two places that previously assumed every `GameType::cases()`
  entry could resolve a room route. `roomRoute()`'s own `Quartiles` arm throws a
  `LogicException` instead of returning a route that doesn't exist, so a caller that
  skips the `supportsRooms()` filter fails loudly rather than routing somewhere broken —
  caught by Larastan (`match.unhandled`) the moment the case was added without an arm,
  not discovered at runtime.
- No dedicated stats depth beyond `App\Services\Stats\QuartilesStatsService` (`gamesForUser()`/
  `statsFor()`/`bestMetrics()`, closest to `FarkleStatsService`'s lighter shape rather than
  Boggle's own `topLongestWords()`/`topFrequentWords()` — solo, no opponent, so no
  win-rate concept either) — best-metric tiles are Highest score, Most words found, Most
  Quads found (omitted if nobody's ever found one, same "omit rather than a misleading
  zero" rule every other stats service follows).

**Same-day follow-up: a live "words found" list during play, plus resumable in-progress
games (2026-08-20).** v1 above only ever showed found words at the End Game reveal, and
never persisted anything until then either — a page reload lost all progress, same
limitation Boggle's own solo mode has. Both requested afterward; full design in
`docs/specs/quartiles.md` section 4a.

- A plain alphabetized `$foundWords` list, added below the tile grid during play
  (`getFoundWordsSortedProperty()`, `ksort()` over the existing map) — the underlying
  data already existed, this was pure template work, no new state.
- **Resumable games needed a new nullable `game_sessions.user_id`** — the first time any
  game in this app has needed to look up "this signed-in user's own game" outside of
  `GameParticipant` (which only ever exists for a *finished* game). Every room game
  already identifies players via `room_code`/`RoomSeat`; every other solo game only
  writes its `GameSession` once already over. Scoped narrowly: nullable, used only by
  Word Blocks' in-progress rows, `GameSession::user()` added for completeness.
- **Three decisions confirmed with the user before building** (same "ask, don't guess"
  approach as puzzle generation itself): **Restart** resets progress but keeps the same
  puzzle (the only reading that makes it different from New Game, which discards
  everything); resuming is **signed-in-only** (guests stay exactly as v1 — ephemeral
  accounts already get pruned, nothing durable to resume against later); and visiting the
  page with a saved game **auto-resumes immediately**, no "Continue?" prompt — Restart/
  New Game/End Game are just always-available buttons instead, so nothing is forced on
  the common case of simply continuing.
- **The `GameSession` row now gets created the moment a signed-in user's puzzle is first
  generated**, not deferred to `endGame()` — `state` holds everything needed to fully
  reconstruct play (`tiles`, `groups`, `unfoundOrder`, `foundGroups`, `foundWords`,
  `score`), re-saved after every scoring submission and every shuffle. Deliberately not
  persisted: `selected` (the in-progress tile picks) — resuming clears the current
  selection rather than trying to preserve a half-built word across a real session gap,
  which nobody would expect to survive a logout-and-return-later anyway.
- **At most one in-progress row per signed-in user, enforced at the application layer**,
  not a DB constraint — `startNewSession()` reuses whatever unfinished row already
  exists for that user (abandoning its puzzle) rather than accumulating duplicates when
  New Game/Restart runs, but explicitly *never* touches an already-*finished* row (found
  via `whereNull('ended_at')`) — reusing a finished row for a new puzzle would silently
  rewrite that older game's own recorded stats out from under it. `endGame()`
  correspondingly now `update()`s the existing row for a signed-in user instead of always
  `create()`ing a new one; guests are untouched, still getting a fresh row created only
  at `endGame()`, exactly as v1.
- **Restart doesn't call `PuzzleGenerator` at all** — since the puzzle's tiles/groups are
  already known (either just generated or loaded from a resumed session), restarting is
  just reshuffling `unfoundOrder` from the existing tile ids and zeroing every progress
  field, then persisting that to the same row.
- **An explicit `#[Url] $seed` always wins over auto-resume** — checked first in
  `mount()`, before the resume lookup even runs. A deliberate "play this specific puzzle"
  request (unused by any UI yet, but exactly the mechanism Task 11's future daily-puzzle/
  shared-puzzle-code links will need) generates that puzzle and becomes the new
  in-progress row, rather than being silently overridden by whatever was already saved.
- Covered by 9 new tests in `tests/Feature/Games/QuartilesGameTest.php`: early session
  creation for a signed-in user vs. none at all for a guest, progress actually saved
  after a scoring submission and after a shuffle, a full leave-and-revisit cycle resuming
  the exact saved puzzle (not a regenerated one — same tile count either way, so the
  assertion checks a specific tile's content, not just `count()`), Restart keeping the
  same puzzle while zeroing progress, New Game reusing vs. correctly *not* reusing a row
  depending on whether it's already finished, and an explicit seed overriding resume.

**Real bug, reported and correctly diagnosed by the user: a found Quad sometimes didn't
register.** Nothing in `PuzzleGenerator` prevented two independently-split words from
producing the same fragment text (e.g. two different tiles both showing "ER") — and
since a Quad is matched by exact tile *id*, not text (section 3 of the spec), two
tiles with identical text are genuinely ambiguous to look at. Clicking the
visually-right-but-actually-wrong one of the pair still spelled the correct word (scored
normally) but silently never matched the intended group's own tile-id set. The user's own
diagnosis — "are the tiles linked to the quads... maybe I'm clicking the wrong tile" —
and their own proposed fix — "it might be better if all of the tiles were unique" — were
both exactly right, so that's what got built, at generation time rather than patched
around in the UI: `attemptUniqueFragments()` now rejects any attempt with a colliding
fragment and retries (consuming more randomness from the same seeded `Randomizer`, so a
given seed still always produces the same eventual puzzle) up to 100 times before giving
up with a `RuntimeException`. Verified two ways in `tests/Unit/Quartiles/
PuzzleGeneratorTest.php`: 200 seeds against the *real* dictionary asserting no puzzle
ever has a duplicate tile (the actual conditions the bug was found under), and a
deterministic case (five 8-letter, single-repeated-character candidate words, e.g.
`"aaaaaaaa"`) where the *only* legal 4-fragment split is forced to collide with itself
every single time, proving the retry loop's own exhaustion path throws rather than
silently returning a colliding puzzle.

**Two more `docs/notes.md` follow-ups: dropped the "Quad found" label, and a two-column
desktop layout.** The per-found-row text label (`<p>Quad found</p>`) was removed outright
per the request — the found row's own distinct color/border plus its position, pinned to
its own row at the top of the grid, remain as the non-color signal the spec's
accessibility section calls for (section 5), just without the redundant caption repeated
once per Quad. Separately, the grid+controls and the found-words list used to be one
single stacked column capped at `max-w-md` regardless of viewport, so a longer found-
words list pushed the page tall enough to need scrolling even on a wide desktop screen
with plenty of horizontal room going unused. Now `lg:flex-row` (grid on the left, found-
words list on the right, `lg:sticky lg:top-24` so the list stays in view while scrolling
the grid) — collapses back to the original single stacked column below that breakpoint,
where there's no room for two columns side by side anyway. `tests/Browser/
QuartilesFoundQuartileTest.php`'s hand-verified seed-1 fixture word changed from
"dingiest" to "carjacker" as a side effect of `attemptUniqueFragments()`'s own retry loop
consuming randomness differently than the original generator did — re-verified live
(`PuzzleGenerator::generate(1)`) rather than assumed still correct.

**Restart removed; New Game only shown once the game has ended.** Restart (section 4a of
the spec: same puzzle, zero progress) turned out to have no real use once New Game is
always one click away — there's no case where replaying the identical puzzle from
scratch beats just starting a fresh one. Removed entirely: the button, the
`restartGame()` Livewire method, its dedicated test, and the mentions in this file and
`docs/specs/quartiles.md` (superseded by that doc's own section 4b rather than rewritten
in place, consistent with how the rest of this log treats past decisions). Separately,
New Game's button is now conditioned on `$ended` and only rendered in the post-game
reveal state, rather than being available throughout play — with Restart gone, showing
"abandon this puzzle" as an option before a player has had a chance to finish it no
longer served a purpose. `docs/specs/quartiles.md` section 4b has the full reasoning.

**Daily puzzles, puzzle-code sharing, and Challenge a Friend (`docs/TODO.md`'s Task 11, 2026-08-21), scoped to Boggle and Word Blocks only — Task 10's NYT-style games don't exist yet.**
- **Puzzle-code sharing**: `#[Url] public ?int $seed` had already been wired into `mount()`/`GameSession.settings` since the very first version of this game (see above), specifically anticipating this task, but was never surfaced in the UI. Wiring it up turned out to need nothing more than a copy-link box on the reveal screen (`route('quartiles.play', ['seed' => $resolvedSeed])`, reusing the `[data-copy]` delegated click handler `resources/js/app.js` already had for Boggle's own share link) — no new route, unlike Boggle's `/boggle/share/{gameSession}` redirect, since Word Blocks has no other per-session parameters (board size, timer) to hide behind one; the seed alone is already the complete puzzle identity.
- **Daily puzzles**: new `App\Services\Puzzles\DailyPuzzle::seedFor(GameType, CarbonInterface)` (`crc32("{$type->value}-{$date->toDateString()}")`) derives a deterministic seed from a game type + date, shared with Boggle. Deliberately **no new `daily_puzzles` table** — every puzzle here was already just an `int $seed` fed to a generator, so "today's puzzle" needs no persisted row of its own, only a pure function of the date. `#[Url] public ?string $dailyDate` drives a new `mountDaily()` branch, checked before the existing seed/resume logic in `mount()`. **Only *today's* date is locked to one signed-in play per day** — mirrors this game's own existing `resumeInProgressGame()`/`startNewSession()`/`persistState()` machinery (an early `GameSession` row at puzzle-generation time, not deferred to game end), with new `findDailySession()`/`startDailySession()` counterparts scoped by `is_daily`/`daily_date`. A guest, or any date that isn't today (an archive entry), is just an ordinary seeded puzzle instead — no early row, no lock, freely replayable, matching Wordle-style archive UX.
- **A real scoping bug caught during planning, not after**: with dailies added, a signed-in user can legitimately have *two* separate in-progress rows at once (their regular random game and today's daily). `resumeInProgressGame()`/`startNewSession()` originally scoped "this user's one in-progress row" by `(user_id, type, whereNull(ended_at))` only — both gained a `->where('is_daily', false)` clause so they can never see or clobber the daily row, and vice versa. Regression-covered directly (`tests/Feature/Games/QuartilesDailyTest.php`'s "in-progress random game and in-progress daily coexist" test).
- **`daily_date` is a deliberately uncast plain string on `GameSession`, not Eloquent's `date` cast** — caught by a failing test, not assumed correct: Eloquent's `date` cast reformats to a full `Y-m-d H:i:s` on *write* (it only truncates to date-only on *read*), so under sqlite's dynamic typing (used by the test suite) the stored value never matched a bare `->where('daily_date', $date->toDateString())` equality lookup, silently creating a second session row every time instead of finding the first. Real MariaDB's `DATE` column type would have masked this (it truncates on insert regardless of what Eloquent sends), meaning this would have worked in production while quietly failing in every sqlite-backed test — fixed by dropping the cast entirely rather than switching to `whereDate()`, so the exact same `Y-m-d` string is written and compared everywhere, no DB-driver-dependent behavior either way.
- **Reveal screen**: `$dailyDate === today` renders a new shared `<x-daily-countdown>` component (plain Alpine, no `$wire`) instead of the copy-link box, counting down to the next UTC-midnight reset — covers both a just-finished today's daily and a restored, already-done one in the same check, rather than only the latter. The `New Game`/`End Game` teleported button bar gained a matching branch: `$dailyDate !== null` links to the new `/daily-puzzles` archive instead of offering a reroll (a daily's board is fixed for the day, same reasoning Boggle already applies to a room/challenge game).
- **Challenge a Friend**: new `App\Notifications\GameChallengeReceived` + `App\Services\Social\GameChallengeService` (modeled directly on `FriendRequestReceived`/`FriendService::sendRequest()`, same guest-recipient guard) send a real queued email via a shared `<livewire:challenge-friend>` component (friend-picker `<x-dialog>`, `@auth`-gated, hidden for a daily puzzle same as the copy-link box). Word Blocks' own link is the plain independent seed link from above — unlike Boggle's async challenge, there's no shared/ranked `GameSession` to join here, so a friend just gets an identical, unranked replay. `GameType::isChallengeable()` gates which games get this button at all (Boggle and Word Blocks today).
- Migration adds `is_daily`/`daily_date` to the existing `game_sessions` table (shared with Boggle, see its own DEVLOG entry above for the column details) plus a composite `(user_id, type, is_daily, daily_date)` index — no changes needed to `game_participants` or any other table.
- New shared `/daily-puzzles` route/page (`pages::daily-puzzles`) lists the last 30 days with a link into each game's own `?dailyDate=...`, built once and reused by both games rather than duplicated — see Boggle's own DEVLOG entry above for why it's one page, not two.
- Tests: `tests/Unit/Puzzles/DailyPuzzleTest.php` (same game+date always the same seed; a different date or game type differs), `tests/Feature/Games/QuartilesGameTest.php` (the reveal screen's copy-link box), `tests/Feature/Games/QuartilesDailyTest.php` (deterministic puzzle for a date; early row tagged `is_daily`; a finished daily restores read-only on reload; an in-progress daily resumes; the random/daily coexistence regression test; a past date is always freely replayable; the New Game button links to the archive; the countdown replaces the copy-link box), `tests/Feature/Games/QuartilesChallengeFriendTest.php` (button visibility including the daily exclusion, notification recipient/link), `tests/Feature/Puzzles/DailyPuzzlesArchiveTest.php` (30 days listed, reachable by guests), `tests/Browser/QuartilesChallengeFriendDialogTest.php` (real click-through: open, send, confirm).

**App-wide button-padding sizing audit, and a real fix here (`docs/TODO.md`'s former Task
31, 2026-08-23).** Word Hive's own build (see its section below) found and fixed a
`<button>`-specific bug live — a `<button>`'s intrinsic minimum size, driven by its own
padding/content, silently overrides a smaller explicit `w-*`/`aspect-square` Tailwind
size, with no visual error and no class-based test able to catch it — and flagged that
this game's own `.quartile-tile` buttons were a confirmed, unfixed instance of the exact
same bug (a live tile measured 99px wide instead of the intended 56px square). Auditing
every `<button>` element app-wide (grepping `aspect-square`/fixed `w-*`/`h-*` classes,
then checking each hit's actual tag) found only two real instances, both already
identified: this game's two `.quartile-tile` buttons and Word Hive's two hex-letter
buttons. Every other fixed-size clickable tile in the app (Wordle's guess grid, its
profile history tiles, Yahtzee/Farkle's dice, Family Feud's answer tiles, Clear the
Deck's cards) is a `<div>` with a `wire:click`, not a `<button>`, so none are affected —
confirmed directly per game rather than assumed from one example.
- **Fixed with a new shared `.tile-btn` class (`padding: 0`, `app.css`), not another
  ad hoc `p-0` utility** — same shape as the existing `.icon-btn` class solves for
  icon-only buttons, so any future button-based tile grid (Pips' domino cells, per
  `docs/TODO.md`'s Task 10) has a ready-made class instead of re-discovering this bug.
  Word Hive's own two buttons were switched from their original inline `p-0` fix to the
  same shared class, so the fix isn't split across two different mechanisms for the
  identical problem.
- **`padding: 0` alone wasn't sufficient here, unlike Word Hive** — this game's tiles had
  no explicit `w-*` class at all (`aspect-square min-h-14` only), so with padding removed
  the button's width was still governed by the grid item's own stretch-to-fill-column
  sizing (~99px in a 4-column grid), with `aspect-square` then matching height to that,
  same wrong-size symptom via a different mechanism than the padding-driven one Word Hive
  hit. Needed an explicit `w-14` alongside `min-h-14` (matching Word Hive's own outer
  letter buttons) so the grid item has a definite width instead of stretching. Caught by
  the same `getBoundingClientRect()` geometry check, not assumed fixed after adding the
  class alone — the first attempt (`.tile-btn` with no `w-14`) still measured 99px live.
- New `tests/Browser/QuartilesTileSizingTest.php` asserts a real tile's rendered
  `getBoundingClientRect()` is exactly 56×56px, the same geometry-check pattern
  `tests/Browser/SpellingBeeLetterTapTest.php` already established.

**Applied `CommonWordFilter` to answer-word selection (`docs/TODO.md`'s former Task 20, 2026-08-26).** `PuzzleGenerator::pickWords()` originally drew its 5 answer words uniformly from every 8–16 letter dictionary word, obscurity and all — `docs/specs/quartiles.md` section 1 already flagged "an occasional one of the 5 answer words may be obscure" as a known rough edge. Wired in the same `App\Services\Words\CommonWordFilter` Wordle's `AnswerPicker` already uses: `PuzzleGenerator`'s constructor now takes a `CommonWordFilter` and calls `mostCommon($candidates, self::ANSWER_POOL_SIZE)` on the loaded word list before anything else touches it, so every downstream pick already comes from the curated pool.
- **Didn't reuse `AnswerPicker`'s `ANSWER_POOL_SIZE = 1500` verbatim.** That number was tuned against Wordle's own ~4,800 5-letter candidates for a single answer the whole puzzle rests on; Word Blocks' pool spans 8–16 letters across ~36k frequency-corpus-matched candidates, and a flat 1500-word cap would have left the 16-letter end of that range with single-digit choices. Measured empirically instead (`php -r` sampling frequency ranks against the real `storage/app/word_frequency.txt`, not guessed): rank-8000–20000 words (`cytoplasmic`, `sympathize`, `goaltending`) still read as recognizable rather than obscure, and a **12,000**-word cap keeps every fragment length from 8 through 16 letters meaningfully represented (23 sixteen-letter words survive at that cutoff vs. 10 at 8,000) while still cutting the truly obscure tail.
- `PuzzleGenerator`'s constructor now throws `RuntimeException` if the common-word filter leaves zero candidates (mirroring `AnswerPicker`'s own empty-pool guard) — fails loudly at construction instead of `pickWords()` later throwing a confusing "not enough candidate words" `InvalidArgumentException` for an unrelated-looking reason.
- **Test fallout was the bulk of this change.** `tests/Unit/Quartiles/PuzzleGeneratorTest.php` doesn't boot the Laravel framework at all (`tests/Pest.php` only extends `TestCase` for Feature/Browser suites), so its made-up word fixtures (`backpack`, `chocolate`, ...; the repeated-character `aaaaaaaa`-style collision fixture) would have been silently filtered down to nothing by a real `CommonWordFilter` pointed at the app's actual frequency corpus. Added a `commonWordFilterFor(array $words)` helper that builds a throwaway frequency file marking exactly the fixture's own words as common, so each test's `CommonWordFilter` treats its small made-up vocabulary as the whole common-word universe rather than filtering it against real English frequency data. The one test that *should* exercise the real corpus (`'no two tiles ever share the same text'`, run across 200 seeds) was pointed at the real `storage/app/word_frequency.txt`/`excluded_words.txt` explicitly, matching how it already used the real `storage/app/words.txt`. Added a new `'throws if no candidate word survives the common-word filter'` test for the empty-pool guard above.

## Wordle

A solo, daily/random 5-letter word-guessing puzzle (`docs/TODO.md` #10, the first of five NYT-style games in that task), ships under the display name **"Five Letters"** — "Wordle" is NYT Games' trademarked name, same category as Boggle/Yahtzee/Family Feud/Quartiles/Apples-to-Apples (see "Game renaming" below). Full build spec, including every resolved design decision: `docs/specs/wordle.md`.

- **Design decisions resolved collaboratively before any code was written**, same "propose, get approval" process as every other renamed/spec'd game: hard mode shipped as a v1 toggle rather than deferred; sharing relies solely on this app's existing challenge-link mechanism, not a second NYT-style spoiler-free emoji-grid format; the classic 5-letter/6-guess shape was kept as-is; and the display name ("Five Letters," proposed alongside "Word Guess"/"Letter Logic") was picked by the user.
- **The answer list needed a new, deliberately reusable piece of infrastructure: `App\Services\Words\CommonWordFilter`**, backed by a new `storage/app/word_frequency.txt` asset (~61k words, `word\tcount`) — the intersection of this app's existing `storage/app/words.txt` dictionary with Peter Norvig's `count_1w.txt` (aggregated from the Google Web Trillion Word Corpus, `norvig.com/ngrams`), so every entry here is already a word this app considers valid. `Wordle\AnswerPicker` filters `words.txt`'s 5-letter subset down to `CommonWordFilter`'s top 1,500 most-common (roughly NYT's own possible-answer list size) before ever picking a daily/random answer — the full, unfiltered dictionary remains the valid-*guess* check (via the existing `Boggle\Solver::isValidWord()`, reused directly, no second dictionary load). Built game-agnostic on purpose, living in its own `App\Services\Words` namespace rather than inside `Wordle\`, since the user explicitly asked for it to be reused for Word Blocks' own answer-word selection later — tracked as follow-up work in `docs/TODO.md`, deliberately out of scope for this game's own build.
- **The one genuinely new secrecy problem this game posed: the answer must never reach the client, for *every* player, not just signed-in ones.** Unlike Boggle/Word Blocks (nothing about a solo puzzle's own content is ever secret from the one person playing it), Wordle's entire puzzle *is* one secret word. Resolved the same way Best Match/Clear the Deck's per-seat secrecy works (`docs/DEVLOG.md`'s own sections on both): the answer is never a public Livewire property, resolved fresh from `GameSession.settings['answer']` inside `submitGuess()` only, via a new `resolveAnswer()` — with `$answerReveal` populated *only* by `finishGame()`, once revealing it is actually correct.
- **This forced a real architectural split from Word Blocks' own daily/session lifecycle, not just a copy-paste.** Word Blocks' guest players get no early `GameSession` row at all — nothing to hide, so nothing needs persisting until `endGame()`. Wordle's guests *do* need an early row for an **ordinary** (non-daily) game, since there's no public seed the answer could be regenerated from on demand (a deliberate choice — see the spec's own reasoning for why an ordinary game's seed, unlike Boggle/Word Blocks', is never exposed at all). A **daily** game is the one case that still needs no row for a guest: the answer is always recomputable from `$dailyDate` itself via `DailyPuzzle::seedFor()`, so `resolveAnswer()` branches on `$this->gameSessionId !== null` (true everywhere except guest-daily) rather than on daily/ordinary or guest/signed-in directly.
- **Async "Challenge a Friend" mirrors Boggle's shared/ranked-session model, not Word Blocks' plain seed link** — `route('wordle.share', ['gameSession' => $session->id])` redirects into `wordle.play?challenge={id}`, and the friend's guesses resolve the *same* stored `settings.answer`, never a regenerated one (Word Blocks' own seed-link approach was never an option here, since exposing a seed would let a determined client reconstruct the answer offline — the whole reason no ordinary-game seed is public at all). `GameType::isChallengeable()` gained a `Wordle` case; `GameChallengeService::linkFor()` gained a matching arm.
- **A shared/challenge session's `state` column is deliberately never written to from an in-progress guess** (`persistState()` no-ops when `$isSharedSession`) — unlike an ordinary or daily row (exclusively written by one identity), a `$challenge`-joined session can have multiple different players' components pointed at the same row; letting each one persist their own in-progress guesses there would let them silently clobber each other's. Each player's own guesses live in their own `GameParticipant.stats` instead, populated only at `finishGame()`.
- **Real bug caught by the test suite, not proactively: `joinChallenge()` set `$this->gameSessionId` *before* calling `resetProgress()`, which itself unconditionally resets `gameSessionId` back to `null`.** `resolveAnswer()` then silently fell through to its guest-daily branch (`Carbon::parse($this->dailyDate)` with a null `$dailyDate`, which `Carbon::parse()` happily parses as "now" rather than erroring), deriving a *different* word than the one actually stored on the joined session — a friend's guesses were being evaluated against the wrong answer entirely. `tests/Feature/Games/WordleChallengeTest.php`'s own guess-evaluation assertions caught this immediately (a guess of the session's real stored answer wasn't reported as a win). Fixed by reordering `joinChallenge()` to call `resetProgress()` first, before setting any of the fields it would otherwise wipe out.
- **Hard mode**: `GuessEvaluator::mergeHardModeRequirements()`/`hardModeViolation()` track a running per-position pin (`positions[i]`, set once a letter's ever been revealed correct there) and a per-letter minimum count (`minCounts[letter]`, the highest correct+present count any single previous guess ever revealed for that letter) — folded in after every accepted guess, checked before the next one is. A toggle, locked once `$guesses !== []` (both client-disabled and server-guarded in `toggleHardMode()`), so a mid-game switch can't retroactively invalidate already-made guesses.
- **Scoring, since this app's `GameParticipant.score` needs an integer and Wordle has no natural point total**: `(MAX_GUESSES + 1 - guessCount) * 100` on a win (600/500/400/300/200/100 for 1–6 guesses), `0` on a loss — simple, monotonically rewards fewer guesses, gives `WordleStatsService::bestMetrics()`'s "Highest score" tile something meaningful. `result` (`GameResult::Win`/`Loss`) is deliberately never set, matching Word Blocks' own solo convention (Boggle's shared-session-only ranking doesn't apply here either) — win/loss lives in `stats.won` instead.
- **Duplicate-letter evaluation** (`GuessEvaluator::evaluate()`) is NYT's own two-pass rule: exact-position matches claim their letter from the answer's pool first, then remaining guessed letters are checked against whatever's left — a guess that over-repeats a letter the answer only contains once (or not at all) doesn't over-credit it as "present" more times than it actually occurs. Directly unit-tested with hand-verified letter-by-letter cases (`tests/Unit/Wordle/GuessEvaluatorTest.php`), not just spot-checked, after getting two of the hand-computed expected values wrong on the first pass — an easy mistake to make manually.
- **On-screen keyboard + tile grid, both pure Tailwind utility classes, no new `app.css` rules** — same "no bespoke CSS needed" precedent Word Blocks' own `.quartile-tile` classes set (styling comes entirely from inline utilities; the class names are just DOM hooks). A small non-color mark (✓ for correct, ~ for present) is layered on each tile, and the on-screen keyboard recolors per letter's best-known status (`getKeyboardStatusProperty()`, correct beats present beats absent regardless of guess order) — this app's existing "never rely on color alone" rule, already applied throughout the design system.
- Test coverage: `tests/Unit/Words/CommonWordFilterTest.php`, `tests/Unit/Wordle/{AnswerPickerTest,GuessEvaluatorTest}.php`, `tests/Unit/Stats/WordleStatsServiceTest.php`; `tests/Feature/Games/WordleGameTest.php` (guess evaluation, win/loss, hard mode, the answer never appearing on any public property pre-reveal, the guest-gets-an-early-row divergence from Word Blocks); `tests/Feature/Games/WordleDailyTest.php` (deterministic daily answer across users/guests, the signed-in lock/resume, guest-daily's own no-row behavior, the reveal countdown); `tests/Feature/Games/WordleChallengeTest.php` (share-link redirect, shared-answer resolution, the state-clobbering guard, the Challenge a Friend button/notification); `tests/Feature/ProfileWordleTest.php`; `tests/Browser/WordleGuessTileBrowserTest.php` (real per-tile correct/present/absent CSS classes and on-screen keyboard recoloring, the bug class `CLAUDE.md`'s "Automated Browser Testing" section flags a Livewire-only test can't catch).

**Added an always-visible color/mark legend above the grid (2026-08-22, user-reported: nothing on the page ever explained what a tile's color or corner mark actually meant).** Previously the only explanation was a line of prose inside the Rules dialog ("correct (right spot), present (in the word, wrong spot), or absent") — easy to miss entirely, and it never even mentioned the ✓/~ marks. `tileClasses()`/`tileMark()` were pulled out of the grid and keyboard's own inline `@php match` blocks into shared public methods on the component (previously duplicated verbatim between the two), so the new legend, the grid, and the keyboard now all render from the exact same single source of truth and can't silently drift out of sync with each other.

**Added a sensitive-word exclusion list (2026-08-23, user-reported: a daily answer of "slave" wasn't obscure — it's plenty common — but was off-putting to see as a puzzle answer).** `CommonWordFilter` only ever screened for *obscurity* (frequency in a web corpus), which doesn't screen for content that's common but distressing. Rather than a Wordle-only fix, added the exclusion check directly inside `CommonWordFilter::mostCommon()` against a new hand-curated `storage/app/excluded_words.txt` (slurs, profanity, and words tied to slavery, violence, sexual content, self-harm) — the same choke point `Wordle\AnswerPicker` already routes every candidate through, so Word Blocks' still-pending integration (`docs/TODO.md`) inherits the exclusion for free once it's wired up, with no separate mechanism to keep in sync. Exclusion is checked before ranking, not after truncating to the pool size, so a blocked word never displaces another candidate's spot in the returned pool.
- **Real bug this surfaced, unrelated to the legend's own logic: the tile grid had been rendering at the wrong size all along.** A live screenshot check (prompted by wanting to actually eyeball the new legend, not just check class-name strings the existing browser test already asserted) showed the whole 6×5 grid collapsed to a thin sliver — `getBoundingClientRect()` confirmed a `w-14` (56px) tile was actually rendering at **4px**, just its own border width with zero content box. Root cause: `w-14`/`w-12`/`w-5`/`h-5`/`aspect-square`/`border-dashed` and a few other utility classes used throughout this game's Blade files had never appeared anywhere else in the app, and `npm run build` was never run after adding them — Tailwind v4's content-scanning build never saw them, so they were silently absent from the shipped CSS. `tests/Browser/WordleGuessTileBrowserTest.php`'s own assertions (`className` containing `bg-warn/20`, etc.) still passed the whole time, since checking a class *name* doesn't prove the CSS that class depends on was actually compiled and loaded — only a real geometry check (`getBoundingClientRect()`) or an actual screenshot catches this class of bug. Fixed by running `npm run build`; see the "Automated browser testing" section below and this session's own memory update for the general rule this establishes.

**Moved Hard Mode from an in-game toggle to a pre-game setup screen (`docs/TODO.md`'s former Task 28, 2026-08-26).** Hard Mode used to be a button sitting above the grid, disabled (but still visible) once the first guess was made — read as broken rather than intentional, per the original `docs/concerns.md` report. Every other game with a real pre-game choice (Yahtzee's player count/Undo, Farkle's own setup) already gates on a `$started`-style flag with a dedicated `.setup`/`.setup-row`/`.option-group` screen, so this brought Wordle in line with that shape rather than inventing a new pattern.
- **Bigger than the toggle move alone: `mount()` no longer starts a plain new game immediately.** Previously `mount()` unconditionally called `startNewGame()` (picking a word, creating the early `GameSession` row) the instant the page loaded, with Hard Mode only togglable afterward but before the first guess. Now a plain "brand new solo game" (no daily, no challenge, no resumable session) leaves `$started` false and renders the setup screen instead; a new `startGame(AnswerPicker, DailyPuzzle)` action — bound to the setup screen's own button — is the only thing that actually calls `startNewGame()` now, once Hard Mode is chosen.
- **Every other mount path sets `$started = true` immediately, skipping the setup screen entirely** — joining a challenge link, resuming a signed-in user's own in-progress ordinary game, and restoring an existing (in-progress or finished) today's daily all already have their Hard Mode fixed by whatever session they're rejoining, so there's no real "before you begin" decision left to make for any of them (mirrors Yahtzee's own setup screen only ever appearing for a fresh local start, never when resuming a room-hosted game in progress).
- **A first-time attempt at today's daily (or a guest/past-date daily, which never gets an early row at all) also routes through the setup screen** — `mountDaily()` was split so it only *restores* an already-existing today's session; `startGame()` itself now does the "create the early row" step for today's signed-in case (mirroring `startNewGame()`'s own shape) once Hard Mode is confirmed. The daily badge (`Daily Puzzle — ...`) was deliberately pulled outside the `$started` gate so a player still sees which puzzle they're about to start before committing.
- **`resetGame()` (the "New Game" button) now returns to the setup screen instead of immediately starting a fresh game** — `$started = false` rather than an inline `startNewGame()` call, so Hard Mode is a real choice again on every replay, not just the very first visit. `$hardMode` itself is left untouched by `resetProgress()` (unchanged from before this task), so the setup screen reopens pre-filled with whatever was last chosen rather than resetting to Off.
- **`toggleHardMode()`'s guard changed from `$this->guesses !== []` to `$this->started`** — a strict superset of the old protection (a resumed/joined/daily session with zero guesses made yet used to still accept a client-spoofed toggle call; now any already-`$started` session rejects it outright), and the only guard that actually matters once there's no in-game control left to click anyway.
- **Closed a gap the redesign would otherwise have opened**: `pressKey()`/`backspace()`/`submitGuess()` didn't previously check anything but `$ended`. Once `mount()` stopped guaranteeing a session/word exists by the time the page renders, a spoofed action call before `startGame()` would have hit `resolveAnswer()`'s guest-daily fallback with a null `$dailyDate` — `Carbon::parse(null)` silently resolves to "now" rather than throwing, deriving a nonsense answer instead of erroring loudly. All three now also guard on `! $this->started`.
- Test fallout: nearly every existing test in `WordleGameTest.php`/`WordleChallengeTest.php`/`WordleDailyTest.php` that exercised the plain or first-daily-attempt flow needed an explicit `->call('startGame')` inserted after mounting, since mounting alone no longer starts anything for those paths — challenge-join and already-existing-daily-session tests were unaffected, since those paths still set `$started` immediately. New coverage: a Feature test confirming actions are no-ops before `$started`, and `tests/Browser/WordleSetupScreenTest.php` (Pest browser test, not just `Livewire::test()`) driving the real setup screen — clicking the Hard Mode option and Start Game, confirming the old in-game toggle element is gone entirely from the rendered page.

**Added tap-to-edit-a-single-letter in the current guess (`docs/TODO.md`'s former Task 29, 2026-08-26).** Guess input was purely append/truncate before this — `pressKey()` always appended to the end of `$currentGuess`, `backspace()` always removed the last character, and the grid cells themselves had no click handler at all, so fixing an early typo meant backspacing through every letter typed after it. Added a new `?int $selectedCol` property (null = the original "append at the end" behavior) and a `selectCell(int $col)` action bound to each in-progress-row tile's `wire:click`.
- **`pressKey()` branches on whether a column is selected**: with one active, it overwrites that exact index in `$currentGuess` (via `mb_str_split()`/reassign/`implode()`, which handles both a true mid-word overwrite and an append-at-exactly-the-current-length in one code path — no special-casing needed) and auto-advances `$selectedCol` to the next column, matching a typical PIN-entry/OTP-input feel. Auto-advance is purely index-arithmetic (`$selectedCol + 1 < WORD_LENGTH`), not based on the string's own length — an early attempt compared against `mb_strlen($currentGuess) < WORD_LENGTH` instead, which broke the very case this feature exists for: overwriting a letter in an *already-fully-typed* 5-letter guess never changes the string's length, so that check was always false and immediately dropped the selection back to null instead of advancing to the next column. Caught by a Feature test overwriting two consecutive columns in one already-complete guess, not by inspection.
- **`selectCell()` only accepts an already-typed column, or exactly the next untyped one** (`$col <= mb_strlen($currentGuess)`) — tapping further ahead into blank cells has nothing to select yet, since `$currentGuess` is a plain string with no concept of a "gap." `backspace()` unconditionally clears `$selectedCol` too — once a character shifts out from under a specific-column selection, "keep editing this exact position" no longer means anything, so it falls back to plain append rather than risk overwriting the wrong letter next keystroke. Both `submitGuess()` (on a successful/rejected submission) and `resetProgress()` also clear it, so a stale selection never survives into the next guess or the next game.
- **Selection UI reuses Quartiles' own `ring-2 ring-accent` convention** for a selected tile (`⚡quartiles/play.blade.php`'s own tile-selection styling) rather than inventing a new visual language for the same concept.
- **A real Pest-browser gotcha hit while writing `tests/Browser/WordleTapToEditTileTest.php`, distinct from anything in `CLAUDE.md` already**: reading `document.querySelector(...).textContent` via `script()` immediately after a `click()` that triggers a Livewire round trip can race that request and read pre-update DOM — unlike `assertSee()`/`assertPresent()`, which poll until the expectation is met, a raw `script()` call has no such wait built in. Confirmed live (`vendor/bin/pest --agent=...` probe): reading grid-tile text right after `click('#key-x')` returned the pre-overwrite letter almost every time, while the same read after an explicit `wait(0.3)` reliably reflected the update.
