A second, expected gotcha also surfaced: reading `#wordle-input`'s own DOM `.value` (rather than the grid tiles' text) never reflected `pressKey()`'s server-side change at all, *even with* a wait — Livewire deliberately leaves a deferred (non-`.live`) `wire:model` input's live DOM value alone across a round trip triggered by a *different* element, to avoid clobbering whatever a user might be actively typing; only the grid tiles (which have no such binding and fully re-render every request) are a reliable signal for this kind of test.
- Test coverage: `tests/Feature/Games/WordleGameTest.php` gained cases for overwrite-in-place, multi-column auto-advance, the "next untyped column" append-equivalent case, `selectCell()` rejecting out-of-range columns, `backspace()` clearing the selection, and the pre-`$started` no-op guard; `tests/Browser/WordleTapToEditTileTest.php` drives a real click on a real rendered tile followed by a real on-screen-keyboard click, confirming the actual DOM wiring (not just the underlying Livewire method) end-to-end.

## Spec resolution: Word Hive (Spelling Bee) and Pips (2026-08-23)

`docs/TODO.md` Task 10's two skeleton specs (`docs/specs/spelling-bee.md`,
`docs/specs/pips.md`) went from "not ready to build from" to full build specs — no
implementation yet, just the design-decision pass every other game in this app got
before building started (`docs/specs/clear-the-deck.md`'s own precedent). Four decisions
genuinely needed a product call rather than a technical default, confirmed with the user
before writing either spec: Spelling Bee's candidate words route through the same
`CommonWordFilter`/`excluded_words.txt` exclusion list Wordle just got (this game
surfaces *every* valid word as a target, not just one secret answer, so an off-putting
word is a bigger exposure here); the new display name is **"Word Hive"** (keeps the
hive/hexagon visual theme without any bee-specific trademark language, which also
answered the rank-ladder naming question — no "Queen Bee," a new "Hive Master" instead);
Pips gets true drag-and-drop built *together with* a full tap-to-select/tap-to-place
alternative, not one built first and the other bolted on later; and Pips gets a real
solution-uniqueness solver for v1 rather than accepting non-unique puzzles as a launch
trade-off.

**That last decision is the one with a real architectural consequence, caught while
writing the spec rather than decided upfront:** a uniqueness solver makes generation
expensive enough (a full board fill, region growth, and a second-solution search,
potentially retried several times) that redoing it on every request for the same seed —
fine for Wordle/Spelling Bee's plain dictionary-pick generators — isn't viable anymore.
Pips therefore needed its own new persisted `pips_puzzles` table (generated once per
seed, cached from then on) instead of the zero-persistence `DailyPuzzle::seedFor()`
model every other game in this task uses, including its own sibling task Spelling Bee.
Despite that, nothing about a Pips board is ever secret from the player the way Wordle's
answer is — a solution row exists only to make the expensive generation step reusable
and to back a future "reveal solution" hint, not to hide anything during play; board
completion is checked live against each region's own rule, never by comparing to a
stored solution directly, so the persisted-table need is purely a performance answer,
not a secrecy one.

Also new: `CommonWordFilter::excluding(array $words): array`, a sibling to the existing
`mostCommon()` — exclusion-only, no frequency requirement or limit, since Spelling
Bee needs the *full* set of words a given 7-letter combination supports (not a
top-N-by-commonness subset the way Wordle's answer pool is) with only the sensitive-word
list applied on top.

## Word Hive

Built from `docs/specs/spelling-bee.md`'s full spec (2026-08-23), the first of Task 10's
two remaining games (Pips is still outstanding). Ships under the display name **"Word
Hive"** — see the spec resolution entry above for why. `App\Services\SpellingBee\
PuzzleGenerator` picks a pangram (7-distinct-letter word) from `storage/app/words.txt`,
already filtered through the new `CommonWordFilter::excluding()`, then tries each of its
7 letters as center (in a seeded-`Randomizer`-shuffled order) until one clears a 20-word
minimum answer count, retrying with a fresh pangram (consuming more of the same seeded
randomness) up to 100 attempts before throwing — same retry-cap shape as
`Quartiles\PuzzleGenerator::attemptUniqueFragments()`. Against the real dictionary this
runs in ~15ms per puzzle (measured directly, 50 seeds, no failures, no excluded word ever
surfacing as a pangram or answer), confirming it fits the zero-persistence
`DailyPuzzle::seedFor()` model exactly like Wordle, unlike its sibling task Pips.

- **No hidden information from a server's perspective, unlike Wordle** — every findable
  word is discoverable by the player from the moment the puzzle loads, so this follows
  Word Blocks' resumable-game shape (`docs/specs/quartiles.md` section 4a: nullable
  `game_sessions.user_id`, auto-resume, New Game only offered once ended) rather than
  Wordle's answer-must-never-touch-a-public-property treatment. `pages::spelling-bee.play`
  mirrors `pages::quartiles.play`'s `mount()`/`resumeInProgressGame()`/`startNewSession()`/
  `mountDaily()`/`findDailySession()`/`restoreDailySession()`/`startDailySession()`/
  `persistState()` shape almost line for line.
- **The full answer-word list is deliberately never a persisted or public Livewire
  property, even though nothing about the puzzle is secret** — computing it once at
  generation time and stashing it in `$state`/a public property would let a player inspect
  the Livewire payload and see every answer before finding it themselves, the same spoiler
  concern Word Blocks' own `$allValidWords` (null until `endGame()`) already established.
  Resolved with a new `PuzzleGenerator::wordsFor(array $letters, string $center): array`
  — a public wrapper around the existing private `enumerate()` — that cheaply (~15ms)
  re-derives the same answer list on demand at `endGame()` reveal time from `$letters`/
  `$center` (both already public/safe, since they reveal nothing about which *combinations*
  are valid words). `$maxScore` (a single int, needed live throughout play for the rank
  ladder) is computed once at generation and kept public directly — exposing a point total
  reveals nothing about the word list the way the list itself would.
- **Real-time word submission re-applies the same exclusion filter generation uses, not
  just a dictionary check** — without this, a player could still score an excluded word
  (a slur, say) by typing it directly, even though `PuzzleGenerator` would never generate
  it as an answer. Caught while implementing `submitWord()`, not left as an assumed given:
  `CommonWordFilter::excluding([$word]) === []` runs alongside `Boggle\Solver::
  isValidWord()`, both folded into the same generic "Not a word." rejection message (no
  distinct message for the exclusion case, so it reads exactly like any other non-word —
  matching how NYT's own curated answer list quietly rejects a word it excludes, not a
  special "that word is banned" callout).
- **Rank ladder compares "best rank reached" across different puzzles by tier
  *position*, not raw score or `maxScore`** — `App\Services\SpellingBee\RankCalculator`'s
  9-tier ladder (Beginner → Getting Started → Making Progress → Solid → Nice → Great →
  Amazing → Genius → Hive Master) is looked up by score/maxScore ratio, and
  `SpellingBeeStatsService::bestMetrics()` picks the best game by `tierIndex()`, not by
  score — two different puzzles' raw point totals aren't comparable (`maxScore` varies
  with how many answer words a given letter set happens to support), but "reached Amazing"
  vs. "reached Genius" is, the same reasoning `PlayerStatsService::avgScoreByMode()`
  already established for Boggle's solo-vs-room score split.
- **Real bug caught by a geometry check, not proactively, while building the hexagon
  input layout:** the center letter's `w-16`/`min-h-16` and each outer letter's
  `w-14`/`min-h-14` were meant to render the center as a visibly larger square, but
  `getBoundingClientRect()` showed both at an identical 66.53px — neither matched their
  own declared size. Root cause: `<button>` elements carry an intrinsic minimum size
  driven by their own padding/content that CSS `width` cannot shrink below without also
  reducing that padding; this app's global `button { padding: clamp(...) }` rule (`app.css`)
  supplies enough padding on its own to make both differently-sized letter buttons collapse
  to the same padding-driven minimum, silently overriding both `w-*` utilities at once.
  Fixed with `p-0` on both button classes, confirmed back to the exact intended 64px/56px
  via the same geometry check. **This is a latent, pre-existing instance of the identical
  bug in Word Blocks' own `.quartile-tile` buttons** (confirmed live: a real tile measures
  99px wide despite `min-h-14`/no `w-*` class at all, `aspect-square` then stretching
  height to match that oversized width instead of the intended 56px square) — not fixed
  here, since it's cosmetic-only (tiles still function, still render as consistent
  same-sized squares across the whole grid) and out of scope for this game's own build;
  worth a follow-up pass across every `aspect-square` button in the app rather than a
  one-off patch.
- Test coverage: `tests/Unit/Words/CommonWordFilterTest.php` (`excluding()` cases);
  `tests/Unit/SpellingBee/{PuzzleGeneratorTest,ScorerTest,RankCalculatorTest}.php`;
  `tests/Unit/Stats/SpellingBeeStatsServiceTest.php`; `tests/Feature/Games/
  SpellingBeeGameTest.php` (word accept/reject in every failure mode, pangram bonus,
  resumable state, End Game reveal); `tests/Feature/Games/SpellingBeeDailyTest.php`
  (deterministic daily puzzle, the signed-in lock/resume, a random game and a daily
  coexisting without clobbering each other); `tests/Feature/Games/
  SpellingBeeChallengeFriendTest.php`; `tests/Feature/ProfileSpellingBeeTest.php`;
  `tests/Browser/SpellingBeeLetterTapTest.php` (the center-letter sizing bug above, plus a
  real tap-to-spell-and-score flow) — the exact bug class `CLAUDE.md`'s "Automated Browser
  Testing" section flags a Livewire-only test can't catch, confirmed live this session.

**Replaced the letter layout with a real tessellating honeycomb** (`docs/concerns.md`
#8) — the original layout (documented above) was a plain center-row-above-a-wrapped-outer-row
approximation, explicitly *not* a literal hexagon shape per its own comment at the time.
New `.hive`/`.hive-row`/`.hive-hex` rules in `app.css`: pointy-top/bottom, flat-left/right
hexagons (`clip-path: polygon(...)`, `--bee-hex-w` flat-to-flat width driving a derived
`--bee-hex-h` via the regular-hexagon ratio `2/sqrt(3) ≈ 1.1547`) tiled in the classic 2-3-2
flower — a 3-hex middle row (center letter flanked by two outer letters, touching
edge-to-edge) with a 2-hex row nested into each of its upper/lower "valleys."
- **Centering both rows in a flex column does the horizontal offset math for free.** A 2-hex
  row and a 3-hex row, both simply `align-items: center`-ed on the same axis, land the
  narrower row's item centers exactly `W/2` either side of 0 — precisely where the wider
  row's own item boundaries fall — confirmed algebraically before writing any CSS, not by
  trial and error. Vertical row spacing uses the standard hex-grid "0.75× height" stacking
  rule via a negative `margin-top` on the middle/bottom rows, pulling each up from the plain
  (0-gap) flow position it would otherwise sit at.
- **All 7 hexes are now the same size**, reversing the original layout's "center square
  modestly bigger" treatment — a honeycomb only actually tessellates if every cell is
  uniform; the center still reads as distinct purely through its accent color/border
  (ordinary `bg-accent/15 border-accent` utilities in the Blade, unchanged from before),
  matching the real board game more faithfully than the original size-based distinction did.
  `tests/Browser/SpellingBeeLetterTapTest.php`'s own geometry assertion flipped accordingly:
  from "center is bigger" to "center is the same size as an outer letter, and touches it
  with zero gap" (`getBoundingClientRect()` on both, checked for equal width/height and for
  one's edge exactly meeting the other's) — confirmed this new assertion actually fails
  against the pre-honeycomb layout before treating it as real coverage.
- Verified visually via real screenshots (desktop and a 390px mobile viewport) before
  trusting the CSS math alone — both rendered the expected seamless flower with no visible
  gaps or overlaps between cells.

**Follow-up fix, reported live immediately after shipping the above: only the center hex
had a visible border, and cells read as overlapping.** Root cause was the `clip-path`
approach itself, not the layout math — `clip-path` only clips whatever a box's border
*already* drew along its own rectangular edges (top/right/bottom/left straight lines); it
never draws a border along the new diagonal edges the clip itself introduces. This hex's
`clip-path` polygon only shared a straight line with the box's actual left/right edges (the
25%–75% vertical span), so only *those* two edges of each hex ever got a visible line — the
four diagonal edges had no border painted on them at all, reading as missing borders and as
"overlap" at every diagonal seam (two adjacent fills meeting with no separating line).
Replaced with real SVG `<polygon>` cells (new `<x-hive-hex>` component, `letter`/`center`/
`disabled` props, absolutely positioned inside `.hive-hex` behind a `.hive-hex-label` span)
— an SVG stroke draws an actual line along all 6 edges regardless of orientation, so a
shared boundary between two touching hexes now genuinely renders on both sides of it. Same
`50,0 100,28.87 100,86.6 50,115.47 0,86.6 0,28.87` point set as the old clip-path's
percentages (just a `100 × 115.47` viewBox instead of `%`), so none of the row-layout CSS
in `app.css` needed to change. Fill/stroke set via inline `style` referencing `--die`/
`--die-border`/`--accent` directly rather than Tailwind `fill-*`/`stroke-*` utility
classes — this file's own `@theme inline` block already documents `bg-accent` silently
failing to generate at all for the identical indirected-custom-property reason, so relying
on the equivalent `fill-accent`/`stroke-accent` utilities working was too big a risk to
take on trust here.

**Second follow-up fix, reported live right after the SVG fix above: the center hex's
bottom-left/bottom-right edges weren't blue — a *different* bug than the first one, not a
regression of it.** Root cause was plain paint order, not geometry: the flower layout's row
divs deliberately overlap vertically (`app.css`'s `-0.25 × height` row margin, needed for
the hex-grid stacking math), and every `.hive-hex` is `position: relative` with no
`z-index` of its own — so in the overlapping band, later-DOM-order content simply paints
over earlier content, same as any other in-flow stacking tie. The bottom row (`E`/`R`) is
declared after the middle row (`C`/center/`L`), so its own gray stroke painted directly over
the center hex's blue stroke along their shared boundary — invisible everywhere else in the
flower, since every *other* row-to-row overlap is gray-over-gray. Fixed with a `.hive-hex-center
{ z-index: 1; }` rule (new conditional class on `<x-hive-hex>`'s root button) that lifts the
whole cell — its SVG and label both, being its own children — above every neighbor
regardless of DOM order, rather than touching the row layout itself. Confirmed via a
before/after screenshot crop showing all 6 edges genuinely blue, not just the 4 that
happened to belong to earlier-painted rows.

**Added per-length "found/total" progress columns during live play** (`docs/concerns.md`
#9: "Four Letters (5/11)") — the "Words found" panel previously listed every found word in
one flat alphabetical wrap, with no sense of how many words of a given length still exist
to find. New public `$totalWordsByLength` (`array<int, int>`, length → count) computed once
at generation time in `loadNewPuzzle()` alongside `$maxScore`, from the same
`$puzzle['words']` list `Scorer::maxPossible()` already consumes there — safe to expose for
the identical reason `$maxScore` already is: a length's own word *count* reveals nothing
about which specific words qualify, only the words themselves (still never touched until
`endGame()`) would be a spoiler. New `getFoundWordsByLengthProperty()` groups
`$foundWordsSorted` by length (mirroring `getWordsByLengthProperty()`'s existing
post-game grouping, but safe to compute live since every word in it is one the player
already found). The panel now renders one column per length present in
`$totalWordsByLength` — even a length with 0 found yet still gets its own `"(0/N)"` column,
so the puzzle's overall shape is visible from the first render — headed by
`"{length} Letters ({found}/{total})"` and listing only that length's already-found words
underneath.
- **Old in-progress sessions re-derive the count on their next reload instead of showing an
  empty panel.** `hydrateFromState()` gained a `PuzzleGenerator $generator` parameter (threaded
  through from `mount()`/`mountDaily()`, both of which already receive one) and falls back to
  `$this->countsByLength($generator->wordsFor($this->letters, $this->center))` — the same
  ~15ms re-enumeration `endGame()`'s own reveal already relies on — whenever a saved
  `GameSession.state` predates this property and has no `totalWordsByLength` key at all,
  rather than defaulting to `[]` and leaving a resumed game with no progress columns until
  its next New Game.
- Test coverage: `tests/Feature/Games/SpellingBeeGameTest.php` (`totalWordsByLength` matches
  a real `wordsFor()`-derived count for a hand-verified seed, `foundWordsByLength` groups
  correctly with points attached, and the pre-existing-session fallback re-derives a
  non-empty result) and `tests/Browser/SpellingBeeLetterTapTest.php` (a real find moves a
  length column from `"(0/N)"` to `"(1/N)"` and lists the word underneath it) — the latter
  specifically because a Livewire-only test can prove the underlying grouping is correct
  without proving the column actually renders that way in the page.

## Punchline

A Quiplash-style room game (players privately write funny answers to prompts, then vote,
scored by vote share), ships under the display name **"Punchline"** — "Quiplash" is Jackbox
Games' trademark. Unlike Boggle/Yahtzee/Family Feud/Word Blocks, the safe name was chosen
*before* any code existed, so there's no internal-vs-display-name split to maintain: the enum
case, routes, and DB values are all just `Punchline`/`punchline`. Full build spec, including
every resolved design decision: `docs/specs/punchline.md`.

- **The one genuinely new problem this game posed: concurrent, independently-progressing
  matchups within a single round.** Every other room game in this app (Boggle rooms, Yahtzee,
  Family Feud, Farkle, Best Match, Clear the Deck) has exactly one shared phase for the whole
  table at a time. Punchline's rounds 1–2 run several 2-or-3-player matchups in parallel, each
  moving through writing → voting → closed on its own clock — an early-finishing matchup can be
  mid-vote while a slower one is still being written. Modeled with two independent enums
  (`PunchlinePhase` for the round-wide shell, `PunchlineMatchupStatus` per matchup) rather than
  one — a matchup's own `status` can be ahead of the round-level `phase`, which is the entire
  mechanism behind early voting, not a special case bolted on top.
- **A real contradiction in the original ask, caught and resolved before writing any pairing
  code, not discovered by a failing test.** The request called for "one prompt answered by 3
  players" per odd-player-count round *and* "every player answers exactly 2 prompts, each with
  a different partner" — these can't both hold: total seat-appearances in a round must equal
  `2 × playerCount`, and `3·(triples) + 2·(pairs) = 2 × playerCount` only balances with an
  **even** number of triples. One triple for a whole round is mathematically impossible.
  Resolved as two triples per odd round (one per pairing "pass"), which for 3 players is simply
  the same trio answering both prompts — actually simpler than the single-triple reading would
  have been, and still matches the "three-way matchup is simpler and reads fine on screen"
  preference behind the original ask. Documented explicitly in `docs/specs/punchline.md` §2 so
  a future reader doesn't "fix" it back to one triple.
- `App\Services\Punchline\PairingService::generateRoundMatchups()` builds each pairing round via
  the standard round-robin **circle method** (fix one seat, rotate the rest between the two
  passes) — hand-verified for 4 players against the exact pairs Best Match's own precedent
  established this style of proof against, then generalized to a `range(3, 8)` dataset test
  asserting the real invariants (every seat appears in exactly 2 matchups, matchup/prompt count
  matches parity, odd counts produce exactly one triple per pass). **5 players' core pair
  provably can't avoid repeating across both passes** (hand-verified by full case enumeration,
  not assumed) — accepted and documented as an unavoidable result of the underlying
  combinatorics, not a bug, rather than chasing a "rotate the triple for fairness" nicety that
  a from-scratch check showed buys nothing for 3 or 5 players anyway.
- **Scoring: each matchup's point pool (1000 / 2000 / 3000 across rounds 1–3, escalating
  specifically so a slow start never eliminates a player) splits proportional to vote share**,
  rounded to the nearest 50. `App\Services\Punchline\ScoringService::scoreMatchup()` is a pure
  function with no knowledge of voter identity — the caller aggregates a Cache-backed vote tally
  into counts first, keeping this class testable in complete isolation. **Rounding is
  deliberately not remainder-corrected back to an exact pool total** — a hand-traced 1/1/4-of-6
  vote split leaves a real 50-point shortfall — accepted as bounded, cosmetic drift rather than
  adding a largest-remainder correction pass, the same "simplest correct thing, document the
  edge case" bias `Farkle\Scorer` already established. A 100%-of-votes answer ("sweep") gets a
  flat `round50(pool × 0.2)` bonus on top of its already-full pool share; the same unanimous
  definition applies at round 3's much larger scale (up to `3 × playerCount` total votes) rather
  than inventing a lower "landslide" threshold for that round specifically.
- **Round 3 is not a separate game mode — it's one matchup with an unusual shape.** Every
  seated player answers the same shared prompt with `answersPerSeat = 3` instead of `1`, and
  `seatIndices` covers every seat instead of 2–3, but it's the exact same submit → anonymize →
  vote → tally machinery as a pairing-round matchup. This fell out naturally once voting
  eligibility was written as one general rule (`eligibleVoters = allSeats - matchup.seatIndices`,
  falling back to `matchup.seatIndices` with self-votes disallowed whenever that set is empty)
  rather than a `playerCount === 3` special case — round 3 and a 3-player game's degenerate
  pairing-round trio both hit the exact same fallback for the exact same reason (the matchup
  already contains everyone), so there's only ever one rule to test, not two.
- **A new pattern for this app: a countdown timer synchronized across multiple devices in one
  room.** Boggle's own timer (the only prior precedent) is single-shared-screen pass-and-play
  only. Generalized its `wire:poll`/`tick()` idea: `writingEndsAt`/`votingEndsAt` are plain
  epoch timestamps broadcast in `GameSession.state`, so every browser renders its own local
  countdown without a per-tick round trip; every connected client (not just a turn-holder) polls
  `wire:poll.2s="checkTimeout"`. **Because any client can trigger it concurrently — unlike every
  other mutating action here, which is naturally exclusive to one seat's own turn —
  `checkTimeout()` needed a stronger correctness guarantee than `syncOnlineState()`'s existing
  lock-then-write pattern**: it re-locks and re-hydrates the entire component from the database
  *inside* the transaction, re-checking the deadline against that fresh read rather than
  whichever properties this connection already had loaded, so a second concurrent poll tick
  arriving just after the first already applied the timeout finds it already handled and does
  nothing (double-awarding points was the concrete risk a naive read-then-write would have
  hit). A missing answer at the writing deadline is auto-filled from
  `App\Services\Punchline\FillerAnswers` (deterministic per missing slot, not random, so a
  round-3 player who timed out partway through their 3 answers gets 3 *different* filler lines,
  not the same one repeated) — no default score is ever awarded to an absent author.
- **Shared-abstraction question, asked and answered before writing any game code, not
  retrofitted afterward.** The user is also planning a future Fibbage-style submit-then-vote
  game and asked whether Punchline, Best Match, and that future game should share a "submit →
  vote" skeleton now. Decided **not to build one yet**: below the room-lifecycle layer already
  generic and shared by every room game, the three diverge more than they overlap (Best Match's
  judge makes a single subjective pick with no vote tally at all; Fibbage's defining trick — a
  real answer seeded among the lies, two-sided scoring for fooling people vs. finding the truth
  — fits neither this game's nor Best Match's shape). The one genuine overlap — track who's
  submitted, reveal anonymized once complete, track who's voted, tally votes — is real but had
  exactly one confirmed consumer. Built as `App\Services\Punchline\BallotService`, deliberately
  decoupled from Punchline-specific pairing/scoring concepts in its own public API (plain ids/
  seats/text, no `Prompt`/`Matchup` types) so a future extraction is a mechanical move once
  Fibbage is a second real consumer — but it stays inside `app/Services/Punchline/`, not a new
  shared namespace, and **Best Match's own working, tested judge-pick flow was not touched**.
  Retrofitting an abstraction against one real game plus a guess at a second, undesigned one's
  needs would have been the premature-abstraction version of the same decision.
- **Extends Best Match's own secret-attribution-via-`Cache` pattern one level further: two
  independent secret lifecycles, not one.** Answer text and its author mapping are revealed
  together at a matchup's writing→voting transition (author names still withheld, text made
  public) and fully at close (`results.authorsByAnswerId`) — the deliberate "reveal" moment,
  mirroring Best Match's `roundAttribution()` exactly, just keyed per-matchup instead of
  per-round since matchups close independently. **Who-voted-for-what is a second, stricter
  secret that's never revealed, not even at close** — only the aggregated per-answer vote count
  ever leaves `BallotService::tally()`; secret-ballot norms mean an individual vote shouldn't
  surface publicly the way authorship deliberately does as the game's own payoff moment. Covered
  by a dedicated test asserting the entire broadcast `GameSession.state` blob never contains a
  `votes` key, and that no still-open matchup's `results` is populated early.
- **No new `room_seats` columns needed at all — genuinely simpler than Best Match here.**
  Unlike Best Match's `hand`, nothing in this game needs durable per-seat DB storage:
  in-progress draft text is an ordinary Livewire property (never broadcast, since Livewire only
  ever broadcasts the explicit `RoomGameStateUpdated` payload, not a component's own properties)
  and submitted-but-unrevealed state lives entirely in `BallotService`'s own Cache keys.
- **Player count: min 3, max 8 — enforced**, a deliberate, explicit deviation from every other
  room game's unenforced soft ceiling, because `PairingService`'s circle-method construction is
  only defined and tested for that range; going higher needs new combinatorics work first, not
  just a UI suggestion. Surfaced the same way Best Match surfaces its own minimum-player
  check — a `$startError` string on the host's own connection, not a 403 abort.
- Prompt content is a flat, hand-written 153-prompt JSON bank (`database/seeders/data/
  punchline_prompts.json` + `PunchlinePromptSeeder`), no deck/set concept — deliberately the
  same static-bank approach Best Match's own cards already established, not new LLM-generated
  content. This app's only existing LLM use (`FamilyFeud\JudgeService`) judges a guess against a
  fixed answer list rather than *authoring* content, and `docs/TODO.md`'s Task 19 already
  deferred Connections/Strands specifically because this app has no resolved precedent for AI
  content authoring — reusing the proven static-bank pattern here avoided reopening that
  question for a game that didn't need to reopen it.
- No content moderation on player-submitted answers, matching the app's one existing
  precedent exactly: Family Feud's free-text team names are already broadcast to other players
  unfiltered.
- `GameParticipant.stats` gets `pointsByRound` (a 3-int array) and `sweepsWon`, read back out by
  `App\Services\Stats\PunchlineStatsService` (`bestMetrics()`: Highest score, Most sweeps won,
  omitted rather than shown as a misleading zero if nobody ever swept — same rule every other
  stats service already follows). New `profile.punchline`/`profile.punchline.show` pages adapted
  directly from Best Match's own pair, including a new per-round points breakdown panel on the
  detail page.
- Tests: `tests/Unit/Punchline/{PairingServiceTest,ScoringServiceTest,BallotServiceTest,
  FillerAnswersTest}.php` (68 + 13 + assorted assertions, pure-function coverage with no
  Livewire/DB involved beyond `BallotServiceTest`'s own documented need for a booted app to use
  `Cache`/`now()`, the same `uses(TestCase::class)` pattern `tests/Unit/FamilyFeud/
  JudgeServiceTest.php` already established for `Http::fake()`); `tests/Feature/Games/
  PunchlineRoomTest.php` (12 tests: pairing/min-max gating, matchup reveal-on-complete,
  authorization including the P=3 self-matchup-voting fallback, secret-attribution-never-leaks,
  writing/voting timeout behavior, round-3 vote stacking, a full 3-round game producing correct
  `GameParticipant` rows); `tests/Browser/PunchlineRoomBrowserTest.php` (5 tests covering the one
  genuinely new CSS/DOM-driven behavior here — several matchups rendering independent
  writing/voting/closed states on the same page at once — the exact class of bug
  `CLAUDE.md`'s "Automated Browser Testing" section flags a Livewire-only test can't see);
  `tests/Unit/Stats/PunchlineStatsServiceTest.php`; `tests/Feature/ProfilePunchlineTest.php`.

**Same-day follow-up: a real "sometimes voting bounces you to the homepage" bug, reported from
actual play (2026-08-24), plus a writing-timer increase.** Root cause: this app already has a
global Livewire error handler (`resources/js/app.js`, predates this game) that silently
redirects to `/` on any failed Livewire request other than a 422 — a deliberate choice to avoid
flashing a raw HTML error dump, and correct for the rest of the app's turn-based, one-
phase-at-a-time games. `submitAnswer()`/`castVote()` validated against `$this->matchups`, this
connection's *last-known* snapshot, not a fresh read — and unlike every other room game here,
Punchline has several matchups progressing independently plus `checkTimeout()`'s own 2s poll
running on every connected browser, so that snapshot can genuinely go stale in the gap between
a broadcast update and a click landing: another player's vote or a timeout elsewhere can close a
matchup, or a fast double-tap can beat the DOM's own re-render, and either one hit an
`abort_unless(..., 403)` that the global handler turned into a silent redirect. **Fixed by
re-hydrating fresh from `GameSession.state` at the top of both actions** (`refreshFromDatabase()`,
a plain read, not `checkTimeout()`'s own locked read-modify-write, since this is just catching up
before validating) **and treating "this specific thing already happened" — matchup already
closed, already voted, already filed this slot — as a quiet no-op instead of a hard 403.** A
genuine violation (wrong seat entirely, invalid answer id, self-vote) still aborts for real.
Reproduced directly rather than assumed: two new regression tests in `tests/Feature/Games/
PunchlineRoomTest.php` mount a Livewire test component, mutate the database out from under it on
a *separate* connection (closing a matchup / triggering a writing timeout), then call the
original stale component's own action and assert it no longer 403s. Also bumped the writing
timer from 75s/150s to 120s/240s (same round-3-doubles-it relationship kept) after the same
real-play feedback found the original "roughly 60-90s" estimate too tight to actually think of
something funny — voting's own 30s/90s was left as reported, unaffected.

**Second same-day follow-up: the fix above wasn't complete, plus a genuinely separate bug
(2026-08-24).** Reported from continued real play: buttons — including the round-3 "Cast
Votes" button specifically — still occasionally bounced to the homepage, and a room itself
went missing ("page not found") mid-game after a vote.

- **`advanceRound()` had the identical unfixed race** — "any seated player may advance" is
  explicitly a shared, non-exclusive action (same as Best Match's `nextRound()`), which is
  exactly the kind of click two players make at once right after reading the same reveal
  screen together. The first click's `abort_unless($this->phase === RoundReveal, 403)` guard
  was never updated when `submitAnswer()`/`castVote()` got their staleness fix earlier the same
  day — a real gap in that pass, not a new bug. Fixed the same way: `refreshFromDatabase()`
  first, then a quiet no-op (not a 403) if the phase already moved past `RoundReveal` — the
  desired outcome (advancing) already happened via someone else's click.
- **`findMatchupIndex()`'s `abort(404)` needed the same treatment** — a stale click referencing
  a *previous* round's matchup id (round advancement replaces `$this->matchups` wholesale) is
  the same class of benign staleness as everything else here, not a real "matchup doesn't
  exist" error. Changed to return `null` instead of aborting; both call sites (`submitAnswer()`,
  `castVote()`) now return quietly when it's null.
- **The room-vanishing report was a real, separate, pre-existing bug — not new, and not
  Punchline-specific.** `Room.expires_at` (`app/Models/Room.php`) is set once, fixed, at room
  *creation* time (`self::LIFETIME_MINUTES` = 60) and — confirmed by grepping the whole app —
  is never extended anywhere by anything, for any game. Harmless for a short game, but a real
  bug for a long one: Punchline's 3 rounds of real writing/voting/discussion time can plausibly
  run past 60 minutes on their own (more so now that the writing timer above just got longer),
  quietly expiring the room while people were still actively mid-game, after which every page
  resolving it via the `notExpired()` scope (including `pages::room.show`) started 404ing —
  exactly "went back to the room page and it says page not found." **Fixed once, centrally, for
  every room game at once, not just Punchline**: new `Room::extendExpiry()` (resets expiry to a
  full `LIFETIME_MINUTES` from *now*, not merely adding time to whatever remained — same
  "still active = a full session's worth of time left" reasoning `markPendingGame()`'s own cache
  TTL already uses) is called from a single `Event::listen(RoomGameStateUpdated::class, ...)`
  registered in `AppServiceProvider::boot()`, since every room game (Boggle, Yahtzee, Family
  Feud, Farkle, Best Match, Clear the Deck, Punchline) already dispatches that exact event on
  every real state change. Deliberately **not** wired into each game's own `syncOnlineState()`
  individually — this app has no `Listeners` directory/`EventServiceProvider` precedent, but a
  single provider-registered listener on the one event every game already shares was less
  invasive than adding a call to six already-shipped, tested files, and covers all of them
  uniformly with one small, additive change.
- New tests: `tests/Feature/Games/PunchlineRoomTest.php` gained two more (two connections
  clicking "Next Round" at once doesn't error or double-advance; acting on a matchup id from a
  round that already moved on elsewhere quietly no-ops instead of 404ing); `tests/Feature/
  Models/RoomTest.php` gained `extendExpiry()` coverage (resets to a full window, can revive an
  already-expired room) and a direct assertion that dispatching `RoomGameStateUpdated` alone —
  the real integration point — extends a room's expiry.

**Third same-day follow-up: the actual root cause, found only by reproducing live against
production (2026-08-24).** The two follow-ups above were real fixes for real races, but the user
kept hitting the redirect specifically when casting a vote — including, always, on round 3 — and
was explicit that they were driving one browser at a time, never clicking two things at once, so
it couldn't be a race at all. That correction was the key: every race-based theory was wrong.

**Reproduction discipline that got to the real bug**: rather than continuing to reason from code,
confirmed step by step against the actual deployed environment — (1) this repo turns out to
auto-push on commit and the Laravel Cloud environment auto-deploys on push (confirmed via `cloud
deployment:list`), so both earlier fixes really were already live, ruling out a deploy gap; (2)
built a real game session directly on the production database (`cloud tinker`) and, using a real
login (not a guest — guest sessions don't survive across separate Playwright processes, a dead
end hit first), clicked an actual "cast vote" button on the live site — it worked cleanly,
proving a plain, unhurried vote wasn't inherently broken; (3) attempting a genuine 3-real-browser
concurrent reproduction hit the exact multi-`visit()`-page hang this file's own "Automated
browser testing" section already documents for this app's local test suite — turns out to apply
to live Playwright-vs-production sessions too, not just local sqlite — abandoned after two
timeouts rather than a third blind retry.

**The real bug, once the user's "I'm never clicking two things at once" correction reframed the
search**: they were testing with exactly 3 players — Punchline's minimum, and the specific case
where `eligibleVoters()`'s fallback (`docs/specs/punchline.md` §2) makes *every* matchup include
every seat, not just round 3's. That means every player is simultaneously an author of a matchup
they're also asked to vote on. The anonymized answer list (`matchup['answers']`, deliberately
carrying no author name — the entire point of anonymization) never excluded the *viewer's own*
answer from the buttons rendered to them. A voter had no way to visually tell their own answer
apart from anyone else's, so clicking it was pure chance — and the server's real, correct "never
vote for your own answer" guard (present since the game's original build, never buggy itself)
rejected it with a 403, which this app's global Livewire error handler turned into the reported
silent redirect home. Round 1–2 in a 3-player game: 1-in-3 odds per vote, matching "intermittent."
Round 3: 3 of the 9 total answers are always the voter's own, and they're asked to spread 3 votes
without any way to avoid them — matching "always." A normal 4+ player external voter never
authors any answer in a matchup they're voting on, so this was invisible in every other
configuration, including every Feature test written for the two earlier follow-ups today (none
of which used exactly 3 players *and* actually inspected the rendered vote options against
`mySeatIndex`).
- **Fixed at the source**: new `matchupVotableAnswers(array $matchup): array` filters the
  viewer's own answer(s) out of whatever gets rendered as clickable, used by both the round 1–2
  single-vote buttons and round 3's vote-allocation stepper. A no-op for a normal external voter
  (none of a matchup's answers are ever theirs), so this only changes behavior in exactly the
  fallback case that was broken.
- **`castVote()` also hardened as defense in depth, not just the UI**: instead of `abort_unless`-
  rejecting the *entire* vote the instant any submitted answer id happens to be self-authored, it
  now silently filters just that id out of the submission (a no-op if nothing valid remains) —
  matching this file's own "benign staleness" philosophy from the two earlier follow-ups today,
  extended to cover an honest client-side mistake, not just server/client state drift. The self-
  vote rule itself is unchanged and still fully enforced; only *how* a violation is handled
  changed, from a hard reject to a quiet drop.
- New tests directly reproducing the actual reported mechanism: a 3-player game where
  `matchupVotableAnswers()` is asserted to exclude the viewer's own answer; a vote for one's own
  answer no longer 403s and is confirmed never recorded; a round-3 allocation mixing a real
  choice with a self-vote keeps only the real one once the matchup closes and scores.
- Verified live against the deployed environment, not just locally, after this fix — same
  `cloud tinker`-seeded-game-plus-real-login technique used to find the bug, confirming the fix
  actually resolves it where it was reported, not just in test isolation.
- **Host-only round advancement, reversing an earlier deliberate design decision** (docs/
  concerns.md): `advanceRound()` originally let *any* seated player click "Next Round", on the
  theory that it's a shared "we're ready to continue" moment everyone taps in unison (documented
  at the time as intentional, mirroring Best Match's own `nextRound()`). In practice this meant
  anyone could rush the whole room past a reveal others were still reading, so it's now gated to
  `$this->isHost` exactly like `advanceRound()`'s Farkle/Family Feud counterparts already gate
  their own manual actions. The re-sync-first/return-quietly-on-stale-phase handling stayed —
  still needed for the host's own double-click (e.g. two tabs), just no longer for a race between
  different players. Non-host seats now see "Waiting for the host to continue…" instead of a
  button.
- **Added a host-only manual "End Game" action** (docs/concerns.md) — previously the only way to
  end a Punchline game was playing all 3 rounds to completion; there was no way to stop early.
  `endGameManually()` mirrors `pages::farkle.play`'s own `endGameManually()`/
  `getCanEndGameManuallyProperty()` shape exactly, and reuses `finishGame()` unchanged: that
  method already just finalizes `GameParticipant` rows from whatever `$this->scores`/
  `pointsByRound`/`sweepsWon` currently hold, which *is* "stop now, score what's happened so far"
  — the same thing round 3's reveal-to-`GameEnd` transition does already, just triggered early.
  Wired into the `#game-actions` Game-menu dialog with the same confirm-dialog pattern as
  Farkle's own End Game button (a `data-dialog-open` trigger + `<x-confirm-dialog>`), not a bare
  click, since this ends the game for every seat at once.

## User profiles & stats

- `/dashboard` (own stats) and `/profile/{username}` (public, `is_public`-gated).

Decisions:
- `App\Services\Stats\PlayerStatsService::forUser()` groups a user's `GameParticipant` rows by their `GameSession`'s `type` (via Eloquent collections, not a raw SQL `join`/`groupBy` — the game-history size for a single hobbyist player is nowhere near "needs a DB aggregate" territory) and computes games played / win rate / average score per type. `recentGames()` needed `->latest()->latest('id')` rather than just `->latest()` — a real bug caught by a test: two participants recorded in the same second (plausible, e.g. a multiplayer room where every player's `endGame()` fires within the same clock second) sort in an undefined order without an `id` tiebreaker.
- **Boggle-only win-rate scoping** (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #1). Boggle is the only game type where `GameParticipant.result` stays `null` — solo/practice play has no opponent to win or lose against — so every solo game was inflating the win-rate *denominator* (`gamesPlayed`) while never able to land in the numerator, silently deflating win rate the more solo games someone played. Yahtzee and Family Feud never hit this: both always compute a real Win/Loss/Draw, even for local pass-and-play, so they needed no change. Fixed with a Boggle-only branch in `forUser()` (`boggleWinRateEligible()`) that restricts the win-rate numerator/denominator specifically to online-room games, further excluding any session where *every* participant scored 0 (an aborted room nobody actually played, not a real loss) — a session where only the acting user scored 0 but someone else in it scored above 0 still counts as a genuine loss. Deliberately narrow: `gamesPlayed` and `avgScore` on the same dashboard card still cover every game including solo play, since the bug (and the original ask) was specifically about the win-rate number, not those two. Covered by five new tests in `tests/Unit/Stats/PlayerStatsServiceTest.php`.
- **Repurposed `/home` into the real `/dashboard`** rather than keeping both — `/home` was always documented as a placeholder until this phase. Updated `config/fortify.php`'s `'home'` redirect target, the passkey-login JS fallback redirect, and the two auth tests asserting a `/home` redirect. Deleted the placeholder view/route entirely rather than leaving a compatibility redirect, since nothing external linked to it yet.
- **Dropped "friends activity"** from the dashboard (mentioned once in the original plan) — no friends/social-graph table existed anywhere in the schema at the time, so this read as aspirational filler rather than a concretely specified feature. Later built as its own feature — see "Friends & friend activity" below.
- **Image avatars (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #3, 2026-08-18).** `<x-avatar>` can now render an uploaded image instead of the generated initial-letter badge. Real infrastructure decision, not just an app-code one: this app's own analytics work (see "Infrastructure & tooling" above) had already established that Laravel Cloud's filesystem is ephemeral and not shared across replicas, so plain local-disk storage wouldn't reliably survive a redeploy in production. Asked the user directly rather than assuming Gravatar-only was the safe default; they'd already provisioned a real S3-compatible bucket through Laravel Cloud specifically for this, so real upload was the answer, not a workaround.
  - `composer require league/flysystem-aws-s3-v3` (approved directly by the user, who'd already hit the "it requires this package" message setting up the bucket) — the Flysystem S3 adapter isn't bundled with Laravel by default. `config/filesystems.php`'s `s3` disk (already present, wired to `AWS_*` env vars, unused until now) needed no changes.
  - New nullable `users.avatar_path` column (migration, `#[Fillable]`) stores the S3 object key, not a URL — `User::avatarUrl(): ?string` computes `Storage::disk('s3')->url($this->avatar_path)` on read, `null` when no path is set. `<x-avatar>` gained an `avatarUrl` prop: an `<img>` when set, the existing initial-letter `<div>` otherwise — same component, both call sites (`⚡dashboard.blade.php`, `pages::profile.show`) just pass the new prop through.
  - **Upload/remove UI lives on `/profile`**, `Livewire\WithFileUploads` on a `#[Validate('nullable|image|max:2048')] public ?UploadedFile $avatarUpload`. `uploadAvatar()` deletes any previous S3 object first (so changing file extensions doesn't leave the old one orphaned in the bucket), then stores under a fresh random name each time (`->store(path: 'avatars', options: ['disk' => 's3', 'visibility' => 'public'])`) — a random name rather than a deterministic per-user one is also a free cache-bust, since the URL genuinely changes on every re-upload. `removeAvatar()` deletes the S3 object and clears the column. Preview before saving uses Livewire's own `$avatarUpload->temporaryUrl()`, guarded behind `isPreviewable()` — an invalid (non-image) upload still needs to render *something* for the file input's error state without throwing, since `temporaryUrl()` itself throws for a non-image MIME type.
  - **Avatar upload size limit raised from 2MB to 10MB, and a crop-before-upload step added (`docs/concerns.md`, 2026-08-21).** Two separate asks, both about the same upload: the 2MB validation cap was too small, and users had no way to pick which part of a photo becomes their (square, `rounded-full`) avatar — a wide/tall photo just got stretched to fit.
    - **The 2MB limit wasn't only a Livewire validation rule.** `#[Validate('nullable|image|max:2048')]` → `max:10240` alone would have silently kept failing above 2MB anyway: `upload_max_filesize`/`post_max_size` in `docker/php/Dockerfile`'s php-fpm image defaulted to 2M/8M (matching the old Laravel limit by coincidence, not by any shared config), and `docker/nginx/default.conf` had no `client_max_body_size` at all, meaning nginx's own 1M default was actually *smaller* than the Laravel limit it was supposedly guarding — a latent bug predating this change, uploads between 1–2MB would have already 413'd at the proxy before Laravel ever saw them. Fixed all three together (nginx `client_max_body_size 12m`, a new `docker/php/conf.d/uploads.ini`-equivalent `RUN echo` block setting `upload_max_filesize=10M`/`post_max_size=12M`, Laravel's `max:10240`), plus the non-Docker local path (`composer.json`'s `dev` script now runs `php -d upload_max_filesize=10M -d post_max_size=12M artisan serve`) — three independent layers, all needing to agree, not just the one the validation error message points at.
    - **Cropping via `cropperjs` (v2.1.1, first new npm dependency added to this app beyond what `laravel/laravel` ships with).** `<input type="file">` deliberately lost its `wire:model` binding — the raw file the user picks never reaches Livewire at all. Selecting a file now only opens a new `#avatar-crop-dialog` against a local `blob:` URL (no network round trip) with a live Cropper.js selection box; only the square region the user confirms is ever handed to Livewire, via its documented JS upload API (`Livewire.find(id).$upload(name, file, ...)` — the one entry point built for exactly this "third-party file library hands me a File, not an `<input>` change event" scenario) rather than the normal `wire:model` auto-upload. Everything downstream of that (`$avatarUpload` property, `uploadAvatar()`'s S3 store/validate) is the unchanged, already-tested code from the entry above.
    - **First implementation attempt hand-authored Cropper.js's whole custom-element tree (`<cropper-canvas>`/`<cropper-image>`/`<cropper-selection>`/etc.) directly in Blade, copying attribute names from a fetched doc page.** It rendered — a real image appeared inside a real dialog — but `<cropper-selection>` was silently stuck at 0×0 the entire time: confirmed only by actually capturing its own `$toCanvas()` output onto the page and screenshotting it (a blank sliver), since the selection box *looked* present on screen (the image itself filled the canvas, easily mistaken for a working selection). The visible "border" around the image was `<cropper-shade>`, not an initialized selection. Root cause, found by reading `node_modules/cropperjs/dist/cropper.esm.raw.js` directly rather than trusting fetched documentation further: the library's real entry point is `new Cropper(imgElement, options)`, which injects its own verified `DEFAULT_TEMPLATE` fragment *after* the image's `src` is already set — the sizing/`initial-coverage` logic apparently only computes once, right at that moment. Statically declaring the same tags in Blade with no `src` yet (set later, once the user picks a file) left that one-time computation running against a sourceless image. Fixed by switching to the imperative `new Cropper(image, { container, template })` call in `app.js`, using the library's own `DEFAULT_TEMPLATE` (copied verbatim from its source, not re-guessed) with only an `aspect-ratio="1"` added to `<cropper-selection>` for a square crop — Blade now just provides a plain `<img id="avatar-crop-image" class="hidden">` for the constructor to attach to. Re-verified the same way (a checkerboard test image, `$toCanvas()`'s output rendered and screenshotted) before trusting it: a proper square selection with visible resize handles, and a correctly-cropped, non-degenerate output image.
    - **New `tests/Browser/ProfileAvatarCropTest.php`** covers what's actually new and unverifiable any other way — that the real Cropper.js element tree initializes against a real selected photo with a genuine non-zero, square selection, and that `$toCanvas()` produces a correctly-sized non-empty image — stopping short of the final "Use This Photo" → `$wire.$upload()` → "Save Avatar" step. That step hits the exact same pre-existing, already-documented Livewire/Pest-browser-driver bug as `tests/Browser/ProfileAvatarUploadTest.php` below (`WithFileUploads::_finishUpload()` throwing `Undefined array key 0`) — confirmed by reproducing it again here, unchanged, and not chased further for the same reason.
  - **A real, reproducible bug hit wiring up test coverage, not just a theoretical one:** `Storage::disk('s3')->url()` throws a genuine `InvalidArgumentException` ("requires non-empty parameter: Bucket") the moment `AWS_BUCKET`/credentials aren't configured — confirmed live via `php artisan tinker` before assuming a cause. Root cause: `AwsS3V3Adapter::url()` only skips calling the AWS SDK's own `getObjectUrl()` (which needs a real bucket) when `config('filesystems.disks.s3.url')` (the `AWS_URL` env var) is explicitly set; otherwise it always reaches through to the SDK, `Storage::fake('s3')` or not, since faking swaps the *disk* but this check runs on the *unfaked* config values regardless. This meant the app would 500 in this exact "bucket not configured yet" state — true of this local machine right now, and would have been equally true in production before the user's real `AWS_URL`/`AWS_BUCKET` env vars are set on Laravel Cloud — and would have 500'd in CI/every future contributor's machine too, permanently, without a fix. Fixed at the *test* layer, not the app: `phpunit.xml` now sets a real, fake-but-well-formed `AWS_URL`/`AWS_BUCKET` (`https://example-bucket.test`/`testing-bucket`), which routes `url()` through its early-return branch and avoids the SDK path entirely — matching what a real deploy needs to set anyway, just pointed at a placeholder. `.env.example` documents the four `AWS_*` keys this feature actually needs (`AWS_URL`/`AWS_ENDPOINT` were previously missing entirely, even though `config/filesystems.php` already read them). Real credentials still need setting as actual Laravel Cloud environment variables separately, the same caveat every other Laravel Cloud secret in this app already carries (`SESSION_LIFETIME`, `MAXMIND_*`, `ANALYTICS_ADMIN_EMAILS`).
  - Added `tests/Feature/ProfileAvatarTest.php` (`avatarUrl()` null/non-null, upload stores on the fake `s3` disk and records the path, a second upload deletes the first object, non-image rejected, remove deletes the object and clears the path, both the dashboard and a public profile page render the real `<img>` once a path is set).
  - **Driving a real `<input type="file">` through Playwright's `->attach()` to test the actual upload interaction hit a genuine, reproducible bug in Livewire itself** (`WithFileUploads::_finishUpload()` throwing `Undefined array key 0` on `$tmpPath[0]`, confirmed via the real server-side stack trace, not a flaky timing issue) — this app has zero prior precedent anywhere of browser-testing a file input, so there was nothing to compare the failure against, and it wasn't chased further given `CLAUDE.md`'s own guidance on repeated browser-tool-call failures. `tests/Browser/ProfileAvatarUploadTest.php` instead covers the one thing the Feature tests genuinely can't see without going through that flow: seeded directly with `avatar_path` set (bypassing the live upload), it confirms the rendered `<img>` is an actual visible, correctly-shaped (`object-fit: cover`, a real pixel `border-radius`) image in a real browser, not just present in the HTML string. **This test caught a real bug on its first run**: `object-fit` computed as `fill`, not `cover` — `resources/css/app.css` hadn't been rebuilt (`npm run build`) since `object-cover` was newly added to `<x-avatar>`'s class list, so Tailwind's compiled output didn't contain that utility yet. Passed clean once rebuilt.
- **"Clear All Stats"** on `/profile` (new "Danger Zone" panel) deletes only the acting user's own `GameParticipant` rows (`$this->user()->gameParticipants()->delete()`), gated behind `<x-confirm-dialog>` (see "Low-stim confirm dialogs" above). Deliberately scoped to the user's own rows, not the `GameSession`s themselves — a shared multiplayer session (e.g. a Family Feud room) stays intact along with every other player's own history on it; clearing never cascades across players. Covered by a Feature test asserting exactly that (another player's row on a session they shared survives) and `tests/Browser/ProfileDangerZoneDialogsTest.php` for the dialog's own open/cancel/confirm behavior.
- **"Delete Account"**, same panel, same dialog component. **Hard delete**, not soft — the schema already anticipated this: `passkeys.user_id`/`room_seats.user_id` cascade-delete, `rooms.host_user_id` cascade-deletes any room the account hosted, but `game_participants.user_id` merely nulls out (nullable FK, `nullOnDelete`), so a shared session's `GameParticipant` rows for *other* players survive with their own score/stats intact — only the deleted account's own attribution is severed. A genuinely dead row is also what makes "register again with the same email creates a new account, not a resurrected one" trivial: nothing to collide with, no soft-delete flag to remember to check everywhere else in the codebase. The action itself mirrors Fortify's own logout controller (`Auth::guard(config('fortify.guard'))->logout()`, `session()->invalidate()`, `session()->regenerateToken()`) before deleting, then redirects to `/`. Covered by three Feature tests (user gone + logged out + redirected; passkeys/hosted-room cascade vs. game-participant nulling; a real `POST /register` with the same email afterward succeeds and authenticates a different user id) plus the dialog's own open/cancel coverage in the same browser test file as Clear All Stats.
- **"All Games" page** (`/profile/games`, route `profile.games`), new `PlayerStatsService::allGamesPaginated()` (same "id breaks ties" `->latest()->latest('id')` ordering as `recentGames()`, just `->paginate()` instead of `->limit()`). Registered before the `/profile/{username}` wildcard, same reasoning as `/profile/boggle` etc. Uses Livewire's built-in `WithPagination`/`->paginate()` rather than a hand-rolled "load more," but with a plain custom Previous/Next + "Page X of Y" control instead of publishing Livewire's default Tailwind pagination view — the stock view's link styling doesn't fit this app's muted button/`.option` vocabulary, and a page-number strip would need per-page links this app has never needed elsewhere. Deliberately no per-game-type filter UI (each of the three per-game "All Games" tables already exists on their own `/profile/{game}` page) — this page's whole purpose is the cross-type view those don't provide, linked from the dashboard.
- Public profile privacy: an unknown username 404s, but a private profile that *does* exist still renders `200 OK` with a plain "This profile is private" message — deliberately distinguishable from "no such user," and matches how the owner viewing their own private profile needs to see a real 200 with their own stats, not a 404.
- Found the same Larastan/`casts()` inference gap twice (first on `FamilyFeudQuestion`, then here): `GameSession::type`/`GameParticipant::result` resolved to `string` instead of their enum types in one call chain (`$participant->gameSession->type->value` inside a `groupBy()` closure), even though `casts()` declares them correctly. Fixed with explicit `@property` PHPDoc annotations. Worth remembering as a recurring gotcha with this Larastan version.
- **Homepage: auth links moved into the top bar.** `welcome.blade.php` used to render "Create an account"/"sign in" (guests) or "Your dashboard" (logged in) as a plain text line below the game grid, with no logout button anywhere on the homepage. Moved into the shared button-bar's right side, matching every other page's convention: added a genuinely new `$barRight` slot to `layouts/app.blade.php` — deliberately a **plain** slot, not a `@teleport` target, since `welcome.blade.php` is a classic `@component`/`@slot` Blade view with no Livewire round trip that could ever go stale (contrast with `#game-actions`, which needs `@teleport` specifically because game pages *do* re-render). `welcome.blade.php` fills it with `@guest`/`@else`: Sign In + Create an Account (guest), or Dashboard + a real `POST /logout` form (authenticated). Also set the layout's existing (previously unused anywhere) `$manyButtons` flag on the homepage, tightening padding/gap at narrow widths for the now three-button row. **Blade gotcha hit while wiring `$manyButtons`:** `@slot('manyButtons')1@endslot` on one line rendered the literal text `1@endslot` onto the page — `@endslot` needs whitespace/a newline before it, not to be butted directly against preceding content. Verified via a scripted headless-Chrome (CDP) session (guest/authenticated states at desktop and mobile widths, no wrapping, logout round-trips correctly) since there was no prior JS/DOM behavior to assert on with Pest here; added `tests/Feature/WelcomePageTest.php`.
- **Dashboard: Edit Profile/Log Out buttons moved into the top bar** the same way, for the same "one consistent chrome convention across every page" reasoning as the homepage change above.
- **Family Feud's homepage card described the implementation, not the game** (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #1). The old copy — "an AI judges guesses server-side, so there's no hidden host view to manage" — explains an architecture decision covered in this file's own Family Feud section, not what a first-time visitor would actually be clicking into. Rewritten to describe the actual gameplay (two teams guessing the most popular survey answers, points per match, three strikes hands the other team a steal), matching how the Boggle/Yahtzee cards next to it already describe their own gameplay rather than their implementation.
- **Boggle's blended avgScore split by mode (`docs/TODO.md`'s former Task 12, `docs/concerns.md`, 2026-08-21).** `GameParticipant.score` doesn't mean the same thing in every Boggle mode: solo accumulates a raw point total, never touched again, but an online room runs `SharedSessionFinalizer::finalize()` afterward, which overwrites every participant's score to an "exclusive" score — a word found by more than one player in the room scores zero for everyone, not just the literal "points more than other players" the report described, but still a different scale from solo's raw total. `PlayerStatsService::avgScore` blended both into one number regardless of mode, so a user who played a mix of solo and room games got a figure that didn't cleanly represent either. Asked the user directly rather than guessing at the fix; they picked "store both, split avgScore by mode" over excluding room games entirely or leaving it undocumented.
  - **No schema change or redundant storage needed** — `found_words`'s own `word => points` map already survives `SharedSessionFinalizer` untouched (only the `score` column gets overwritten), so a raw total was already recoverable by summing it; nothing new needed persisting. Followed this file's own established precedent (`BoggleStatsService`'s docblock: stats are "derived entirely from a participant's stored `found_words`... rather than stored redundantly") rather than adding a new column, which also means it's correct for every historical participant row with no backfill.
  - `PlayerStatsService::forUser()` gained `avgScoreByMode` (`{soloGames: int, soloAvg: float, onlineGames: int, onlineAvg: float}`, computed for every game type, not just Boggle) alongside the existing `avgScore` — left computed exactly as before, blended, for any other consumer that still wants the single number. `<x-player-stats>` only renders the split for Boggle's card, checking `$stat['type'] === GameType::Boggle` rather than relying on the data itself being absent elsewhere.
  - **Every field in the new shape is deliberately non-nullable — an unplayed mode is `{soloGames: 0, soloAvg: 0.0}`, not `{solo: null}`** — after a confirmed Larastan v3.10 false positive blocked the original, more obvious design. `avgScoreByMode: ?array{solo: ?float, online: ?float}` (and every nullable variant tried in its place: non-nullable outer with nullable inner floats, two flat `?float` keys with no nesting at all, no conditional branching in the code at all) reliably produced `"Method ... should return Collection<int, X> but returns Collection<int, X>"` — textually *identical* types on both sides of the error, with `Template type TValue on class Illuminate\Support\Collection is not covariant` as the only explanation. Root-caused (not just worked around blind) with a from-scratch, unrelated minimal reproduction in a scratch file — `Collection<int, array{a: int, b: ?float}>` returned from `collect([1,2,3])->map(fn ($x) => ['a' => $x, 'b' => $x > 1 ? (float) $x : null])->values()` — which hit the *exact* same error, proving it has nothing to do with `GameType`/`GameParticipant` or this app's code at all: any `Collection<int, array{...}>`-returning method with *any* nullable/union field in its row shape hits this, regardless of construction method (`->map()`, `->values()`, `array_map()`, `Collection::make()`, `collect()`, and a bare `new Collection(...)` were all tried and all reproduced it identically). Fixed by removing the nullability instead of suppressing the check (no `@phpstan-ignore`, baseline entry, or `@var` override, per this project's own standing rule) — a `0` game count already unambiguously means "not played," so there was no real need for `null` to mean the same thing a second way.
  - `<x-player-stats>` shows two lines ("N avg score (solo)"/"N avg score (rooms)") for Boggle's card instead of the single blended line, each only rendered once that mode's own game count is above zero — a user who's only ever played solo just sees the one line, not a redundant/misleading "0 avg score (rooms)".
  - New tests in `tests/Unit/Stats/PlayerStatsServiceTest.php` (the split is correct while `avgScore` stays blended as before, a `0`/`0.0` count/average for an unplayed mode, the data is present-but-UI-ignored for a non-Boggle type) and `tests/Feature/DashboardTest.php` (the dashboard actually renders both labeled lines, not the blended fallback).

## Friends & friend activity

`docs/TODO.md`'s Task 4 flagged that "friends activity" (mentioned once in the original app plan, see "User profiles & stats" above) needed an actual design decision before any building could start — no friends/social-graph table existed, and "friends" is genuinely ambiguous (mutual? one-way follow?). Asked the user directly rather than guessing; three decisions came back: **mutual, request/accept friendship** (not a one-way follow); **activity = completed games only** (not room/lobby presence, which isn't currently tracked as a persistent log at all); and **a dedicated `/friends` page + nav link**, not a dashboard widget.

- New `friendships` table: `requester_id`/`addressee_id` (both `users` FKs, cascade-delete), `status` (`FriendshipStatus` enum: `Pending`/`Accepted`, cast on the `Friendship` model), unique on `(requester_id, addressee_id)`. The DB constraint only catches a same-direction duplicate — preventing the *reverse*-direction duplicate (B→A while A→B already exists) is application logic, not something a plain unique index can express directly.
- `App\Services\Social\FriendService` centralizes every friendship query/mutation (`friendsOf()`, `pendingReceived()`/`pendingSent()`, `relationshipStatus()`, `sendRequest()`/`accept()`/`decline()`/`remove()`, `activityFeed()`) — same "service owns the query logic, page component just calls it" pattern as `PlayerStatsService`, rather than growing `User` into a god model. `sendRequest()` is a deliberate no-op (not an exception) for a self-request or an already-pending/accepted pair in either direction, since the only caller (the page's search results) is already filtered to exclude those cases — this is a defensive guard, not a user-facing error path.
- **`activityFeed()` needed no "completed" filter beyond the row existing at all** — confirmed by checking where `GameParticipant` rows actually get written (e.g. `pages::boggle.play`'s `endGame()`): every game type only ever creates one at game end, never mid-game, so there's no in-progress state to accidentally leak into a friend's feed.
- **Friend activity deliberately ignores `is_public`** (the flag that gates a *stranger's* view of `/profile/{username}`'s stats) — becoming mutual friends is a stricter, explicit pairing than the public-profile toggle, so a friend seeing your recent games doesn't depend on whether your profile happens to be public too.
- `pages::friends`, single-file Livewire component, no sidebar (matches `/profile`'s own post-sidebar-removal layout, not the stats sidebar's per-game link list). Sections: username search-and-add (excludes the current user, guests, and any user with no `username` set — someone without a chosen username has no public identity to search by), incoming requests (Accept/Decline), outgoing requests (Cancel), the friends list (Remove, links to `/profile/{username}` when the friend has one), and the activity table itself. `removeRequest()` deliberately handles both Decline and Cancel with one method — both are just deleting the same still-pending row before it's accepted, regardless of which side of the request the acting user is on.
- **Authorization is enforced by scoping the lookup query, not by a separate check-then-act.** `acceptRequest()`/`removeRequest()` `findOrFail()` against a query already constrained to rows the acting user is actually part of (`where('addressee_id', ...)` for accept; requester-or-addressee for remove) — a `Friendship` id belonging to two other users 404s (via Laravel's standard `ModelNotFoundException` handling) rather than ever loading a row this user has no business touching.
- Nav link added to the navbar avatar dropdown (`resources/views/components/user-menu.blade.php`), alongside Stats/Edit Profile/Logout — not the stats sidebar, since Friends isn't a per-game-stats view.
- No dedicated Browser test — this page is plain lists/buttons with no `<dialog>`, `@teleport`, or responsive-layout logic (the categories `docs/CLAUDE.md` calls out as needing one), so `tests/Feature/FriendsPageTest.php`'s `Livewire::test()` coverage (search filtering, send/accept/decline/cancel/remove, cross-user authorization 404s, activity-feed scoping) was sufficient.

**Email notification on a received friend request, and a pending-count badge on the avatar dropdown (`docs/concerns.md`, 2026-08-21).** First use of Laravel's `Notification`/`app/Notifications/` in this app — nothing existed there before. `FriendRequestReceived implements ShouldQueue` (mail is already Redis-queued elsewhere in this app, e.g. `RecordPageview`), sent via `$to->notify(...)` from `FriendService::sendRequest()` right after the `Friendship` row is created. **Guest accounts are explicitly excluded** (`! $to->is_guest` guard before notifying) — `User::createGuest()` gives every guest a placeholder `@guests.invalid` email specifically so it never resolves, and would otherwise just generate failed-job noise on the queue for an address nobody reads.
- The avatar dropdown badge (`resources/views/components/user-menu.blade.php`) reuses `FriendService::pendingReceived($user)->count()` directly in a `@php` block — deliberately **not** a reason to convert the component to Livewire (see this file's "Navigation" section below for why it's plain Blade on purpose): a plain server-rendered count is already correct on every page load, and nothing here needs a live round-trip mid-page the way Rooms' own notifications do.
- **Originally rendered twice** from the one computed count: a small absolutely-positioned dot-with-number over the avatar circle itself, plus a matching number next to the "Friends" link inside the open panel. **The avatar-circle dot was removed the same day, on direct user instruction** — the badge should only ever appear next to "Friends" inside the dropdown, not on the toggle itself. `#user-menu-toggle` is back to rendering nothing but `<x-avatar>`; the Friends-link badge (now `id="user-menu-friends-badge"`, previously unidentified) is the only surviving copy.
  - (Historical, from the now-removed avatar-dot version, kept for the record: verifying it in a browser test caught a real gotcha — the `-top-1 -right-1` positioning classes were new to this file and the last `npm run build` predated them, so Tailwind's JIT output didn't contain those utilities yet and the badge rendered with no `position` offset at all. Confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` rather than assumed from reading the Blade file, fixed by rebuilding — the same "frontend change not visible until rebuilt" class of issue `CLAUDE.md` already calls out generally.)
- Tests: `tests/Feature/FriendsPageTest.php` (notification sent to the addressee with the right requester, not sent to a guest addressee), `tests/Feature/UserMenuTest.php` (badge absent with no pending requests, count correct and excludes requests the user *sent*, and the badge's markup only ever appears after `#user-menu-panel` opens in the raw HTML, never inside the toggle button), `tests/Browser/UserMenuBrowserTest.php` (the avatar toggle itself renders no badge element at all; the Friends-link badge is visible once the panel is opened).
- **Never actually delivered in production — root cause was infrastructure, not app code (`docs/concerns.md`, 2026-08-21).** Reported as "does no work"; email verification on sign-up worked fine the whole time, which was the key clue. Fortify's `VerifyEmail` notification sends synchronously (no `ShouldQueue`), but `FriendRequestReceived` and `GameChallengeReceived` (Challenge a Friend, see "Word Blocks" above) both deliberately queue onto Redis — and `cloud background-process:list` showed **zero background processes provisioned for this app, ever** (`inst-...`'s `backgroundProcessIds: []`), despite a real Redis/Valkey cache already attached since 2026-08-16. Every queued job for either notification had been silently piling up on the `default` Redis queue with nothing ever consuming it — not a bug in the notification code itself, which was correct as designed and already documented above. Fixed by provisioning the missing worker directly: `cloud background-process:create <instance> --type=worker --connection=redis --queue=default -n --env=production`, which runs `php artisan queue:work redis --queue=default --tries=1 --backoff=30 --sleep=10 --rest=0 --timeout=60 --quiet` continuously. No app-code change — this is purely a Laravel Cloud environment gap, the same category of "infra decision, not just an app-code one" this file's own "Image avatars" entry above already called out for the S3 bucket.

**"Challenge back" on the activity feed (`docs/TODO.md`'s Task 11, 2026-08-21)** — the Friends-page half of the puzzle-codes/daily-puzzles/Challenge-a-Friend work; see "Boggle" and "Word Blocks" above for the `GameChallengeReceived`/`GameChallengeService` infrastructure this reuses rather than duplicates. Each activity-feed row for a challengeable game type (`GameType::isChallengeable()`, currently Boggle/Word Blocks) gained a "Challenge back" button, re-sending the challenge email for the *exact* puzzle that row represents — `GameChallengeService::linkFor(GameParticipant)` resolves the right link per game type (Boggle's own shared/ranked session link; Word Blocks' direct seed link), so the page component itself needs no game-specific branching. New `$challengedParticipantIds` array (mirrors `<livewire:challenge-friend>`'s own `$sentFriendId`) drives an inline "Sent!" confirmation in place of the button once clicked. Hidden for a non-challengeable game type and for a guest-authored row (`$participant->user === null`) — the latter is currently unreachable in practice, since a guest has no username and so can never actually become a friend in the first place, but `challengeBack()` still guards it defensively since it's a public Livewire action, not just UI-hidden.
- Tests: `tests/Feature/FriendsPageTest.php` (`challengeBack` notifies with the correct per-game-type link; the button only renders for a challengeable game type).

## Navigation

`docs/TODO.md`'s former #1 ("redesign site navigation") flagged several specific inconsistencies, all in the Dashboard/Profile/Stats area of the app: an individual game page (e.g. `profile/boggle/91`) lost the Dashboard button every other page in that area had, using a plain text link instead; the top bar's button set changed page to page; "Edit Profile" sitting in the Dashboard page's own top bar felt arbitrary; and "Dashboard"/"Log Out" occupied the same nav position on different screens. Explicitly deferred: renaming "Home"/"Dashboard" (the TODO's own suggestion was to revisit that only once a real structure existed to clarify their roles — premature before this).

- **New persistent sidebar** (`resources/views/components/app-sidebar.blade.php`), covering exactly the pages the complaints were about — `/dashboard`, `/profile`, `/profile/{boggle,yahtzee,family-feud}` (index and show), `/profile/games` — and deliberately **not** the actual game-play pages (`/boggle/play` etc.), whose own button-bar chrome (Rules, fullscreen toggle, teleported per-game actions) is a separately-tuned, working system this doesn't touch. One fixed link list — Dashboard, All Games, Boggle Stats, Yahtzee Stats, Family Feud Stats, Edit Profile, then Log Out — replacing what used to be each page's own ad hoc `@teleport('#game-actions')` Dashboard button (or, on the three individual-game show pages, a plain "← X Stats" text link with no Dashboard button at all). Active link highlighted via `request()->routeIs(...)` against each link's own route name(s) — deliberately *not* a bare `routeIs('profile*')` wildcard for "Edit Profile," since that would also match every `profile.boggle`/`profile.yahtzee`/`profile.games` route (all genuinely prefixed `profile.*`) and highlight two links at once.
- **Opt-in via a layout slot** (`<x-slot:sidebar>1</x-slot:sidebar>`, same `@isset()` pattern `$rules`/`$fullscreenToggle` already use), not a second layout file — `layouts/app.blade.php` wraps `$slot` in an `.app-shell` (sidebar + `.app-main`) only when a page opts in, so every other page (game-play, homepage, auth) is completely unaffected structurally. The existing top-level button-bar (Home, Rules, fullscreen toggle) is untouched either way; the sidebar is an addition below it, not a replacement.
