- **Mobile: an off-canvas panel, not a second nav paradigm.** Below the app's existing 900px breakpoint, the sidebar becomes `position: fixed`, hidden by default, toggled by a new "Menu" button added to the button-bar's left side (only rendered when `$sidebar` is set). Toggling is a plain `display` swap with **no transition/animation** — this app avoids motion everywhere else (see the Rules dialog's backdrop, and Family Feud's spec's own "no fast or bouncing animation" rule), so an animated slide-in drawer would have been inconsistent with that, even though it's the more common pattern for this kind of UI elsewhere.
- **JS bound directly, not delegated** (`resources/js/app.js`, right next to the Rules dialog's own binding) — the sidebar and its toggle button live in the layout, rendered once per page load, never inside a Livewire component's own re-rendered root, so unlike the generic `<x-confirm-dialog>` wiring (delegated on `document` for exactly this reason — see "Low-stim confirm dialogs" above) there's nothing here that a Livewire morph could ever silently detach a listener from.
- **Removed, not just hidden**: the per-page `@teleport('#game-actions')` Dashboard buttons on `/dashboard`, `/profile`, and all three stats index pages, and the "Edit Profile"/"Log Out" buttons that used to live in the dashboard's own top bar — all now redundant with the sidebar. The individual game show pages' own "← Boggle Stats"-style text links were kept alongside the new sidebar (a contextual "back to the list I came from" affordance, not truly redundant with the sidebar's general-purpose "Boggle Stats" entry).
- **A real browser-testing gotcha hit while testing the mobile backdrop's click-to-close behavior**: `click('#app-sidebar-backdrop')` hung rather than erroring. The backdrop covers the full viewport, but Playwright's `click()` targets the element's *geometric center* by default — at a 375px mobile width, that center point sits directly under the sidebar itself (`z-index: 20` over the backdrop's `19`), so the backdrop is never "actionable" at that specific point and the click waits indefinitely rather than failing fast (unlike a genuinely ambiguous *selector*, which errors immediately — see `tests/Browser/FamilyFeudConfirmDialogsTest.php`'s own docblock for that distinct gotcha). Fixed in the test, not the app, by dispatching the click directly (`$page->script("document.getElementById('app-sidebar-backdrop').click()")`) rather than fighting Playwright's hit-testing geometry — still exercises the real listener, just not via a simulated screen-coordinate click.
- Added `tests/Feature/AppSidebarTest.php` (sidebar present on every covered page, absent on the homepage and a game-play page, exactly one link active at a time, guests redirected) and `tests/Browser/AppSidebarMobileMenuTest.php` (opens via the Menu button, closes on a link click, closes on a backdrop click) — the latter needed for the same reason every other CSS-visibility change in this app does (see "Automated browser testing" below): a Feature/HTTP test can assert the right classes/markup exist but not that the actual show/hide behavior works in a real browser.
- **The mobile off-canvas panel had a transparent background the whole time (`docs/concerns.md`, 2026-08-06)**, reported as "should not have transparent background in dashboard" — `.app-sidebar { background: var(--surface); border: 2px solid var(--surface-border); }` referenced custom properties that were never actually defined anywhere. This file's own `@theme inline` block defines `--color-surface`/`--color-surface-border` (the Tailwind-consumable tokens `bg-surface`/`border-surface-border` resolve to, both aliasing `--die`/`--die-border`) but never a bare `--surface`/`--surface-border` — an undefined `var()` with no fallback makes the whole shorthand invalid at compute time, silently falling back to the property's initial value (`transparent` for `background`), which let page content directly underneath show straight through the open mobile menu. Confirmed live via a real `getComputedStyle()` (`rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)`) before assuming the cause. Fixed by switching both rules (plus `.app-sidebar-divider`'s identical `var(--surface-border)`) to the plain-named `var(--die)`/`var(--die-border)` this file's own component classes already use everywhere else (confirmed: 26 other call sites, zero using the Tailwind-token spelling) — this was never mobile-specific, `.app-sidebar`'s background rule isn't inside any media query, so the desktop sidebar had the identical bug the whole time; just less visible there since it sits beside page content in a flex row instead of overlaying on top of it. New regression test in `tests/Browser/AppSidebarMobileMenuTest.php` asserts the real computed background color isn't fully transparent.

**"Home"/"Dashboard" renamed to "Play"/"My Stats" (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #5, 2026-08-18).** The "Navigation" section above deliberately deferred this exact rename until the new sidebar existed to clarify each page's actual role — it now does, and the two names still read as near-synonyms once you're looking at both at once (a persistent "Home" button next to a sidebar's own "Dashboard" entry). Decided directly rather than via a propose/approve round like the trademark renames below, since these aren't legally-motivated substitutions with multiple reasonable candidates — each new name just describes what the page actually does: **`/`'s persistent top-bar button, "Home" → "Play"** (it's the page you go to *to start playing a game*, welcome.blade.php's own game-picker grid), and **`/dashboard` → "My Stats"** (confirmed by reading `pages::dashboard` itself — avatar, name, a public-profile link, and `<x-player-stats>`; there's no other content on that page, so "stats" is a literal description, not a euphemism).
- Same scope discipline as the trademark renames below: user-facing copy only. Route name (`dashboard`), the Livewire view namespace (`pages::dashboard`), the sidebar link's internal `'id' => 'dashboard'`, and every code comment that describes the `/dashboard` *route* generically (`app.js`'s "App sidebar navigation (Dashboard/Profile/Stats pages only)", `app.css`'s matching comment) were all left alone — none of that is user-visible, and the route grouping itself didn't change. A couple of comments that specifically described the renamed *button*, not the route, were updated for accuracy anyway since they'd otherwise actively mislead a future reader about what's on screen (`error-page.blade.php`'s own docblock, `⚡yahtzee/play.blade.php`'s button-bar layout comment, two spots in `app.css` describing the button-bar's Home/Rules pairing).
- Changed: `layouts/app.blade.php`'s persistent button, `error-page.blade.php`'s "Go to Home" CTA, `welcome.blade.php`'s authenticated-state top-bar button, `app-sidebar.blade.php`'s first link label, and `pages::dashboard`'s `<x-slot:title>`.
- Test updates followed the identical pattern the trademark renames below already established: only assertions checking literal visible text needed to change (`WelcomePageTest`, `AppSidebarTest`, `ErrorPagesTest`, and a browser test's `click('Home')` → `click('Play')` in `RoomResumeSeatTest`) — `DashboardTest`'s own route-name-based assertions (`route('profile.boggle')`, `/dashboard` paths) were untouched, since those were never checking the button text in the first place.

**The "Home" → "Play" half of the rename above was reverted the same day, on direct user instruction — "Home" is the name being kept.** The "Dashboard" → "My Stats" half is untouched and stands. Reverted every "Play" spot the rename above introduced back to "Home": `layouts/app.blade.php`'s persistent top-bar button, `error-page.blade.php`'s CTA and docblock, the two `app.css` comments, `⚡yahtzee/play.blade.php`'s button-bar comment, and the two test files (`RoomResumeSeatTest`'s `click('Play')`, `ErrorPagesTest`'s "Go to Play"/`assertSeeInOrder` assertions) — the same discovery process that flagged the original rename's own scope (a grep for the literal string) is what found every spot to undo here too. Caught in the first place because the user's own separate `wip` commit had already reverted just the one `layouts/app.blade.php` line by hand, which desynced it from `RoomResumeSeatTest`'s still-`click('Play')` assertion and manifested as that test hanging (Playwright waits indefinitely for a locator with no match, rather than failing fast) — diagnosed via `git log`/`git show` on the unexpected commit rather than assumed to be a test bug.

**Navbar avatar dropdown replaced the sidebar's "Edit Profile"/"Log Out" and the homepage's own one-off "My Stats"/"Log Out" bar buttons (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #24, 2026-08-19).** A follow-up polish pass on the sidebar/navbar split above and Task 3's earlier image-avatar upload: those two account-level actions used to live in two different, inconsistent places depending on which page you were on (the sidebar, Dashboard/Profile/Stats pages only; the homepage's own `$barRight` slot everywhere else) instead of one consistent spot.

- **Per-game sidebar labels dropped their " Stats" suffix** (`app-sidebar.blade.php`'s `$links` array) — "Word Grid Stats" → "Word Grid", etc. — now that "Edit Profile" (the one link that genuinely needed disambiguating from the per-game entries) has moved out of the list entirely, the suffix was only ever pulling its weight for that one entry.
- **New `<x-user-menu />`, rendered directly in `layouts/app.blade.php`'s `.right`** (next to `<livewire:rooms-menu />`, not inside it) so it appears on every authenticated page — homepage included, which the old per-page-duplicated approach never achieved without copy-pasting the same three links everywhere. Deliberately **plain Blade, not a Livewire component**: unlike Rooms (real per-request server state — notifications, membership), this is three static links with nothing a Livewire round trip would ever need to update, so a Livewire component would have been pure overhead. `@auth`-gated (matching how `welcome.blade.php`'s own old `@guest`/`@else` split already worked) — a throwaway room guest account is still `Auth::check()`-true and gets the same menu as a registered user, same as the app's existing guest-account precedent elsewhere.
- **Toggle button is `<x-avatar>` itself** (`auth()->user()->name`/`avatarUrl()`, the same component Dashboard and public profile pages already use), stripped of the base `@layer base button` chrome via plain Tailwind utilities (`p-0 border-none bg-transparent`) so it reads as a round avatar, not a bordered rectangle with a picture inside it.
- **Open/close/position JS factored into a shared `registerNavbarDropdown(toggleId, panelId)` helper** (`resources/js/app.js`), replacing what used to be Rooms-menu-specific inline code — called once for `rooms-menu-toggle`/`-panel` and once for the new `user-menu-toggle`/`-panel`, rather than a second near-duplicate ~40-line block. Same delegated-`document`-click reasoning as the original (survives a Livewire morph resetting Rooms' own open state) and the same JS-computed position (not bare CSS `right-0`/`top-full`, which broke once before — see the Room system section above — once `.button-bar .right` wraps on a narrow viewport).
- **Menu items match `<livewire:rooms-menu>`'s own established look** rather than inventing a new one: "Stats"/"Edit Profile" are plain `<a>` tags (no `@layer base` button chrome applies to anchors at all, so they read as flat menu rows with just a hover highlight — the same as Rooms' own room-list rows), and "Logout" (a `<form>`-wrapped submit button, since logging out needs a real POST+CSRF) keeps the base button's bordered/backgrounded look unstripped, matching Rooms' own "Join a Room"/"Create a Room" buttons rather than special-casing just this one to look different.
- **`welcome.blade.php`'s `barRight` slot dropped its `@else` branch entirely** — guest-only now (Sign In/Create an Account); an authenticated visitor gets the navbar-wide dropdown instead of a page-specific duplicate.
- Regression tests: `tests/Feature/UserMenuTest.php` (guest sees nothing, authenticated sees the toggle/links/routes, renders identically on a sidebar page too, `<x-avatar>` actually wired in with the right name), `tests/Browser/UserMenuBrowserTest.php` (open/close, each of the three links actually navigating/logging out, the same narrow-viewport positioning check `RoomsMenuBrowserTest.php` already has for its sibling dropdown), and updates to `AppSidebarTest.php`/`WelcomePageTest.php` for the moved content — the sidebar's own "Edit Profile"/" Stats"-suffix assertions are checked against just the `<aside>` HTML fragment, not the whole page, since e.g. a stats page's own `<h1>` legitimately still says "Word Grid Stats" (a page heading, out of scope here) and would otherwise collide with a page-wide `assertDontSee`.

**Same-day follow-up: the avatar sat too close to the Rooms button, and wasn't the same height as it.** `ml-2` on the toggle's wrapping div adds real space beyond `.right`'s own 10px flex gap. Height-matching turned out to need a real fix, not a one-line one: the first attempt was pure CSS (`align-self: stretch` + `aspect-square` on the toggle button, `<x-avatar size="w-full h-full">` filling it) — reasoning that `.right` has no `align-items` override, so its default (`stretch`) should already size every child to the row's tallest sibling (ordinarily the Rooms button, whose own height is `clamp(vmin)` padding/font-size-driven, not a fixed px value — see app.css's base `button` rule). Confirmed live via `getBoundingClientRect()` that it didn't work: the toggle collapsed to ~9.5px instead of matching the Rooms button's real ~58.5px, almost certainly a percentage-height (`h-full`) child failing to resolve against a parent whose own height comes from flex-stretch interacting with `aspect-ratio` rather than an explicit CSS `height` — a genuinely gnarly combination, not reliable across engines. Replaced with `syncAvatarToggleSize()` in `app.js`: measures `#rooms-menu-toggle`'s real rendered height and copies it directly onto `#user-menu-toggle`'s inline `width`/`height`, re-run on `resize` (the `clamp(vmin)` height genuinely changes with viewport) and once `document.fonts.ready` resolves (the fallback font at first paint under `font-display: swap` can have slightly different metrics than the real one) — the same "measure it for real, don't try to derive it in CSS" approach `registerNavbarDropdown()` already uses for the dropdown panels' own position, applied to sizing instead. New browser test asserts the two toggles' real heights match (within 1px), the avatar stays square (not squashed by a mismatched width), and the gap exceeds the base 10px.

**Second same-day follow-up: the Edit Profile page (`/profile`) dropped its own sidebar** — now that "Edit Profile" is reached via the navbar avatar dropdown rather than a sidebar entry, the sidebar's remaining purpose is stats navigation only, and `/profile` isn't a stats page. Removed `pages::profile`'s own `<x-slot:sidebar>1</x-slot:sidebar>`, falling through to `layouts/app.blade.php`'s plain (no `.app-shell`) rendering path — the same one every non-sidebar page already uses, so no new layout code was needed. Confirmed live via a real screenshot that the page still centers correctly without it. `AppSidebarTest.php`'s "appears on every Dashboard/Profile/Stats page" test dropped `/profile` from its list (renamed to "Dashboard/Stats"), and gained a new sibling assertion (`the sidebar does not appear on the Edit Profile page`) matching the existing homepage/game-play-page "does not appear" cases.

**Third follow-up (2026-08-20): the avatar's position in `.right` shifted per page, and could render non-round at some sizes.** Both requested together, after Word Blocks shipped with its own `#game-actions` buttons (Restart/New Game/End Game) sitting *before* the avatar — every page's own extra buttons (a game's End Game, the fullscreen toggle, `$barRight`) rendered ahead of `<x-user-menu />` in the DOM, so the avatar's on-screen position varied with however many buttons a given page happened to add next to it.

- **Fixed by moving `<x-user-menu />` to render last in `layouts/app.blade.php`'s `.right`** — after `#game-actions`, `$barRight`, and the fullscreen toggle, rather than immediately after `<livewire:rooms-menu />`. The dropdown panel itself (`absolute right-0 top-full`, positioned relative to its own wrapping div) needed no changes — its position was already independent of where that div sits in the row. New regression test (`tests/Browser/UserMenuBrowserTest.php`): visits a page with real extra buttons in `.right` (Word Grid's play page — End Game + fullscreen toggle) and asserts the avatar toggle is inside `.right`'s *last* child, not just checking a button-free page like the homepage where the bug could never have shown up.
- **Oval bug: reproduction was inconclusive despite extensive live testing** (resize events at 10+ widths across 3 pages, a genuinely fresh mobile-viewport load via device emulation to rule out a resize-event self-correction masking a load-time-only bug, extreme widths down to 180px to try to force `.right`'s flexbox to actually compress something) — every measured case came back a perfect square. Rather than leave it unfixed for lack of a reliable repro, closed two real, independently-defensible gaps found along the way: (1) `syncAvatarToggleSize()` (`app.js`) was applying the Rooms button's raw fractional `getBoundingClientRect().height` (essentially never a whole number, since it comes from `clamp(vmin)` font/padding math) directly as both `width` and `height` — numerically identical fractional CSS lengths can still rasterize to a different device-pixel count per axis under some zoom/DPR combinations, which reads as a hairline-not-quite-circle; now rounded to a whole pixel first, which rasterizes identically on both axes everywhere. (2) Neither `#user-menu-toggle` nor its wrapping div carried `shrink-0` — unlike `<x-avatar>` itself, which already defends against exactly this — leaving both flex-shrinkable (`flexShrink: 1`, confirmed live) inside `.right`'s row, a latent risk for exactly the width-only-compresses symptom described even though it couldn't be forced to manifest in this environment. Both fixes are cheap, correct regardless of which (if either) was the real mechanism on the user's own device, and were kept rather than reverted for lack of a red test to turn green.

**Fourth follow-up (2026-08-20): the oval bug was still present, because the third follow-up never measured the right element.** Both prior investigations (this section and the one above) only ever read `#user-menu-toggle`'s own `getBoundingClientRect()` — the *outer* button, whose width/height are set directly and equally by `syncAvatarToggleSize()`, so it was always going to measure square regardless of what was actually visible. The real bug was one level deeper: the *inner* `<x-avatar>` element (`w-full`/`h-full` of the button's own content box) is what a user actually sees, and its shape depends on the button's padding too, which that JS never touches at all.

- **Root cause: two plain, unlayered rules in `app.css` — `.button-bar button` (`max-width: 360px`) and `.button-bar.many-buttons button` (`max-width: 480px`) — silently beat the avatar toggle's own `p-0` (Tailwind, also unlayered).** Both are unlayered, so this app's own cascade-layers convention (see this file's comment at `@layer base` in `app.css`: "an unlayered utility class always wins over anything in `base`") doesn't apply between them — ordinary specificity decides, and `.button-bar button` (`0,1,1`) or `.button-bar.many-buttons button` (`0,2,1`) both outrank a single-class `.p-0` (`0,1,0`). Both rules set *only* `padding-left`/`padding-right`, never top/bottom — so the button's outer box (sized by the JS above) stayed square, but its inner content box (padding eats into it, and that's exactly what `w-full`/`h-full` measures against) became narrower than tall: a portrait oval. `welcome.blade.php` sets the `manyButtons` slot unconditionally, so this fired on the homepage at any real width ≤480px — one of the most common phone-width ranges there is — which is exactly what the user reported and the prior two investigations, both scoped to the outer toggle, never caught.
- **Confirmed live specifically via `on()->iPhoneSE()` device emulation, not `resize()`.** `resize(320, 667)` to the identical `window.innerWidth` the device preset reports left the affected element's computed padding at `0px` in this environment (`window.matchMedia('(max-width: 480px)').matches` was `true` either way, so the media query itself wasn't the variable) — a genuine, reproducible discrepancy between the two that CLAUDE.md's "Automated Browser Testing" section now documents as a third `pest-plugin-browser` gotcha, not root-caused further. This is almost certainly why the third follow-up's own resize()-based sweep came back clean.
- **Fixed with `:not(#user-menu-toggle)` on both selectors** — the same exclusion pattern `.button-bar .right button:not(#chrome-toggle-btn)` already uses elsewhere in `app.css` for a different button that needs to opt out of a shared rule. `npm run build` re-run to verify locally (`public/build` is gitignored, rebuilt on deploy).
- **New regression test** (`tests/Browser/UserMenuBrowserTest.php`, "the avatar stays circular, not oval, on a real mobile viewport") measures the *inner* avatar element specifically, via `on()->iPhoneSE()` — confirmed it actually fails without the fix (24×36 measured, not 36×36) before confirming it passes with it, rather than trusting a green test that might not have been exercising the bug at all (the same trap the third follow-up's own hand-verification fell into).

**Daily-puzzles discoverability gap, caught by the user right after the feature shipped (2026-08-21).** The Task 11 work above (see "Boggle"/"Word Blocks") built the whole daily-puzzle mechanism — the lock, the resume, the `/daily-puzzles` archive — but never actually linked to any of it: no nav entry anywhere, no way to reach today's puzzle except hand-typing `?dailyDate=...` in the URL or already being on the archive page (itself unreachable), and no on-page indicator while playing that a given game *was* a daily rather than a random puzzle, or which date. Fixed with three additions, none of which needed touching the underlying daily-puzzle logic itself:
- **Site-wide nav link**: a "Daily Puzzles" button added to `layouts/app.blade.php`'s persistent top-left bar, next to Home — deliberately not gated `@auth` (guests can browse/play the archive same as any random puzzle) and not homepage-only, so it's one click away from literally any page.
- **Today pulled out of the archive table into its own card.** `pages::daily-puzzles` previously listed all 30 days, today included, as just the list's first row — easy to miss and no more emphasized than any other day. `getDaysProperty()`'s return type changed from a plain array to a `Collection` so the template can `->skip(1)` past today for the table itself; today now gets a bordered, accent-colored card above it with two large "Play Today's Word Grid"/"Play Today's Word Blocks" buttons.
- **In-play "Daily Puzzle — {date}" badge**, added to both games' own HUD area (`$isDaily`/`$dailyDate` were already tracked server-side, just never rendered anywhere except the post-game reveal screen's countdown) — visible from the moment the board loads through the reveal screen, not just after finishing, so there's no point during play where it's ambiguous whether the current game is the daily or a random one.
- Tests: `tests/Feature/Puzzles/DailyPuzzlesArchiveTest.php` gained the nav-link check and the "Play Today's..." card check (also picked up a missing `RefreshDatabase` on this file while touching it — its two original tests were only passing by accident of cache/test-ordering, not because the DB was actually set up), `tests/Feature/Games/{Boggle,Quartiles}DailyTest.php` each gained a test confirming the in-play badge shows for a daily and is absent for a random game.

**Full navbar redesign (2026-08-21) — the top bar had drifted into a different shape on nearly every page type, and wrapped onto multiple rows on mobile.** User-reported: the bar mixed global site navigation (Home, Daily Puzzles, Rooms) with per-game controls (Rules, End Game/New Game/Reveal/Watch only/..., the fullscreen "hide chrome" toggle) in one undifferentiated row, so a bare page showed 2-4 items, a game-play page showed up to 7, and an online-room game (Farkle/Yahtzee's own `many-buttons` case) could wrap to 2-3 lines on a narrow phone — `.button-bar .right`'s `flex-wrap` (added earlier specifically to stop that overflowing off-screen, see the Room system section) only ever contained the symptom, not the cause. The existing per-page sidebar (see this section's "New persistent sidebar" entry above) already solved exactly this class of problem for stats pages; this redesign generalizes that pattern site-wide instead of inventing a third one.

- **The bar is now a fixed, small set of items on every page, never more**: `[Home, Menu]` on the left, `[Rooms, Game Menu (game pages only), fullscreen toggle (game pages only), Avatar]` on the right — 2-5 items regardless of how many actions a given game needs, since those actions no longer render as loose buttons in `.right` at all.
- **`<x-app-sidebar>` generalized from a Dashboard/Profile/Stats-only sidebar into the one site-wide "Menu" drawer**, rendered unconditionally in `layouts/app.blade.php` (the old `@isset($sidebar)` gate is gone, and with it `.app-shell`/`.app-main`'s flex-row layout — the drawer is a pure overlay at every viewport width now, not persistent-on-desktop/off-canvas-on-mobile, so there's one interaction model instead of two). Its link list grew to cover what used to be scattered across the bar: Daily Puzzles, the existing per-game stats links, Friends (with its pending-request badge, moved out of the avatar dropdown — see below), About, Privacy Policy, and (`@guest`) Sign In/Create an Account, previously only reachable from the homepage's own one-off `$barRight` slot and now available from anywhere.
- **New "Game Menu" dropdown** (`#game-menu-toggle`/`#game-menu-panel`, same `registerNavbarDropdown()` open/close/position pattern as Rooms/the avatar) holds Rules and the `#game-actions` teleport target — every per-game action button (End Game, New Game, Reveal, Watch only, Scores, Back to room, ...) that used to render directly in `.right`. Gated on `isset($rules) || isset($fullscreenToggle)`, not just the latter — Quartiles sets only `$rules` (no fullscreen toggle), so gating on `$fullscreenToggle` alone silently dropped both the Rules button *and* the `#game-actions` div for that one game, and Livewire's own `@teleport` throws (`placeInDom`: `Cannot read properties of null (reading 'appendChild')`) the instant its target page tries to render into a target that doesn't exist, rather than no-op — caught via a real Playwright `pageerror` listener (`page.on('pageerror', ...)`, run standalone since `assertNoJavaScriptErrors()` only surfaces the bare message, no stack) after `tests/Browser/QuartilesFoundQuartileTest.php` hung clicking a submit button that Livewire's `wire:click` had never actually bound to (the teleport crash aborted Livewire's own tree init for that page entirely).
- **The fullscreen/chrome-hide toggle stays its own persistent icon button, deliberately *not* inside the Game Menu dropdown** — it's the one control that has to remain reachable after it hides everything else in the bar (`body.chrome-hidden`'s existing CSS), including Game Menu's own toggle button; nesting it inside a panel that's itself hidden by default would leave no way back to un-hide the chrome.
- **Rooms kept as its own persistent bar icon, not folded into the Menu drawer** (a deliberate deviation from the first version of this plan, which had it moving into the drawer alongside Daily Puzzles/Stats/Friends) — like the avatar, it carries live state (room-switch/game-starting notifications pushed via presence channels, an up-to-date room list) that benefits from single-click access from anywhere rather than open-drawer-then-click.
- **Avatar dropdown trimmed to Edit Profile/Logout only** — Stats and Friends (previously duplicated between the avatar dropdown and, after this redesign, the new Menu drawer) moved into the drawer exclusively, so the two navbar menus stay disjoint: the avatar answers "who am I logged in as and how do I manage that" (the rendered avatar image itself does that before the dropdown even opens), the drawer is site content/navigation.
- **`.many-buttons`'s whole compact-mode CSS block removed** (`app.css`), along with its two JS opt-ins (`Farkle`/`Yahtzee ⚡play.blade.php`'s `document.querySelector('.button-bar')?.classList.add('many-buttons')` on entering an online room) and the `body.scrollable:has(.button-bar .right > :nth-child(2))` extra-top-padding reservation for a bar that might wrap to 2 lines — none of it can be triggered anymore now that `.right` is bounded. Removing the reservation freed a few px of vertical space on every page, which was enough to flip one existing regression test's boundary assertion (`FarkleMobileLayoutTest`'s 8-player scoreboard at 390×500 no longer needed to scroll to reach every row: `scrollHeight`/`clientHeight` both landed at exactly 401 instead of `scrollHeight >` `clientHeight`) — confirmed as a real, correct side effect (more available height, not a regression) by testing a range of viewport heights before accepting it, not just patching the assertion blind.
- **Two more `assertSee`/`click()` collisions found via the same class of bug already documented in `FamilyFeudConfirmDialogsTest.php`'s own docblock** (Pest's browser plugin doesn't fail fast when a selector/text match is ambiguous or hidden — it hangs until the browser's own timeout): the site footer's existing "Privacy Policy" link now collides with the drawer's own new "Privacy Policy" entry (`tests/Browser/PrivacyPolicyFooterLinkTest.php` fixed to click a footer-scoped CSS selector instead of plain text), and several existing tests asserted `Sign In`/`Daily Puzzles`/game-action button text directly rather than opening the Menu/Game Menu drawer first now that that content lives behind them (`HomepageSmokeTest`, `BoggleDailyResumeTest`, `ButtonBarOverflowTest`, and ~15 other Browser tests across every game that clicks a teleported action button, updated to `click('#game-menu-toggle')`/`click('#sidebar-menu-toggle')` before interacting with content now inside either drawer).
- **A stale `public/build` cost significant debugging time before being identified as the actual cause of several apparent test hangs**: editing `resources/js/app.js`/`resources/css/app.css` without re-running `npm run build` left the real browser loading the *old* compiled JS mid-investigation, so a brand-new click handler (`registerNavbarDropdown('game-menu-toggle', ...)`) silently didn't exist yet — Playwright's `click()` on the resulting permanently-hidden `#rules-btn` then hung indefinitely rather than erroring, reading exactly like a bug in the new interaction code rather than a build-freshness gap. Confirmed via `public/build/manifest.json`'s mtime predating the source edits; resolved by rebuilding before every subsequent browser-test run.
- Full suite (866 tests: 741 Unit/Feature + 125 Browser) green after all of the above. `tests/Feature/AppSidebarTest.php` rewritten for the drawer's new universal scope (appears on the homepage/a game-play page/every stats page now, not just the latter; lists Daily Puzzles/Friends/About/Privacy alongside the existing stats links; guest-only Sign In/Create an Account) and `tests/Feature/UserMenuTest.php` for the trimmed avatar panel (Edit Profile/Logout only, explicitly asserts Stats/Friends are *not* present in that panel's own HTML fragment).

**Same-week follow-up (2026-08-22): user feedback in `docs/concerns.md` on the redesign above — Home and Rooms should live in the Menu drawer too, the Menu toggle should be an icon not a word, "Game Menu" should just say "Game," and the Game Menu covered the score/timer HUD on mobile.** All five addressed; `docs/concerns.md`'s own Menu section cleared afterward per its role as a live backlog (mirrors `docs/TODO.md`'s "delete once done, the writeup lives here instead" convention) — this entry is that writeup.

- **Home and Rooms moved out of the bar into the Menu drawer.** Home became `<x-app-sidebar>`'s first link (`id="sidebar-link-home"`, active-highlighted via `request()->is('/')`). Rooms was a bigger change: `<livewire:rooms-menu>` itself dropped its own toggle-button/dropdown-panel wrapper entirely (it used to be a second, independent dropdown living in the bar) and now renders its content — the room list, guest-name field, Join/Create a Room — directly as static-looking rows inside the drawer, reusing `.app-sidebar-link` styling rather than its old bespoke panel classes. The fixed-position notification toasts (`onGameSwitched`/`onGameStarting`) needed no changes — they were never part of the dropdown panel's own box to begin with. Net effect: the bar's left side is down to one button.
- **The Menu toggle is now a plain 3-line hamburger SVG, not the word "Menu."** Its layout/sizing rules were split out of `.fullscreen-toggle` into a new shared `.icon-btn` class (`clamp(34px, 7vmin, 46px)`, centered content) so both it and the fullscreen toggle draw from one definition instead of duplicating it.
- **The avatar toggle switched from a JS-measured size to the same fixed `.icon-btn` sizing.** It used to copy the Rooms button's own real rendered height at runtime (`syncAvatarToggleSize()` in `app.js`) specifically because a font/padding-driven text button has no fixed px height to match in CSS alone — but Rooms just moved out of `.right` entirely, so there was nothing left in the bar to measure against most of the time. Deleted `syncAvatarToggleSize()` and its resize/`fonts.ready` listeners outright rather than leaving now-dead measurement code around; the avatar's regression test was rewritten to compare against the fullscreen toggle (a real sibling `.icon-btn` on game pages) instead of the now-gone Rooms button.
- **"Game Menu" → "Game," and — the actual `docs/concerns.md` bug report — converted from an absolutely-positioned dropdown into a real `<x-dialog>` modal.** Confirmed live before touching anything: opening the old dropdown on Word Grid at a 320px-wide viewport produced a panel spanning y≈66–174px, directly overlapping the HUD's own y≈96–140px showing time/score/words — a real, reproducible bug, not a vague complaint. A modal makes the takeover an obvious, deliberate one (the same dimmed backdrop every other dialog in this app already uses — Rules, Join a Room, every confirm dialog) instead of an ambiguous partial overlap that reads as a layout bug. Opened via the same `data-dialog-open` delegated handler every other dialog uses now, not `registerNavbarDropdown()` — it's not a dropdown anymore, so `resources/js/app.js` lost that registration along with the Rooms one, leaving the helper with exactly one consumer (the avatar).
- **Converting to a real modal introduced a new bug of its own, caught by the existing test suite, not assumed away**: `#game-menu-dialog` now blocks interaction with anything behind it while open, but every teleported per-game action button (End Game, New Game, Reveal, ...) used to render into a plain, non-modal panel that never blocked anything — so after clicking one, the dialog stayed open on top of whatever page content the action revealed. Concretely: Word Grid's own post-game "Challenge a Friend" button renders in the page body, not inside the dialog, and Playwright's `click()` on it hung waiting for an element that was present but non-interactive behind the modal's own backdrop. Fixed with a new delegated listener in `app.js`: closes `#game-menu-dialog` on any click within `#game-actions` that has a `wire:click` ancestor. The `wire:click` condition matters, not just "any click in `#game-actions`" — Farkle/Family Feud's own confirm dialogs (`<x-confirm-dialog>`) are *also* teleported inside `#game-actions`, so they're nested inside `#game-menu-dialog` too; closing the outer dialog unconditionally on the trigger's own click (`data-dialog-open`, no `wire:click`) made the nested confirm dialog invisible right along with it — confirmed live (`getComputedStyle()` showed `display: none` despite the confirm dialog's own `.open` being `true`) — because a closed ancestor `<dialog>` forces `display: none` on every descendant regardless of the descendant's own open state, `showModal()` notwithstanding. Scoping the close to `wire:click` elements correctly leaves the dialog open for a trigger (opens a nested confirm on top) and a plain Cancel button (`data-dialog-close`, no `wire:click` — returns to the Game dialog's own options), while still closing on the confirm button itself (`wire:click` + `data-dialog-close` together) once the action is actually taken.
- **That same "closing the dialog now requires reopening it before the next teleported click" consequence rippled through ~10 existing Browser tests**, each written when these actions lived in an always-open, non-modal panel: any test that clicked two teleported buttons in sequence (End Game → New Game, Watch only → Player view, a confirm button → the button its resulting state exposes) needed a `click('#game-menu-toggle')` inserted between them. Found by running the full suite repeatedly and bisecting each fresh hang to its exact failing click (`BoggleNewGameTest`, `QuartilesGameActionsButtonsTest`, `FarkleLocalPlayTest`, `FarkleBackToRoomTest`, `FamilyFeudConfirmDialogsTest`, `FarkleRoomActionsTest`, `YahtzeeRoomBrowserTest`, `ClearTheDeckRoomBrowserTest`) rather than trying to enumerate every affected sequence up front from reading the code alone.
- Full suite (866 tests) green afterward.

## Game renaming (trademark avoidance)

`docs/TODO.md`'s former #15 flagged that "Boggle," "Yahtzee," and "Family Feud" are all real trademarked game names this app had been using verbatim for its own reinterpretations. The task's own scope note (carried over from when it was written): rename the games everywhere a user can actually see the name — routes, enum case names/values, PHP class names, CSS classes, `wire:key`/element ids, and internal test code were all deliberately left alone, since none of that is user-facing and churning it site-wide would have been a large, risky diff for zero user-visible benefit.

- **Names chosen collaboratively**: asked the user whether to propose candidates or have them dictate directly; they chose "propose and I'll approve." Recommended and got approval for **Boggle → "Word Grid"**, **Yahtzee → "Yacht"** ("Yacht" is in fact the real, older, non-trademarked dice game Yahtzee itself was commercially derived from — same category structure, so the rename reads as a return to the original name rather than an arbitrary substitution), and **Family Feud → "Survey Says"** (the show's own iconic catchphrase, immediately recognizable without reusing the protected title).
- **`GameType::label()` (`app/Enums/GameType.php`) is the single highest-leverage change point** — it's what the dashboard tiles, sidebar links, recent-games table, and the All Games page all already rendered through (`$stat['type']->label()`, `$participant->gameSession->type->label()`), so fixing it there propagated the new names to every one of those surfaces for free. Enum case names/values (`Boggle`, `Yahtzee`, `FamilyFeud`) were deliberately left unchanged — internal identifiers, not user-visible, and changing them would have touched routes, DB-persisted `type` columns, and dozens of code references for no user-facing gain.
- **`YahtzeeCategory::Yahtzee`'s own `label()`** (the five-of-a-kind scoring category, historically named after the game itself) changed from `'Yahtzee'` to `'Yacht'` too, for the same reason as the game's own name — the real historical "Yacht" game's own top category is identically named, so this isn't a new invention, just the same substitution applied one level deeper. The enum case name and its `'yahtzee'` value were left alone, same rationale as `GameType`.
- **Everything not derived from those two enums needed a manual per-file sweep**: every `<x-slot:title>`, page `<h1>`/`.heading` div, the homepage's game cards, in-game rules text (Yahtzee/Yacht's own rules prose literally explains the "Yahtzee"/now "Yacht" category and its bonus, so that had to change too, not just the page title), empty-state copy ("No Boggle games recorded yet." etc.), back-links ("← Boggle Stats"), the Family Feud "no questions seeded" error message, and the site-wide default `<meta name="description">` fallback in `layouts/app.blade.php`. Confirmed complete via a final `grep -rniE 'boggle|yahtzee|family feud' resources/views/` sweep — every remaining hit was a route name, class name, PHP variable/array key (`yahtzeeBonusCount`), CSS class (`boggle-play`, `yahtzee-play`), element id, or code comment, all correctly out of scope per the task's own instructions.
- **Test assertions needed the same fix where they asserted literal old-name visible text** (`assertSee('Boggle')`, `assertSee('Yahtzee Room')`, etc., in `AppSidebarTest`, `PublicProfileTest`, `DashboardTest`, `YahtzeeLocalPlayTest`, `GameLobbySwitchGameBrowserTest`) — these assert what a real user sees, so they're a genuine (if narrow) part of "everywhere a user can see the name," unlike the many test *function names*/array keys (`'boggle'`, `yahtzeeBonusCount`, `boggleParticipantFor(...)`) that were correctly left alone as internal-only.

**Revisited the routes/URLs half of the same tradeoff, deliberately left alone at launch (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #9, 2026-08-17).** URLs are more "visible" than the internal identifiers the original rename above left untouched — shared, bookmarked, and shown in the address bar — so `/boggle`, `/yahtzee`, and `/family-feud` (and every sub-path under them) were renamed to match the already-approved display names: `/word-grid`, `/yacht`, `/survey-says`. Exactly mirrors how Farkle's own `/farkle` → `/10000` rename was done (see the Farkle section above) — same reasoning, same scope boundary, just applied to the three games it hadn't reached yet:
- **Path segments only.** `routes/web.php`'s literal path strings changed (`/boggle` → `/word-grid`, `/boggle/play`, `/boggle/group`, `/boggle/room/{code}`, `/boggle/share/{gameSession}` and their Yahtzee/Family Feud equivalents; `/profile/boggle` → `/profile/word-grid`, `/profile/yahtzee` → `/profile/yacht`, `/profile/family-feud` → `/profile/survey-says`). Route *names* (`boggle.lobby`, `boggle.play`, `boggle.room`, `boggle.share`, `yahtzee.play`, `yahtzee.roller`, `yahtzee.room`, `family-feud.play`, `family-feud.room`, `profile.boggle`, `profile.boggle.show`, `profile.yahtzee`, `profile.yahtzee.show`, `profile.family-feud`, `profile.family-feud.show`), the `pages::boggle.*`/`pages::yahtzee.*`/`pages::family-feud.*` Livewire view namespaces, `GameType`'s enum case names/values, `GameType::roomRoute()`/`playRoute()` (both return route *names*, so needed zero changes), PHP class/namespace names, CSS classes, and element ids are all untouched — since every other caller already goes through `route()` by name, none of them needed to change, the same "one central rename point" property the Farkle path rename already established.
- **No compatibility redirects for the old paths**, same as Farkle's own rename — consistent with this app's general avoidance of backwards-compat shims absent an actual request for one, and matching the "large, risky diff for no clear benefit" reasoning the original 2026-08-06 rename decision already used to justify *not* touching routes at the time.
- Every literal hardcoded path string outside `routes/web.php` also needed updating — found via `grep` for `'/boggle`, `'/yahtzee`, `'/family-feud` (and their `/profile/...` counterparts) rather than assumed complete: `tests/Feature/Games/{Boggle,Yahtzee,FamilyFeud}{Room,Game}Test.php`, `tests/Feature/{AppSidebar,Profile{Boggle,Yahtzee,FamilyFeud}}Test.php`, and `tests/Browser/{BoggleHostedRoomsDropdown,GameLobbySwitchGame,Yahtzee*,FamilyFeud*,StatsTableMobileScroll}Test.php`. No hardcoded `href`/`url()` path literals existed in `resources/views` (everything there already went through `route()`), and no path strings existed in `public/sw.js`/`resources/js` either — confirmed by grep, not assumed, before calling the sweep complete.
- Full suite (624 tests) green afterward, plus `php artisan route:list` confirming the 15 renamed routes register with their new paths and the 3 old top-level paths (`/boggle`, `/yahtzee`, `/family-feud`) no longer resolve to anything at all.

**Word Blocks → "Quads" (`docs/TODO.md`'s former Task 24, 2026-08-26).** Same scope boundary as every rename above: `GameType::label()`'s `self::Quartiles` arm, plus every `<x-slot:title>`/heading/button/meta-description that rendered the old name (homepage card, `/daily-puzzles`' intro copy and per-day links, the play page's title/description/rules-title, and the profile stats index/show pages) — internal `Quartiles` enum case name/value, `quartiles.play`/`profile.quartiles` route *names*, PHP class names, and CSS classes all left alone, matching the original "in code isn't important" precedent `GameType::label()`'s own docblock already documents. Checked before committing to the name (per this task's own note to "confirm 'Quads' is similarly safe"): the game already uses "Quad" as its in-page term for a found 4-tile bonus group (`docs/specs/quartiles.md`), so naming the whole game "Quads" means the game's own copy now reads "Quads: find all 5 hidden Quads for a bonus" — a real self-referential overlap, flagged to the user directly rather than guessed past; they chose to proceed with "Quads" for both anyway rather than renaming one of the two.
- **Also renamed the URL path this time, not just the display name and route-adjacent text**: `/word-blocks` → `/quads` and `/profile/word-blocks` → `/profile/quads` in `routes/web.php` (route *names* `quartiles.play`/`profile.quartiles`/`profile.quartiles.show` unchanged, so every `route()` call site needed zero changes). Unlike the original three-game rename above, which left routes for a deliberately separate later pass, this one folded the path rename in immediately — the established pattern by this point is that every other game's path already matches its current display name (`/word-grid`, `/yacht`, `/five-letters`, `/word-hive`), so leaving `/word-blocks` behind post-rename would have been the one inconsistent holdout rather than a deliberate scope boundary.
- Found via the same grep-before-declaring-done discipline as the routes revisit above: `grep -rln "Word Blocks"` and a separate `grep -rln "word-blocks"` across `resources/`, `app/`, `routes/`, and `tests/` before and after editing, rather than trusting the TODO item's own (undercounted) file list. Remaining `"Word Blocks"` hits after the pass are all internal PHPDoc comments referencing the old name as a familiar shorthand for shared code patterns (e.g. "same shape as Word Blocks' own `$foundWords`") — correctly out of scope, not missed.
- Test fallout: path literals in `tests/Browser/Quartiles*Test.php`/`tests/Feature/{Games/Quartiles,ProfileQuartiles}Test.php`, plus literal `assertSee('Word Blocks')`/test-name-string occurrences in `WelcomePageTest`, `GameCardResumeTest`, `QuartilesStatsServiceTest`, and `DailyPuzzlesArchiveTest` — all updated, full suite green afterward.

## Site-wide design system & chrome

Every earlier phase above was functionally complete on its own, but the front end was initially a Tailwind-utility *reinterpretation* of `legacy/*.css`'s design system rather than a faithful port, Boggle's word-building was a tap-per-die + Submit/Clear pair (not the real drag/pointer gesture), two whole legacy pages (`boggle-static.html`, `yahtzee-roller.html`) and the About page had never been built, and there was no consistent button-bar/site-footer/rules-dialog chrome across pages. This section covers that cross-cutting design/chrome work, plus every bug found in it since (several of which affected every game at once, not just the one being tested when they were caught).

**Design system port + drag gesture:**
- Ported `legacy/shared.css` + `legacy/boggle-shared.css` into `resources/css/app.css` near-verbatim as real CSS classes (full OKLCH token set, `.button-bar`, `.site-footer`, `dialog.rules-dialog`, `.setup`, `.option`, the fullscreen/chrome-hide system, Boggle's board/word-list panel classes), rather than Tailwind-utility reinterpretations.
- Rebuilt `layouts/app.blade.php` with a real button-bar (Home + optional Rules + optional fullscreen-toggle via named Blade slots) and site-footer, consistent on every page.
- Rebuilt Boggle's play page with the real pointer-capture drag/tap word-building gesture (`stepPath`/`nearestCandidateCell`/`processDragPoint`, diagonal-neighbor bias, coalesced-pointermove sub-stepping) via a `@script` block operating on client-only path state, submitting the finished word to a new `submitPath()` action in one round trip. The countdown timer moved from `wire:poll.1s` to a pure client-side `setInterval` — a per-second server round trip would let Livewire's morph clobber the JS-only `.selected`/order-badge state mid-drag. Deliberately not ported: the live "valid word in progress" dictionary check (would ship a second copy of the dictionary to the client) and JS-measured layout fine-tuning (CSS fallback values used instead).
- Added Boggle's group-play variant (`pages::boggle.group` at `/boggle/group`), the Yahtzee dice-only roller (`pages::yahtzee.roller` at `/yahtzee/roller`, pure Alpine, no Livewire round trips), the About page, and a fidelity pass on `welcome.blade.php`.
- Two real, previously-invisible bugs found while doing this pass (both silently broke color/sizing on **every** page using `bg-accent`, `px-*`, etc.):
  1. `@theme { --color-accent: var(--accent); }` silently failed to generate `bg-accent` (while `text-accent`/`border-accent` worked) — Tailwind v4 only partially resolves utilities from theme tokens that reference *other* runtime CSS custom properties unless the block is `@theme inline { ... }`. Every `bg-accent`-styled CTA site-wide was silently rendering as the plain surface color instead of accent blue until fixed.
  2. Cascade-layer priority, not specificity, decided the fight between new global `button {}`/`input {}` rules and Tailwind's own utility classes — an unlayered custom rule always outranks any rule inside a `@layer` regardless of specificity or source order. Fixed by wrapping the element-default rules in `@layer base`.
- Also worth remembering: a CSS `/* ... */` comment containing the literal substring `*/` anywhere in its own text closes the comment early and turns the rest into broken real CSS — surfaces as a cryptic `CssSyntaxError: Missing opening (` pointing nowhere near the actual cause.

**The `@teleport` button-bar bug (the single most-repeated bug class in this app's history):**
- **First discovery**: every dynamic button-bar button was permanently broken. Boggle's End Game/New Game toggle, Yahtzee's New Game button, Family Feud's Reveal/End Game/New Game — all originally passed through a `<x-slot:barRight>` on the *layout*, but a Livewire full-page component's AJAX round trip only ever re-renders and morphs the component's own root element; layout-level named slots are evaluated exactly once, at the very first full-page load, and never touched again. Confirmed empirically: after starting a game, the live DOM had zero "End Game" button. This shipped in the first pass because verification only ever drove the drag gesture and checked initial screenshots, never clicked the button-bar's own dynamic buttons afterward. Fixed with Livewire's `@teleport`: the layout exposes a stable, always-present anchor (`<div id="game-actions" style="display: contents;">`) inside `.button-bar .right`, and each game page teleports its own state-dependent button(s) into it from within its own component template — unlike a layout slot, this *does* participate in the component's normal re-render/morph cycle. Verified end-to-end via headless-browser automation for Boggle, Yahtzee, and Family Feud.
- **Second discovery, months later, while working on Yahtzee's fullscreen/spectator UI**: `.button-bar .right > button:not(#chrome-toggle-btn)` — the CSS rule meant to dim/hide every action button except the fullscreen toggle itself when chrome is hidden — used a `>` direct-child combinator, which **never matched a single teleported button, for any game, the entire time**. `@teleport` wraps its content in nested `display: contents` wrapper `<div>`s (`#game-actions` itself, plus `@teleport`'s own wrapper); `display: contents` only affects box generation for layout purposes — it flattens those wrappers out of the *rendered box tree* so their children look like direct flex items of `.right` visually, but the actual DOM tree (what a `>` combinator matches against) still has them nested two levels deep. Confirmed live (`element.matches('.button-bar .right > button')` returned `false` on a real teleported button) rather than assumed. This explained why Yahtzee's "Watch only"/spectate toggle never hid in fullscreen — and meant every other game's own action buttons had probably never actually dimmed in fullscreen either, silently, since the very first `@teleport` fix above. Fixed by removing the `>` in all three affected rules (the dimming rule, its `chrome-hidden` counterpart, and the `prefers-reduced-motion` transition-disabling rule), making them plain descendant selectors.
- **The one deliberate exception**: Yahtzee's `#scores-btn` (the mobile scoreboard drawer trigger) needed to stay visible/opaque through fullscreen mode specifically — a casting/TV device is exactly the scenario fullscreen mode is for, and checking the shared scoreboard without leaving fullscreen matters most there. Added `body.chrome-hidden .button-bar .right button#scores-btn { opacity: 1; pointer-events: auto; ...concrete values... }` — needed concrete values rather than a bare `revert`, since `@layer base`'s own `button` rule (where these values would normally come from) is a *layered* rule that never wins over this unlayered one regardless of source order. Its visibility then created a follow-on layout bug: `body.scrollable.chrome-hidden`'s padding-top shrink assumes nothing remains visible in the bar once fullscreen — with `#scores-btn` now staying visible by design, it visually collided with the status text beneath it. Fixed with `body.scrollable.chrome-hidden:has(#scores-btn) { padding-top: clamp(80px, 10vmin, 96px) !important; }`.

**Button-bar spacing:**
- No minimum gap ever existed between `.left` and `.right`'s own button groups — `justify-content: space-between` alone hands *all* leftover space to whichever gap is smaller, shrinking to zero (buttons touching) once both groups together are wide enough to fill the bar, which is exactly what started happening once Yahtzee's online-room work added two more right-side buttons on a narrow screen. Fixed with an explicit `gap: 10px` on `.button-bar` itself (and a smaller `6px` at the `max-width: 480px`/`.many-buttons` breakpoint), matching the existing internal button-to-button gap within each group so all three gaps read as one consistent rhythm.
- The "Rules" button's label was shortened to a bare `?` (`aria-label="Rules"` kept for accessibility) to save horizontal room on narrow screens — a global change in `layouts/app.blade.php` affecting every game's Rules button at once, not a Yahtzee-only tweak.
- **This bug class recurred a third time, in a fourth game, because the fix above was still opt-in.** `docs/concerns.md`: Clear the Deck's own "Back to room" button (teleported into `.button-bar .right` alongside `<livewire:rooms-menu/>`) rendered 63px past the right edge of a 390px-wide viewport — fully clipped off-screen, confirmed live via `getBoundingClientRect()`, not just visually "touching the edge." Root cause: `.right` only wraps onto a second line instead of overflowing once a game's own `@script` block adds `.many-buttons` (see "Second discovery" above) — only Farkle and Yahtzee had ever added that JS, so Boggle, Family Feud, Apple Match, and Clear the Deck were all equally exposed any time their own online-room `.right` grew past one line's worth of width, and Clear the Deck (online-rooms-only, so `.right` always has at least Rooms + one game button) hit it first. Fixed by making `.right`'s `flex-wrap: wrap`/`justify-content: flex-end` **unconditional** rather than `.many-buttons`-gated (costs nothing when content already fits, since wrapping only engages once it doesn't), and switching the reserved-top-padding media rule from `body.scrollable:has(.button-bar.many-buttons)` to a structural `body.scrollable:has(.button-bar .right > :nth-child(2))` check — triggers whenever `.right` actually has 2+ children (the always-present Rooms-menu button plus any teleported game action), regardless of whether that particular game remembers to add the class. Closes the whole bug class for every game, current and future, instead of requiring each one to opt in individually. `tests/Browser/ButtonBarOverflowTest.php` covers it directly against Clear the Deck (the reported case, and the one game with no prior `.many-buttons` handling to fall back on).

**Color misuse and other Round-3-era fixes:**
- **Accent-blue was overused** — `--accent` (the same bright, saturated blue as a selected Boggle die or a found word) had leaked onto page headings and generic "primary" CTA buttons that legacy never color-codes at all (legacy's buttons are always the same muted `--btn` tone; the only per-instance color legacy applies to a button is `option.active`'s *text*, not its background). Added a `--color-bar` Tailwind alias (`text-bar`/`bg-bar`) for genuine headings/branding, moved every heading-like use to it, stripped `bg-accent`/`text-accent` off buttons and transient status messages. Left `--accent` only where legacy's own convention uses it: selected-die/order-badge/`option.active` state, a found word, the controlling Family Feud team, a won game result.
- Fixed the dashboard username literally reading `@{{ auth()->user()->username }}` on screen — `@{{` is Blade's escape syntax for `{{ }}` (meant for JS template literals sharing a page with Blade); there's no such coexistence need here.
- **On-brand error pages** (`docs/concerns.md`): no `resources/views/errors/` directory existed at all before this, so any 404/500/etc. (with `APP_DEBUG=false`, the production case) fell all the way back to Laravel's own plain, unstyled default views — no dark mode, no nav, no home link, completely disconnected from the rest of the app. Added a small `<x-error-page :code :heading :message>` anonymous component wrapping its content in `@component('layouts.app')`, the same static-page pattern `welcome.blade.php` already uses — this gets the real color tokens and the layout's own persistent "Home" button in the button-bar for free, so `resources/views/errors/{403,404,419,429,500,503}.blade.php` are each just a one-line `<x-error-page .../>` call. `tests/Feature/ErrorPagesTest.php` needed `config(['app.debug' => false])` to actually exercise these views at all — this app's own `.env` has `APP_DEBUG=true` and `phpunit.xml` never overrides it, so without forcing debug off, every test environment request would render Laravel's detailed debug trace instead, silently never touching the new views.
- The online-room code was shown in a `.heading`-classed `<div>`, and `.heading` carries `user-select: none` — never selectable/copyable by design of a class meant for page titles. Replaced with the readonly-input-plus-`[data-copy]`-button pattern already used for Boggle's challenge-share link.
- Copied the missing legacy static assets into `public/` (`manifest.json`, favicons, `og-image.png`, web-app-manifest images) — the layout's `<head>` had referenced all of these since the design-system port, but none existed, so every page 404'd on its manifest/favicon/social-share image. Added `public/sw.js` + registration — **not** a straight copy of legacy's service worker (which precaches a fixed list of static file paths that don't exist in this Livewire/Vite architecture); this one only cache-first's the fingerprinted `/build/*` Vite output (safe by construction — a new deploy always gets new filenames) and leaves every HTML response to the network, since caching a Livewire page risks serving a stale CSRF token or an unrehydratable `wire:snapshot`.
- Converted Yahtzee's scorecard and Family Feud's board/scoreboard/panel markup from generic Tailwind reinterpretations to legacy's actual classes/structure. Converted Boggle's setup/room/lobby pages to the same `.setup`/`.option`/`.option.active` classes used everywhere else.

**Low-stim confirm dialogs (`<x-dialog>`/`<x-confirm-dialog>`), replacing `wire:confirm`:**
- Family Feud's Skip Question/End Game/Reveal actions were the app's only remaining use of `wire:confirm` (native browser `confirm()`) — Boggle's and Yahtzee's own End Game/New Game buttons never had a confirmation at all, so `docs/TODO.md`'s earlier note describing all three games as using it was inaccurate and has been corrected. The passkey-removal dialog on `/profile` (`resources/views/pages/⚡profile.blade.php`) had already hand-rolled the *right* pattern once — a custom `<dialog>` styled like `.rules-dialog`, driven by vanilla JS, instead of `confirm()` — but as one-off markup + one-off `app.js` wiring with nothing to reuse.
- Generalized that pattern into two anonymous Blade components: `<x-dialog>` (the low-stim `<dialog>` primitive — muted `oklch()`-based backdrop matching `.rules-dialog`'s own, `js-dialog` class for its shared CSS, optional title) and `<x-confirm-dialog>` built on top of it (message + Cancel button + a named `confirm` slot the caller fills with whatever the actual confirming action is — `wire:click`, a form submit, anything). The `confirm` slot (rather than the component hard-coding what "confirm" does) is what lets the same component serve Family Feud's `wire:click="skipQuestion"` today and Edit Profile's clear-stats/delete-account actions next, without the component needing to know about Livewire at all.
- **Generic, delegated JS instead of one-off wiring per dialog.** `resources/js/app.js` gained a single `document`-level delegated click listener (`[data-dialog-open]` opens the target dialog by id, `[data-dialog-close]` closes the nearest ancestor `<dialog>`, a direct click on a `.js-dialog`'s own backdrop closes it) rather than binding listeners to specific elements at `DOMContentLoaded` the way the Rules dialog and passkey dialogs both do. That direct-binding approach only works because those dialogs are static, page-load-only markup; Family Feud's new confirm dialogs live inside Livewire-managed, conditionally-rendered markup (the Options panel, teleported action buttons) that gets morphed/replaced after almost every action — a listener bound directly to one of those elements at load time would silently stop working the first time Livewire re-rendered it. Delegating on `document` sidesteps that entirely.
- Confirm buttons carry **both** `wire:click` and `data-dialog-close` — clicking Confirm closes the dialog immediately, client-side, rather than waiting on the Livewire round trip to morph it away implicitly (which does also happen, since the dialog's `open` attribute isn't part of the rendered Blade output and morphdom would strip it, but relying on that felt like an implementation detail rather than an intentional close).
- Replaced all three Family Feud `wire:confirm` call sites (`revealManually`/`endGame`/`skipQuestion` in `pages::family-feud.play`) with a trigger button (`data-dialog-open`) plus an adjacent `<x-confirm-dialog>`, kept next to their trigger (rather than hoisted to one shared location) so each dialog's presence in the DOM tracks the same `@if` condition as its trigger's own visibility.
- Covered by `tests/Browser/FamilyFeudConfirmDialogsTest.php` (6 tests: each of the three dialogs opens on trigger click, Cancel closes it with no side effect, and Confirm actually performs the action) — the underlying game actions themselves were already covered at the Livewire level in `tests/Feature/Games/FamilyFeudGameTest.php`, so these are purely about the dialog's own open/cancel/confirm JS behavior. See "Automated browser testing" below for a real testing-infrastructure gotcha found while writing them (text-based `click()` hanging on an ambiguous match).

**Site footer rising over content mid-scroll on tablet landscape (`docs/concerns.md`).** `.site-footer` is `position: fixed; bottom: 0`, and `body`'s own `min-height` was a plain `100vh` — which resolves to a mobile browser's *largest* possible viewport (address-bar toolbar hidden) even while the toolbar is actually showing, so the page's own box already extended past the visible fold by roughly one toolbar's height before any real content overflow. Landscape orientation is where this bites hardest: a tablet's viewport is short enough there for the toolbar to auto-hide/reveal on scroll the same way a phone's does, and a `fixed` bottom element re-anchoring to the newly-revealed (or newly-covered) true bottom as that happens is what read as the footer visibly rising up and covering content mid-scroll. Couldn't be reproduced directly in this environment's headless Chromium — like the earlier notch/safe-area fix (see above), there's no way to simulate a real device's dynamic toolbar show/hide from a test — so this is a targeted, low-risk mitigation rather than a verified-live fix: switched `body`'s `min-height` to `100dvh` (the dynamic viewport unit, already used elsewhere in this codebase for exactly this kind of toolbar-aware sizing — see the Yahtzee section's `body.scrollable.yahtzee-play.in-game` rule), and added `transform: translateZ(0)` to `.site-footer` to promote it to its own compositor layer, mitigating mobile Safari/Chrome's known tendency to lag a bottom-fixed element's repaint behind an in-progress scroll gesture. Confirmed only that this doesn't regress the normal (non-toolbar-animating) case, at both a phone-portrait and a tablet-landscape viewport size.

**Verification-process lessons worth keeping, cumulative across all of the above:**
- A "200 OK" smoke test proves a route resolves, not that the right content is on the page — the root `/` route served Laravel's unmodified default welcome page through several early phases' own docker verification before anyone actually looked at what came back.
- The `@teleport` bug (first discovery) existed through an entire "done" pass because verification checked *what renders*, never *what happens after a state-changing click on chrome that lives outside the component's own re-rendered root*. Any future layout/slot mechanism carrying Livewire-state-dependent content needs that specific check — render once, change state, click the thing again.
- The `@teleport` bug (second discovery, the CSS selector) went unnoticed for the entire time between the two discoveries because Pest-level tests (`Livewire::test()`) invoke component methods directly and never render real DOM/CSS at all — they can prove a button's *label* is correct after a state change, but nothing about whether it's actually visible, clickable, or positioned. The Yahtzee online-rooms pass repeated a milder version of the same mistake (see that section above) before switching to mandatory live-browser verification for anything UI/CSS-related. This app's new Pest browser-testing setup (Playwright-backed, see below and `CLAUDE.md`) exists specifically to start catching this class of bug automatically instead of relying on remembering to check by hand every time.

**A stale/never-built Tailwind CSS bundle is a fifth verification-process trap, distinct from the four above: it makes an already-passing browser test lie (`docs/DEVLOG.md`'s Wordle section has the full story, 2026-08-22).** Adding a brand-new utility class to a Blade file (`w-14`, `aspect-square`, ...) that had never appeared anywhere else in the app doesn't error anywhere — without a fresh `npm run build`, Tailwind v4's content-scanned build simply never includes it, and the element renders at whatever size its *other* rules leave it (confirmed: a 56px tile collapsed to 4px, no exception, no visual placeholder). A browser test asserting the class *name* is present (`className` containing `bg-warn/20`) or that text is visible still passes the whole time, since neither of those actually depends on the missing CSS having loaded. Only a real geometry check (`getBoundingClientRect()`/`getComputedStyle()`) or genuinely looking at a screenshot catches it — added to this session's own memory as a standing rule: run `npm run build` before trusting a browser test's result whenever a change introduces a Tailwind class not already used elsewhere in the app.

**Edge-swipe back navigation (2026-08-26).** Swiping left-to-right from near the screen edge triggered the browser's own back-navigation gesture instead of whatever the swipe was meant to do in-game — reported as especially bad on Word Hive, whose honeycomb runs edge-to-edge on narrow screens, but not actually Word-Hive-specific. `overscroll-behavior-x: none` (already present on `html, body` — see the "Stop horizontal drags..." comment in `resources/css/app.css`) only covers the rubber-band/scroll-chaining style of swipe navigation (mainly Chrome); it does nothing for iOS Safari's edge-swipe-back gesture, a separate system gesture recognizer that isn't governed by `overscroll-behavior` at all. Fixed with a global `touchstart`/`touchmove` listener in `resources/js/app.js`: records the touch's start position, and if it started within 24px of the left or right edge *and* the drag is more horizontal than vertical, calls `preventDefault()` on the `touchmove`. Deliberately scoped that narrowly rather than a blanket `touch-action: pan-y` on `html`/`body` — several pages (Farkle's banked-dice row, Clear the Deck's opponents row, the mobile stats tables covered by `StatsTableMobileScrollTest.php`) rely on real horizontal touch-scrolling, and `touch-action` restrictions intersect down the DOM tree (a descendant can't loosen a restriction an ancestor already applied), so a page-wide `pan-y` would have silently broken all of those. Handled globally in `app.js` rather than per-game since the same edge-drag can start from any page. Real OS-level back-navigation isn't reproducible in headless Chromium, so `tests/Browser/EdgeSwipeNavigationTest.php` dispatches synthetic `TouchEvent`s instead and asserts the listener's own `preventDefault()` decision directly (edge + horizontal → prevented; edge + vertical scroll, or horizontal but not near an edge → left alone) — verified this doesn't regress the horizontal-scroll or Boggle drag-gesture browser tests.

## Automated browser testing

Pest v5 (already this project's test framework) ships first-party browser testing support powered by Playwright, via the `pestphp/pest-plugin-browser` package — chosen over Laravel Dusk specifically because it's the same `Livewire::visit(...)`/`it(...)` syntax as every other Pest test in this suite (`php artisan test` runs both kinds together), rather than a separate PHPUnit-style test case class and a separate `php artisan dusk` invocation. See `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` for the concrete setup (what's installed, where browser tests live, and when to reach for one instead of `Livewire::test()`) — that's the file kept current for "how do I write one of these," so it isn't duplicated here.

Also installed `pestphp/pest-plugin-agent` alongside it specifically to close the exact gap flagged during Yahtzee's online-rooms work above: an agent claiming a UI change works from reading the code, or from a Pest assertion that never touched real DOM/CSS, rather than actually checking. Its `--agent='...'` flag runs a one-off snippet (optionally driving a real Playwright browser via `visit(...)`) inside the project's real Pest configuration and reports a genuine pass/fail — a fast verification probe for mid-task use, not a replacement for a real test.

**First real coverage: Yahtzee, both local play and online rooms** (`tests/Browser/Yahtzee{LocalPlay,Mobile,RoomBrowser}Test.php`), 12 tests. Local play drives a full solo game start-to-"Game Over" through 13 real click→Livewire round trips (rolling then scoring whichever category is first pickable each round, since dice are real `random_int()` rolls with no deterministic target) and confirms a kept die's `.kept` class survives a second roll's re-render. Online-room coverage seeds a `Room`/`GameSession.state`/`RoomSeat` trio directly at the database level — the exact shape `pages::yahtzee.room`'s own `onRoomStarting()` writes — rather than driving two real logged-in browsers at once, and drives a single real seat's browser against that seeded state: hosting a room and joining one by code through the real setup-screen UI, a seated player rolling/scoring for real, an unseated visitor correctly landing in the read-only spectator view (no `#rollBtn` in the DOM at all, not just disabled), and the mobile scores drawer opening and staying open across a simulated `onStateUpdated()` call (standing in for a live broadcast, which `BROADCAST_CONNECTION=null` suppresses in the test env exactly as it does for `tests/Feature/Games/YahtzeeRoomTest.php`'s own manual-dispatch approach).

Two real, non-obvious limitations of `pestphp/pest-plugin-browser` v5.0.0 surfaced while building this out, both confirmed via the `--agent` probe before being designed around rather than fought:
- **Two concurrent `visit()` pages reliably hang** (30-60s+, had to be killed) the moment a content assertion (`assertSee`/`assertDontSee`) runs on one page followed by a fresh `visit()` for a second — reproduced identically whether the two pages were opened via two separate `visit()` calls or the array form (`visit([$url1, $url2])`), and independent of the session driver (`array` vs `file`). Plain concurrent visits with no login/assertions interleaved (`visit(['/', '/about'])`, immediate `assertSee` on each) work fine and fast, so this isn't concurrency itself failing — it's specifically that pattern of interleaving. Given `BROADCAST_CONNECTION=null` in the test env means no real broadcast would reach a second live browser during a test anyway, genuine two-simultaneous-browser multiplayer testing wasn't worth fighting for: every room test here uses exactly one real browser page plus DB-seeded state for "the rest of the room," mirroring the Feature test's own manual-dispatch approach.
- **`->on()->mobile()` silently drops every click.** Confirmed with the simplest possible case — a plain `<a><button>Home</button></a>` link with no Livewire involved at all still didn't navigate after `click('Home')` under `on()->mobile()`, with no exception thrown. `resize(width, height)` (also in the plugin's API) sets the same narrow viewport without touching device/touch emulation and clicks through it work identically to desktop, so every mobile-viewport test in this app uses `resize(390, 844)` instead of `on()->mobile()`.
- **A plain-text `click('Some Label')` hangs (not errors) the moment more than one element on the page contains that text — including a match inside a currently-closed `<dialog>` or an unrelated `<strong>` in the Rules panel.** Found while writing `FamilyFeudConfirmDialogsTest` (see "Low-stim confirm dialogs" below): `click('Options')` hung 60s+ because the Rules panel's own copy also contains the word "Options" in a `<strong>` tag; `click('Cancel')` hung the same way once a second confirm-dialog (closed, `display: none` via the UA default `dialog:not([open])` rule) existed elsewhere on the page with its own "Cancel" button. Unlike a genuinely ambiguous CSS *selector* — which Playwright rejects immediately with a clear "strict mode violation: resolved to N elements" error — a text-based click silently waits on whichever match it resolved to (sometimes the wrong, never-visible one) until the browser timeout, which reads as an indefinite hang with no diagnostic. No fix from the plugin side; every click in that test file targets a unique `id` instead of a label once more than one dialog/element can share the same wording.

Setup specifics: `composer require pestphp/pest-plugin-browser pestphp/pest-plugin-agent --dev`, `npm install playwright@latest --save-dev`, `npx playwright install chromium`. Added a `Browser` testsuite to `phpunit.xml` (mirroring `Unit`/`Feature`) and added `'Browser'` to `tests/Pest.php`'s existing `pest()->extend(TestCase::class)->in(...)` call — without that second part, a browser test has no Laravel app container bootstrapped at all and fails immediately with `Target class [config] does not exist.`, since (unlike `Unit`, which opts in per-file with its own `uses(TestCase::class, ...)`) `Feature` had been the only directory wired up globally. `tests/Browser/Screenshots` added to `.gitignore` (Pest's own convention for screenshot output). Seeded with two real tests, not placeholders: `YahtzeeRulesDialogTest` (opens/closes the rules `<dialog>` via a real click and asserts visible text before/after — directly the same class of bug as the scoreboard-dialog's `[open]`-scoping bug above, just guarding a dialog that never actually broke) and `HomepageSmokeTest` (`assertNoJavaScriptErrors()`/`assertNoConsoleLogs()`, plus guest auth-link visibility) — full suite confirmed green together (`php artisan test --compact`, 262 tests) and the `--agent` probe confirmed working with a real one-off snippet before calling this done.

**A top-level helper `function` declared in a Browser test file is not actually process-isolated the way it first appears.** Four existing files (`YahtzeeRoomBrowserTest`, `AppleMatchRoomBrowserTest`, `FamilyFeudRoomBrowserTest`, `ClearTheDeckRoomBrowserTest`) each independently declare an identical top-level `function loginAsAndVisit(...)` — and this works, seemingly proving Pest gives every Browser test file its own PHP process. It doesn't, reliably: adding a fifth file with the same function name (while building the Analytics dashboard's own browser test, see below) produced a real `Cannot redeclare function` fatal error the moment the *full* suite ran (`php artisan test`, no path filter) — even though `php artisan test tests/Browser/AnalyticsDashboardTest.php` in isolation, or the existing four-file set with the fifth removed, both passed cleanly. Whatever mechanism lets the existing four coexist (Pest's parallel/browser-process scheduling, not per-file isolation as such) is apparently load/scheduling-dependent rather than a guarantee. Fixed by giving the new file's helpers unique names (`loginAsAndVisitAnalytics`, `seedAnalyticsAdmin`) instead of relying on that behavior — the safe rule going forward: never reuse a top-level helper function name across Browser test files, even though four pre-existing files make it look safe.

**Full audit of every `@teleport('#game-actions')` action across all six games, prompted directly by the Boggle "New Game" bug (see that section above) and the user's own follow-up question — "was there no test for this, and do we need more testing?"
