(2026-08-18).** Line/branch code coverage tooling (`php artisan test --coverage`, Xdebug already installed) was considered and explicitly rejected as the tool for this specific gap: `resetGame()` was already 100% covered by `Livewire::test()` before the bug was found, since that call *does* execute the PHP method — the bug lived entirely in the client-side Alpine/Livewire teleport wiring, which a PHP coverage report cannot see at all. The actual fix was a manual inventory: every interactive element inside every game's own teleport block, cross-referenced against `tests/Browser/` for a **real click**, not just an `assertSee`.
- **Inventory (17 distinct actions across 6 games) found 10 with no real click test**, split cleanly into two repeating shapes: a local "New Game" button (Yahtzee, Farkle, Family Feud all had this — each existing full-playthrough test stopped at `assertSee('Game Over')`/`'Game Over'`-equivalent and never took the one further click), and an online room's "Back to room" link (Yahtzee, Family Feud, Clear the Deck, Best Match — Clear the Deck's own test came closest, checking the button's on-screen *position* at a narrow viewport, but still never actually clicked it). Farkle's "Scores" drawer button and both Farkle's and Yahtzee's own "Watch only"/"Player view" toggle (the exact pattern this app built once, for Yahtzee, then copied into Farkle and Clear the Deck — only Clear the Deck's copy ever got a real click test) rounded out the list.
- **Every gap got a real click test, run before being trusted** — same discipline as the Boggle fix itself, not just "add an assertion and move on": each new test was actually executed and watched pass on the first real run before being kept, specifically so a second silent bug wouldn't slip through the same way the first one did. None of the 10 gaps turned out to be a second hidden bug — every one was genuinely untested-but-working code, confirmed rather than assumed.
- **New Game's actual post-click state differs by game, confirmed by reading each `newGame()` method rather than assumed from Boggle's own shape**: Boggle's own `resetGame()` goes straight back into a fresh, already-started game (per Task 18's earlier work), but Yahtzee's/Farkle's/Family Feud's `newGame()` all instead return to their local setup screen (`$this->started = false`) — an early version of these new tests asserted `#rollBtn` visible immediately after the click and failed on Yahtzee's real (correct) behavior, corrected to assert the setup screen's own `Start Game` button instead once the actual method was read.
- Added `tests/Browser/BoggleNewGameTest.php` (new file), `tests/Browser/FarkleRoomActionsTest.php` (new file — Watch only/Player view, Scores drawer), and one new test each in `tests/Browser/YahtzeeRoomBrowserTest.php` (Watch only/Player view, Back to room), `tests/Browser/ClearTheDeckRoomBrowserTest.php` and `tests/Browser/AppleMatchRoomBrowserTest.php` (Back to room); extended the existing full-playthrough tests in `tests/Browser/YahtzeeLocalPlayTest.php`, `tests/Browser/FarkleLocalPlayTest.php`, and `tests/Browser/FamilyFeudConfirmDialogsTest.php` with one further click each (New Game) rather than duplicating their setup in a new test. Full suite (641 tests) confirmed green together, not just each new file in isolation.

## Analytics

First-party, cookieless analytics (`docs/specs/analytics.md`) — daily pageviews/visitors, per-page and per-country breakdowns, referrers, UTM campaigns, a live-visitor count, and a `/analytics` dashboard. The design goal (spelled out at length in the spec) is that the whole system is consent-exempt: no cookies or client-side storage, IP addresses hashed with a salt that rotates daily and is hard-deleted after 48 hours, so no visitor is ever linkable across days by anyone, including the site owner. Everything lives under `app/Analytics/` rather than `App\Models`/`App\Http\Middleware`/etc., per the spec's own instruction to keep it self-contained.

**Deliberate deviations from the spec, adapting it to this app's actual stack instead of inserting it verbatim:**
- **Livewire 4 single-file "page" components, not Livewire 3.** This app's Livewire 4 already has its own established convention — `resources/views/pages/⚡name.blade.php`, a `new class extends Component { ... } ?>` PHP block followed directly by the Blade template in one file, routed via `Route::livewire()` — used by every other authenticated page (`⚡dashboard.blade.php`, `⚡profile.blade.php`, etc.). The dashboard (`resources/views/pages/⚡analytics.blade.php`) follows that exactly rather than the spec's Livewire-3-era "a class plus a separate Blade view" shape.
- **Inline server-rendered SVG chart, not Chart.js.** Asked the user directly rather than guessing: this app has no charting library and a deliberate low-stim/no-motion design ethos (see "Site-wide design system & chrome" above), and the spec itself offers inline SVG as its own fallback for an app with no Vite pipeline. The user picked SVG over adding a new npm dependency even though this app *does* have Vite — it fits the aesthetic better and needs zero animation-suppression work, since a static, server-computed `<polyline>` has no animation to suppress in the first place. `pages::analytics`'s `getChartProperty()` computes point coordinates and a handful of evenly-spaced axis-label ticks in PHP; no client-side charting code at all.
- **MaxMind-only country resolution, no `header` driver.** The spec's default driver reads a proxy-set country header (`CF-IPCountry` or similar), falling back to MaxMind only if `analytics:doctor` (see below) finds no such header on a deployed URL. Asked the user directly here too: rather than depend on a header that only exists if this app happens to sit behind a proxy that sets one — and could silently disappear if that proxy configuration ever changes — `App\Analytics\Geo\CountryResolver` only implements `'maxmind'` and `'none'`. `analytics:doctor` still dumps any header matching `/country/i` for diagnostic purposes, just not as something a driver depends on.
- **`ANALYTICS_ADMIN_EMAILS` env allow-list, not an `is_admin` column.** This is a single-owner personal project with no admin concept anywhere else in the schema — the spec itself offers this as the fallback for exactly that situation. `Gate::define('view-analytics', ...)` in `AppServiceProvider::boot()` lower-cases and compares against a comma-separated list from `config('analytics.admin_emails')`.
- **`jaybizzle/crawler-detect` added as a real dependency**, per the spec's own recommendation and the user's confirmation — wrapped behind `App\Analytics\BotDetector::isBot()` so it stays swappable, with a small supplementary pattern list (`HeadlessChrome`, common uptime monitors) for anything the library itself misses.
- **No privacy-policy page updated.** The spec asks to add disclosure copy to "the app's privacy policy" — this app doesn't have one anywhere yet, and adding a new public page was judged out of scope for implementing the analytics system itself (the user agreed, opting to skip rather than build a minimal page). The drafted copy from the spec is reproduced here, ready to drop into a real privacy page whenever one exists:

  > **Analytics.** We count visits to understand which pages are useful. We do this ourselves, on our own servers, without cookies or any other storage on your device, and without any third-party analytics service.
  >
  > When you load a page we record the page address, the country your request came from, and the site that linked you, if any. To estimate how many separate people visited on a given day, we combine your IP address, browser identifier, and a secret value that we replace every day into a one-way fingerprint. Your IP address is never written to disk. The daily secret is destroyed after 48 hours, at which point the fingerprints cannot be linked to each other or to you, even by us.
  >
  > This means we cannot recognise you when you return, cannot follow you to other sites, and hold nothing that identifies you.

**`analytics:doctor` is a route-plus-command pair, not a bare console command.** The spec describes `analytics:doctor` as an artisan command that "dumps, for the current request context, the resolved client IP..." — but a CLI process has no request context to inspect; there's no real HTTP request happening inside `php artisan analytics:doctor`. Split into `GET /analytics/doctor` (`App\Analytics\Http\Controllers\DoctorController`, deliberately unauthenticated — it only reflects the *requester's own* connection info back to them, nothing about any other visitor, so there's nothing for a gate to protect) returning the diagnostic JSON, and `analytics:doctor {url?}` (`App\Analytics\Console\Commands\AnalyticsDoctor`) which fetches that endpoint over a real `Http::get()` call against a deployed URL and pretty-prints the result. This is also why the route is excluded from tracking via `config('analytics.except')` rather than needing special-casing in the middleware.

**A `date` cast on an Eloquent model is not safe here, and broke cross-database query correctness silently.** `AnalyticsEvent`/`AnalyticsDailyTotal`/`AnalyticsDailyDimension`/`AnalyticsSalt` all originally cast their `date` column via `'date' => 'date'`. Eloquent's `date` cast serializes a Carbon instance back to a *full datetime string* (`'2026-08-16 00:00:00'`) on write, not a bare `'2026-08-16'`. A real `DATE` column (MariaDB, Postgres) silently truncates that on insert, so the bug never appeared against the app's real database — but SQLite (this app's local-dev and, critically, its *entire test suite's* database per `phpunit.xml`) has no real column typing and stores the string verbatim, so every `where('date', $date)` comparison against a plain `'Y-m-d'` string — which `analytics:rollup`, `analytics:prune`, and `AnalyticsRepository` all do, extensively — silently matched nothing. Found because `analytics:rollup`'s totals came back as 0 despite seeded events existing. Fixed by dropping the cast entirely on all four models: every write path already produces a clean `'Y-m-d'` string (`now()->toDateString()`), so `date` is just left as a plain string attribute end-to-end rather than round-tripped through Carbon at all.

**Laravel's `Application::terminate()` never clears its own `$terminatingCallbacks` list, and `dispatch_mode: 'after_response'` depends on that.** `RecordPageviewJob::dispatch($data)->afterResponse()` (spec section 6.6) registers its execution via `Illuminate\Foundation\Application::terminating()`, which appends to `$terminatingCallbacks` and is drained by `terminate()` — but `terminate()`'s `while` loop (`vendor/laravel/framework/.../Application.php`) never actually empties that array afterward. In a real deployment this is harmless: Laravel Cloud runs a fresh PHP-FPM process per request (no Octane), so `terminate()` only ever runs once before the process exits. In a *test*, though, a single test method issuing two or more sequential `$this->get()` calls reuses one `Application` instance for its whole duration — so the first request's analytics job silently re-fires on every later request's `terminate()` call in that same test, duplicating its `AnalyticsEvent` row once per subsequent request. Caught via `tests/Feature/Analytics/RecordPageviewMiddlewareTest.php`'s referrer test (two sequential requests, second one's row unexpectedly getting the *first* request's referrer value) — traced by logging every `terminate()`/job-write call and finding the first request's write firing a second time exactly when the second request's own `terminate()` ran. Not something to "fix" in application code (this is a real, if obscure, property of `Application::terminate()` itself, and would presumably also bite a future Octane deployment the same way tests do here) — worked around by making the *test environment specifically* use `dispatch_mode: 'queue'` instead (`<env name="ANALYTICS_DISPATCH" value="queue"/>` in `phpunit.xml`), which dispatches straight through `Bus::dispatch()` with no `terminating()` registration at all; `QUEUE_CONNECTION=sync` (already the test default) still runs it synchronously, just without the accumulating-callback behavior. Worth remembering if this app ever adopts Octane: `after_response` dispatch would need the same reconsideration there that it needed here for tests, for the identical reason (one long-lived `Application` instance handling many requests).

**Symfony's `RedirectResponse` defaults its `Content-Type` to `text/html`, which the spec's own literal skip-rule wording doesn't account for.** Section 6.2 lists two independent skip conditions — response status must be 2xx *or* 3xx, and `Content-Type` must be `text/html` — implying a redirect (3xx, and it turns out genuinely `text/html` by default, a small "Redirecting…" body) would pass both and get recorded as a pageview for a page the visitor never actually saw. `RecordPageview`'s status check was narrowed to 2xx only (`$status < 200 || $status >= 300`), matching section 10's own acceptance test ("redirects... write nothing") over section 6.2's looser prose, which is the more clearly-intentional of the two given how explicit that test list is.

**An ad-hoc `Route::get(...)` registered inside a test doesn't get the `'web'` middleware group — and therefore doesn't get tracked — unless `->middleware('web')` is added explicitly.** Only routes loaded from the file passed to `withRouting(web: ...)` (`routes/web.php`) get that group applied automatically; a route registered at runtime via the `Route` facade from anywhere else (a test method, in this case) gets no middleware at all by default. `tests/Feature/Analytics/RecordPageviewMiddlewareTest.php`'s dynamic-route and POST/JSON/redirect/404 tests both needed `->middleware('web')` added to their inline routes once this surfaced — without it, `App\Analytics\Http\Middleware\RecordPageview` (appended to the `'web'` group in `bootstrap/app.php`) simply never ran, and the tests "passed" for the wrong reason (nothing was tracked because nothing tried to be, not because the skip logic under test actually fired).

**`selectRaw()` aggregate queries against an Eloquent model need `->toBase()`, not just a docblock, to satisfy Larastan.** `analytics:rollup` and `AnalyticsRepository` both run `AnalyticsEvent::where(...)->selectRaw('count(*) as pageviews, ...')` — the results aren't really `AnalyticsEvent` instances (they represent a whole day or dimension-value's aggregate, not one event row), but Eloquent still hydrates them as such by default, and Larastan correctly flags `$row->pageviews` as an access to an undeclared property. Rather than adding `@property-read` annotations that would be misleading on every *normal* `AnalyticsEvent` instance (which never has a `pageviews` attribute), every aggregate query chain got `->toBase()` added before `->get()`/`->first()`, converting to the underlying query builder and returning plain `stdClass` rows instead — the more honest type for what these actually are, and it satisfies static analysis without special-casing the model. `AnalyticsPrune::deleteInChunks()` hit a related generics issue (a `Builder<AnalyticsEvent|AnalyticsSalt>` parameter rejecting a `Builder<AnalyticsEvent>` argument, since Larastan's model generics aren't covariant) — fixed by making the method itself generic (`@template TModel of Model` / `@param Builder<TModel> $query`) instead of unioning the two concrete model types.

**Country names come from PHP's `intl` extension (`Locale::getDisplayRegion()`), not a hand-maintained array.** The spec calls for "full names via a static ISO map" for the countries panel. Rather than hand-copying a ~250-entry ISO 3166 table into the codebase, `App\Analytics\Geo\CountryNames::name()` uses `Locale::getDisplayRegion("und-{$code}", 'en')`, backed by ICU's own built-in region data — genuinely static, deterministic, no network call, just not a literal PHP array in this repo. Falls back to returning the raw code if `ext-intl` isn't available (`class_exists(Locale::class)` guard), since the extension isn't a declared Composer dependency and its presence on any given deployment target isn't guaranteed.

**Operational notes for whoever deploys this, not yet acted on:**
- `analytics:doctor <deployed-url>` needs to actually be run against the live Laravel Cloud URL once deployed, confirming `bootstrap/app.php`'s `trustProxies(at: '*')` resolves a real client IP (not the load balancer's own address) before trusting any numbers the dashboard shows — this is section 6.1's stated acceptance gate for the whole recording pipeline and hasn't been exercised outside this local/test environment.
- `MAXMIND_ACCOUNT_ID`/`MAXMIND_LICENSE_KEY` are unset in `.env`/`.env.example` (left blank) — the countries panel will stay empty until they're set and `analytics:geoip-update` has run at least once.
- Laravel Cloud's filesystem is ephemeral, so the weekly `analytics:geoip-update` schedule (`bootstrap/app.php`) alone isn't sufficient — the `.mmdb` won't exist after a fresh deploy until that schedule next fires. Needs either a deploy-step run of the same command or the file moved to durable storage (S3) and pulled on boot; neither is built here.
- `ANALYTICS_ADMIN_EMAILS=psrearick@gmail.com` was set in the local `.env` (gitignored) so the dashboard is actually reachable in local dev; the equivalent needs setting as a real Laravel Cloud environment variable separately, the same caveat `SESSION_LIFETIME` already carries (see "Infrastructure & tooling" above).

Covered by `tests/Feature/Analytics/{RecordPageviewMiddlewareTest,RollupAndPruneTest,DashboardTest}.php` (16 tests covering every case docs/specs/analytics.md section 10 lists) and `tests/Browser/AnalyticsDashboardTest.php` (3 tests: seeded figures render and the range selector's `wire:click` actually flips `aria-pressed` state, a non-admin gets the app's real `Forbidden` error page rather than a generic 403, and the dashboard collapses to one column under 768px) — `php artisan test --compact` confirmed green as a full suite (612 tests) alongside the rest of the app's existing coverage, not just in isolation.

**Real production bug, found immediately after first deploy: `AnalyticsRepository::totals()` intermittently returned `__PHP_Incomplete_Class` instead of `Totals`.** Reproduced deterministically — clearing the Redis cache fixed the very next page load, but the load after *that* broke again, every time. First hypothesis: `totals()` was the only cached method that serialized an app-defined class instance (`new Totals(...)`) directly into Redis, and a brand-new class is only ever autoloaded on first real use, unlike a framework class loaded unconditionally on every bootstrap -- so some request-serving process might have a stale view of the autoloader that hadn't picked up `Totals` yet. Fixed by caching a plain array and constructing `Totals` from it *after* reading from cache.

**That hypothesis didn't survive contact with the next request: `timeseries()` then failed the identical way, and it only ever cached `Illuminate\Support\Collection` and plain arrays** -- no app-defined class involved at all, and `Collection` is a framework class loaded before any app code runs on every single request, with no scenario where an autoloader wouldn't already have it. That rules out "class not yet autoloaded" as the actual mechanism; something about round-tripping *any* object (not just a new one) through this production Redis is unreliable, for a reason not fully diagnosed from the application side alone -- checked `config/database.php`'s Redis options for anything unusual (a custom serializer, mismatched client config) and found nothing; a genuine infrastructure-level cause (e.g. multiple app instances not fully consistent with each other) remains plausible but unconfirmed. Rather than chase it further per-method as each one's turn came up, every `Cache::remember()` call in `AnalyticsRepository` now stores and retrieves a plain array exclusively -- `timeseries()`/`dimension()` cache `->all()` and rewrap with `collect()` after reading, matching `totals()`'s own pattern -- removing every object from the cache layer at once rather than incrementally discovering which one breaks next. If this recurs even for a plain array, that would conclusively point past application code to the Redis/cache infrastructure itself.

**Deployment verification, closing out `docs/TODO.md`'s former #12 (2026-08-17).** `docs/specs/analytics.md`'s own acceptance gate (section 6.1) had never actually been exercised outside local dev/tests until now.
- **`analytics:doctor` run against the live `lowstimplay.com` twice, from two different vantage points, since the first run alone was ambiguous.** The first run was via Laravel Cloud's web "Commands" tab (equivalent to `cloud command:run` — executes on a live app instance, inside Laravel Cloud's own AWS infrastructure), which resolved a client IP in AWS's us-east-2 range. That's expected, not a red flag, once traced through: `analytics:doctor` itself makes the HTTP request from wherever it's run, so for that run the "client" genuinely was the Cloud instance's own outbound connection — its egress IP is an AWS address by construction. The single `X-Forwarded-For` entry matched the resolved IP exactly and differed from `REMOTE_ADDR` (an internal Cloudflare/ingress hop), proving `trustProxies(at: '*')` correctly walks past the internal proxy layer to the true originating connection rather than stopping on an internal address — the mechanism that matters, just not yet proof against a genuine outside client. **Confirmed for real** with a second check from an actual home network browser (opening `/analytics/doctor` directly): `resolved_client_ip` matched the real public IP, not an internal Cloudflare/Laravel Cloud address. `bootstrap/app.php`'s `trustProxies(at: '*')` is verified working end-to-end in production.
- **`MAXMIND_ACCOUNT_ID`/`MAXMIND_LICENSE_KEY` and `ANALYTICS_ADMIN_EMAILS` set as real Laravel Cloud environment variables** (previously only in the local, gitignored `.env`). Confirmed working: the dashboard is reachable in production and the countries panel is populated with real data.
- **`analytics:geoip-update` wired as a Laravel Cloud Build Command, not a Deploy Command — the distinction matters and isn't obvious from the names.** Laravel Cloud's own docs are explicit that Deploy Command filesystem writes are discarded before the app goes live (`php artisan storage:link` is called out by name as pointless there for the identical reason), while Build Command output is baked into the deployed image and therefore present on every replica from boot. Confirmed the app was configured correctly (Build Command, not Deploy Command) before treating this as done.
- **The weekly `analytics:geoip-update` schedule (`bootstrap/app.php`) was removed, not kept as redundant safety net.** Laravel Cloud's filesystem is ephemeral, reset on every redeploy, and **not shared across replicas** (confirmed via Laravel Cloud's own docs, which use `php artisan down`'s maintenance-mode state as the illustrating example: a write on one replica never reaches its siblings). A scheduled run only ever updates whichever single replica happens to catch it, never reaches any other replica, and gets wiped on the next deploy regardless — so in production the schedule accomplished nothing beyond what the Build Command already provides. This does mean the `.mmdb` now only actually refreshes on deploy rather than weekly, accepted as fine rather than moved to Object Storage: MaxMind's own GeoLite2 data updates roughly monthly, and this app deploys at least that often, so deploy-cadence freshness isn't a real loss for a single-owner hobby app. Object Storage (S3-compatible, Laravel Cloud's own recommended pattern for a file every replica needs persisted independent of deploys) remains the correct fix if that assumption ever stops holding — e.g. deploys become rare, or the `.mmdb` needs to update within a single deploy's lifetime.

**The chart shipped with no y-axis reference numbers** — caught by the user immediately after deploy, looking at the real thing rather than a description of it. `getChartProperty()` only ever computed x-axis (date/hour) tick labels; there was no numeric scale for the two polylines at all, undermining "Numerals are the subject" (section 9.4's own stated design principle) since the lines' shape was visible but their actual values weren't. Added `yTicks` (five evenly-spaced values, max down to 0) rendered as a flex-column label gutter to the left of the `<svg>` paired with matching horizontal gridlines drawn inside it (`stroke-opacity: 0.12`, `vector-effect="non-scaling-stroke"` so the line width doesn't distort under the chart's non-uniform `preserveAspectRatio="none"` scaling) — kept as plain flexbox-aligned HTML/SVG rather than positioning text inside the SVG's own coordinate space, since that space is intentionally stretched non-uniformly and would distort glyph shapes.

**Privacy policy page built (`docs/TODO.md`'s former #13, 2026-08-18).** `/privacy` (`pages::privacy`, mirroring `pages::about`'s own plain single-file-component-with-no-logic shape), linked from the site-wide footer (`layouts/app.blade.php`, present on every page) alongside the existing About/Support Me links. Section 12's drafted analytics disclosure copy is reused verbatim, not paraphrased — it was already deliberately worded, including its "your IP address is never written to disk" and "cannot recognise you when you return" claims, which are only true because of exactly how `App\Analytics\*` actually works. One line added beyond the spec's own draft: MaxMind's country lookup is a local, downloaded-and-kept `.mmdb` file (see "Deployment verification" above), not a live third-party query, worth a reader knowing since "we look up your country" reads ambiguously about where that lookup happens without it.
- **Covers the rest of what this app actually does with data, not just analytics** — account fields (name/email/password, optional username/avatar/public-profile toggle), passkeys/2FA credential storage, per-player game history in shared rooms, cookies (a single first-party session cookie, no tracking/ad cookies), and the other third-party services in play: Resend for transactional email (verification/reset), and OpenRouter (Family Feud's `JudgeService`, see that section above) for guess-judging — called out specifically that only the typed guess text is sent, never name/email/any other account data. Written in the same plain, first-person voice as `pages::about` rather than legal boilerplate, with an explicit "written in plain language, not legal boilerplate" framing up front — this is a single-developer hobby app, and the analytics spec's own section 12 already flags that a real legal review would be needed before treating this as a substitute for one.
- **Self-service data controls pointed at, not duplicated**: rather than re-describing "Clear All Stats"/"Delete Account" mechanics (see "User profiles & stats" above), the page just names them as the place to go.
- `.about-content h2` is the only new CSS added — the page reuses `.about-content`'s existing container/paragraph styling verbatim (same width/gap/color), since subheadings breaking prose into sections is the only thing About's own single-`<p>`-per-paragraph page never needed.
- Added `tests/Feature/PrivacyPolicyPageTest.php` (renders with the verbatim drafted copy and the account/cookie sections; the footer link appears for both guests and authenticated users) and `tests/Browser/PrivacyPolicyFooterLinkTest.php` (the footer's plain `justify-content: space-between` row — no `flex-wrap`, no explicit shrink handling — doesn't overflow horizontally at a real 375px phone width now that a fourth item was added to it; and the link actually navigates to a working page), per `CLAUDE.md`'s browser-testing rules for exactly this class of layout change.

**State/region-level geography added (`docs/TODO.md`'s former Task 13, `docs/concerns.md`, 2026-08-21).** The countries panel only ever had country-level precision, since `App\Analytics\Geo\CountryResolver` read MaxMind's `GeoLite2-Country` database, which has no subdivision (state/province) field at all. Feasibility was checked *before* any code was written (a live authenticated request against `https://download.maxmind.com/geoip/databases/GeoLite2-City/download` returned MaxMind's normal `302` redirect, not `401`/`403`) — confirmed the existing `MAXMIND_ACCOUNT_ID`/`MAXMIND_LICENSE_KEY` already had access to the City edition, the only one with subdivision data, with no new signup needed.
- **Switched the maxmind driver from `GeoLite2-Country` to `GeoLite2-City` entirely, everywhere the edition name appears** — `AnalyticsGeoipUpdate::EDITION`, `config('analytics.geo.database_path')`'s default filename, and `CountryResolver` calling `$reader->city($ip)` instead of `$reader->country($ip)`. This needed no new Composer dependency (`geoip2/geoip2` was already required) and doesn't change country resolution at all: `GeoIp2\Model\City extends GeoIp2\Model\Country`, so `$city->country->isoCode` is identical to what `$reader->country($ip)->country->isoCode` returned before. `geoip2/geoip2`'s `Reader` self-detects which edition a `.mmdb` actually is from the file's own metadata and throws if a method doesn't match it (`->country()` demands `"Country"` in the type string, `->city()` demands `"City"`) — so the edition swap and the resolver's method-call swap had to land together, not incrementally.
- **New nullable `analytics_events.region` column**, an ISO 3166-2 *composite* (`"US-CA"`, country + subdivision code together) rather than a bare subdivision code — a bare `"CA"` is ambiguous (California vs. a province elsewhere), so `CountryResolver::resolveRegion()` always returns the paired form or `null`. `null` covers both "IP couldn't be resolved at all" and "resolved fine, but this country/IP has no subdivision data in the free GeoLite2 dataset" — the latter is common (confirmed live: `8.8.8.8`/`1.1.1.1`, two well-known anycast IPs, both resolve country fine but return no subdivision at all).
- **`CountryResolver` memoizes the single most recent IP's `City` lookup** (`$lastIp`/`$lastCity`) — `resolve()` and `resolveRegion()` are both called once per request, back to back, in `RecordPageview::terminate()` for the identical IP, and without this each call would hit the mmdb reader independently for no reason. Deliberately just the one most-recent entry, not a growing cache, since the singleton only ever serves one request's pair of calls at a time.
- **`AnalyticsRollup::DIMENSIONS` gained a `'region' => 'region'` entry** — the one line actually needed, since `rollupDimension()` is already fully generic over column/dimension-name pairs (grouping, the 500-row overflow-into-`'(other)'` cap, everything) and the whole dimension list is iterated automatically. `AnalyticsRepository::regions()` mirrors `countries()` exactly (`dimension($period, 'region', 'region', $limit)`), including the same plain-array `Cache::remember()` shape every other method here already uses (see the `__PHP_Incomplete_Class` production bug above) — new code following an established pattern, not a new design.
- **No subdivision-name lookup table added, on purpose.** `CountryNames`'s own docblock already rejected hand-maintaining a ~250-entry country table in favor of ICU (`Locale::getDisplayRegion()`); subdivisions would be a much larger table, and — confirmed live before assuming it — PHP's `intl` extension has no display-name support for them at all: `Locale::getDisplayRegion("en-US-u-sd-usca", "en")` (the real BCP47 mechanism for a subdivision, per `getKeywords()` correctly parsing out `sd => usca`) still just returns `"United States"`, silently ignoring the subdivision extension entirely. New `App\Analytics\Geo\RegionNames::label()` instead shows the raw subdivision code next to the country's full name — `"CA, United States"` — enough to actually identify a US state, legible for anywhere else, and never at risk of showing a wrong or made-up name.
- **Confirmed end-to-end against real public IPs, not just unit-level fallback behavior** — after running `analytics:geoip-update` locally (needed `-d memory_limit=1G` for the one-off CLI run; the City edition's archive is large enough to exceed PHP's default 128M CLI limit, unlike Country's), a handful of real IPs resolved exactly as expected: `12.0.0.0` → `US-MT`, `76.14.0.1` → `US-CA`, `24.0.0.1` → `US-NJ`, while `8.8.8.8`/`1.1.1.1` correctly came back with a country but no region.
- New dashboard "Regions" panel, `<x-analytics-breakdown>` reused as-is (no new CSS) — a straight sibling of the existing Countries panel, added to `⚡analytics.blade.php`'s panel grid right after it.
- Tests: `tests/Unit/Analytics/RegionNamesTest.php` (label formatting, including the no-subdivision and null/empty cases), `tests/Unit/Analytics/CountryResolverTest.php` (both `resolve()`/`resolveRegion()` stay safely `null` for a null IP, a non-maxmind driver, and a missing `.mmdb` file — the only paths testable without a real database file, which this repo deliberately never commits), and extended `tests/Feature/Analytics/{RollupAndPruneTest,DashboardTest}.php` for the new `region` dimension end to end (rollup grouping, dashboard rendering the composite label).
